# mayatk — Changelog

## 2026

- **2026-08-06 — Scene Exporter: *Add New Preset* / *Delete Current Preset* are gone from the preset option box.** A `.fbxexportpreset` is a plain file and the option box already opens the folder holding them (*Open Preset Directory*, `b007`), so the two one-shots did — worse — what the file browser does for free: `b003` could only copy an existing preset file in, and `b004` deleted the selected one outright, no confirmation, no undo. Both handlers are removed; `b007`'s tooltip and the panel's "no presets found" warning now point at the folder as the place to add, rename, and delete. Managing the directory directly is consequently the *only* add/delete path, which the scan cache already handles — the directory's `st_mtime_ns` rides its key precisely so changes made outside the panel show up on the next open — and its test now covers an external delete as well as an external add. `test_scene_exporter` 127 green in mayapy.

- **2026-08-06 — Scene Exporter: *Max Size (MB)* is a spin box instead of a free-text field.** A texture budget in megabytes is a bounded number, but the row was a `QLineEdit`: it accepted `16mb` or `sixteen` just as happily as `16` and the check then skipped itself with a warning buried in the log, and turning the check off meant knowing to clear the field to empty. It is now a uitk `SpinBox` — `set_limits [0, 4096]`, `value_method: "value"` so the check receives an int rather than text — with `setCustomDisplayValues({0: "OFF"})`, making 0 the OFF position and labelling it as such in the field (the check already treats a falsy limit as disabled). `check_texture_file_size` still parses numeric strings, because templates saved before this change carry `"16"` and uitk's `ValueManager` coerces it back into the spin box; a pre-change template holding `""` restores as the default 16 rather than as disabled, the one behavior that does not carry over.

- **2026-08-06 - Scene Exporter: the six bare rows in Tasks / Checks now carry captions.** `Max Texture Size (MB)` had a placeholder, but the field ships with `setText("16")` - so the placeholder never rendered and the row read as a naked `16`. Same for `ignore_groups` (defaults to `temp`). The four combos (`export_visible_objects`, `set_linear_unit`, `check_framerate`) and `version` had no caption at all. Each now declares `set_row_label` (`Scope`, `Units`, `Ignore`, `Version`, `Framerate`, `Max Size (MB)`), rendered by uitk's `WidgetComboBox` row caption. Definitions stay pure data - both DCCs already forward `**params` to `set_attributes`, so no slot changed - and the execution path reads its keys explicitly (`widget_type` / `object_name` / `value_method`), so the extra key is inert there. New test asserts the invariant rather than the six strings: any definition whose `widget_type` renders no text of its own must supply a caption, so a future combo or line edit added without one fails.

- **2026-08-06 - The in-Toolbag / in-Painter RPC plugins now run on one shared core.** `marmoset_rpc` and `substance_rpc` each carried their own `server.py` + `registry.py` + `main_thread.py`; the substance copy's docstring said outright it was "a vendored copy of marmoset_rpc's server", and the measured difference between them was docstrings, a host module name, an env prefix, a port and a thread label. Both payloads now carry a staged `_rpc_core.py` (verbatim from `pythontk.net_utils.rpc.plugin_core`, written by `m3trik/scripts/sync_rpc_core.py`, drift-gated by `--check`) and contribute only their `RpcPlugin` config plus their own ops. Net effect beyond the dedup: `substance_rpc` gains `system.describe`, which it never had, because liveness/introspection now come from the core rather than from each plugin remembering to define them - `RpcClient.list_ops()` invokes `system.list_ops` as an *op*, so a plugin that forgot it broke a documented client method. Op modules import `register` / `run_on_main_thread` from their package rather than from sibling modules. **Never hand-edit a staged `_rpc_core.py`** - edit pythontk's source and re-stage. Also: `test/test_bridge.py` renamed to `test_edit_bridge.py`, since it tests the mesh *bridge* modeling op and not any of the six app hand-off bridges it sat next to.

- **2026-08-06 — Scene Exporter: deleting an FBX preset left the combo still showing the preset that no longer existed.** `SceneExporterSlots.presets` caches its directory scan — `cmb000_init` re-runs on every panel show and the scan is recursive over the whole Maya user app dir (`userAppDir`, where the FBX presets sit several levels down) — but the cache key was the preset **directory path** alone. Deleting a preset does not change that path, so the refresh `b004` fires immediately after `os.remove` was handed the pre-delete dict, repopulated the combo with the deleted file and re-selected it. The same staleness hid a preset **added** through *Add New Preset*, and one restored from a scene template carrying an embedded preset. The key now carries the directory's `st_mtime_ns` as well, **and** the three writers in this class call `_invalidate_preset_cache()` — belt and braces on purpose: a filesystem timestamp is quantized to the system clock tick (~15ms on Windows) and the write-then-refresh inside `b004` lands within one tick, which the mtime-only first cut proved by passing alone and failing in the suite. mtime still covers what has no hook to call — Maya's own preset editor, files dropped into the folder by hand. Three adjacent defects fixed with it: a preset directory that has gone missing kept serving its last good scan instead of falling back to `"None"`; `b004` built its delete path as `os.path.join(preset_dir, <combo data>)` when the combo's data **is** the full path — it only ever worked because `join` discards the left side for an absolute right side; and `cmb000_init` re-read the scan four times, so its "no presets found" warning and its selection-restore could each disagree with the list actually shown — it now reads once into a local, as blendertk's mirror already did. +6 tests driving the real slots, each writer's test run with the directory's mtime **frozen** so it can only pass through the explicit invalidation (all three fail without it — verified by neutering the call); test_scene_exporter 128 green in mayapy. blendertk's mirror rescans per call and was never affected.

- **2026-08-06 — Arnold lights: only the FIRST light of a type got its shape named, and a caller-supplied name never reached the transform.** `cmds.shadingNode(..., asLight=True)` is backwards from every other primitive here — it returns the **transform** (`transform1`) and puts the requested name (or the node type) on the **shape** — so the code renamed the transform to `aiAreaLight1` while its own child still held that name. Maya's uniquifier then pushed the transform two indices past the shape, the `shape short name == transform short name` guard read false, and the rename was skipped: light #2 onward shipped with a *shape* called `aiAreaLight2` and a transform called `aiAreaLight3`, numbering permanently out of step. Creating a single light cannot see any of this, which is exactly what the existing test did. Both nodes are now renamed in the order the collision demands — shape first (it holds the name the transform wants), then the transform — and the shape name is re-derived from the **final** transform name, since Maya still uniquifies it when the scene already holds that name. The `name=` path is fixed with it: `create_default_primitive("Arnold", "area", name="myKeyLight")` left the transform as `transform1`, contradicting the docstring's promise that the split "applies to a caller-supplied name too"; it now yields `myKeyLight` / `myKeyLightShape`. Verified live against mtoa 7.3 (probed rather than assumed — the return-value semantics are the whole bug). Two new tests, both failing on the old code: three lights in one scene, and the named case.

- **2026-08-06 — `mayapy-package-manager.bat` is now a self-sufficient single-file download, and the bats survive GitHub's raw endpoint.** The wrapper hands off to the shared `package-manager.bat` beside it — shipped in the wheel by `sync_shared_bat.py`, but absent when someone follows the README link and downloads just the one file: it errored out. On a miss (wrapper dir *and* monorepo fallback) it now curls the shared menu from the repo mirror and normalizes its line endings before calling it. The second half matters as much: `.gitattributes` said `*.bat text eol=crlf`, which materializes CRLF at *checkout* but stores the blob as LF — and raw.githubusercontent serves the blob, so every direct-download bat arrived LF-only, which cmd.exe mis-scans (dropped characters in `else` branches and labels; observed live). The attribute is now `*.bat -text` with the files renormalized, so the committed bytes are CRLF end-to-end — correct at checkout on every OS and on the raw endpoint. Verified: a stubbed single-file wrapper in an empty dir detects Maya, fetches the menu (bare-LF count 0 after normalization), and hands off cleanly.
- **2026-08-06 — WebXR preview: the scene sidecar now honours `EMBED_TEXTURES=False`.** The panel's *Include Textures* toggle promises a fast, flat-material push, but the sidecar walked the texture manifest and embedded base-colour/emissive maps as data URIs regardless — smuggling textures back in after the FBX skipped them, and paying the shader-graph walk the toggle exists to avoid. `_scene_sidecar` now takes `include_textures` (fed from the request's `EMBED_TEXTURES`) and carries constants only when it is off; the manifest walk is skipped entirely. Verified in a fresh mayapy (standardSurface with an emission map): with textures the emissive map rides the sidecar, without it only the constant base colour travels. Mirror of the blendertk change.

- **2026-08-06 — `WebXrPreview`: push the selection to a live browser / WebXR preview.** A hand-off bridge whose target is not an application but a loopback `pythontk.PreviewServer` — press once and a tab opens; press again and the page already open, *including one open inside a headset*, swaps to the new version on its next poll. The whole module is export defaults plus a `push()`: `MayaExportMixin` already supplies the selection read and the FBX export every Maya-originating bridge shares, and `ptk.PreviewDeliverer` (see pythontk's entry) owns the FBX → GLB → publish half, so there is no Maya-side pipeline here to drift from blendertk's.

  Localhost is the design rather than a limitation: `navigator.xr` is only exposed on a *secure context*, `http://localhost` is one by definition, and a PC-tethered headset (Quest over Link / Air Link, Index, WMR) runs its browser on this machine — so a real `immersive-vr` session costs no certificate, no proxy and no tunnel. Exposed in tentacle's Rendering panel as its own `tb002` button — see that entry for why it is not an option on Render.

  **The scene sidecar is the one genuinely Maya-side piece.** Measured: `lambert.incandescence` and `blinn.incandescence` survive to glTF, while `aiStandardSurface.emissionColor` and `StingrayPBS` arrive with `emissiveFactor` **absent entirely** — Maya's FBX exporter maps emissive only from its own legacy shading models, so every modern shader previewed unlit. `_produce` now attaches an emission manifest to `Payload.extras` (the Marmoset bridge's sidecar shape), written to a real `.emissive.json` beside the payload as well, because the point of the panel's toggle is being able to look at what travelled. It is applied to the GLB after conversion; switched off, the preview shows exactly what the FBX carried, which is the only way to tell a dropped channel from a mistranslated one.

  **Base colour is lost the same way, and that is what actually hid the emissive.** Measured per shader type: `lambert` / `blinn` / `phong` carry colour through FBX (scaled by Maya's `diffuse` weight), while `aiStandardSurface` and `standardSurface` arrive at a flat `[1,1,1,1]` — every modern shader previewed as white plastic, and emissive being an *additive* term had no headroom to read against a surface already clipping at white. The sidecar now carries a `base_color` section alongside `emissive`, deliberately **skipping** the legacy shaders (`FBX_NATIVE_SHADERS`): their colour already arrives with the diffuse weight folded in, and re-asserting the raw value would preview brighter than the FBX intends.

  The emission **weight** lookup is an explicit `{node_type: (attribute, mode)}` table (`EMISSION_WEIGHT_ATTRS`), measured rather than guessed. It replaced a list of candidate attribute names tried against every shader, of which `emissionWeight` and `emissive_intensity` matched **nothing at all** — and guessing here is actively unsafe, because StingrayPBS attributes are graph-dependent, so a same-named attribute with different semantics and a 0 default would silently drop a material that had been previewing correctly. A shader absent from the table is ungated. `openPBRSurface` carries `emissionLuminance` in nits rather than a 0-1 weight, so it gates emission on/off but never scales the colour.

  The material list and texture manifest are resolved **once** in `_scene_sidecar` and shared by both readers, rather than each deriving its own: `MatManifest.build` walks every assigned shader graph, and paying for that twice per push is pure waste on the one operation that is supposed to feel immediate.

  The reader adds no new per-shader table: `ShaderAttributeMap` already maps the `emission` channel for every relevant type (`emissionColor` on aiStandardSurface / standardSurface / openPBR, `TEX_emissive_map` on StingrayPBS, `incandescence` on lambert / phong) and `MatManifest` already resolves the connected texture per channel — a fourth copy of that mapping is exactly what the registry exists to prevent. Two traps found by measuring rather than reading: **`MatManifest` cannot enumerate** the materials here, because it drops any material whose slot dict is empty — i.e. every colour-only emissive, which is the common case — so enumeration goes through `MatUtils.get_mats` over the same group-expanded set (the first cut iterated the manifest and found nothing). And the separate emission **weight** must be folded into the colour: it defaults to **0** on aiStandardSurface, so reading `emissionColor` alone reports a bright emissive on a material that renders black.

- **2026-08-06 — `Primitives.create_default_primitive` gained an `arnold` base type: the create list could only make Maya's native lights.** Prompted by an area light that rendered as nothing in Arnold IPR. The light was fine — it was a native `areaLight` at Maya's defaults (`intensity 1`, `aiExposure 0`, `normalize` **on**, scale 1) in a **centimetre** scene, sitting ~430 cm out. Normalize divides emission by area, so a 2×2 cm quad at that distance contributes essentially zero; `ignoreLights` was off and nothing else was suppressing it. Native-vs-Arnold is exactly the distinction the create list was hiding, so `arnold` is its **own base type** rather than extra entries under `light` — the two are not interchangeable at render time and the list must not blur which renderer a light belongs to. Five subtypes: `area` / `skydome` / `mesh` / `photometric` / `portal`. That set was probed against MtoA 5.4.5, not assumed: of the eight `ai*` node types matching "light", only those five are real DAG lights — `aiLightBlocker` and `aiLightDecay` are light *filters* and `aiImagerLightMixer` is an imager. `mtoa` loads on demand via `EnvUtils.load_plugin` (loading it boots the whole renderer and costs seconds, so it must not be paid at import), and an unmapped subtype still raises `KeyError` so the panel reports it rather than silently no-opping. Both the transform **and the shape** are renamed: `shadingNode(asLight=True)` auto-names only Maya's *own* light types, so an Arnold light lands as `transform1` over a shape called `aiAreaLight1` — and renaming just the transform leaves the pair sharing one short name, so `cmds.ls("aiAreaLight1")` returns TWO nodes and every short-name lookup on that light is ambiguous. Maya splits them (`areaLight1` / `areaLightShape1`); the Arnold path now mirrors that by inserting `Shape` before the transform's trailing digits, keeping the pair's numbering in step. +4 tests (all five subtypes create their `ai*` shape, the transform rename, the shape/transform short-name collision, the unmapped-subtype raise); test_edit_tools_misc 27 green, 94 across the edit suites.

- **2026-08-06 — `Ctrl+Shift+G` had nothing to bind to: the macro set shipped `m_group` with no inverse.** New `EditUtils.ungroup_objects` + the `m_ungroup` macro over it, so the group hotkey round-trips. Dissolves each selected group — children reparented one level up with world transforms preserved (`ungroup absolute=True`), then the emptied group removed. The "is this a group" test is the existing `NodeUtils.is_group` rather than a fresh predicate; it already encodes both the transform-with-no-shape rule and the deliberate `noIntermediate=True` choice (an orig shape must not make a group look like geometry), and it is queried per node so the paths stay long — its `filter=True` form returns short names, which would break the ordering below. Two details a naive version gets wrong: groups are processed **deepest-first**, so dissolving an inner group cannot invalidate the path of an outer one still queued; and freed children are tracked by **UUID**, because `ungroup` rewrites their paths and can rename on a clash at the new level, so a name captured up front is stale by the last dissolve (a leaf under nested groups is also handed up once per level, hence the dedupe). A childless group is deleted outright — `ungroup` errors on one, and it is the exact inverse of `group_objects`' empty-selection `null`. Non-group selections are skipped with a warning rather than deleted. `cmds.ungroup` leaves the freed children selected; that is now **asserted** rather than assumed, because blendertk's mirror has to reproduce it by hand. +5 tests (round-trip preserving world position under a moved group, children left selected, non-group untouched, nested outer+inner in one call, empty group removed); test_group_combine 16 green, test_macros 90, test_edit_utils 67 in mayapy. Mirrored in blendertk; `ctl+sht+g` bound in the `m3trik` macro preset for both.

- **2026-08-06 — `AnimUtils.get_static_curves` never reported a single-key curve, so `optimize_keys(remove_static_curves=True)` could not clean the most static curve there is.** The om2 fast path opened with `if n <= 1: continue`, which reads as a guard against an empty curve but also dropped every 1-key curve — one value held forever, i.e. maximally static. The skip existed because the first/last/mid sampling below it is meaningless at `n == 1`, not because such a curve should be preserved: the docstring's own test ("all keyframe values are identical") is trivially true for one key. Now only `n == 0` bails, and `n == 1` falls straight through to the shared resting-state guard, so a single key **off** its default is still preserved exactly as before — the guard, not the arity check, is what protects a held position. Found chasing phantom curves on the VDATS `OFFICE_ENV` / `OFFICE_SPACE` modules: Maya's Set Key (`S`) on a 4-object selection leaves 10 single-key curves per transform (t/r/s + visibility) recording the resting pose, and `optimize_keys` did not consider a single one of the 40. It now reaches them, but **this alone does not clean such a scene**: the resting-state guard still holds back every curve parked off its attribute default, which on `OFFICE_ENV` is 3 of 20 (the two `translateX` at 4.9999995 and one `translateY`). Removing those safely means baking the held value onto the plug first, which no public helper does — filed in the workspace backlog together with the measurement that the guard's stated premise is false for a plain `cmds.delete(curve)` (the plug keeps its value, and Maya writes a real `setAttr` for it, verified across save + reload). +2 tests (single key at default is reported; single key off default is not) — the first fails against the old guard with exactly that symptom. test_anim_utils 133 green in mayapy; 386 green across the anim/material suites. The preset combo's per-item tooltips are sourced from `pythontk.MapRegistry`, so they gained that package's new `Targets: …` platforms automatically; the help panel's own "UE/Unity/film/etc." shorthand did not, and it was the one place a user looks before opening the combo. It now names Godot and glTF 2.0 (the WebXR profile) too.

- **2026-08-05 — Naming: the Rename field's wildcard grammar was half-implemented and its tooltip advertised a feature that did not exist.** Everything past a single plain Find term silently did nothing (root cause and measurements in pythontk's `find_str_and_format` entry): a piped Find like `*_L|*_R` collapsed to one literal that matches no name, so `*chars*` and strip became no-ops and `*chars` / `chars*` degraded to a blind append — which is *why* the failure was easy to miss, since `pCube1` → `pCube1_GEO` looks like a rename succeeding rather than the suffix replacement being skipped. Regex was worse: the pattern selected the right objects and then formatted them wrongly or not at all, and **Ignore Case** never reached the substitution. Meanwhile the tooltip's "Combine terms with `|` for multiple renaming criteria (e.g. `prefix*|*suffix`)" was never implemented in any form — one mode was inferred from the whole string, so that documented example renamed every matched object to the literal text `prefix*|*suffix` (illegal-char stripped into `prefix__suffix`).

  The grammar is now real rather than aspirational. Piped Rename terms pair positionally with Find's, so `*_L|*_R` → `*_lt|*_rt` renames each side differently in one pass, and a single term still applies to every Find term. With **Regular Expression** on, Find's capture groups are available in Rename as `\1` / `\2` / `\g<name>`. `**` alone is once again the no-op it reads as instead of deleting the matched token. The tooltips were rewritten around the mental model that was previously left for the user to infer — **the asterisk marks the part of the existing name that is kept**, which is what makes one asterisk a replace and a doubled one an add — and they now state the append fallback that fires when Find is empty or absent from a name, rather than leaving it as a surprise. No UI controls were added: the modes are the grammar, and a mode combobox would have duplicated it.

  Separately, **a filtered rename skipped duplicate short names.** The filtered path deduplicated through pythontk's `find_str`, so renaming two objects that share a leaf name — the everyday case of `Cube` under two different groups — produced one rename pair, renamed the first, and left the second on its old name with no warning. Only the *filtered* path was affected; `test_rename_duplicates_in_hierarchy` passes no filter and so takes the per-name loop, which is why the gap survived. Fixed in `find_str_and_format` (details in pythontk's entry) rather than worked around here.

  With the formatter now preserving duplicates and accepting an empty filter directly, `Naming.rename`'s two-branch dispatch collapsed to the single batch call blendertk already used. The per-name loop existed only to work around those two limits, and the `except Exception` fallback inside it hand-rolled the grammar *wrongly* — it treated `*chars` as append when the grammar makes it replace-suffix, so had it ever fired, one object in a batch would have formatted differently from the rest with no warning. It is now provably dead (the fuzz above finds no input that raises) and deleted rather than left as a trap. Net: 34 lines to 13, one code path instead of two, and the Maya and Blender engines finally the same shape. +5 tests driving real objects through `Naming.rename` (duplicates under a filter, paired terms, per-term filter text, regex backref, regex replace-suffix cutting at the match instead of appending); test_naming 30 green in mayapy. Mirrored in blendertk, where the duplicate case cannot arise — Blender object names are globally unique.

- **2026-08-05 — Texture scopes can now drop the images Maya ships with itself.** A StingrayPBS material carries real file nodes for its preset environment maps (`diffuse_cube.dds` / `specular_cube.dds` under `MAYA_LOCATION/presets/ShaderFX/Images`), so every material-scoped query returned them next to the user's maps — and the Map Converter, whose whole job is writing, then tried to rewrite files inside a read-only install tree. New `MatUtils.is_bundled_texture(path)` answers "does this live inside Maya's own installation" (path-only, no scene access, false when `MAYA_LOCATION` is unset), and `get_texture_paths` takes `exclude_bundled=` to filter on it. **Default off**: a query that inventories a scene wants every wired map, and only tools that *write* have a reason to skip these. Applied in tentacle's four Map Converter scope providers, which is where the policy belongs — the panel stays app-agnostic. The install-dir prefix beat the alternatives on the same test: "non-writable source" would also silently skip a legitimately locked user asset, and "environment-map slot" is shader-specific and misses the same file wired anywhere else. +2 tests (the predicate incl. the sibling-directory false positive the trailing separator guards against, and the inclusive default vs the filtered call).

- **2026-08-05 — RizomUV bridge: "Pre-scale gives the same result no matter what value I choose."** Reported against the `pack` preset. The plumbing was exonerated first — rendering `pack.lua` through the production path emits `Scaling={Mode=0}` / `{Mode=1}` / `{Mode=2}` correctly — and RizomUV *does* honour the field: on a probe mesh of two cubes at 4:1 linear (16:1 in 3D area) carrying **identical** incoming UV islands, `Mode=0` keeps the packed big/small island-area ratio at **1.000** while `Mode=1/2/3/4` all take it to **16.000**. The knob is simply unobservable at the shipped defaults, for two independent reasons. **(a)** Modes 1 and 2 differ only by a *global* uniform factor: at `LayoutScalingMode=0`, mode 1 leaves islands at literal world scale (per-face UV area 4.000 / 0.250 — exactly the 3D face areas, layout spanning u[0, 5.53]) while mode 2 normalizes into the tile (0.042353 / 0.002647, sum 0.270) — and the default Layout Scale "Best fit (uniform)" renormalizes that factor away, making the two equivalent. **(b)** When the selection's incoming UVs already have consistent texel density — the normal state after any unwrap — all three modes land on numerically **identical** areas and bounds. So Pre-scale only bites on a selection whose objects disagree on texel density, and only comparing 0 against 1-or-2; the tooltip now says exactly that instead of implying three distinct behaviours. `Scaling.Mix` is separately a **measured no-op** on 2020.1 (on and off save a byte-identical result; determinism control: the same config re-run from a separate script into a separate file reproduced its numbers exactly), so "Mix Scale" now carries that warning too — which also makes `optimize.lua`'s hardcoded `Mix=true` decorative. All measurements are recorded in `rizom_headless_probe.py`'s results table, including the trap that the already-consistent mesh still saves *different* `vt` digests across the three modes even though areas and bounds match to the last digit — the packer merely permutes equal-sized islands between equivalent slots, so re-probes must compare per-island **areas**, not a whole-file digest. Tooltips only: removing `SCALING_MIX` or collapsing the Pre-scale choices is structural panel surface (a saved JSON preset carries the key) and stays in the workspace backlog pending a ≥ 2022 re-probe. uv_rizom_bridge 36 green. Mirrored in blendertk.

- **2026-08-05 — RizomUV bridge: `pack` had to be sent twice before the layout filled the UV tile, worst when packing instances.** Reported against the `pack` preset. The bridge itself was exonerated first, by measurement rather than by reading: it is deterministic (identical input → byte-identical output), and the UV transfer back into Maya is **exactly lossless** — 0.00% area delta with identical `uvShell` / UV counts on every send — so the whole difference lives inside RizomUV. `ZomPack` is a heuristic solver whose result depends on its input, so re-sending is a fresh dice roll rather than a refinement; that is why the second send "worked" and why an all-unique-mesh scene actually *oscillates* (tile coverage 0.2124 → 0.2243 → 0.2107) instead of converging. Root cause: `ZomPack`'s `Resolution` field was being stripped on RizomUV 2020.1 by the `MIN_VERSIONS` gate. That gate was never a probed crash — its own comment called it "a conservative midpoint" between Titus's 2020.1-era reference and the adevra 2024 bridge — and `Resolution` (distinct from the genuinely crashing `MapResolution`) is safe there: rc 0, file saved, across ~12 runs. **`PACK_RESOLUTION` is now ungated**, so 2020.1 sends it and the panel's Resolution row stops auto-hiding. Verified in mayapy through the real bridge (FBX + island groups + real transfer), two sends per config: 4 DAG instances went `0.5766 → 0.6655` (the reported "send it twice"), and now land at **0.6777 on send 1, unchanged by a second send** — better than the old two-send result; a mixed instances+unique scene gains **+11.3%**, likewise converged. Recorded trade-off: an all-unique scene is slightly worse at 1024 (send-1 coverage 0.2124 → 0.2088). Instances were the worst case because the bridge correctly collapses DAG instances to one representative (shared shape ⇒ one unwrap serves every copy), leaving the packer fewer, larger islands and less freedom to fill.

  `MaxMutations` and `Rotate.Enable` **stay gated, now for a measured reason rather than caution**: `MaxMutations` changes the stacked/instances result by *nothing* at any value 25–250, helps only a single-mesh grid (0.8084 → 0.8394), and needs 250 to be stable at 8× the runtime (78s vs 10s) — with 1000 costing 212s and 5000 blowing past 75 minutes of CPU, so any future probe of it must cap the timeout. `Rotate.Enable` changed nothing measurable. Four in-Lua fixes were tried and **rejected on measurements** before landing on the gate: repeating the group+pack block (a regression through FBX — 0.6371 → 0.6187 — even though it looked like a win on the OBJ probe, which is why recipe changes must be verified through the Maya path), repeating `ZomPack` alone (exact no-op), a `ZomNormalize` pre-pass (access violation `0xC00000FF` on 2020.1), and in-session save+reload cycles (helped one scene, hurt another). All findings are now recorded in `rizom_headless_probe.py`'s results table, including the probe trap that a second injected `Rotate=` key is silently overridden by Lua's last-key-wins. `optimize.lua` references the same token and so now also sends `Resolution` on 2020.1, but is provably unaffected: its `ZomPack` step has **no effect at all** (every `LayoutScalingMode` / grouping-mode variant returns byte-identical UVs) — which is its own, far worse defect, filed in the workspace backlog rather than fixed here: from a packed in-tile layout the preset returns UVs spanning ~9×9 UDIM tiles with area inflated 84×, confirmed through the real bridge. +1 test (fails against the old gate with the exact symptom); uv_rizom_bridge 36, uv_utils 113, uv_diagnostics 35, uv_cleanup_actions 7, uv_snapshot 6 — 197 green, plus the full headless preset probe PASS. Mirrored in blendertk.

- **2026-08-05 — Reference Manager: renaming the *open* scene stranded the session on the old filename.** Rename (context menu and inline double-click) only touched disk, so Maya kept `sceneName` on a path that no longer existed: edits made since the last save had nowhere to go, and the next Ctrl+S silently re-created the old file beside the renamed one — two scenes in the panel where the user renamed one. Renaming the open scene is now save-then-reopen: unsaved edits are flushed to the old file first (saving after the rename would just re-create the old name), and the renamed file is re-opened last, after any `{name}` folder move, so the session lands on the final path. A save that fails aborts the rename rather than stranding the edits. Both rename paths now share one `_rename_scene_file` (sidecar metadata + folder move included); an open `.fbx` renames on disk only — Maya opens one as a scene but it is not one to save over, per the new `EnvUtils.SCENE_SAVE_TYPES` (extension → save type, which also de-duplicates the same mapping open-coded in `save_autosave_to_original`). Nothing is left under the old name: the file and the `{name}` folder are *moved* (no stale copy, no empty leftover dir), the `.metadata.json` sidecar follows, and — new — so does Maya's `incrementalSave/<scene>.ma/` folder, which is keyed to the scene's filename and used to be orphaned by a rename (a later scene reusing the old name would silently inherit that history). A folder that *can't* be renamed (target exists) is now reported instead of only logged — the scene itself was renamed, so staying silent left the file sitting under the old scene's folder with nothing to explain it. Covered by mocked ordering tests plus five that drive the real controller against a real scene on disk — proof that Maya tolerates its open scene file being renamed out from under it and that the flushed edits survive the re-open, which the mocks can't show.

- **2026-08-05 — Substance bridge: "Send To" ignored the panel's Map Resolution and left every texture set at Painter's 1024 default.** `ensure_rpc_plugin` gated on presence (`Installer.is_installed()`), so once `substance_rpc` was installed it was never refreshed — and without Developer Mode the install is a `copytree` snapshot, not a symlink. An install predating the setup ops kept serving 9 of the 12 shipped ops; `project.set_resolution` was simply not registered, so the dispatch failed as an unknown op and the FBX still opened, at 1024. `bake.set_high_poly` and `textures.apply_mesh_maps` were dead the same way, so Export Bake Source and per-texture-set mesh maps silently did nothing too. Now gated on content via the new `Installer.is_current()` (over pythontk's `PluginInstaller.is_plugin_current`), and the log distinguishes *Installed* from *Refreshed*. A refresh still needs **Python ▸ Reload Plugins Folder** (or a Painter relaunch) to take effect in an already-running Painter — a fresh launch, which `import.py` forces, picks it up automatically.
- **2026-08-05 — Hand-typed text fields grew the clear button that only Naming and Channels had.** The affordance was never automatic — `option_box.clear_option` is opt-in per field, and most `*_init` slots simply never set it, so identical-looking fields behaved differently. The rule now applied: a clear button belongs on a field **the user types into**, where empty is a state worth reaching. Added to Reference Manager's filter, Lightmap Baker's name affix, Image→Plane's material suffix, Emissive Groups' group name, Tube/Wheel Rig's rig name, and Blendshape Animator's name prefix. Deliberately *not* added to browse-driven or auto-populated paths — Reference Manager root dir, Scene Exporter output dir, Workspace Editor project root, Image Tracer path, Shot Manifest CSV path — where the value arrives from a file dialog or is derived, is rarely hand-edited, and empty just means broken. Also skipped: Wheel Rig's numeric height, and Channels' `txt001`, which displays the current target read-only rather than taking free text. Mirrored 1:1 in blendertk; parity sweep clean.

- **2026-08-05 — Preview is now optional: Create commits straight from the selection, no preview pass required.** Every previewed panel (Bevel, Bridge, Mirror, Cut on Axis, the three Duplicates, Curtain, Curve to Tube, Shadow Rig) shipped its Create button `enabled=false` in the `.ui`, and only `Preview.enable()` ever re-enabled it — so a user who just wanted the operation had to check Preview, wait for the hermetic build, then click Create. The button is now asserted live at construction (rather than left to whatever each `.ui` carries) and a click with no preview running goes through the new `_commit_direct`: the same gate as `enable` (selection → `validation_func` → the one-shot `prepare_operation` precondition → shading snapshot), then the same `_replay_under_undo` the previewed commit uses, so the committed result and its single Ctrl+Z-able chunk are identical either way. `prepare_operation` runs *inside* the chunk on this path — with no preview session to keep it out of, a bypassed commit should revert whole — and a gate rejection commits nothing, so `finalize_func` (Curtain's rail discard, Duplicate Radial's regroup) correctly stays unfired. The old contract is preserved as opt-in: `Preview(..., require_preview=True)` keeps Create dead until Preview is on. The gate itself (selection + `validation_func`) is now one `_gated_selection()` shared by `enable` and the commit, so the two entry points can't drift apart; the unused `progress_callback` constructor parameter — stored since the class was written, never read, never passed by any panel — is dropped. Mirrored in blendertk, which already committed without a preview but skipped the precondition hook while doing it (see its CHANGELOG — a real corruption path there, not just a missing lever). +9 tests (`TestPreviewBypass`); preview 47, plus bridge / curtain / curve_to_tube / duplicate_radial / edit_tools ×2 / shadow_rig — 285 green. Live-GUI pass owed.

- **2026-08-05 — `get_scene_mats` silently dropped materials two different ways, so the materials panel couldn't reach a material that was assigned to the selected mesh.** Surfaced as tentacle's "Get Material" reporting a filtered material with both of cmb002's list filters unchecked (slot half in tentacle's CHANGELOG); both causes are here, and both are filter-independent. **First, the source list was `cmds.ls(materials=True)` alone**, which reports `defaultShaderList1` — and only `shadingNode -asShader` registers a node there. A shader built with `createNode`, or wired straight into a shading engine by an importer/plugin, is assigned to geometry yet invisible to that query, so it could never appear in the combo (probed in mayapy: `get_mats(cube)` returns `rawMat`, `get_scene_mats()` doesn't list it). The source is now that list UNIONED with the shaders wired into the scene's shading engines (new `_shading_engine_shaders`); the union only ADDS genuinely-wired shaders, and `exclude_utility_nodes` still drops the mis-flagged `bump2d`/`aiMultiply` helpers it was written for. The shading-engine -> material hop it needs was already written inside `get_mats` as a closure, so both now go through one `_sg_shaders(sg, slots)` over a `_SG_SHADER_SLOTS` / `_SG_EXTRA_SHADER_SLOTS` pair rather than two copies of the same plug walk (the existence check the optional mtoa-only `aiSurfaceShader` slot needs included). **Second, name filtering round-tripped through a `{short_name: material}` dict**, and short names are not unique across namespaces — `nsA:mat` and `nsB:mat` collapse to one key, so whichever came first vanished from *every* return path, filtered or not, list or dict. Filtering now runs over the list with `map_func=short_name` (same inc/exc semantics), and `as_dict` builds its keys through the new `_unique_name_map`, which keys every member of a colliding group on its namespace-qualified name so the pair reads as `nsA:mat` / `nsB:mat` instead of one row that hides the other. +3 tests in `test_mat_utils`, all three failing against the old code with the reported symptom. mat_utils 67, plus mat_utils_extended / material_updater ×3 / arnold_bridge / mat_manifest / mat_snapshot / mat_bake_sets / marmoset_bridge — 147 green. Live-verified end to end by driving the real tentacle panel in a fresh Maya against all three scenes.

- **2026-08-05 — Color ID: Reset wiped every channel instead of the checked ones, and the Material channel's Select By Color could never match.** Reported as "resets everything instead of just the checked channels". `ColorIdSlots.b000` called `reset_colors(objects)` with **no** flags, so all five `reset_*` parameters fell back to their `True` defaults — Set Color and Select By Color both honored the checkboxes, Reset alone ignored them. Clearing a wireframe tint therefore also reassigned `lambert1` and deleted the object's materials, dropped its outliner color, deleted its vertex-color sets and dissolved its ID set. All three actions now read one `_channels()` dict (the blendertk twin's shape), through a `_channels_or_warn()` guard that reports rather than acting on an empty channel set — with nothing checked, **Select By Color used to clear the user's selection** (a zero-check query matches nothing, and it selects its result) and Set Color silently did nothing. Same fix mirrored in blendertk, where the unscoped reset also cost the ID material, the outliner stamp and the ID collection.

  Verifying the fix turned up three more defects on the same paths, all confirmed in mayapy before fixing. **`get_material_color` and `get_objects_by_color`'s material branch queried shading engines on the *transform***, but assignment connects them to the **shape** — `listConnections(<transform>, type="shadingEngine")` returns `None`, so the read handed back `None` and Select By Color with **Material** checked matched *nothing at all* (probe: `[]` for the object it had just colored). Both now resolve through the shapes via one `_material_colors` helper — the select path had been carrying a second, equally blind copy of the walk, so unifying them fixes both at once; multi-shader meshes report every assigned color rather than only the first. And the whole **Ctrl+click "reset everything" sweep undid nothing this tool had applied**: it passes `cmds.ls(geometry=True)`, which yields *shapes*, while Set Color writes the outliner and wireframe channels on the *transform* (what the user selects) — and `reset_vertex_colors` then filtered its input down to transforms, so the shapes it was handed dropped out and no mesh's vertex colors were cleared either. The sweep now resolves through `NodeUtils.list_transforms(geometry=True)` — the same walk Select By Color uses, so "everything" means the same set in both — and `reset_vertex_colors` accepts shapes as well as transforms. Consolidating that walk let `get_objects_by_color` drop its hand-rolled per-shape parent loop (one batched call instead of one per node), and its wireframe branch now calls `get_wireframe_color` instead of respelling that getter's three-attribute gate inline. +7 tests (`TestColorIdSlotsChannelScope`, driving the real slot with stub checkboxes); five fail against the old code with the reported symptoms. display_utils 29, display_extras 20, node_utils 52, mat_utils 64 — green. Live-GUI pass owed.

- **2026-08-05 — Every project-relative texture path resolved one folder too deep, so tools reported textures that were sitting right there as missing.** Reported against the Map Converter's Maya scopes: optimizing a selected material printed `Skipping (file not found): …/Brazing/sourceimages/sourceimages/FLOOR_mat_AO.png` for maps that exist. `_paths_from_file_nodes` joined a relative `fileTextureName` onto the **sourceimages directory**, but Maya resolves a relative texture path against the **project root** (that is what `workspace -expandName` does) — and the stored value already carries the rule folder, so the usual `sourceimages/tex.png` came back with `sourceimages` doubled and pointing at nothing. Reproduced in mayapy against a real workspace before the fix (`…/sourceimages/sourceimages/rel_tex.png`), which is exactly the reported string. Resolution now goes through Maya's own expansion first (new `_expand_texture_path`), with the old sourceimages join demoted to an existence-checked **fallback** so a pipeline that stored a bare `tex.png` doesn't regress; an unresolvable value comes back as Maya's expansion rather than a doubled path, so a genuinely broken link is reported at the location Maya looks in. The relative form (`absolute=False`) now round-trips back to what Maya stored instead of emitting the doubled path.

  Consolidating that resolution turned up a second defect in the primitive it now delegates to: **`resolve_path`'s repair hunt hardcoded `<root>/sourceimages`**, ignoring the `sourceImages` file rule it is named after. A rule may point at `textures/` or at an absolute path outside the project (`Workspace.resolve`: "workspace-relative unless absolute"), so on any such project the hunt searched a folder that doesn't exist and repaired nothing. It now asks `EnvUtils.source_images_dir()` — the same SSoT the rest of this module uses — and bails when there is no workspace. `_absolute_texture_path` delegates its resolution to `resolve_path(search=False)` rather than re-deriving it (`search=False` deliberately: the repair hunt's basename match would retarget the converter at a same-named file the node doesn't point at, and it goes on to *overwrite* what it is handed); the UDIM-aware existence check is now one `_texture_exists` instead of a closure plus a copy. +1 test (a workspace whose rule names `textures/`), which fails against the old code.

  The blast radius was wider than the panel that surfaced it — every consumer of this helper silently dropped the same textures: `MatManifest.build` handed the **Marmoset and Substance bridges** dead paths for any relative-pathed material (a bake's surface transfer reads whole images off those paths, so the source arrived untextured with nothing logged), and `copy_textures_to_sourceimages` skipped files it decided were missing. +2 tests in `test_mat_utils` (the doubling itself, and the sourceimages-relative fallback that had been the only working case); the first fails against the old code with the reported path. mat_utils 64/64.

- **2026-08-05 — Marmoset bake roundtrip: the rewire wrote backslashed texture paths, and a scratch-copy fallback could be wired in without a word.** Audit of the post-bake rewire, prompted by the path bug above. Two defects, neither fatal but both silent. `_relocate_outputs` builds its results with `os.path.join`, so on Windows the baked maps reached `fileTextureName` backslashed while every other stored path in this package is forward-slashed (manifest build, sourceimages copy, `remap_file_nodes`' lookup keys) — mixing the two makes later path comparisons miss on identical files. `_assign_baked_materials` now normalizes before wiring. Separately, when a copy out of the local bake scratch can't be size-verified, `_relocate_outputs` deliberately keeps the scratch original and surfaces *that* path — which the rewire then bound into a production material, even though the scratch store is age-swept on a later bake. The wire step now takes the run's `output_dir` and names any map wired from outside it, so the maps that will disappear are called out while they still exist. The rest of the return path checks out: outputs are absolute, size-verified, and bucket to materials correctly. +3 mock tests.

- **2026-08-05 — RizomUV bridge: a pack could not be undone — Ctrl+Z reverted the UVs but left the round-trip's imported meshes behind, and every run leaked a shading group.** Reported against `pack`; reproduced in mayapy driving the real bridge with only the executable stubbed. Two defects, one root cause: the whole round-trip sat in one undo chunk, but `file -import` is **not** undoable while the `cmds.delete` that removes the imported nodes **is**. Undoing therefore replayed only half the pair — the imported `*__RZTMP` transforms came back, along with an empty `RizomUVImport` namespace, and nothing would ever remove them again. Separately, cleanup deleted only the imported *transforms*, so the shading network the FBX carries stayed: one orphaned `*__RZTMPSG` per run, accumulating in the outliner. The fix splits the two halves — the UV transfer is the only thing recorded; the scaffolding (temp duplicates, the import, the namespace, the shading network it drags in) runs under the new `CoreUtils.undo_disabled()` and is torn down explicitly: a UUID-keyed sweep of everything the import created (names no longer describe those nodes — the transfer sources get reparented), then the namespace dropped with `deleteNamespaceContent=True`. That flag is half the leak fix on its own: `mergeNamespaceWithRoot`, which this used to use, *rehomes* whatever is left into the user's scene root, which is precisely how the orphaned shading groups got loose in the outliner.

  Getting there mapped two Maya undo traps worth recording, both found the hard way (each is a **fatal** access violation in `TdeleteCmd::undoIt`, not an exception). First: `transferAttributes` + `delete -ch` leave an undo entry that rebuilds the history node and reconnects it to the transfer *source*, so simply deleting the imported source undo-disabled makes the next Ctrl+Z crash Maya. The transfer now sources from a **recorded duplicate** of the import, deleted by a recorded command too, keeping the whole recorded chain self-contained. Second: fixing up that duplicate afterwards is not allowed either — mutating an undo-recorded node while recording is off silently corrupts the `duplicate` command's own undo entry, and the proxy then survives the undo as scene garbage (traced by UUID: the node undo restored was never the node undo tried to delete). Hence detach-then-duplicate — the *import* is cut loose from its parent and shading group first (it is a `file -import` node, which the queue never owned), so the proxy is born holding nothing that cleanup will delete. Distilled to one rule now carried in the code: **the recorded set and the undo-disabled set must not touch each other.**

  New `CoreUtils.undo_disabled()` context manager (`stateWithoutFlush=False` + restore — never `state=False`, which flushes the user's history). +2 tests, both of which fail against the old code with the exact reported symptoms (`['undoNested_1__RZTMPSG']` leaked; `undoNested_1__RZTMPShape` resurrected by undo) over a scene with a material and a grouped mesh, so the shading and parent links are both exercised. rizom_bridge 34, uv_utils 113, core_utils 31 — all green. The blendertk twin is unaffected: Blender's undo is snapshot-based (one consolidated push), and its import orphans were already purged against a pre-import snapshot.

- **2026-08-04 — Scene exporter: "Fix Mangled Names" silently invalidated the export set, and the next check blamed every object but the offender.** Reported as `check_duplicate_locator_names` failing with `No object matches name: ['|STATIC|AIRDUCT', … 22 paths …]` in a scene containing **no locators at all**. The check was innocent: `conform_shape_names` (Phase 2.5) renames export nodes through `SceneDiagnostics.repair_mangled_names` — resolving them by UUID, so the rename itself is safe — but nothing then re-derived `TaskManager.objects`, which still held the pre-rename long names. Probed on Maya 2025: `cmds.listRelatives`/`cmds.select` echo the **entire argument list** when any one entry fails to resolve (a type filter that matches nothing returns `None`, so "no locators" was never the trigger), which is why the error named all 22 objects and none of them was the stale one. The reporter's scene made it inevitable — `DIRT_STAIN_FBXASC03203` decodes to `DIRT_STAIN 03`, cleans to `DIRT_STAIN_03`, which already existed, so Maya uniquified the rename and the stored path died. Checks run alphabetically, so `check_duplicate_locator_names` was simply the first one to hand the whole list to `cmds`. The same stale list also reached `cmds.select` for the export itself: with an animated scene it would instead have hit `smart_bake`'s `cmds.ls` refresh first and **silently dropped the renamed geometry from the FBX**. Fixed at the source — `conform_shape_names` snapshots UUIDs before repairing and re-resolves `self.objects` to the new paths afterwards, order preserved, identity decided by UUID rather than path existence (a repair can *free* a name — delete `FOO` and `FOO_FBXASC03203` cleans straight onto its path — so an `objExists` test would resurrect the deleted entry as a duplicate of the node that legitimately moved there; caught by its own test) — plus a read-side guard, `_live_objects()`, now shared by every check that feeds the object set to `cmds` (locator/mangled-name/LOD-suffix/below-floor/hidden-geometry/overlapping-duplicate/keyframe checks, the sidecar hierarchy set, and smart_bake's own refresh), so a node a task legitimately deletes can no longer abort a run from whichever check happens to sort first. The export selection resolves the same way and now names anything that vanished in a warning instead of dying on it. +5 tests (`TestExportSetStalePaths`; two fail against the old code with the exact reported error). scene_exporter 120, scene_data_sidecar 49, diagnostics 48 — all green.

- **2026-08-04 — Scene exporter: relative texture paths now survive Embed Media, and `check_valid_paths` gates write-time resolution.** Root cause of "The following texture(s) will not be embedded" pinned by a mayapy probe matrix: the fbxmaya plugin locates media with plain OS path resolution — relative `.ftn` against the **process CWD**; the workspace is never consulted (correct workspace + foreign CWD drops every relative texture from the embed, with *no console warning in batch*; foreign workspace + CWD at the project root embeds fine — refuting `set_workspace`'s old docstring claim, and the earlier "relative can never embed" reading). `set_workspace` now also aligns the process working directory with the workspace root for the write (staged via `stage_deferred_restore("cwd")`, restored after export), and `check_valid_paths` re-probes every Maya-resolvable path the way the plugin will locate it — so an export that would end in that warning aborts up front instead of shipping a silently textureless FBX. Also: `check_texture_file_size` swaps its fixed ComboBox steps for a QLineEdit (any MB value, arrives as text; empty disables) — coercion + `"OFF"` handling already in place. Both the QLineEdit and the text coercion mirrored in blendertk. The same defect existed on every other embed-media write path, so the alignment is now a shared primitive — new `FbxUtils.embed_media_write_cwd()` context manager (chdirs to the workspace root when the live `FBXExportEmbeddedTextures` setting is on; restores the caller's CWD) — consumed by `FbxUtils.export` (the bridges' write path: handoff_export/blender-send default embed True), `EnvUtils.export_scene_as_fbx` (tentacle scene.py tb003, embed default True), and the scene exporter's own `cmds.file` write (covers runs with Auto Set Workspace off or checks overridden via b009). +8 tests (setAttr's auto-expansion of resolvable relative paths modeled via the `MPlug.setString` bypass, as production scenes store them); scene_exporter 115/115, env_utils 22, fbx_utils 19, fbx_export_preparers 8, blendertk suite 60/60. Deletes the corresponding workspace-backlog entry.

- **2026-08-04 — Texture Path Editor: new "Make Paths Absolute" command, and the table now shows the stored path verbatim.** The panel could only relativize (Normalize Paths); nothing wrote absolute — a gap that matters because FBX Embed Media cannot consume relative `.ftn` (proven in mayapy: embed + relative silently drops every map). The new header action rewrites relative paths (selection or all) to absolute against the project root — pure path rewrite, no file relocation, UDIM tokens survive the prefix join, prior paths recorded for the "Previous:" tooltip, undoable chunk. With it, the display had to stop lying: `get_file_nodes(raw=True)` relativized any in-project path before returning, so an absolutize would have *appeared* to do nothing and "Select Absolute Paths" could never match an in-project absolute (GUI-verified earlier — a fresh scene open loads nodes absolute yet the panel showed relative). The table now displays the stored `.ftn` verbatim, matching the blendertk twin, which always did. Mirrored in blendertk (its engine's `normalize_texture_paths("absolute")` mode existed but was never exposed in the panel, and its slot guards on an unsaved .blend — `//` has nothing to resolve against).

  Writing the command made the duplication impossible to ignore: "resolve a stored `.ftn` to absolute" was inlined **six** times across the panel, in three slightly different spellings (some normpath-only, some forward-slashed), and a seventh copy — `_resolve_absolute_texture_path` — was both caller-less *and* wrong, joining relative paths onto *sourceimages* rather than the workspace root. Reproduced in mayapy: a stored `sourceimages/foo.png` resolved to `C:/proj/sourceimages/sourceimages/foo.png`. That helper is **deleted** (zero production callers; its tests only ever covered the absolute and empty branches, so the broken branch never ran) and every site now goes through one `_to_absolute(path, workspace)` primitive — the declared inverse of `_project_relative_converter`, pure string logic, so its regression tests need no scene.

  Unifying them fixed a **data-loss bug** nobody had noticed: `_set_texture_dir_flat` returned an already-absolute `old_path` *verbatim* while `new_abs` was forward-slashed, so the "is it already at the destination?" guard compared `C:\proj\...` against `C:/proj/...` and never matched. A texture whose stored path was absolute-with-backslashes and already sat in the target directory was therefore treated as a relocation source, collided with *itself* at the destination, passed the same-size check — and **move mode ran `os.remove` on the file it was repathing to**, deleting it. Confirmed with `os.path.samefile(src, dst) == True`; now locked by `test_move_does_not_delete_a_texture_already_in_the_target_dir`.

  Building the inverse also exposed that **Normalize Paths could write a path that resolves to nothing**. Maya finds a relative `.ftn` from the *project root*, but the panel builds the form relative to *sourceimages* — and those agree only while sourceimages sits under the root. A `sourceImages` file rule may be absolute and point anywhere (`Workspace.resolve`: "workspace-relative unless absolute"); reproduced through `Workspace.load` with a rule pointing at `D:/shared`, where an out-of-project `D:/shared/foo.png` was stored as `shared/foo.png` — i.e. `<proj>/shared/foo.png`, which does not exist. `_project_relative_converter` now emits a relative path only when it **round-trips** back through `_to_absolute` to the same file, and otherwise leaves it absolute: the only form that still finds it. The guard is a no-op in the normal layout, so it can only turn broken output into working output. Its inline fourth copy in the Find & Copy remap is gone too — that workflow now calls the converter, so `os.path.relpath` appears exactly once in the module. +14 tests total (54 in the module, 231 with `mat_utils` + `scene_exporter`); parity sweep clean; live-GUI pass owed.

- **2026-08-04 — Lightmap Baker's affix tooltip lost the `<object>` placeholder it exists to explain.** Same defect as the Mesh Convert `<name>` fix (see extapps' CHANGELOG): the `.ui` carried `&amp;lt;object&amp;gt;`, which XML-decodes to a literal `<object>` — and Qt, which parses a tooltip containing markup as rich text, swallowed it as an unknown tag. The line rendered "is treated as a suffix → `_Lightmap`", hiding exactly the substitution it was describing. Now `&amp;amp;lt;object&amp;amp;gt;` in the `.ui`, which reaches Qt as a literal `<object>`. The blank line between the two paragraphs was being collapsed by the same rich-text parsing, so it is now an explicit `<br/><br/>`. Verified through `QTextDocument` (the placeholder renders, the break survives) rather than by eye; `m3trik/scripts/check_tooltips.py` — which flagged it — is clean again. Mirrored in blendertk.

- **2026-08-04 — Marmoset bake pairing: `_source` becomes the default suffix, group-root tagging is an explicit knob, and the suffix rows go inert while a Bake Source set exists.** The vocabulary rename left the suffix choices behind — the panel still offered `_high` / `_hi` / `_HP` for a control now labelled *Source Suffix*, so the naming convention the docs describe (`engine_source`) matched nothing. `_source` joins the list as the **default** (`_target` joins the target list; both keep the old entries, and the bridge's own fallback for a programmatic caller now reads `template_params.DEFAULTS` instead of a second hardcoded `"_high"`). The ancestor walk that lets one suffixed **group root** tag every mesh under it was real but undiscoverable and unconditional; it is now the new **`SUFFIX_INCLUDE_CHILDREN`** parameter (default on, threaded through `split_source_target` / `_classify_by_chain` / `_classify_maya_chain` / `build_bake_pairs_manifest`). Off, only a mesh's own name counts — the escape hatch for a suffixed group holding a mix of source and target geometry, which the walk would otherwise adopt whole. Note the sidecar is what makes group tagging work at all: Toolbag flattens empty parent transforms on import, so the ancestor names are gone by the time the template looks. And because an explicit **Bake Source set classifies both sides outright**, the three suffix rows are now *greyed* whenever one exists, carrying the reason as a tooltip — they stay on screen (the value is still readable) but stop implying they steer the bake. That needed a general answer, so `uitk.bridge.slots` grew `set_param_enabled()` + a `_refresh_param_enablement()` hook that fires on every template change *and* every panel show; the panel also resyncs after Set/Select/Clear, which catches a set deleted from the Outliner. A new drift guard pins the two default registries against each other — `template_params.DEFAULTS` (what a headless `send()` renders with) and `parameters.PARAMS[k].default` (what the panel opens on) describe the same knobs and are edited by hand in two files, so a half-finished rename could previously leave the panel and a scripted send quietly disagreeing. Fixed on the way: the texture-set log line joined `getTextureSetName` results raw, so a Toolbag build returning a non-string would take down the whole bake setup on a log statement. **Also repaired 18 stale mock tests** that the Maya runner skips (they only run under plain pytest) and that the previous cycle's renames had broken — sidecar values still spelled `high`/`low`, `setField` still asserted against a path instead of the `mset.Texture` the wiring now binds, `BAKE_BITS` still asserted as a user-tunable param, and a `.psd`-only output assertion superseded by `OUTPUT_FORMAT`; that last one is now a subtest over png/tga/psd that also pins the constant `bake` stem. +9 tests across the mock and live-Maya modules (group-root tagging and its off switch verified against a real Maya DAG, managed padding/bits proven to ignore caller overrides), +5 uitk tests for the enablement helper. Engine slices re-mirrored byte-identical to blendertk and extapps.

- **2026-08-04 — Marmoset bridge: the transfer bake finally samples the maps it was handed — Toolbag's module scalars were silently multiplying them — and the roundtrip now restores the source's packed-map template on the way back in.** Live report on the production TOOL_SET atlas-bake scene (multi-material source → single-mat-ID target, one joint-packed UV atlas): metalness baked pure black and roughness at ~30%, with all 44 slots wired correctly. Probed headless on Toolbag 5.02: an FBX import leaves non-identity multipliers on the material modules (`microsurface.Roughness = 0.3`, `reflectivity.Metalness = 0.0`) and `setSubroutine` *preserves* them, so they scale any map bound afterwards — albedo escaped only because `albedo.Color` happens to import white. `wire_materials_from_manifest` now resets a module's scalar fields to identity right after binding a texture into it (new `MODULE_NEUTRAL_FIELDS`, guarded per-field via the live module's `getFieldNames`): once a map drives the module, the map is the authority. Verified on the real scene: metalness/roughness carry full per-region detail, and the baked atlas albedo matches the pooled source base colors at distribution level (means within 4/255, 32-bin histogram cosine ≥ 0.977). Two params stopped being params, per the manage-it rule: **edge padding** now derives from the map size via the ecosystem's single padding primitive (`MathUtils.calculate_uv_padding` — the marmoset bridge was the last holdout re-exposing it as a widget) and **bit depth** from the per-map-type `ptk.OutputTemplates` specs (bake-wide max across the enabled maps), both computed by the new `TemplateParams.derive_bake_values` and overwriting any caller value. The packed-source handling closed its loop: `_stage_manifest_textures` already unpacked MSAO / MetallicSmoothness / ORM sources for the transfer bake and now also *records* each material's packing (`{mat: packed_type}`); `_assign_baked_materials` restores that template on rewire — the baked components repack into the same layout via the matching `GameShader.create_network` flag (MSAO → `mask_map`, etc.; a bucket with no recorded source, i.e. the new-target-material single-set case, inherits the dominant packing across sources). Real-scene result: `<target>_BAKED` StingrayPBS wired Base_Color + Normal + repacked MSAO (smoothness inverted from baked roughness; channels verified R=metal/G=AO/A=smooth), assigned to all 13 targets. Panel-side, per the same review: template management left the header for the template combo's own menu, and the High Poly set actions became the bake params' `HIGH_POLY_SET` action row (new uitk `action` kind, auto-wired by the slots base) — mirrored in the substance panel, whose Set-From-Selection still auto-ticks Export High Poly. Two review directives landed on top: the whole bake vocabulary renamed **high/low -> source/target** (`BakeSourceSet` in `bake_sets.py`, canonical scene set `bakeBridge_source` with BOTH prior names adopted transparently, companion export now `<base>_source.fbx`, `source_model_path_for` with the high-poly names kept as back-compat aliases, `split_source_target` + 'source'/'target' sidecar values, Source/Target Suffix labels -- the set is usually a texture-set donor, not an actual high poly), and baked maps **no longer carry the scene-name prefix**: the bake template writes under a constant `bake_` stem and the roundtrip relocation strips it, so production files land as `<material>_<map>.<ext>` -- the texture set / material is the identity. Engine slices re-mirrored byte-identical to extapps AND blendertk (vendor-sync gates all green; the extapps `SCOPE` divergence entry is thereby resolved — the token echoes are inert without a registered spec). Scoped Maya tests 143 green; extapps 545 green (one stale helper test updated to the Texture-object binding contract).

- **2026-08-04 — Marmoset bridge: the bake roundtrip actually works now — explicit bake-from/to via the shared High Poly set, per-material texture sets, true surface transfer, and baked maps wired back as StingrayPBS.** Verified end-to-end against the production `TOOL_SET_baking.ma` scene (STATIC → HIGH retopo/UV-transfer layout), whose *identical mesh names on both sides* made the old leaf-name pairs sidecar silently self-overwrite and suffix pairing match nothing — the bake never had a source. The substance bridge's `HighPolySet` is promoted to shared `mat_utils/bake_sets.py` (canonical `bakeBridge_highPoly` objectSet; legacy `substanceBridge_highPoly` adopted transparently) and the marmoset panel gains the same Set/Select/Clear High Poly header actions: the scoped selection is the bake *target*, the set is the bake *source*, exported as a companion `<name>_high.fbx` and imported separately by the template so classification survives any naming. One bake group per matching leaf name isolates each pair's rays; `tileMode=1` gives one texture set per material (a single shared set would stack every material's UV layout into one image). Surface-transfer maps (Albedo/Roughness/Metalness, opt. Emissive) joined the geometry maps, with packed MSAO/MetallicSmoothness/ORM manifest slots unpacked into single-channel components via `MapFactory` (smoothness inverts into roughness) before wiring. Four Toolbag traps each cost a full bake to isolate: FBX imports default to Gloss/Specular subroutines (a roughness map in a 'Gloss Map' field means the opposite), `setSubroutine` rebuilds the material so it must precede *all* texture binds, `saveScene` before the bake re-serializes bindings into a colorspace-shifted state, and mutating `sRGB` on a bound field texture severs the binding — with those ordered correctly, an sRGB-flagged color source round-trips as an identity copy (baked-vs-original masked means within 2–11/255 on the real scene; the flat-color probe pins the writer at `sRGB_encode(sample)`). Roundtrips now bake into a local `TempArtifacts` scratch dir and copy out verified — Toolbag writing straight to the cloud-synced output dir dropped a PNG's first 8 bytes. On return, `ASSIGN_MATERIAL` (default on) builds a `<mat>_BAKED` StingrayPBS network per texture set via GameShader and assigns it to the recorded bake-target meshes. Also fixed on the way: `MatManifest.build` expanded to hierarchy roots (a group selection produced an *empty* manifest — `get_mats` reads direct shapes only — silently disabling material wiring in both bridges), `_toolbag_helpers`' emissive module attr (`emission`, not `emissive`), the suffix-fallback sidecar lookup now strips FBX `.001` duplicate suffixes (the docstring claimed it; only the chain walker did it), bridge/engine temp fallbacks moved to `ptk.TempArtifacts`, and PNG/TGA/PSD output via `OUTPUT_FORMAT` (production formats replace the old single `bake.psd`). The `<stem>_high` companion-path convention now has one home — `HighPolySet.companion_path` — with both bridges' `high_poly_path_for` delegating to it. `test_mat_marmoset_bridge` +8 (pairs-collision drop, set splitting, two-file export, group-selection manifest, output bucketing), new `test_mat_bake_sets` (legacy adoption); engine/helpers mirrored byte-identical to `extapps/marmoset_workflow`. Repaired on the way: Toolbag itself crashed at every launch on this machine since the June NVIDIA driver update — corrupt `workspaces` state dir, bisected and quarantined (`workspaces.broken` + full appdata backup kept).

- **2026-08-04 — Game Shader: a DirectX normal map was wired into Maya as-is, so every surface lit inverted.** The panel's Normal Type combo reaches `ptk.MapFactory.prepare_maps`, which green-flips and renames the file to `Normal_<type>` (measured: GL→DirectX flips, DX→OpenGL flips, matching pairs pass through). Nothing then undid it: `_connect_texture` handed the DirectX file straight to StingrayPBS's `TEX_normal_map` and to `bump2d` (`bumpInterp=1`) for standardSurface / openPBR, and Maya's tangent space is OpenGL (Y+). Every `invert` / `reverse` in the module is smoothness→roughness — there was no green flip anywhere. So picking DirectX (the `WF.UE` preset's own default) produced backwards lighting, while the **blendertk twin panel, given the identical setting, renders correctly** because it flips the green back in the node graph. Two panels, one combo, opposite results.

  Fixed at the FILE, because Maya offers nowhere else: probed on Maya 2025 / MtoA 7.3.4, `bump2d` carries no flip/invert attribute at all, and its tangent-space idiom needs the file node directly upstream (Maya traces back through the `outAlpha` hook), so a flip node cannot be grafted in between either. `_resolve_map_conflicts` now calls `ptk.MapFactory.resolve_normal_maps(target_format="OpenGL")` alongside the `filter_redundant_maps` call it already makes, and the converted OpenGL sibling joins the kept list exactly like an extracted packed channel does. The combo keeps its meaning for what lands on disk — the DirectX file is still written, it is the export deliverable — while the shader is always lit right, which is exactly the contract blendertk gets from its flip nodes.

  That same call closes a second hole: `Normal`, `Normal_OpenGL` and `Normal_DirectX` are three distinct map types, so `filter_redundant_maps` never collapsed them and a set carrying two drove the same slot twice with the last connection silently winning. Exactly one normal now reaches the shader, and every losing file is retired. The reduction and the conversion are app-agnostic path/image work with no `maya.cmds` in them, so they live in pythontk — only the *choice* of OpenGL as the target is Maya's, and that is the one thing passed in. Losers are reported in the outcome table rather than silently dropped. Verified live in a fresh Maya across all three shader types × both combo positions: the wired file is `Normal_OpenGL` with green matching the source every time.

- **2026-08-04 — Game Shader: OpenPBR lost its normal map entirely on Maya 2025.** `connect_open_pbr_nodes` connected `bump2d.outNormal` to `openPBRSurface.geometryNormal`, which does not exist on 2025 — the node exposes the classic `normalCamera` (probed: `geometryCoatNormal`, `normalCamera`, `triangleNormalCamera`, no `geometryNormal`). The connect raised "The destination attribute cannot be found", aborting the whole map. The plug is now resolved by probing, preferring the OpenPBR-spec name where a newer Maya provides it and falling back to `normalCamera`, with the standard missing-slot report if neither exists. Found while verifying the DirectX fix end to end.

- **2026-08-04 — `test_render_opacity`'s restore assertion was blind to per-channel connections.** `test_textures_restored_after_graph_swap` and `test_textures_restored_on_split_duplicate` failed with `None != '.../test_roughness.png'`, which reads as a lost texture — the production code was right and the helper was stale. `TEX_roughness_map` / `TEX_metallic_map` are `float3` while the channel map declares the scalar `outColorR`, so `ShaderAttributeMap` correctly drives `…X/Y/Z` individually (a scalar cannot connect to a compound parent), and `listConnections` on the parent then reports **nothing** — verified on Maya 2025. The assertion only ever queried the parent; it passed previously because the old blanket `outColor` retry connected the parent instead, which is exactly the behaviour that was removed for wiring RGB into scalar slots. `_get_connected_texture_path` now falls back to the child plugs. No production change.

- **2026-08-04 — Removed dead `GameShader.filter_for_correct_normal_map`.** No production caller in any of the six packages — only its own definition, its tests, and a commented-out copy of an older version parked at the bottom of the file. `create_network` has long delegated normal conversion to `prepare_maps`. Both copies deleted; its tests were replaced by ones driving `_resolve_map_conflicts`, which is the path that actually runs. (One of them, `test_multiple_normal_maps_same_type`, never tested what it claimed: its "duplicate" fixture `model_Normal_OpenGL_2.png` classifies as None, since a trailing `_2` puts the alias off the end.)

- **2026-08-04 — Substance bridge: staged textures reach the right texture set, and channel-packed maps stop arriving unreadable.** Two defects in the texture leg, plus the parameter that fixes the third.

  **(1) Every staged texture was passed as `--mesh-map`.** That flag is for Painter's *baked-geometry* inputs — AO, normal, thickness — and Painter files one by reading its filename suffix. A base-color or roughness map handed to it has no slot to land in, so the bridge was shipping Painter files it has no use for. `--mesh-map` now takes only what `MapFactory.resolve_map_type` classifies as a mesh map (new `SubstanceBridge.MESH_MAP_TYPES` / `mesh_map_files`). Material channels still ship — they ride the FBX's embedded textures and the manifest — they just stop being announced as something they aren't.

  **(2) `--mesh-map` can only apply a map to EVERY texture set**, so on a multi-material asset one material's AO was smeared across all of them. There is no CLI form of "this map, that texture set". The manifest — which `import.py` has always built, with a comment saying it existed "so a future Painter plugin can map FBX material slots back to Maya shaders + baked map files" — now carries a `mesh_maps` section, `{material: {usage: staged_path}}`, and the plugin's new `textures.apply_mesh_maps` op consumes it: Painter names each texture set after its FBX material, so the match is by name, and a set with no entry is left alone (safe against a project the user has already reorganised). Matching is by **base texture name**, not path — the manifest records where a map lives in the *Maya scene* while the file Painter reads is the staged copy, which may have been renamed by the texture prefix or produced by unpacking, in which case no manifest slot points at it at all. `MapFactory.get_base_texture_name` strips the map suffix from both sides, so `body_ORM.png` → `body_AO.png` still resolves to the material that referenced the ORM. Painter's `MeshMapUsage` member names have moved between releases, so each usage is resolved case-insensitively against whatever the running build exposes and an absent one is skipped with a named message rather than failing the whole assignment.

  **(3) New `PAINTER_UNPACK_MAPS` (default on).** Painter has no concept of a channel-packed texture, so an ORM / MRAO / MSAO / MetallicSmoothness / AlbedoTransparency file was staged and then ignored — and only its AO channel is a mesh map at all; the rest are material channels. Packed sources are now split into components in the output folder via `pythontk`'s existing `MapFactory.unpack_*` family (verified against real PNGs: an ORM at RGB(10,120,240) yields `_AO`=10, `_Roughness`=120, `_Metallic`=240, each round-tripping back through `resolve_map_type`). Components inherit the texture prefix through the same idempotent rule as the copy path. A corrupt or unreadable packed file falls back to a plain copy rather than sinking the staging pass — an unusable file in the folder still beats no texture. `_stage_assigned_textures` keeps its flat-list return, so nothing downstream had to change.

  Three details the first cut got wrong, caught in review: the texture **prefix** renames the staged copy, so the manifest's Maya-side name and the shipped filename disagree and the whole wiring section came back empty for anyone who set one — `get_base_texture_name` already takes a `prefix=` kwarg, so it is now threaded through. A material whose slots span two base names resolved a usage via **set iteration order**, i.e. potentially a different file per run; the bases are sorted. And **height/displacement** were listed as mesh maps — Painter bakes normal, world-space normal, ID, AO, curvature, position and thickness, with no height slot, so shipping one was the same mistake as shipping a base color. On the plugin side `resource.Usage.TEXTURE` was hard-coded next to a carefully-resolved `MeshMapUsage`; it now resolves the same way, since a reshaped enum would otherwise AttributeError mid-assignment.

  The wiring op joins resolution and high-poly on the same defer-or-apply path, and is dispatched only when a `mesh_maps` section was actually written.
