The deferred-replay callback now isolates each knob, so one failure can't swallow the other two. `test_substance_bridge.py` +22 (classification, per-material assignment incl. the unpacked-component and prefix cases, unpacking against real images, the every-registered-unpacker-exists guard), `test_substance_rpc_plugin.py` +11 (per-set targeting, unknown usage skipped, resource-usage resolution incl. the no-usable-member report, missing file skipped, deferral, one-knob-fails isolation). 269 tests green in Maya including the adjacent `mat_utils` / `mat_manifest` suites. Mirrored in blendertk; the six DCC-agnostic helpers are AST-identical across both packages. Deferred to BACKLOG: the project **normal-map format** is still New Project dialog territory — unlike resolution it has no known post-creation setter, and the entry records the exact `system.eval` probe to settle it against a live Painter.

- **2026-08-04 — Substance bridge: a scene-persistent high-poly bake source, and the Document Resolution knob Painter took away.** Two panel parameters on `import` / `reimport` / `bake_lighting`. **Export High Poly** ships a companion `<name>_high.fbx` beside the paintable mesh and points Painter's baking options at it; **Map Resolution** (default 4096) resizes every texture set. Neither has a command line — Painter dropped `--resolution` and never had a high-poly flag — so both travel over the `substance_rpc` plugin's new `project.set_resolution` / `bake.set_high_poly` ops.

  **The "export it visible even though it's hidden" dance turned out to be unnecessary, which is the whole design.** Measured in mayapy 2025 across four ways of hiding geometry — `visibility 0` on the node, the node selected alone, a hidden parent group, and display-layer + `lodVisibility` — Maya's FBX exporter wrote **full vertex data every time**; the only trace of hidden-ness is a `P: "Visibility", …, 0` property on that node's `Model` record, and the display-layer case leaves no trace at all. So the export needs no unhide/re-hide pass, which matters because `visibility` is frequently **locked or connected** (`setAttr` then raises `RuntimeError` with no clean recovery) — a force-visible-then-restore leg would have had an unfixable failure mode for exactly the rigged/referenced assets most likely to carry a high-poly source. The bridge therefore **never modifies the scene**: "restore afterwards" is satisfied by never leaving. The one bit of state the leg does touch is the selection, because `FbxUtils.export` selects what it exports — snapshotted and restored in a `finally` so the send leaves behind whatever the main export left, not the high-poly set.

  Membership lives in the **scene**, as an `objectSet` named `substanceBridge_highPoly` (`HighPolySet`), the same pattern as `EmissiveGroups` and `ColorId` — it saves with the file, shows in the Outliner, and cannot go stale against a scene it was never captured in, which a panel-stored name list would. It is read *instead of* the export scope, never merged into it, so **"Visible Only" stays exactly as wide as the user set it** even though the members are hidden. Defined from the header menu (Set From Selection / Select / Clear); the checkbox only decides whether to ship it.

  Painter-side, the ops **apply now or arm themselves**: the bridge dispatches right after launch, when the user still has the New Project wizard open and `project.is_open()` is False, so each op stores its value and replays it on `ProjectEditionEntered` (preferred over `ProjectOpened` — baking parameters need an *editable* project) — which is what makes both knobs work on a one-click new project, not just on reimport. Pending values are last-write-wins and consumed on apply, so a second send supersedes the first rather than queueing and a value can never leak into an unrelated later project. `close_plugin` tears the subscription down, so disabling the plugin doesn't leave a callback that keeps rewriting the next project that opens. The high-poly parameter is matched **case-insensitively against a candidate list** in `BakingParameters.common()` rather than hard-coded: Painter has never guaranteed the spelling, and an unmatched key now raises with the keys Painter actually offers instead of a bare `KeyError`.

  Both ops are added **per run by the bridge**, not declared in the templates, so a send that asks for neither keeps `RPC_OPS` empty and still launches Painter without first waiting on the plugin's endpoint — and they go **ahead of** the template's own ops, not after: `mesh.reload` (reimport) is asynchronous, returning `{"started": True}` and finishing via callback, so appending would have mutated texture sets while a reload was in flight. Ahead of it they act on a settled project, and baking parameters survive the reload that follows.

  New `test_substance_bridge_scene.py` (15) is the Maya-runtime half — `test_substance_bridge.py` is deliberately Maya-free and errors on collection under the venv if given a `MayaTkTestCase`. It pins what a mock would paper over: the hidden 242-point sphere's geometry reaching `<name>_high.fbx` while the low export holds only the cube's 8, visibility and selection and set membership unchanged afterwards, the hidden member absent from `get_visible_geometry`, no `_high` file when unticked, an empty set warning instead of sinking the send, and — the case that rules out a force-visible pass — a **locked** `visibility` exporting fine and staying locked. `test_substance_bridge.py` +14, `test_substance_rpc_plugin.py` +17 (deferral, consumption, last-write-wins, key matching, empty/zero no-ops, teardown). Mirrored in blendertk (Collection instead of `objectSet`); panel parity sweep clean.

- **2026-08-04 — `test_helpers_survive_missing_maya` asserted against ambient state and only passed when it ran first.** It claimed "in this venv the module-level `from maya import cmds` failed, so the name doesn't exist" — but `test/conftest.py` injects a MagicMock `maya.cmds` before any test module loads, so the import *succeeds* and `cmds` is that mock; under mayapy it's the real thing. Neither run ever reached the degradation path the test was written to guard, and the assertion passed or failed on whether something else had touched the name first. The absence is now staged explicitly (`del sb.cmds` + `addCleanup` restore), so the case exercises the `NameError` → no-op contract deterministically in both environments. Found while adding the high-poly leg; it was failing before that work and fails identically with it stashed.

- **2026-08-04 — `get_similar_mesh` compared where objects SIT, not what they are: with the "Bounding Box" metric enabled it could never match anything that had been moved, and "World Area" could reject a merely *rotated* duplicate.** Reported from tentacle's Selection panel as "Select Similar selects nothing, even though I duplicated identical geo around the scene", with Vertex/Edge/Face/World Area/Bounding Box checked at tolerance 0. Two independent defects, both measured in mayapy against a cube and three duplicates (moved, moved+rotated, moved+scaled). (1) **Every Maya bounding-box query reports a WORLD axis-aligned box** — `polyEvaluate -boundingBox`, `xform -q -bb`, and `xform -q -bb -ws` all returned `((9.0, 11.0), …)` for a cube translated 10 units — so the raw `(min, max)` pairs the comparison used encode placement. A copy 10 units away was a guaranteed non-match, which is why the whole button appeared dead: one checkbox vetoed every candidate regardless of the others. Reducing them to extents fixes position but not orientation (a 45°-rotated cube's world AABB is 2.83 wide, not 2.0), so the metric now uses the shape's `boundingBoxMin/Max` — the only orientation-free measure Maya exposes — times world scale, which is exactly blendertk's `object.dimensions`, the fork that already had this right. Position and rotation no longer disqualify; scale still does. (2) **`worldArea` comes back in single precision**, so an identical mesh evaluated through a world transform reports e.g. `599.9999389648438` against `600.0` — inside float32 round-off, but a hard reject at tolerance 0. Drift is transform-dependent, not constant: the same cube rotated 45° about Y drifted when translated on X and landed exact when translated on Z, which is why this reads as intermittent. Float metrics now carry a relative epsilon floor, and **it is per coordinate space, because a first cut calibrated on a cube at the origin still needed a hand-set tolerance on real geometry.** Measured across cube / sphere / torus / plane from 6 to 160,000 faces: the drift tracks **distance from the origin, not mesh size or face count** — the float32 ulp at distance *d* is *d*·2⁻²³, so a 0.05-radius sphere parked at (-1000, 500, 2000) drifts 6.7e-3 relative while a 160k-face plane at the origin drifts 5e-6. `worldArea` (the only world-space metric) therefore gets 1%; `area` and the bounding-box dimensions are object-space and measured bit-identical across translation, rotation and scale (1e-16), so they get a token 1e-9; **integer counts keep the caller's tolerance verbatim**, so a 250k-vertex mesh off by one is still a non-match. 1% is far below any real scale difference — a 1.01x copy already differs 2% in area — and worldArea is never a lone gate in practice, since the counts and dimensions compare exactly. Swept after the fix: every combination of six mesh densities and five placements out to (5000, 5000, 5000) matches at tolerance 0.000, while 1.01x and 1.002x scaled copies stay excluded. Comparison also moved from one flat `are_similar` call to per-metric, so each metric's tolerance is derived from its own magnitude, and `polyEvaluate` now runs once per scene mesh instead of once per (query, mesh) pair. `test_edit_utils.py` +5 (bbox matches a moved and a rotated twin while excluding a 2x-scaled one and a larger cube; a rotated sphere survives worldArea drift at tolerance 0; a small dense mesh 2.3k units from the origin does too; a 1.01x scaled copy is still rejected; the floor never loosens integer metrics and stays nominal for object-space floats) — all verified failing against the pre-fix source, and the epsilon is mutation-pinned from both sides: back at 1e-5 the distance test goes red, at 100% the scaled-copy test does. Deferred to BACKLOG: `CoreUtils.get_bounding_box(world=False)` and `XformUtils.get_bounding_box(world_space=False)` have the same "object space" claim over the same world-space queries.

- **2026-08-04 — `transfer_uvs` sampled in TOPOLOGY space unconditionally, so transferring onto any mesh that wasn't already a topological twin did nothing at all — silently.** Reported from the tentacle UV panel as "Transfer UVs is not working in Maya", with the two meshes "a bit different". The docstring promised the method was "topology-agnostic and can work with different mesh structures", but the call hardcoded `sampleSpace=4`, which is `transferAttributes`' **topology** space — the one mode that requires source and target to correspond vertex-for-vertex. Measured in mayapy by sweeping every sampleSpace against four target shapes: an identical cube and a merely *scaled* cube transferred fine (same vert/edge/face counts), while a subdivided cube and a sphere came back bit-identical to their starting UVs under sampleSpace 3 and 4, and transferred correctly under 0/1/2. No error, no warning, no history node left behind — the button simply had no effect, which is why it read as a dead control rather than a failed operation. The similarity gate was a red herring: a cube→sphere pair still scores ~0.51, far above the 0.1 default, so it was passing the filter and then no-oping at the Maya call.

  `sample_space` is now a real parameter defaulting to `"auto"`, which picks **per pair**: matching topology keeps sampling in topology space (exact, and independent of where either mesh sits — the RizomUV and Ministry-of-Flat round-trips rely on that, since those engines rewrite UVs without touching topology), and anything else falls back to a spatial sample. **Object space, not world**, decided by measurement rather than by copying Maya's own Transfer Attributes default: a UV donor is normally staged off to the side of its target, and with a 20-unit offset world space collapsed the whole target onto whichever corner of the source was nearest (U range `[0.913, 1.250]` against the source's `[0.250, 1.250]`), while object space reproduced the source layout exactly in both the coincident and the offset placement. This is the two-tier shape blendertk's `transfer_uvs` has always had (exact loop-buffer copy when the counts match, `data_transfer` otherwise) — mayatk was the fork missing the fallback tier.

  The five codes are published as `UvUtils.SAMPLE_SPACES`, named the way **Maya's own option box** names them rather than by inference: `scripts/others/performTransferAttributes.mel` builds the radio group World / Object / UV / Component (plus Topology in a shared-collection group offset by 4) and passes `selection - 1` as the flag, which pins world=0, object=1, uv=2, component=3, topology=4. Worth reading the vendor's UI code for this — probing empirically got the codes right but would have published code 1 as "local", a name Maya never uses and that disagrees with the object-frame vocabulary `mirror` / `cut_along_axis` already share.

  The topology probe cannot use a naive truth test: **`polyEvaluate` answers a non-mesh with the truthy STRING `'Nothing counted : no polygonal object is selected.'`** rather than raising or returning None, so a locator would have compared equal to another locator and been declared a topological match. `_mesh_topology_signature` returns None unless all three counts are genuine ints, and an unmeasured node never claims a match — so a non-mesh still fails at `transferAttributes` with Maya's own `RuntimeError: No deformable objects selected.`, exactly as before. Also dropped the hardcoded `sourceUvSpace="map1"` / `targetUvSpace="map1"` in favour of each mesh's current UV set: those arguments name a set explicitly, and a mesh whose primary set was renamed has no `map1` for them to resolve against. The method now **returns** one `(source, target, sample_space_used)` per transfer performed — previously `None`, so a caller could not tell a completed run from a similarity search that paired nothing, which is what the UV panel needed to report the outcome. `transfer_uvs` became a `@classmethod` to reach the new helpers; the flat `mtk.transfer_uvs` binding is unaffected. `test_uv_utils.py` +6 (differing topology transfers, survives a world offset, matching topology stays exact and reports `"topology"`, the per-pair report, the non-mesh signature and its preserved failure mode, and an unknown `sample_space` rejected) — all six verified failing against the pre-fix source.

- **2026-08-03 — `auto_unwrap` (Ministry of Flat) came back with EVERY edge of the mesh cut.** Reported from the tentacle UV panel: a Hard Surface unwrap left the whole model as one shell per face. Not a seam-detection failure — **Ministry of Flat emits one `vt` per face corner and never shares a UV index between two faces**, even where the coordinates are bit-identical. Confirmed by running `UnWrapConsole3.exe` on a 6-quad cube: 24 `vt` lines for 14 distinct coordinates, `f 1/1 2/2 4/3 3/4` / `f 3/5 4/6 …` — indices never reused. Maya's OBJ import honours that indexing literally (one UV point per corner), so the payload arrived with every edge a UV border and `transfer_uvs` faithfully copied that topology onto the user's mesh. Measured in mayapy: a 2×2×2 cube round-tripped to 96 UVs / 24 UV shells for 24 faces; a 240-face torus to 960 UVs / 240 shells. `_unwrap_one` now welds coincident UVs on the imported payload (`polyMergeUV`) before the transfer — the same cube returns 39 UVs / 1 shell, the torus 273 / 1. `polyMergeUV` can only merge UVs belonging to the same *vertex*, so it cannot fuse islands the engine meant to keep apart; the distance is a float-noise guard rather than a seam-closing radius (results identical from 1e-6 to 1e-3, all far under the engine's island gutter). BFF deduplicates its own `vt` table, so the weld is a no-op on the organic path — and blendertk needs no counterpart, since Blender stores UVs per loop and derives islands from coordinates, making the quirk invisible there. The existing tests missed it because the stub engine rewrote `vt` *values* in place and so inherited Maya's own index sharing; the new stub reproduces MoF's per-corner indexing. Verified end-to-end against the real executables on cube/cylinder/pipe/torus (MoF) and sphere (BFF): sane shell counts, UVs inside the 0-1 tile.
- **2026-08-03 — Mirror's pivot was silently the object's local ORIGIN, not its pivot — and `use_object_axes` never reached the mirror plane at all.** Reported as "the mirror pivot is completely broken and unpredictable" (Instance off, i.e. the `polyMirrorFace` path), and it was two defects stacked. (1) **`_mirror_pivot_point` special-cased `"object"`/`"original"` to `MPoint(0,0,0) * worldMatrix`** — the world position of the transform's local origin — instead of the rotate pivot every other op reads via `XformUtils.get_operation_axis_pos`. Those coincide on a fresh primitive, which is why it looked fine, and diverge the moment the pivot is dragged (mirror plane stays behind at the origin) or the object is FROZEN: `makeIdentity` leaves the rotate pivot at its world position while the local origin snaps to the world origin, so mirroring a frozen asset "about itself" mirrored it about the world, half a scene away. Measured in mayapy by inferring the plane from the post-mirror bbox (the union of a point set and its reflection is symmetric about the plane): dragged pivot at x=7 mirrored about x=5; frozen object at x=5 mirrored about x=0. The object-frame branch was ALSO a mathematical identity for `"manip"`/`"baked"` (`p * M⁻¹ * M == p`), so it never did anything except break the two pivots it special-cased. (2) **The mirror plane's normal was always a WORLD axis** despite `use_object_axes=True` and a docstring promising the object's own — `polyMirrorFace` was hard-coded `worldSpace=True` and the instance path built an axis-aligned reflection matrix. Proven by mirroring a 45°-rotated asymmetric mesh: the new points matched a world-X reflection EXACTLY and an object-X reflection not at all. The flag had been "fixed" in Feb behind a test that only asserted the object still existed, which is true under any plane. Now `_mirror_frame` resolves `(world_point, frame_matrix)` through the same `OBJECT_FRAME_PIVOTS` gate `cut_along_axis` already used, so a rotated object cuts and mirrors about the same plane. `mirror` mirrors in the node's local space (`worldSpace=False`, pivot folded in — probed bit-identical to the world path for unrotated objects, and correct under rotation, negative scale, non-uniform scale, and a rotated PARENT, the case that would break every grouped object if `polyMirrorFace`'s local space disagreed with the world-matrix frame). `mirror_instance` builds the same operation rather than a lookalike: `_mirror_reflection` conjugates the plain axis-aligned reflection with the frame (`F⁻¹ · R · F`), which is exactly what `polyMirrorFace` does with `worldSpace` off, so the two paths cannot drift — including under shear, where a general-normal Householder (the first cut at this) would silently have been a *different* operation, since the world image of a sheared local reflection is not a Householder at all. Conjugation also means any frame works, so `"original"`'s pre-freeze axes — which are an axis of nothing Maya can be asked for directly — are honored exactly by `mirror_instance`; `polyMirrorFace` has no plane-rotation flag and cannot express them, so `mirror` warns by name and falls back to the live axes. This brings mayatk into line with blendertk, whose `_plane_frame` has always rotated the normal for `"object"`. `test_edit_tools_geometry.py` +16 (pivot fidelity: dragged pivot, frozen object, agreement with `get_operation_axis_pos` across all four object-frame pivots, flag-independence, the instance path; object axes: tilted plane, rotated parent, forced world, world-pivot unaffected, unrotated no-regression, instance/geometry agreement, separate mode, frame-and-pivot composition, the announced `"original"` fallback, and `mirror_instance` genuinely reflecting across the pre-freeze plane), and the vacuous `test_mirror_use_object_axes` now compares the two frames on a rotated object with an off-center pivot — a centered cube is symmetric in both frames and cannot tell them apart. Deferred to BACKLOG: the `cut_along_axis` → `delete_along_axis` → `mirror` handoff passes a bare world tuple, so an object-frame CUT is still followed by a world-frame mirror (pre-existing, reachable from Cut On Axis's `pivot="manip"` default), and "manip" now means object-axes in Maya but world-axes in Blender.

- **2026-08-03 — `EnvUtils.saved_scene_path()`, and the "scene has not been saved" guard that never fired in batch.** `cmds.file(q=True, sceneName=True)` is documented as `""` for an unsaved scene, and IS in the GUI — but batch/standalone returns a phantom **extensionless** `<project>/untitled` instead (measured in mayapy). So every `if not scene_path:` guard written against the documented behavior silently passed there: `export_scene_as_fbx()` called with no path wrote `Documents/maya/projects/default/untitled.fbx` instead of raising, and tentacle's Scene panel did the same for its "Alongside Scene File" export mode. The rule that actually distinguishes them — a real scene name always carries a type extension, since Maya cannot save without one — was already written out inline in `SceneExporter` and `PlayblastExporter.scene_name`; it is now one helper the whole package shares, and both `export_scene_as_*` route through it. Deliberately does NOT probe the disk: a stray file left at the phantom path must not re-legitimize it. `test_env_utils.py` +3 (an unsaved scene reads unsaved even though the raw query is truthy; a renamed-not-saved scene still reads as saved; a planted file at the phantom path changes nothing).

- **2026-08-03 — `EnvUtils.export_scene_as_obj()`: the OBJ sibling of `export_scene_as_fbx`.** Maya's OBJ translator is named `OBJexport`, its options are a semicolon-delimited string, the plugin needs loading first, and it has no `-s` equivalent — the scope is chosen by `exportSelected` vs `exportAll`. Four details no caller should carry, which is exactly why the FBX twin exists. `blendertk` ships the same-named twin with the same parameters (`selection_only` / `materials` / `smoothing` / `normals` / `groups`), so tentacle's new Export Scene format combo dispatches to either without a per-DCC branch. Returns the written path (the FBX twin predates that convention and still returns None). Live-verified in mayapy: whole scene → both objects' group records + normals + UVs + the `.mtl` sidecar; `selection_only` → the cube alone (8 verts, not 390); `materials=False` → no sidecar. `test_env_utils.py` +4.

- **2026-08-03 — Three defects in the StingrayPBS path, all of which produced a wrong-but-silent material.** Surfaced by making the game shader the bridge default (the live send harness caught every one). (1) **`ShaderAttributeMap` declared StingrayPBS opacity as `TEX_opacity_map`, an attribute that does not exist** — probed live against Maya 2025: `Standard_Transparent.sfx` carries a SCALAR `opacity` (plus `use_opacity_map`) and `Standard.sfx` has no opacity slot at all, so every Stingray opacity rescue silently no-oped. (2) **`connect_stingray_nodes` tagged no color space**, so Maya's per-extension default read every data map as sRGB — roughness/metallic/normal/AO arriving gamma-DECODED, i.e. wrong values with no error. The file node is now `Raw` by default with the color/emissive pair opting out, mirroring the standardSurface path, and the Opacity branch sets `alphaIsLuminance` since it reads through `outAlpha`. (3) **A ShaderFX slot is inert until its `use_*_map` toggle is set**, which the classified path (`_wire`) has always done and the manifest-replay path never did — a rescued texture was connected AND invisible, the worst thing to debug because the graph looks correct. `ShaderAttributeMap.connect_channel` now sets the toggle after a successful connect, so both routes agree. The naming rule itself is one method on the SSoT (`ShaderAttributeMap.map_toggle_attr`) that both routes read — `_wire` used to inline a bare `TEX_` -> `use_` substitution, which leaves a non-TEX slot (`opacity`) pointing at ITSELF, so its two call sites had to name the toggle by hand; the shared rule handles both shapes and that `flag` escape hatch is gone. Related: a shader decides its SLOTS at creation, so an opacity that only the bridge manifest knows about (a product-named cutout map, unclassifiable by filename) is declared via config BEFORE the build — discovering it afterwards left nothing to wire into and the cutout arrived fully opaque. `test_shader_attribute_map.py` +3, built against real ShaderFX graphs (a bare `shadingNode("StingrayPBS")` has no graph in batch mode and exposes none of the TEX_* slots, which made the old fixture prove nothing).

- **2026-08-03 — A rebuilt material keeps its source name, and the rebuild defaults to the game shader.** Every material arriving from Blender was renamed: `MAT_VDATS_instruments` → `MAT_VDATS_instruments1`, compounding on each re-send (live production report). The rebuild is necessarily built while the FBX-carried material still OWNS the name, so Maya hands it the clash spelling; the FBX one is purged moments later and the name falls free, but nothing reclaimed it. `_claim_material_name(sg, desired)` now takes it back after the purge, carrying the shading group along (`M_x1SG` → `M_xSG`, both naming conventions handled) and **yielding silently when the name is still in use** — the object-level fallback runs exactly when the FBX material was never matched, so it may still be assigned. Names are the binding for a game-engine-bound asset, which made this a destructive transfer. `test_scene_import.py` +2 (reclaim, and never-steal-a-live-name), plus a live-harness assertion on exact names. Separately, the rebuild default moved from `standard_surface` to **`stingray`**: these hand-offs feed a game engine, it matches `GameShader`'s own default, and it is the only shader family that DECLARES its texture slots — so its maps survive the trip back out instead of being re-guessed from filenames. `standardSurface` remains the fallback floor where ShaderFX is absent. `BlenderSceneImport.import_scene(..., shader_type=...)` exposes the same choice on the pull direction, which runs the identical rebuild and must not disagree with the Blender-side send panel.

- **2026-08-03 — The bridge's material rebuild can target OpenPBR Surface or Stingray PBS, not just Standard Surface.** `GameShader` has built all three for a long time (`shader_type` = `standard_surface` / `open_pbr` / `stingray`); the bridge simply hardcoded `"standard_surface"` at one line in `_rebuild_material`, so the capability was unreachable from a send. `_apply_texture_manifest(..., shader_type=...)` and `_rebuild_material(..., shader_type=...)` now carry the choice through to the engine, using **GameShader's own vocabulary** rather than a second spelling to keep in step. `standardSurface` was the default at first (it renders in any viewport with no plugin and exists in every supported Maya); the entry below moved it to `stingray` and kept `standardSurface` as the universal fallback floor. A type this Maya cannot build (openPBR needs a recent Maya 2025+, Stingray needs the ShaderFX plugin) degrades to it with a NAMED warning instead of losing the material — probed by *attempt*, not by capability query, because `StingrayPBS` only registers as a node type once its plugin loads, so a pre-flight check reports a false negative. The "nothing classified, rescue only" fallback builds a bare shader of the requested type too (via the existing `setup_*_node` methods, each of which brings its own shading group) rather than hardcoding a standardSurface. Driven from Blender by the new **Rebuild Shader** panel option (see blendertk's CHANGELOG). `test_scene_import.py` +1 and the stubbed seam now asserts `(files, name, slots, shader_type)` WHOLE — the applier's per-entry `except` swallows a signature mismatch, so the call has to be pinned or the choice silently no-ops. Live-verified through the real bridge on Maya 2025: `open_pbr` → `openPBRSurface`, `stingray` → `StingrayPBS`, default → `standardSurface`.

- **2026-08-03 — `MatUtils.connect_to_channels` is now all-or-nothing (and the opacity connector delegates to it instead of duplicating it).** The primitive connected a scalar source to a compound slot's three children with no exception guard, so a child that refused (locked, wrong type) raised out of a method whose contract is a `bool` — after it had already broken the parent's existing input and driven one or two children. Both callers (`GameShader._connect_channel`, `MatUtils.enable_viewport_opacity`) treat `False` as "report and move on", so the raise was never handled. It now rolls back the children it made AND restores the parent input it broke, returning `False` — a failure is a genuine no-op. `ShaderAttributeMap._connect_plug` (added earlier the same day) had hand-rolled its own broadcast; it now tries the declared plug directly and falls through to this primitive on an arity mismatch, so there is ONE implementation of "single channel into a compound slot" rather than two.

- **2026-08-03 — A texture's COLOR was being wired into `opacity`, so color drove transparency on every rebuilt material (live report on the Blender→Maya send).** Every PBR shader in `ShaderAttributeMap` correctly declares `opacity` as `outAlpha` — opacity IS the image's alpha — but the *attribute* is a **float3** (`standardSurface.opacity`, `openPBRSurface.geometryOpacity`, `lambert.transparency`), and Maya rejects a scalar→compound connection outright ("Data types of source and destination are not compatible", verified in mayapy). `MatManifest.restore` then retried on `outColor`, which made the types line up by **changing which data flows** — the texture's RGB landed in opacity. The same invalid connection sat in `GameShader.connect_standard_surface_nodes`' `Opacity` branch (`outAlpha → standardSurface.opacity`), so a *classified* Opacity map raised and never connected at all; the openPBR twin and the `Albedo_Transparency` branch already did it right by hand ("Opacity is RGB, connect alpha to all channels"), which is the knowledge the other two paths lacked. Fixed at the SSoT instead of a fourth time: **`ShaderAttributeMap.connect_channel(file_node, logical, shader)`** is now the one place that turns a declaration into real connections — it broadcasts a scalar source across a compound destination's children (rolling back partial broadcasts), and calls the new `_prepare_alpha_source`, which enables `alphaIsLuminance` when the image has **no** alpha channel (without it `outAlpha` is a constant 1.0, so a grayscale Opacity map connects perfectly and does *nothing*) while never overriding a real alpha channel. `MatManifest.restore` and **all three** GameShader sites that knew this rule now route through it — the broken `standardSurface` `Opacity` branch plus the two hand-rolled R/G/B loops (`standardSurface` `Albedo_Transparency`, `openPBRSurface` `Albedo_Transparency` / `Opacity`) — so "opacity is RGB, broadcast the alpha" lives in exactly one place instead of the four-way split that let one copy be wrong. The arity fallback also strictly improves the StingrayPBS `TEX_*` case the old comment described (broadcasting the declared `outColorR` beats swapping to `outColor`). Note for future probes: `listConnections` on a compound PARENT reports nothing when only its children are connected — assertions must read per child. `test_mat_manifest.py` +6 (alpha-not-color, shared file node, luminance rescue, real-alpha untouched, scalar channels unregressed, lambert unregressed) and `test_shader_attribute_map.py` +7 pinning the connector's own contract (broadcast, direct scalar, direct compound, unmapped / unknown-type / missing-attribute all False, and a locked child proving a partial broadcast is rolled back); full `test_game_shader` (85) and `test_mat_utils` (57) green; live-verified through the bridge (blendertk's `send_live_e2e.py` cutout leg).

- **2026-08-03 — USD pull: the instance replay is now GUARANTEED-OR-FAIL — a failed rebuild fails the conversion atomically instead of silently flattening the scene.** Closes the deferred silent-failure entry (BACKLOG 2026-08-02): all four USD legs swallowed a failed `_apply_instance_manifest` (warn/print-and-continue), and in the bake a `None` engine skipped it by design — the scene then carried N independent shapes, rendered correctly, and only betrayed itself when an artist edited one "instance" and its siblings didn't follow. Worse, the matching itself was silently fragile: the sidecar recorded RAW Blender object names while the exporter writes SANITIZED prim names (probed live on Blender 5.1: `Chair.001` → `Chair_001`, and a leading digit is PREFIXED — `1digit` → `_1digit`, NOT `TfMakeValidIdentifier`'s replacement), so Blender's own Alt+D duplicate naming flattened every time; and a same-named node in the open scene made mayaUsd rename the incoming transform out from under the leaf-name matcher. The v2 contract closes every hole: the conversion ALWAYS writes the sidecar (empty groups included, `version: 2` / `format: "names"`, sanitized), fails the export loudly on a sanitize-collision (the exporter renames colliding prims apart unpredictably — `a.b`/`a_b` → `a_b`/`a_b_001`, probe-verified), and DELETES the USD when the sidecar can't be written — success is judged by the artifact, so a payload that would import flattened never ships. The import lands in an isolation namespace (`UsdUtils.import_scene(namespace=…)`, so prim→node names hold no matter what the scene already contains), the strict replay accounts for every member (unmatched / ambiguous / shapeless master ⇒ `RuntimeError`, stale v1 sidecar refused), and only then does the namespace merge to the root — MDagPath handles carry the clash renames (`_merge_import_namespace`). On ANY failure the namespace is deleted wholesale: the scene is exactly as it was. The bake leg raises BEFORE saving, so the child exits 1 with no artifact and the parent's judged-by-artifact contract reports a failed bake instead of caching a flattened `.ma`; `bake_scene` refuses upfront when the conversion's sidecar is missing. FBX stays the default route; USD stays opt-in — now with instancing parity guaranteed rather than hoped. `test_scene_import.py` +10 (strict-replay class incl. behavioral clash-survival, rollback, and a sanitizer pin against the live probe); live e2e green on both routes.

- **2026-08-03 — Blender-scene imports restore Empties as the CORRECT node types, and both directions carry group/locator identity in the manifest (live report: transform/locator hierarchy lost on the Blender→Maya send).** Maya's FBX importer gives EVERY null a locator shape, and `BlenderSceneImport._restore_empty_groups` then demoted every parent to a plain group — including locators that legitimately parent geometry (snap points, rig handles). The repair is now manifest-aware (`_manifest_empty_rules`): the sender records each Empty's `empty_display_type` (+ the `maya_node_type` custom property round-tripped scenes carry) in the sidecar's `empties` section; an explicit tag wins outright, a non-`PLAIN_AXES` display type is a deliberate author marker and stays a locator EVEN WITH children, `PLAIN_AXES` falls through to the children heuristic — and a malformed sidecar degrades to the pure heuristic rather than aborting (best-effort contract, pinned by `test_malformed_manifest_never_aborts`). The pull conversion (`templates/_import_scene.py`) collects the section (`collect_empties`); the reference bake (`templates/_bake_scene.py`) now runs the repair too — a referenced `.blend`'s hierarchy no longer arrives as locators all the way down. The send direction closes the loop: `BlenderBridge._write_manifest` (renamed from `_write_texture_manifest`; written even materials-off) walks each seed's whole SUBTREE (Maya's export-selection ships descendants, so a group send now manifests its nested meshes' materials too — previously seed-only) and records `transforms` = `{leaf: "locator"|"group"}` for the shapeless and all-locator transforms (ambiguous leaf names dropped — a wrong tag is worse than the heuristic); the Blender-side `import` / `_save_scene` templates stamp it as a `maya_node_type` custom property on the Empties they create, so Maya→Blender→Maya round trips restore node types exactly. "Entire Scene" scope also routes through `_scene_objects` (DAG roots minus startup cameras) instead of a mesh-only query, which silently flattened the far side's scene graph the same way. Live-verified both directions with blendertk's new `test/send_live_e2e.py`; `test_blender_bridge.py` / `test_scene_import.py` cover the rules headlessly.

- **2026-08-03 — `BlenderBridge.save_as("asset.blend")`: write a real `.blend` from Maya, no Blender window.** Maya could hand a selection to an interactive Blender and Maya could *read* a `.blend`, but it could not WRITE one — the one direction with no route, even though the pull side has baked foreign scenes into native files for months. It is the same hand-off, delivered differently: identical FBX export, identical `.manifest.json` material sidecar, `_produce` called exactly once — only the delivery swaps from "launch a GUI" to "run `blender --background --factory-startup` and wait for the file" (the `save_as` mode; see pythontk's CHANGELOG for the seam). New `templates/_save_scene.py` is the Blender-side recipe: start from an EMPTY factory scene (the startup cube/camera/light must not land in a file the artist thinks holds only their selection), import the payload with the same call `import.py` makes, replay the manifest through the ONE existing applier (`blendertk.MayaSceneImport._apply_texture_manifest`), absolutize image paths (the payload FBX lives in TEMP), save. Underscore-prefixed, so the bridge panel's template list is unchanged. `objects=None` saves the WHOLE SCENE — `MayaExportMixin._scene_objects` returns the DAG roots minus the startup cameras (queried, not name-matched, so a renamed `persp` is still furniture). Saving OVER an existing `.blend` is safe — the write is staged and promoted only on success (pythontk's CHANGELOG has the mechanism). Live-verified Maya 2025 → Blender 5.1: cube + parent group + lambert → an 86 KB `.blend` in 1.8s, reopened headlessly as `E2ECube` (8 verts, `E2EMat` assigned) parented to the `E2EGroup` Empty, with no startup objects, and a pre-existing file at the target replaced rather than truncated. `test_blender_bridge.py` +9 → 25.

- **2026-08-03 — The FBX hand-off export restored the EXPORTED set as the selection, not the artist's own.** `MayaExportMixin._export_fbx` ends by re-selecting whatever it exported (the FBX exporter selects what it writes, and the strip path deletes its temp copies). That looked like a no-op while the exported set *was* the selection, and silently changed the live selection the moment it wasn't: an explicit `send(objects=…)`, the panel's "Entire Scene" / "Visible Only" scopes, and every `save_as`, which defaults to the whole scene — so saving a `.blend` from a live session left the artist with their entire scene selected. The prior selection is now captured before the export and put back (filtered through `cmds.ls`, since a node captured beforehand can be gone by then and `select` raises on a missing one — which would mask the real error when the `finally` runs on an exception path). This is what the Blender mixin already did. `test_blender_bridge.py` +2 (the artist's selection survives an explicit-object send; an empty selection stays empty).

- **2026-08-03 — "Entire Scene" scope crashed instead of falling back, for a panel with no bridge yet.** `MayaBridgeSlotsBase.resolve_scope_objects` reached for `self.bridge._scene_objects()` directly, so the fallback its own docstring promises ("bridges without the hook keep the renderable-geometry set") was unreachable — a slots instance whose bridge is not built raised `AttributeError` instead. Resolved through `getattr` now. `test_unity_bridge.py` +2 and the scope tests updated for the new semantics (the hook returns DAG ROOTS, so "all" is asserted by mesh REACHABILITY plus an explicit "the group transform travels" case — a mesh-only "all" re-roots every child on the far side).

- **2026-08-03 — Bridge parameter DEFAULTS moved to the Qt-free engine module.** `params_defaults()` read them out of `parameters.py`, which imports uitk → Qt, so any headless caller of the bridge had to have a UI toolkit to learn that materials default to on. The values now live in `_blender_bridge.DEFAULTS` and the `AttributeSpec`s read THAT, so there is one source of truth and a missing key is a loud import error rather than drift; `params_defaults` / `render_context` fall back to it (and to pythontk's plain literal formatting) when uitk is unavailable. Panel behavior is unchanged. `test_blender_bridge.py` pins the registry against the widget specs.

- **2026-08-03 — Color ID tooltip silently truncated in the UI.** The `color_id.ui` tooltip read `ID_&lt;HEX&gt;`, which the .ui loader XML-decodes to a literal `ID_<HEX>` — Qt then rich-text-renders that as an unknown tag and eats it along with the rest of the sentence, so the artist never saw what the grouping actually does. Displaying a literal angle bracket needs DOUBLE escaping (`&amp;lt;`) so Qt receives `&lt;`. Caught by `check_tooltips.py`, which decodes exactly as Qt does; both DCC twins fixed.

- **2026-08-03 — Temp artifacts routed through `ptk.TempArtifacts`; two outright leaks fixed.** `HierarchySyncSlots._write_diff_report` wrote a report with `mkstemp` and never deleted it — every diff the user generated stayed on disk permanently. `NamespaceSandbox.export_objects_to_temp` did the same with a whole `mkdtemp` directory per cross-scene export. Both are consumed AFTER the call returns, so a `finally` would be wrong; they now allocate detached, which sweeps stale same-prefix leftovers on a later run. `SceneExporter`'s GLB staging dir and `image_tracer`'s Blue Pencil scratch (a bare `mkdtemp()` with no prefix, so a leftover was indistinguishable from any other tool's) keep their `finally` and gain the same net.

- **2026-08-03 — The Blender→Maya applier rescues textures whose filenames carry no map-type token.** Completes the round trip: `_rebuild_material` now takes the manifest's `slots` and, for images `ptk.MapFactory` cannot classify, wires them through `MatManifest.restore` — which resolves each logical channel to the shader's real attribute via the `ShaderAttributeMap` SSoT rather than hand-wiring plugs. Filename classification stays authoritative (only a filename reveals packing), so a rescue can never displace a classified map. When NOTHING classifies but slots exist, a bare `standardSurface` is stood up rather than dropping the material — previously it returned None and the geometry kept its FBX material. One deliberate asymmetry, documented: a classified map is staged through `MapFactory.prepare_maps` and lands workspace-relative (`sourceimages/x.png`) while a rescued one keeps its ORIGINAL absolute path, because staging is driven by the map type a rescued file lacks. Live-verified Blender 5.1 → Maya 2025 with real production textures: `Agilent_PNA.png` lands on `baseColor` and NOT on `normalCamera`, while a conventionally named Base_color/Normal_OpenGL pair still routes by filename (6/6).

- **2026-08-03 — The texture manifest now carries the shader slots it was throwing away.** `_write_texture_manifest` already had Maya's authoritative channel→file mapping from `MatManifest.build` and flattened it to a bare `files` list, so the Blender side had to re-derive map types from FILENAMES. That works for a conventionally named PBR set and fails completely for a texture named after a product — measured on `VDATS_RF_module.ma`, **6 of 9** materials (`Agilent_PNA.png`, `keysight_5315A.png`, `Rohde&Schwarz_FSU.png`, …) arrived unshaded even though Maya knew each was the `baseColor`. `slots` now rides **alongside** `files` (additive — existing consumers are unaffected). Live end-to-end after the fix: **9/9 materials, 16 wired image nodes**, the three StingrayPBS materials keeping their packed `MSAO` / `Albedo_Transparency` / `Emissive` wiring and the six single-map materials landing on Base Color — verified by socket, not merely by "a texture is attached". `test_blender_bridge.py` +1 → 16.

- **2026-08-03 — The Blender-bridge material fix never actually ran: `blendertk` was not importable in the launched Blender.** Found by testing the 08-02 sidecar fix end-to-end against the real `VDATS_RF_module.ma` (627 meshes, 3 StingrayPBS + 7 standardSurface materials) instead of trusting its unit tests. Blender ignores `PYTHONPATH` entirely, and `blendertk` is not on it in the first place, so `templates/import.py` hit its `except ImportError` branch on every send and logged "blendertk unavailable; keeping the FBX-carried materials" — i.e. the exact unshaded-mesh bug the manifest exists to fix. `BlenderBridge.render_context` now threads `EXTRA_SYS_PATH` (via the new `ptk.HandoffBridge.import_roots`, see pythontk's CHANGELOG) into the template, which inserts it before importing — the same mechanism the *pull* direction has always used via `_bake_scene.py`, which is why that path worked and this one did not. Only the two roots the template needs are passed, never Maya's whole `sys.path`. Measured on VDATS: **0 -> 3 materials rebuilt**, 10 wired image nodes, the packed `MSAO` / `Albedo_Transparency` / `Emissive` game maps included — none of which FBX has a slot for. Not a user-facing PARAM, so it rides the render context beside `FBX_PATH`.

- **2026-08-03 — Corrected the docstrings claiming Maya's game shaders export through FBX "as nothing".** Probed on Maya 2025 -> Blender 5.1: the FBX is **not** lossy. Maya writes StingrayPBS texture bindings as `Maya|TEX_color_map` / `Maya|TEX_normal_map` through `FbxImplementation`/`FbxBindingTable` (standardSurface likewise as `Maya|baseColor`); only lambert uses the native `DiffuseColor` slot. Blender's importer *parses* those bindings and discards them by design, logging `WARNING: material link b'Maya|TEX_color_map' ignored`, leaving the material with no custom properties and no image nodes. So the loss is entirely on the read side, and recovering it without a sidecar would mean parsing the FBX independently. The mechanism matters because it changes which fixes are available; the sidecar remains correct, and is the only route for a Maya FBX this bridge did not produce.

- **2026-08-02 — Blender bridge SEND: the mesh arrived unshaded. Fixed with the texture sidecar the pull direction has always used.** Live production report. FBX carries only the classic material model, and Maya's game shaders (StingrayPBS / ShaderFX) export through it as **nothing** — so a real selection landed in Blender with no materials at all. This was a straight parity gap: `btk.MayaBridge` got exactly this fix on its own send path earlier the same day ("real materials arrived gray"), and the Maya→Blender mirror never did. `BlenderBridge._produce` now overrides the shared `MayaExportMixin` step to sidecar `<fbx>.manifest.json` (each textured material's ORIGINAL image files + the objects carrying it), and the Blender-side `templates/import.py` replays it through the ONE existing applier, `blendertk.MayaSceneImport._apply_texture_manifest` — the established template→paired-engine contract, not a second copy of the rebuild logic. Texture resolution reuses `MatManifest.build`, which reads each shader's **declared** slots via the `ShaderAttributeMap` SSoT rather than walking history (history drags a Stingray network's env/BRDF plumbing in alongside the real maps). Best-effort throughout: a manifest failure never costs the user the send, and without blendertk on the target Blender the FBX's classic materials are kept with a console line saying so. The replay runs regardless of `FRAME_VIEW` — a material rebuild is not a viewport nicety. Untextured materials write no sidecar (a flat color rides the FBX fine); `INCLUDE_MATERIALS=False` skips it entirely. `test_blender_bridge.py` +6 → 15/15.
- **2026-08-02 — Every Maya hand-off bridge now exposes the shared **Scope** combo (Selected / Entire Scene / Visible Only).** Previously only the Unity bridge had it, with the resolver living on that one slot. It moves to `MayaBridgeSlotsBase.resolve_scope_objects` — the common ancestor of all five Maya bridge slots (Blender / Unity / Marmoset / Substance / Rizom) — so scope resolves identically everywhere, and the spec itself comes from uitk (see its CHANGELOG). Each bridge's send site collapses to `self.scoped_objects(params)`, which also emits the scope-aware empty message. `SCOPE` is a HOST-side knob that never reaches the target-app script, so it rides the same echo-comment convention the export knobs already use (`scope=__SCOPE__`); Substance's `render` template deliberately omits it and correctly hides the control. Marmoset keeps a second, Toolbag-facing token registry, so `SCOPE` is registered there too — otherwise the echoed placeholder leaked into the rendered script (caught by that suite's existing no-leak invariant). +2 scope tests incl. one pinning that all five slots share the ONE resolver rather than carrying copies.

- **2026-08-02 — USD export: instances no longer collapse material export, and the Blender→Maya pull stops sampling animation it doesn't have.** Mayatk's half of the two defects a production scene exposed while USD was briefly the pull default (see blendertk's CHANGELOG for the measurements and the Maya→Blender half).
  - **`UsdUtils._DEFAULT_EXPORT_OPTIONS["exportInstances"]` is now `False`** — a pre-existing defect in the general-purpose exporter since the 07-17 USD landing, not just a bridge issue. Measured on Maya 2025 / mayaUsd: with the flag on, a scene holding instanced shapes collapses material export (`def Material` **3 → 0**, `material:binding` **4 → 0**), taking a material bound only to a NON-instanced mesh with it; per-instance assignments are lost regardless, since all instances share one prototype. Materials are the point of an interchange default, so instances are flattened; `options={"exportInstances": True}` restores the prototypes for a consumer that needs them.
  - **`_narrow_frame_range` in the Blender-side conversion template.** Blender's USD exporter reads `scene.frame_start/end` rather than taking a range, so the gate clamps them to the union of the actions' own `frame_range` (bounded by the scene range) and returns False — disabling `export_animation` entirely — when nothing is animated. Constraints / NLA / drivers keep the full range, since their motion can't be derived from key times. Safe to assign: the conversion scene is a throwaway process that is never saved.
  - **The pull path now scrubs the OCIO hand-off** (pre-existing, found while verifying the above). `BlenderSceneImport.convert` launches Blender FROM Maya and passed no env at all, so the child inherited Maya's — including an `OCIO` pointing inside Maya's own install, which would override Blender's color management with a Maya-authored config. The **send** path has stripped exactly this since the bridge hardening pass; the **pull** path was missed. It now reuses `_SPEC.launch_env()` (→ `ptk.AppLauncher.handoff_env`), so a studio config outside Maya's tree passes through untouched.
  - `_narrow_frame_range` uses `math.floor`/`math.ceil` rather than `int()`, which truncates toward zero — a key at 20.5 clipped to 20 (losing motion) and negative frames rounded the wrong way; the owner walk was flattened to one readable loop.
  - **Blender linked duplicates now arrive as real Maya instances (`use_instancing=False` + sidecar rebuild).** Flipping the default to USD silently regressed this: measured on a .blend of 5 linked duplicates + 1 solo, the USD route produced **6 transforms / 6 independent shapes** where the FBX route produced **6 / 2 with 1 instanced shape**. Losing shape sharing wrecks a production scene's memory profile, so the USD export now goes flat (USD instancing hands Maya read-only prototypes, not its shared-shape model, and prototype prim names break 1:1 name matching), the Blender side records linked-duplicate groups in the conversion sidecar, and the new `BlenderSceneImport._apply_instance_manifest` re-instances Maya-side — one member's shape parented under the others (`add=True, shape=True`), each follower's own geometry deleted, and its shading re-applied afterwards because instancing routes assignments through `instObjGroups` and the surviving shape carries the master's shader. Wired into the `.ma` bake template too (the Reference Manager's reference path runs its own headless mayapy). Now matches FBX exactly: 6 transforms / 2 shapes / 1 instanced. New e2e trap `e2e_linked_*` pins all three facts (count, one shared shape, genuinely multi-parented).
  - Pins in `test_scene_import.py` for all of the above (38/38); Blender→Maya live e2e re-run PASS on both routes incl. the new instancing leg. Full suite: **4520 tests, 0 failures, 0 errors, 8 skipped** — this run's GUI pass connected, so all 16 previously-deferred GUI modules executed (no NOT RUN section; badge 4512). That handshake failure is intermittent, not fixed — see `.claude/BACKLOG.md`; a run that DOES report NOT RUN is unverified for those modules.

- **2026-08-02 — Channels: a component selection listed per-point tweak plugs instead of the node's channels, and auto-fit grew the window past the screen with no way to scroll.** Two defects, one screenshot: selecting a few hundred vertices filled the table with `pnts[18].pntx`, `pnts[18].pnty`, … and sized the window taller than the display. (1) `cmds.ls(sl=True)` returns a component selection as a plug-style string (`|pSphere1.vtx[18:39]`), and `cmds.listAttr` on one of those does not list the node's attributes — it expands **one keyable plug per selected point** (verified in mayapy: 22 vertices → 66 rows of `pnts[*]`). New `Channels.resolve_component_targets` collapses components to the transform that owns them, which is what Maya's own Channel Box shows for a component selection; the footer's Shape / History buttons remain the way down to the shape node. An explicitly selected **shape** passes through untouched (otherwise the Shape button would be undone on the next refresh), order is preserved (`single_object_mode` reads the last entry) and duplicates collapse, since many components resolve to one transform. This also un-breaks the node-level context actions (Freeze/Unfreeze) for a component selection, which previously received an unusable component string. (2) `_autofit_window` grew the window to the full content height unconditionally, and its ~55 lines of Qt measurement were duplicated verbatim in the blendertk twin. Both copies are gone: the panel now calls uitk's new `TableWidget.fit_window_to_contents()` (see uitk's CHANGELOG for the cap, the corrected chrome measurement, and its 12 tests) and keeps only the `Auto-fit Window` checkbox gate. Verified live in a fresh Maya (never a user session): 282-vertex selection → 10 channel rows, zero `pnts[*]`, 171×230 window; 300 custom attrs (5400px of rows) → capped at 525 = 50% of the 1050px available height, vertical scrollbar shown, range 0..274, zero slack on both axes; collapsing back to 10 rows shrinks the window and drops the scrollbar; auto-fit unchecked leaves the window untouched. `test_channels.py` +13 → 101 green.

- **2026-08-02 — Blender-scene pull: a complete USD route (`via="usd"`) alongside FBX, and the `.ma` bake accepts either intermediate. FBX remains the DEFAULT.** The default was flipped to USD during this cycle and reverted the same day, before release: both routes now reach the same result, but they fail differently. FBX's instancing is carried by the format itself on both sides (Maya's exporter needs `FBXExportInstances -v true`, which the template sets), so nothing stands between a linked duplicate and a real Maya instance; USD reaches parity only by replaying a recorded grouping, and that replay is best-effort on all four legs — when it fails the scene *looks* correct and merely stops sharing data, a silent structural loss an artist finds hours later. A failed FBX texture replay, by contrast, is immediately visible and structurally harmless. Loud beats fast for a production default (USD measured 15.9s vs FBX 18.2s on VDATS_RF — real but not worth the trade). Making the instance replay loud is logged in `.claude/BACKLOG.md` as the prerequisite for revisiting this. `BlenderSceneImport.import_scene` / `bake_scene` / `convert` / `render_script` / `_cache_key` default `via="fbx"`; `bake_scene` gains `via`; `_bake_scene.py` is source-generalized (`SRC_FILE`, extension dispatch: `.usd*` → native `cmds.file(type="USD Import")` with `mayaUsdPlugin` load, manifest replay self-skips; `.fbx` → the classic path); `bake()`/`render_bake_script()` params renamed `fbx_path` → `src_path`. The Reference Manager grows the mirrored **"Foreign Scenes: Convert via USD / FBX"** header-menu combo (`_foreign_route`, defaulting to FBX), threaded through the foreign-row bake and Unlink-and-Import (`compare_panel_surface --panel reference_manager`: 0 untriaged / 0 item deltas). Item order is append-only — uitk persists a combo by INDEX, so the default moves via `setCurrentIndex`, never by reordering — and the combo's objectName is `cmb_conversion_route`, renamed off `cmb_foreign_route` when the default changed so any already-stored index is orphaned rather than silently pinning a profile to the old route. Verified: `test_scene_import.py` green incl. `TestConversionRoutes` (pins the FBX default AND that `via="usd"` still renders the USD template); live e2e — FBX legs route-explicit and green (manifest rebuild, packed Metallic_Smoothness, per-face assignment), USD leg green (textures native, GeomSubsets keep the two-material cube's bindings), second import cache-hits with no re-conversion. The e2e's cache check was repaired in passing: it pinned pythontk's "Cache hit" message, which moved to ptk's own logger — it now pins the absence of a re-conversion through the engine's logger. Tentacle's "Import Blender Scene" slot inherits the route default (docstring updated).

- **2026-08-02 — Color ID: new "Set Per Color" option (`chk016`, off by default) grouping each color's objects into an `ID_<HEX>` objectSet.** Added in step with blendertk's twin (which uses a color-tagged ID collection — see its CHANGELOG for the Blender half, where this option also frees the Outliner channel to do what Maya's always did: tint the object's outliner *text*). `ColorId.add_to_color_set` / `get_color_set_color` / `remove_from_color_sets`, plus `apply_color(set_per_color=)`, `get_objects_by_color(check_set=)`, and `reset_colors(reset_sets=)`. The set carries the applied color exactly on a `mtk_color_id` `double3` stamp, and **membership discovery keys on that stamp, never on the `ID_` name** — so a user's own set called `ID_*` is never adopted, restamped, or deleted by a reset. A recolor *moves* objects between stamped sets rather than piling up, and sets emptied by the move are deleted (verified in mayapy: deleting an objectSet leaves its members alive, so the cleanup can never cost geometry). Orthogonal to the four color channels, so it composes with any of them or none. `test_display_utils.py` +7 tests → 22/22; `compare_panel_surface --panel color_id` 0 untriaged / 0 prop deltas.

- **2026-08-02 — Smart Bake: "Bake Inherited Visibility" removed from the panel; the option survives as API-only, and its docstring no longer states the opposite of what the FBX exporter actually does.** The checkbox invited artists to enable a bake that is unnecessary in the normal case and destructive in the one case it applies to. Measured live (Unity 6000.3.10f1, synthetic scene + the production C-5M asset): **Maya's FBX exporter already RESOLVES ancestor visibility onto renderable descendants** — the C-5M export carries 78 visibility curves, every one on a `_GEO` child and **none** on the 27 keyed `_LOC` parents, and Unity binds them to `m_Enabled@Renderer` and hides correctly; a statically hidden ancestor resolves at import too. So the docstring's claim that the exporter "only writes visibility curves for nodes that have *direct* animation on .visibility" and that "Unity then never toggles the Renderer" was backwards, and the corresponding `test_c5m_visibility.py` expectation (which looks for curves on `_LOC` names that can never carry them) is a permanent false alarm. The single real gap is the inverse case — a child carrying its OWN `.visibility` keys under a keyed ancestor, where the exporter writes the child's curve alone and drops the ancestor's contribution (reproduced with `FBXExportBakeComplexAnimation` both on and off) — **but that is also exactly the shape a RenderOpacity fade has**: `key_fade` encodes a fade as the GAP between two opposite-value visibility keys, and this bake samples at every ancestor key time plus the bake-range boundaries, splitting it. Measured end-to-end through the real RenderOpacity import path: an authored 10f fade-in + 60f fade-out under an ancestor keyed mid-fade reconstructs in Unity as **four** ramps (in 0.417s / out 0.792s / in 0.833s / out 0.875s) instead of two — the object flickers instead of holding. (Two is the unbaked *count*, not a correct result: the same probe showed the unbaked fade-out already arriving truncated, 60f authored → 21f reconstructed, because the exporter emits a redundant zero-delta visibility key at each ancestor key time and the importer measures the ramp from the immediately preceding key. That is a separate pre-existing defect, logged in `.claude/BACKLOG.md` under unitytk, not something this change introduces or fixes.) The option therefore cannot be offered as a checkbox, since the panel cannot tell the two cases apart. `bake_inherited_visibility` remains on `SmartBake.__init__` for its one real consumer, the Maya→Blender bridge (which needs it for an unrelated reason: Blender's FBX importer drops visibility animation entirely, so the values travel in the conversion manifest); its docstring now carries the measured behavior plus a CAUTION not to enable it on objects carrying an `opacity` attribute, and the two stale in-code comments were corrected to match. The scene exporter never enabled it and still doesn't. Removed: `chk_inherited_vis` from `smart_bake.ui` and its read in `smart_bake_slots.py`, the widget's stub in `test_smart_bake_slots.py` and its name in `mock_tests/test_smart_bake_panel.py`, and the now-dead `chk_inherited_vis` rows in tentacle's `parity_map.py` (the `smart_bake_slots` ledger row and the already-dead-config `animation` row). Engine behavior is unchanged — `test_smart_bake` 60/60, `test_smart_bake_slots` 10/10, `mock_tests/test_smart_bake_panel` 9/9, and `compare_panel_surface.py --panel smart_bake` reports 0 untriaged / 0 pending / 0 stale-maya.

- **2026-08-02 — Blender bridge: pulled hierarchies arrived as locators; the launched Blender could inherit a Maya-private OCIO.** Blender Empties travel as FBX nulls and Maya's FBX importer gives every null a locator shape, so a pulled .blend's whole group tree arrived as locators. New `BlenderSceneImport._restore_empty_groups(new_nodes)`, run after the FBX import (USD needs no repair — Empties travel as Xform prims): a locator-shaped transform WITH transform children is a Blender-Empty-as-group and loses the shape; a childless one stays a locator (point marker). Scoped to the import's new nodes so a pre-existing user locator is never touched; multi-shape transforms skipped. Kept in step by hand with the dependency-free copy in blendertk's send template (`maya_bridge/templates/import.py`), which also now replays the send's new manifest sidecar through this engine's `_apply_texture_manifest` — the same template→paired-engine contract the bake templates use. Mirror hardening on the send side: `_SPEC.launch_env` strips an `OCIO` pointing inside `$MAYA_LOCATION` via the new `ptk.AppLauncher.handoff_env` before launching Blender. `test_scene_import.py` +2 → 28 (parent-vs-leaf split; out-of-scope + multi-shape untouched).

- **2026-08-02 — "Fix OCIO" would re-apply a config Maya cannot load, cementing the exact breakage it exists to repair.** `fix_ocio` validated a candidate config with `PyOpenColorIO` and fell back to a text heuristic when the import failed — and Maya ships **no** `PyOpenColorIO` (verified in mayapy 2025), so the heuristic is the only branch that ever runs. It accepts anything containing `ocio_profile_version` / `roles:`, which includes configs *newer than Maya's own OCIO runtime*: Blender 5.1 ships `ocio_profile_version: 2.5` while Maya 2025 links OCIO 2.3 (`bin/OpenColorIOMaya_2_3.dll`). Point `$OCIO` at a shared config across the two apps and Maya greets every launch with `Color Management Initialization failed: "... is version 2.5. This version of the OpenColorIO library (2.3.2) is not able to load that config version"` — then the repair button re-blessed that same path (step 1 prefers `$OCIO`, step 2 keeps the current pref) and the next launch failed identically. Validation now gates on the declared profile version against a ceiling read off the OCIO runtime Maya ships beside itself (`_SceneDiagnosticsInternal._max_ocio_profile_version` / `._ocio_profile_version`), so a too-new config is rejected **with a named note** and discovery falls through to a Maya-shipped config. An *unknown* ceiling (no library matched) skips the gate rather than rejecting — unknown is not low. `test_scene_audit.py` +4 (header parse, ceiling from the shipped library, too-new `$OCIO` refused, loadable `$OCIO` still honored); module green at 13.

- **2026-08-02 — Workspace review: Maya's half of the shared project model was still hand-rolled, folder-name-hardcoded, and creation-blind.** blendertk's Workspace Editor was built on `pythontk.Workspace`; mayatk had never adopted it, so the two DCCs disagreed about what a project even *is*.
  - **`EnvUtils.find_workspaces` now runs on `ptk.Workspace.find`** instead of its own `os.walk` for `workspace.mel`. Two behavior fixes fall out. (1) `recursive=False` used to blank `dirnames` on the *first* walk iteration, so it inspected `root_dir` alone — unchecking Workspace Map's "Recursive Search" emptied the tree. It now means "root + its immediate children", the twin of `btk.find_workspaces`. (2) `ignore_empty` scanned a **hardcoded `scenes/`** folder; a project mapping its `scene` rule to `shots`, or to `.` (exactly what `promote_workspace` writes for a flat folder), was discarded as empty. It resolves the project's own rule via `Workspace.scene_dir` now, so a Blender-authored project is findable from Maya. New `file_types` parameter (default the new `EnvUtils.SCENE_FILE_TYPES`) lets a caller widen the emptiness scan — `WorkspaceManager` passes its own set, so the Reference Manager's `*.fbx` workspaces finally count. `find_workspace_using_path` delegates to `Workspace.find_containing`.
  - **`get_env_info("sourceimages")` is rule-fed.** It joined `<workspace>/sourceimages` unconditionally, ignoring the project's `sourceImages` rule — wrong for any project that maps textures elsewhere (a promoted Blender project maps it to `textures`). It now routes through the new `EnvUtils.source_images_dir`, fixing ~12 call sites at once (hdr_manager, mat_updater, game_shader, texture_path_editor, lightmap_baker, the exporter's texture stager). `emissive_groups` had already worked around this with its own `workspace(fileRuleEntry=…)` lookup.
  - **New `EnvUtils` twins of the `btk.*` workspace API** — `current_workspace` (Maya's active project as a `ptk.Workspace`, or any explicit path), `set_current_workspace`, `workspace_root`, `scenes_dir`, `source_images_dir`, `create_workspace`, `promote_workspace`, and `list/save/delete_workspace_template` + `workspace_template_rules` over the now-shared `ptk.WorkspaceTemplates`. This is not a second project system: `cmds.workspace` stays the authority on the *active* project. It is the half `cmds.workspace` has no API for — creating a project from a saved rule template, and promoting an existing folder to a shared Maya/Blender project. `save_workspace_template(name)` with no rules captures the active project's own rules, so a hand-tuned Project Window layout can be published as the studio template.
  - **Workspace Map** gained the creation half (header menu ▸ New Project / Mark Root As Shared Project / Save Rules As Template) and lost three defects: the filter repopulated the tree **without clearing it**, appending a duplicate copy of every row on each keystroke; `_analyze_workspace` walked the *entire* workspace tree totalling every file on disk, for every discovered workspace, synchronously per refresh (now one `stat` per scene file, reused for the recent-files sort — `size_mb` is scene bytes); and the header help promised "click a node to set it as the active workspace" when selection only logged. Switching is now bound to **double-click** (a scene-wide side effect shouldn't fire on mere arrow-key selection) alongside the existing context-menu item. Promotion targets the **root** field, not a tree row: discovery is marker-only, so every row in the tree is already a project and a per-row "Mark As Shared Project" could only ever no-op — the folder that needs promoting is the unmarked one the user just browsed to. The scene scan is also unconditionally recursive now, matching the rule `invalidate_workspace_files` already stated (`recursive_search` governs workspace *discovery*; a project's `scenes/sub/` files are still its scenes). Dead code removed: `prefilter_regex` (+ its `re` import), the `handle_tree_selection` stub, `refresh_workspace_data`'s no-op branch, and an unused `maya.cmds` import.
  - **`WorkspaceManager`** inherits `ptk.LoggingMixin` instead of hand-rolling `_logger_debug` behind a `hasattr` probe, normalizes `current_workspace` (Maya's `-rd` keeps a trailing slash, which collapses a later `basename()` to `""`), and drops the `hasattr` guards on fields its own `__init__` sets. `get_workspace_scenes` no longer carries a mutable default argument, and returns `[]` for a missing directory instead of raising.
  - `promote_workspace` and `current_workspace`'s path branch delegate to the new `ptk.Workspace.promote` / `.for_path` rather than duplicating blendertk's copies of the same logic (see pythontk's CHANGELOG).
  - `test_workspace.py` +32 → 50; env_utils / reference_manager / scene_exporter / mat_utils / texture_path_editor / game_shader / shader_templates / emissive_groups / lightmap_baker / texture_baker re-run green (511 tests). Parity sweep exit 0.

- **2026-08-02 — Freeze/unfreeze audit: five tools baked geometry with raw `makeIdentity` and left no history; the stored history is now also READABLE, and six tools that were guessing at a frozen object's authored frame read it instead.** A sweep of every geometry-baking path across the codebase, prompted by the question "is everything actually storing?". It wasn't.
  - **Bakes that stored nothing.** `drop_to_grid(freeze_transforms=True)`, `reset_translation`, `set_translation_to_pivot`, `transfer_pivot(bake=True)` and `Components.get_closest_vertex(freeze_transforms=True)` all called `cmds.makeIdentity` directly, so Un-Freeze silently under-restored — and three of them are user-facing (Transform / Pivot / Polygons panels; the last reached via `snap_to_closest_vertex`). All now route through `XformUtils.freeze_transforms(store=True)`, which also makes `transfer_pivot(bake=True)` with no channel flags a clean no-op instead of makeIdentity's "nothing to do" error. `get_closest_vertex`'s flag is documented as legacy — a query helper is the wrong place to freeze.
  - **`transform_diag`'s residual-skew fallback.** It fires *precisely* when `freeze_transforms` did not freeze — which means it stamped nothing — and then baked anyway. Now stamps first, mirroring the instanced branch beside it.
  - **New read side: `XformUtils.get_stored_transforms(node, prefix)`** → `{translate, rotate, scale, matrix}` or None. The primitive every consumer of the history shares; unlike `has_stored_transforms` (a map keyed by LONG path — a short name silently missed) it resolves the name itself. Absent channels read as identity, so the dict is always complete.
  - **New `"original"` pivot mode** on `get_operation_axis_matrix` / `get_operation_axis_pos` and the `use_object_axes` consumers. A freeze zeroes the rotate channel, so a frozen object's local axes ARE the world axes and `"object"` silently degraded into `"world"` — every axis-based op (mirror, cut_on_axis, delete-on-axis, `get_all_faces_on_axis`, duplicate_radial/linear, move_to) lost the frame the asset was authored in. `"original"` composes the stored rotate bake back on (`L_pre · worldMatrix`), falling back to `"object"` when unstamped so it is always safe to pass. Because the pre-freeze frame is neither world nor the node's current local space, the two bbox-driven ops needed a real extent-in-frame computation (`_EditUtilsInternal._extent_in_frame`) rather than Maya's bbox query.
  - **`freeze_to_opm` is reversible but the UI could not see it.** It stamps history now (`store=True`, plus an `{prefix}_opm_bake` marker) so `has_stored_transforms` reports True and the Channels panel stops greying out Un-Freeze on a perfectly reversible node. An OPM freeze never touched the geometry, so its inverse must not counter-bake any: new `unfreeze_from_opm` clears the OPM and re-pins the world pivots, and `restore_transforms` routes marked nodes there before its geometry pipeline plans anything.
  - **New `restore_original_axes`** — the non-destructive companion to Un-Freeze, surfaced as a "Restore Authored Axes" button in the Un-Freeze option box: aims the manipulator at the pre-freeze frame while the object stays frozen.
  - **Auto-instancer.** `canonicalize_transform` / `center_transform_on_geometry` re-base the local frame and destroyed the authored one unrecoverably; they stamp under a distinct `CANONICAL_BAKE_PREFIX` now, so the re-basing is separately reversible and can never compose onto the user's freeze history. And `get_mesh_signature` used to throw the PCA eigenvalue signature away entirely whenever `scale_tolerance > 0` ("can't normalize for non-uniform scale") — a frozen transform's bake history *is* that normalizer, so the signature is now always computed in authored space, normalizing per node. Gating it instead would have given a frozen copy a signature and its unfrozen twin an empty tuple: they would never have matched at all.
  - **`get_non_orthogonal(detailed=True)`** gains `frozen` / `baked_scale` and a third `cause`, `baked-shear`, so a report can name the freeze that manufactured the skew rather than only measuring the symptom. **`check_root_default_transforms`** likewise reports a root that reads identity *because it was frozen* (with the transform the freeze consumed) — it cannot fail the export, since the scene as it stands really is at identity, but "authored at identity" and "frozen to identity" are no longer indistinguishable.
  - Review pass on the above, all fixed before shipping: the bake normalizer now requires `XformUtils.channels_at_identity` (new, factored out of `repair_stored_transforms` — a legacy stale stamp would otherwise have pushed a matchable mesh out of alignment, turning a stale stamp into a silent MISSED instance rather than a slow one); `freeze_to_opm` refuses to compose onto an existing geometry bake and `restore_transforms` skips OPM nodes on a partial-channel restore (both would have put channels back while the geometry stayed baked — the object doubles); `unfreeze_from_opm` honors `delete_attrs`; `_extent_in_frame` returns None instead of raising on a type with no `.vtx[*]`, and both callers fall back to the ordinary bbox query rather than dropping the node; `get_non_orthogonal` skips the bake lookup and the connection scan when `detailed=False` (`fix_non_orthogonal_axes` re-checks each node several times through that path); and `drop_to_grid` / `transfer_pivot` freeze once after their loop instead of per object, since the engine prints a per-call summary.
  - **Fixed in passing:** `cut_along_axis` handed a nurbs raised `RuntimeError: Current selection doesn't match required selection type` from `polyCut` deep inside the loop, killing the whole call — every other unsupported case there warns and continues. It now skips non-polygon transforms with a warning, so a mixed selection still cuts its meshes.
  - 34 new tests across `test_xform_utils` / `test_edit_utils` / `test_diagnostics` / `test_auto_instancer` / `test_scene_exporter`; full suite 4479 tests green (headless + GUI pass). blendertk moved in the same pass (panel parity sweep clean).

- **2026-08-02 — Unity Bridge: Manage Unity Scripts lists the C# scripts as a checkable set, and the Action applies to whatever is checked.** The template had a single Action combo over an implicit all-or-nothing set — Install deployed every controller whether the project imported that channel or not, and Uninstall could only take the whole package. The panel's parameters are already dynamic (the Unity Version combo fills itself from the installed Editors), so the scripts are surfaced the same way: new **`SCRIPTS`** parameter (uitk's new `check_list` kind), populated at panel init from `unitytk.TemplateDeployer.components()` — one checked row per import channel, each carrying its own tooltip. Nothing about the C# set is written into the parameter registry, so a controller added to unitytk's bundle appears here with no panel edit. Install / Update deploys the checked scripts (additively; the shared core files always ride along), Uninstall removes them (taking the package folder once the last one goes), and Status ignores the checkboxes to report the full picture — a ticked line per component. `_manage_unity_scripts` shrank to its panel-side preconditions: the work and its wording now live in `TemplateDeployer.run_action`, one implementation shared with the blendertk and extapps Unity panels. When unitytk is absent at panel build the checklist stays empty and the actions fall back to the full set, so an Install that installs the engine still does the obvious thing (it fills the list before running, and never re-fills it afterward — that would silently re-check every row the user had cleared). `test_unity_bridge.py` +2; `unity_bridge_ui_check.py` 13/13 (checklist contents, default checks, per-row tooltips, the un-clamped row height, and the empty-checklist fallback in the engine-missing pass).

- **2026-08-02 — Telescope Rig: two segments is now a real build, the locators are optional, the collapse distance derives itself, and the rig can be removed from the panel.** The tool demanded four selected objects (base locator + >=2 segments + end locator), but most telescope rigs in practice are a simple two-piece strut — so every strut cost the artist two hand-authored locators first, and the two-segment path it *did* accept was a silent degenerate: it created a `distanceBetween` node that drove nothing and range-checked a `collapsed_distance` it never used, refusing valid builds.
