Now `setup_telescope_rig` takes `base_locator`/`end_locator` as optional; whichever is omitted is created at the outer end of the strut (the support point of that segment's world bbox along the segments' modeled long axis — read from the axis, not pivot-to-pivot, because a fully nested strut has every segment sitting on top of the others), sized to 5% of the span and recorded in the bundle so teardown takes it back. Two segments builds a sliding strut: both halves ride their locator, no distance node, no driven keys, `collapsed_distance` ignored rather than validated. `collapsed_distance=None` (the new default, surfaced as the spinbox's **Auto** special value at 0) measures the longest segment along the aim axis — fully nested, a telescope is as long as its longest tube. The panel accepts any selection of >=2 segments and reads roles by node type: a leading locator/bare transform is the base, a trailing one is the end, everything between is a segment — so four *meshes* now means four segments, not base+2+end. Also new: `btn_remove`, backed by a JSON build record stamped on the base locator (`telescopeRigData`) plus `TelescopeRig.scene_bundles`/`find_bundles`, so a rig from an earlier session still tears down cleanly; teardown drops the stamp and deletes only the locators the build itself made. Two latent bugs fixed on the way: the auto-handle sizing wrote `localScale` (a *shape* attribute) — on a transform it would have raised, and had it landed there it would have scaled the segments riding it; and the collapsed-distance spinbox kept Qt's default 99.99 maximum, silently capping any strut longer than 100 units. Two more defects fixed in the follow-up review: the geometry probe in front of `exactWorldBoundingBox` only looked at a node's DIRECT shapes, so an imported segment (group → transform → mesh) measured as shapeless — the auto handle fell back to the group's pivot and the collapse distance to an even split. That guard is not optional and now walks the DAG subtree: probed in mayapy, `exactWorldBoundingBox` on a genuinely shapeless transform returns an INVERTED-INFINITY box (`1e20 … -1e20`), not a degenerate one, so measuring without it poisons every derived number. And a second Build on already-rigged nodes is now refused by name (`already carry a telescope rig …`) instead of by the pre-flight's raw channel list — a rebuild would also have overwritten the previous bundle's record, stranding its nodes. The geometry math itself moved onto the existing registry helpers rather than raw `cmds` (`CoreUtils.get_bounding_box` → `BoundingBox.center`/`.size`, `XformUtils.get_translation`), which also drops a duplicate bounding-box evaluation per support point. Covered by 6 new live-Maya tests (`test_rig_utils.py`, 29/29) and 21 mock tests; blendertk's twin moved in the same pass (panel parity sweep clean: 0 deltas).

- **2026-08-02 — Shadow Rig panel: the top resolution option was `8096` — a non-power-of-two typo for `8192`.** From a full both-DCC review of the shadow rig. `ShadowRigSlots.perform_operation` parses the combo label verbatim, so committing at that setting rasterized an 8096² silhouette — an NPOT texture that Unity mip-chains poorly and nothing in the pipeline expects. Fixed in `rig_utils/shadow_rig.ui`; blendertk's twin `.ui` fixed in the same pass (panel parity sweep clean: 0 deltas). The same review's Blender-side lifecycle hardenings — create() rollback, uniquified re-create, evaluated-depsgraph footprint, linked-plane skip, preview-cancel carrier/PNG repair — are Maya-parity ports of behavior mayatk already had; see blendertk's CHANGELOG. The mayatk engine itself needed no behavior change.

- **2026-08-02 — Tube Rig: re-anchoring a tube end now REPLACES its previous anchor instead of stacking on it, and the end-blend falloff defaults to a crease-free profile.** Found in a review of `rig_utils/tube_rig.py` and reproduced headless: `constrain_end_with_falloff` named its anchor joint via `generate_unique_name`, so nothing tied a node to the end it served and a second "Add End Constraints" run on the same end left **two targets on the end control's parentConstraint** — the end then followed the *midpoint* of both anchors — plus the discarded anchor joint stranded on the skinCluster as a dead influence (12 → 13). Every other step in the module advertises replace-on-rerun; this one could only ever accumulate. Now: anchor assemblies are named per-end (`<rig>_anchor_{start,end}_jnt` + matching `_jnt_`/`_target_parentConstraint`, matching blendertk's naming), and a new `_clear_end_anchor` sweep deletes the previous set first. Order matters and is load-bearing — the old joint is removed from its skinCluster *before* deletion, so `removeInfluence`'s renormalization restores the rows its falloff had redistributed (that redistribution was proportional, so the restore is exact: measured drift < 1e-4 across a re-anchor, versus stranded weights if the joint were deleted while still wired in). The skinCluster is also resolved up front and `objExists`-guarded, so a name recorded before a rebuild can no longer reach `apply_falloff` stale. Separately, the falloff `profile` is now a parameter defaulting to `"smoothstep"` rather than a hardcoded `"linear"` — linear is C0 at the radius, leaving a derivative break (a visible crease ring) exactly where the blend zone meets untouched weights. `test_tube_rig.py` +2 (`test_rerun_replaces_previous_end_anchor` asserts one target / one anchor influence and that the end tracks the *latest* anchor, not a blend; `test_rerun_end_anchor_restores_weights` pins the no-drift property). Verified: 189 tests green across tube_rig / skinning / rig_utils / controls / shadow_rig / wheel_rig / telescope_rig. Two adjacent findings from the same review are deferred with probe numbers in `.claude/BACKLOG.md` (the spline IK curve is over-parameterized and default-weighted; anchor mode shears rather than aims at an off-axis end control — a design call, since blendertk's `AnchorStrategy` already aims).

- **2026-08-01 — `mtk.ScriptConsole` did not resolve; `env_utils.script_output` was registered under a name the module never defines.** `DEFAULT_INCLUDE` carried `"env_utils.script_output": "ScriptOutput"`, but that module defines `ScriptConsole` and merely *imports* uitk's `ScriptOutput` widget — so `mtk.ScriptOutput` resolved to a uitk class leaking through mayatk's namespace (a cross-package shadow, never mayatk API), while `mtk.ScriptConsole` raised `AttributeError`. Surfaced when tentacle's editors slot moved to the package-namespace gateway (`mtk.ScriptConsole.toggle(...)` replacing a deep `from mayatk.env_utils import script_output`), caught by tentacle's `test_namespace_references` chain-resolution gate. Now registers `ScriptConsole`, mirroring blendertk's `env_utils.script_output` entry. Full suite re-run: 4407 tests, 0 failures.

- **2026-08-01 — VDATS_module.ma "unfreeze moved everything": the scene carried 481 bake-history attrs from the old stamped-up-front flow, 305 of them on objects whose freeze had been SKIPPED — and the restore made three of its own mistakes on instance-heavy scenes. All fixed; new `XformUtils.repair_stored_transforms` heals legacy scenes.** User-reported against the production file and reproduced headless (read-only): un-freezing composed stale bakes onto channels that were never zeroed (drifts to ~18,000 units), and re-freezing first couldn't help — the fresh stamp composes onto the same poisoned history. Verified after the fixes below: repair leaves **every visible vertex within 7e-5**, recovers 171 transforms' real pre-freeze channels, clears 310 stale/degenerate residues, and a fresh freeze→unfreeze round-trip on the instance-heavy CABINET branch is clean (worst vertex 1.5e-3 at ~20-unit scale).
  - **Instancing-safe restore (the big one).** Shared points cannot be counter-baked — every other instance would move — and the old sweep *warned and let the subtree move anyway* (314 meshes displaced by the restored group's full delta on this scene). An instanced-shape owner now becomes a **boundary**: the restored ancestor's delta is absorbed into its local matrix (`L' = W_cur · A_new⁻¹ · A_cur · W_P_cur⁻¹`, world-exact), and its whole subtree needs no compensation at all. A transform whose OWN shape is shared is likewise never restored non-trivially (warn + bake retained) — writing its channels would displace its siblings; a trivial (identity-bake) restore still runs so no-op residues get consumed. Nested boundaries resolve to the nearest one; the parent-world composition in phase 1 stops at boundaries (everything below one keeps its current world).
  - **`repair_stored_transforms(objects=None, dry_run, clear_stale)`** — triage for scenes stamped by the old flow: `frozen` (channels at identity — the freeze provably ran; restored), `stale` (live channels — a skipped-freeze stamp or a legit frozen-then-moved state; indistinguishable from scene data, so never restored here, cleared only with `clear_stale=True`), `degenerate` (zero/non-finite scale bake — unusable). The stale set's channels are already correct (a skipped freeze never zeroed them), so clearing loses nothing.
  - **Partial-flatten stamps.** `makeIdentity` on a group is not all-or-nothing: a multiply-instanced descendant is skipped with a *warning* (`Cannot freeze below transform X…`) while the rest flattens — observed live on the CABINET branch. `commit_bakes` stamped the whole subtree on the root's success, re-creating exactly the stale bakes the commit-after-freeze design exists to prevent. A descendant is now stamped only on proof the flatten reached it (its freeze channels sit at identity afterwards).
  - **`Matrices.safe_inverse` also rejects non-finite matrices now.** `abs(inf) <= tol` and `abs(nan) <= tol` are both False, so the determinant-only guard passed corrupt matrices straight through to `inverse()` — which returns identity-like garbage for those exactly as it does for singular ones (measured). A corrupt bake attr upstream would have silently corrupted every derived write instead of skipping with a warning. Trivial (identity-delta) restores also stop rewriting every mesh point for an operation that moves nothing.
  - Diagnosis discipline note, twice earned on this scene: `exactWorldBoundingBox` is not a geometry-identity probe (it counts invisible orphaned intermediates, and reports the AABB of a rotated local box — both produced phantom "movers" while every visible vertex was exact), and API 2.0 `MPoint`s indexed out of a `getPoints()` array must be value-copied — held references decay to garbage after the array dies, which manufactured phantom `inf` drift in a probe. Final verification samples plain floats from visible meshes only.
  - `test_xform_utils.py` +4 (boundary preserves instanced children's world while the plain sibling's geometry holds; instanced-owner restore refuses non-trivial bakes; partial-flatten stamps only what flattened; repair triage incl. degenerate + dry-run). `blendertk` needs no twin: the stale-stamp era was Maya-only (its freeze has stamped inside the op from the start) — but see BACKLOG for its multi-user `data.transform` restore audit.

- **2026-08-01 — Scene Exporter audit, implementation pass: duplicate-material merges are now VERIFIED before anything is deleted, relative-path conversion actually stores relative paths, and set-driven keys are excluded from every time operation.** The full build-out of the audit's design decisions (the same-day robustness pass below shipped the mechanical fixes). All verified in mayapy: 371/371 across scene_exporter (108) / mat_utils_extended / mat_utils_resolve_path / edit_utils / anim_utils (131) / scene_data_sidecar.
  - **Duplicate materials are two-phase now** (`find_materials_with_duplicate_textures(verify=True)` + `reassign_duplicate_materials(verify=True)`): the cheap fingerprint (node type + attr→basename-stem) only GROUPS candidates; each is then pairwise-verified against the keeper — equal unconnected scalar attribute values (generic, same-nodeType attr diff), identical place2d placement signature and color space per texture slot, and texture CONTENT identity (size + first/last-64KB hash) when the stored paths differ. Kills the two confirmed false-positive classes (`brick/albedo.png` vs `wood/albedo.png`; one atlas with different tilings) while preserving the consolidation case loose matching existed for (byte-identical copies in different folders still merge). `verify=False` documents the old loose behavior. 5 near-miss regression tests.
  - **`convert_to_relative_paths` never actually stored relative paths** — probed in mayapy: Maya's file node auto-expands a resolvable relative `fileTextureName` back to absolute at `cmds.setAttr` time, so the old copy-then-remap pair silently shipped absolute paths (which the old location-based check then misread as "Relative"). New `MatUtils.stage_textures_relative`: one per-node pass — relativize-in-place under sourceimages (SUBFOLDERS PRESERVED; the old remap flattened them), stage externals with a CONTENT-compared collision policy (a different same-named resident file skips the node loudly instead of rebinding it to the wrong texture — the old pair's worst failure), UDIM tile sets staged via token glob, and the relative form written with `om.MPlug.setString` past the auto-expand, with a `cmds.setAttr` undo anchor first so undo still restores the original path. Locked/referenced plugs skip per-node instead of aborting the task.
  - **`check_absolute_paths` is form-based now** (`isabs`/drive/UNC + a separate "escapes project root" flag for `..` paths), scoped to the same full-shading-history file nodes as `check_valid_paths` — the old location-under-sourceimages test read stored-absolute paths as "Relative" (false negative), flagged legitimate `..` paths as "Absolute", and never saw bump/normal/utility-chained textures at all.
  - **Set-driven-key (unitless) curves are excluded from every time operation**: `listConnections(type="animCurve")` also matches `animCurveUU/UA/UL/UT`, so `snap_keys_to_frames` rewrote DRIVER VALUES (permanent rig corruption), `tie_keyframes` crashed on them (om2 `input()` returns a bare float), and both keyframe checks false-positived on SDK inbetweens. New `_AnimUtilsInternal._filter_time_curves` + `TIME_CURVE_TYPES` back snap/tie (with `include_driven=False` escape hatch) and both checks. Probed clean: `keyframe -q` never returned driver values, so `get_keyframe_times`/the FBX bake range were never affected.
  - **`resolve_invalid_texture_paths`' hunt is gated**: rebind only on a UNIQUE basename match under the whole sourceimages tree (recursive — the old hunt saw only the root; its own docstring admitted it "happily matches a same-named file the node does not point at"), logged at WARNING old→new so it's auditable; ambiguous names are reported, never guessed; `<UDIM>`/`<f>` tokens match by pattern and rebind with the token preserved.
  - **`check_hidden_geometry` reads display layers** (layer-hidden geometry shipped unflagged in every mode) and SKIPS animated/driven visibility — the 'visible' export mode includes those on purpose, so the check could previously only false-positive there. `check_objects_below_floor` filters to SURFACE_TYPES (control curves/locators below Y=0 no longer fail a check named "geometry"). `get_overlapping_duplicates` fingerprints on polyEvaluate's INTEGER counts (the old `str(full dict)` hash embedded object-space floats — a frozen-transform coincident twin was invisible) and EXCLUDES objects whose vertex sampling fails, loudly, instead of degrading to the bbox+counts match that was its false-positive path.
  - **Displacement/volume/aiSurfaceShader textures join the pipeline**: `get_mats(include_displacement=True)` (+ passthrough on `filter_materials_by_objects`; the three duplicated SG-slot walks collapsed into one closure) — the exporter opts in, so those maps are now validated, size-checked, and staged like every other texture instead of being invisible end-to-end.
  - **GLB-only exports roll the sidecar baseline forward only after the deliverable exists** (a failed FBX→GLB conversion no longer makes the next hierarchy diff compare against a phantom), sidecar legacy-migration renames survive transient locks (`_safe_replace` — a locked file used to mislabel a successful export "Failed to export objects"), and a failed final manifest replace cleans up its `.tmp`.
  - Deferred with reasons in `.claude/BACKLOG.md`: legacy copy/remap helpers keep their audited defects for non-exporter callers; root-transform check scope; success-banner check count; opt-in scene rollback after a blocked export.

- **2026-08-01 — Launched Maya no longer dies with `commandPort … (10048)` when its port is stolen mid-boot, and the runner can no longer connect to (or kill) a Maya it didn't launch.** User-reported occasional `Socket Error: No command port created … 10048` in freshly launched instances. Root cause: `get_available_port` bind-probes the port and releases it, but Maya takes 15–60s to boot before the `-command` MEL runs — anything binding the port in that window (a concurrent launch, the user's session opening its default ports) wins it, and the MEL `commandPort -q -name` guard only sees ports opened by *this* session, so the fixed bind died and aborted the title stamp (the anonymous, port-less Maya window was the fingerprint). Worse, the runner's wait loop connect-probed the requested port blindly, so it happily "succeeded" against whichever process owned it — potentially the user's interactive session, the exact hazard `force_new_instance` exists to prevent. Now: (1) the startup MEL **scans `[port, port+PORT_SCAN_SPAN)` for the first port that binds** a fresh python-source port and stamps the ACTUAL port into the window title in both branches (`[Port: N]` / `[Port: FAILED]`); (2) the runner discovers the port from the **title tag of the PID it launched** — authoritative, because PID-owned netstat rows alone can't disambiguate userSetup's own mel/python command ports (observed live: tentacle startup had claimed the `:7002`/`:7003` names, Maya self-healed to 7004, and lowest-owned-port discovery adopted the *mel*-source 7002) — falling back to PID-owned netstat rows (new `get_port_from_pid`, sharing one `netstat -ano` parse with `get_pid_from_port`), then to the legacy connect probe only if netstat itself is unavailable; (3) the `force_new_instance=False` launch fallback **re-picks a bindable port** instead of launching on the port the connect just failed on (its `_is_port_free` connect probe is blind to a zombie's bound-but-not-listening socket — the documented 2026-07-09 failure mode); (4) `ensure_connection` no longer `taskkill /F /IM maya.exe`s **every Maya on the machine** — a session-safety hard-rule violation — it closes only the recorded `_launched_pid` and re-probes before relaunching. `_launch_maya_gui` returns the actual port (truthiness-compatible); `connect()`/`ensure_connection` adopt it into `self.port`. Live-verified: with 7002 squatted by a foreign listener, the launched Maya self-healed to 7003, the runner adopted 7003 (never the squatter's port), and `1+1` evaluated over the discovered port. Follow-up hardening from the self-critique pass: the `ensure_connection` kill is guarded against **PID reuse** (only fires if the recorded PID is still a running Maya; `shutdown` clears it), the netstat parse identifies listening rows by the locale-independent foreign address `:0` instead of the `LISTENING` literal (localized on non-English Windows — the pre-refactor parse wasn't state-filtered), and the bind-vs-connect probe split is now explicit: new shared `_tcp_port_bindable` backs both `get_available_port` and `_find_port_pair` — the in-session pair scan had the same zombie-blindness (`_is_port_free` connect probe, kept only for its correct use: detecting an existing session). `test_maya_connection.py` +15 (`TestPortCollisionResilience`); also defused `test_connect_port_failure`, which had been launching a REAL Maya every run (`launch` defaulted True with only sockets mocked).

- **2026-08-01 — Mangled-name guards: `Naming.conform_shape_names`, a `check_mangled_names` export check, and rename no longer leaves underscore residue.** Root-caused from production `VDATS_module.ma`: the (since-fixed, 7/30) uninstance scratch-name bug accumulated `__uninst_tmp` tokens on shape names; batch-stripping the token with `Naming.rename` left the separators behind (`vdat__uninst_tmp__uninst_tmpShape702` → `vdat____Shape702`, worst case 83 consecutive underscores); and because Maya never auto-renames a multi-parented shape when its transform is renamed, instancing spread the fossils under every instance path — 125 mangled shapes shipping in every `scene_data.json`. Three guards so no future mangling source (import junk, round-trip suffixes, scratch tokens) can persist or ship: (1) **`Naming.rename` collapses separator residue** after strip/replace formatting via the new `ptk.collapse_delimiter_runs` (an explicit `__` typed in the pattern is honored; mirrored in blendertk). (2) **`Naming.conform_shape_names`** renames shapes to Maya's own `<transformBase>Shape<digits>[Orig]` spelling — UUID-deduped so an instanced shape is renamed once, fixing every path. (3) **Scene Exporter**: default-on `check_mangled_names` fails an export whose node set carries `__uninst`/`__RZTMP`/`FBXASC###`/underscore-run names, and the opt-in `conform_shape_names` task repairs them pre-export (permanent scene change, deliberately not reverted). `test_naming.py` +7, `test_scene_exporter.py` +6.

- **2026-08-01 — Scene Exporter robustness pass (first fixes from the tasks/checks audit): a check-blocked export now says the scene was already mutated, stale per-run state can no longer poison later tasks, and Smart Bake actually honors the Optimize Keys toggle for its override-layer curves.** (1) Checks run *after* tasks and the undo-chunk rollback was removed in v0.10.18 — but two comments still promised it, and a blocked export left material cleanup / key snapping / path rewrites in the scene silently; `perform_export` now warns explicitly and the comments state the truth (the user's undo queue is the only rollback). (2) `snap_keys_to_frame` never dropped the `_key_times` cache, so `tie_all_keyframes` bookended to the STALE pre-snap range and re-created the exact fractional keys the snap removed — then failed the pipeline's own `check_floating_point_keys`; new `_invalidate_keyframe_cache` runs after snap and optimize (which deletes curves), and the `objects` setter routes through it. (3) `smart_bake` passed `optimize_keys=False` claiming the separate task handles it, while that task's comment claimed SmartBake handles it — and neither could: baked override-layer curves sit behind animBlendNodes `listConnections` can't traverse. The run's `optimize_keys` value is now forwarded per run via an `_execute_tasks_and_checks` override (blendertk's existing idiom), honoring the tooltip's "Also controls key optimization inside Smart Bake". (4) `resolve_invalid_texture_paths`' "already valid" guard was a bare `os.path.exists` — CWD-relative, so it re-validated nothing a workspace resolves; it now asks `MatUtils.resolve_path(search=False)`, the same question `check_valid_paths` asks. (5) A failed scene-data sidecar write was DEBUG-only — silently freezing the hierarchy-diff baseline the next export compares against; now WARNING. `_hierarchy_check_ran` is reset per run (one checked export no longer makes every later export write baselines). `check_geometry_lod_suffix` logs its informational listing itself — the runner only surfaces messages from *failing* checks, so the always-passing check was a silent no-op. (6) A `SmartBake.restore` reporting failure non-exceptionally now reaches the export log (with the session id for a manual retry) instead of only `cmds.warning`. Tests: `test_scene_exporter.py` +4 (`TestTaskStateHygiene`: snap→tie round-trip, marker reset, toggle forwarding, relative-path guard — the last verified against Maya's own setAttr auto-expansion of resolvable relative `fileTextureName`s, probed in mayapy); test harness de-flaked by replacing all seven `cmds.hyperShade(assign=...)` calls (silent no-op in bare mayapy → vacuous texture tests) with a shared SG-set `_assign_shader` helper.

- **2026-08-01 — `PlayblastExporter` and its result records join the package namespace.** `mayatk.anim_utils.playblast_exporter` had no `DEFAULT_INCLUDE` entry, so tentacle's rendering slot could only reach the engine by deep module path — the one mayatk tool in that panel that wasn't a plain `mtk.X`. Now `mtk.PlayblastExporter` / `mtk.ExportTarget` / `mtk.CaptureResult` / `mtk.ExportResult`. Class-only (no `"*"`), so the co-located `PlayblastExporterSlots` panel stays name-discovered by `MayaUiHandler` like every other tool panel rather than leaking onto the flat surface.

- **2026-08-01 — data_nodes pipeline audit: a takes export no longer leaks sticky bake-complex state into every later export, and the Lightmap Baker joins the producer registry it was documented to be in.** From a DCC→Unity architecture review of the shared scene-data-node system. (1) `apply_takes` sets `FBXExportBakeComplexAnimation -v true` + a union `Start`/`End` — global, sticky FBX exporter options — but `reset_takes` (the `kAfterExport` hook) only cleared the take *splits*, so bake-complex stayed enabled with a stale range for the rest of the session (and silently flipped `set_bake_animation_range`'s enabled check on later Scene Exporter runs). `apply_takes` now captures the pre-takes state once and `reset_takes` restores it — the user's own bake settings survive, rather than being blind-reset to factory. (2) `LightmapBaker` gains the public no-arg **`refresh_export_metadata`** (the other producers' naming) and an entry in `FbxUtils._KNOWN_PRODUCERS` — it was the one documented `data_export` channel invisible to `run_export_preparers`, so a Scene Exporter run shipped whatever the last commit/revert wrote instead of a manifest regenerated from the live markers (its UI help even told the user to include the carrier manually). (3) A known producer that *imports* but *fails* now logs a warning instead of a debug whisper — a broken producer silently shipping a stale channel was quieter than a broken registered preparer; unimportable producers stay debug (they're speculative by design). (4) Scene Exporter: `apply_declared_takes` no longer republishes what `export_data_node` just published (refresh tracked per run — the flag piggybacks on the per-export `objects` re-seed) and is producer-agnostic — its direct `ShotStore` import is gone; with the takes channel absent it degrades to a debug no-op. It also stages `FbxUtils.reset_takes` as a **post-write deferred restore**, so a session with no auto-export hook installed no longer keeps the splits and bake range armed after a Scene Exporter export (idempotent beside the hook's own `kAfterExport` reset, which fires first). (5) `_install_auto_export_hook` unsubscribes its stable owner key before installing, so reloading `fbx_utils` in a live session can't stack a second callback pair and double-run every preparer per export. Docs: `data_nodes.md`'s "because it's hidden" causality corrected (the carrier was never actually hidden — `Visible` mode collects geometry only, `Selected` ships the user's picks), and the channel table gains the missing `shadow_metadata` / `emissive_groups` rows. Scoped suites 280/280 (data_nodes, fbx_export_preparers, fbx_utils, scene_exporter, lightmap_baker, shot_export_view, audio_clips_export, scene_data_sidecar) + preparer rerun 8/8.

- **2026-08-01 — Un-freezing a parent GROUP threw its meshes across the scene; and `XformUtils.freeze_transforms` now stamps its own bake history (`store=True`), so every tool that freezes is reversible.** User-reported. `cmds.makeIdentity` on a group does not merely zero that group's channels — it **flattens the entire subtree**: every descendant transform's translate/rotate/scale is zeroed and the composed matrix is baked into the leaf shape points (verified in mayapy, including through nested groups). `restore_transforms` then counter-shifted only each restored node's **own** shapes — and a group has none — so writing the group's channels back applied the matrix a *second* time on top of already-baked geometry. Measured on a `grp(t=10, ry=30, s=2) > geo` scene: the mesh moved **19.69 units and doubled in scale**, which is why it read as "the scale isn't being unfrozen". The geometry sweep now spans the whole subtree of each restored node (`_plan_restore_geometry`), each shape carried by its nearest restored ancestor-or-self via that ancestor's world delta `A_cur⁻¹ · A_new`; snapshots stay strictly in phase 1, before any write. Round-trip world drift is now **0.0** for: group root-only, group with child channels, nested groups, 3 siblings under one group, and 3× repeated freeze/unfreeze cycles (channels return exactly `[10,0,0] [0,30,0] [2,2,2]`).
  - **`store=True` is the new default on `freeze_transforms`** — the second half of the report ("too many freeze operations that aren't storing"). The bake history is three attributes per transform, so it is cheap enough to be unconditional, and it is what makes the helper safe to call from any tool. The stamp **always traverses the subtree**, independent of `freeze_children`: a group freeze zeroes every descendant's channels, so storing only the roots lost them irrecoverably. Construction-time freezes opt out with `store=False` — rig controls (`controls._safe_freeze`), `wheel_rig`, `create_locator_at_object`'s GRP, and `uninstance(freeze=True)`, whose pre-freeze state is either identity or a DAG that no longer exists.
  - **The stamp is committed AFTER the freeze, only for what actually froze.** Stamping up front (the first cut of this change, and what the panel used to do) gave every *skipped* object — instanced, or connection-blocked under the default `preserve` strategy — a bake matching its still-untouched channels, so a later un-freeze added a transform that was never baked out and **doubled** it. `freeze_transforms` now snapshots the subtree's pre-freeze locals, runs the freeze, and commits bakes for the transforms that succeeded: the makeIdentity path stamps its whole subtree (it flattened it), `freeze_instanced_group` stamps only the operated member (its siblings absorbed a *compensating* matrix, so their pre-freeze local is not a valid bake). Verified in mayapy: a driven and an instanced object both come out of a skipped freeze with no bake attrs at all.
  - **Removed the now-double stores.** `Channels.freeze_transforms` dropped `_store_pre_freeze` and `transform_diag._bake` dropped its conditional `store_transforms` (kept only on the `freeze_instanced_group` branch, which does not route through `freeze_transforms`) — leaving them would compose the history twice and make un-freeze overshoot, which is exactly what `test_store_restore_contract_survives_the_fix` caught (rotateZ restored to 60° instead of 35°). The compose-and-write itself is now one shared `_accumulate_bake`, used by both `store_transforms` (current local) and `freeze_transforms` (pre-freeze snapshot).
  - **`restore_transforms` on a driven node raised mid-batch instead of skipping.** `_apply_clean_local` rewrites translate/rotate/scale wholesale, so any connected TRS child plug threw `A child attribute of '…' is locked or connected` — unlocking cannot help, the plug is *connected* — aborting the run with earlier objects already restored and their geometry already shifted. It now warns and skips, matching how the referenced-node case is handled. `_transform_is_driven` gained a `channels` parameter so the restore can ask about T/R/S only: the default set includes shear, and reporting a driven shear to a caller that never writes shear cost that node its restore for nothing.
  - **`MMatrix.inverse()` has no failure mode** — on a singular matrix it returns an identity-like result rather than raising (measured: `det4x4() == 0` → `result[0][0] == 1.0`), so every `try/except RuntimeError` guarding an inverse in this file was dead code and a zero-scaled node silently baked NaN-adjacent geometry instead of being skipped. New **`Matrices.safe_inverse`** (next to the `Matrices.inverse` it corrects, which now documents the trap) checks the determinant and returns None; `restore_transforms` and the geometry planner route through it. Also fixed: `_nearest_known_ancestor` looped on truthiness, so a name with no `|` re-split to itself forever and **hung Maya** — it now loops on the separator.
  - **Also fixed: restoring a grandparent while skipping the transform between it and a leaf.** Phase 1 resolved the parent world by looking only at the **direct** parent, so a leaf whose direct parent was not part of the call was planned against a stale parent world. It now walks up to the nearest restored ancestor and composes that ancestor's delta. Newly reachable because every freeze now stamps the whole subtree, so users can select any mix of ancestors and leaves.
  - Shapes on several DAG paths are skipped with a warning rather than rewritten — the sweep reaching descendants means a group of instanced props would otherwise have had one shape's points dragged for every member. The subtree walk is one batched `listRelatives` for the whole set, not one per node.
  - **Freezing a MIRRORED instance group in place turned it inside-out.** Found while wiring up the `normal` modifier below. `freeze_instanced_group` bakes the shared points by `B` itself, and when `B` mirrors (negative determinant) the point set changes handedness — the untouched face winding then faces inward, so every member renders inverted. Measured on a negatively-scaled 2-member group: the world face normal flipped from `[0,0,1]` to `[0,0,-1]` for master *and* sibling. `makeIdentity` handles this for itself (verified: the normal freeze path leaves world normals unchanged on a mirrored mesh, with or without `-normal`), but the hand-rolled bake never did. It now reverses the winding on the shared shape once when `B.det3x3() < 0` — one edit every member sees, which is what the whole in-place design rests on. `test_xform_utils.py` +2 (mirrored group keeps normals outward; a non-mirroring bake does not flip).
  - **`freeze_transforms(obj, normal=True)` froze nothing at all.** `normal` is a `makeIdentity` *modifier* (freeze vertex normals, which matters for negatively-scaled geometry), but it was counted among the per-channel flags — so passing it suppressed the freeze-everything default while contributing no axes of its own, and the call returned early. Both `create_locator_at_object` uses (`freeze_locator` / `freeze_object`, **both defaulting True** and documented as freezing) had therefore never done anything. `normal` is now a modifier threaded through to `makeIdentity -normal`, and the two rig calls pass `store=False` like the GRP freeze beside them. Verified before changing it: `makeIdentity(n=1)` is legal on both locators and meshes, and in the real rig the LOC and GEO are *already* at identity when the freeze runs (the GRP is parent-constrained to the LOC before the reparent), so making it real is behaviour-neutral there — 26/26 locator-rig tests unchanged.
  - `test_xform_utils.py` +11 (group root-only, skipped-intermediate, store-by-default across the subtree, `store=False`, 3× cycles, connection-blocked and instanced skips stamping nothing, driven-node skip, short-name ancestor termination, `safe_inverse`, zero-scale restore); `test_transform.py` reworked onto the new contract (19/19). blendertk needed no change — Blender's `transform_apply` folds into each child's `matrix_parent_inverse` instead of flattening, which `btk.restore_transforms` already inverts, and its `freeze_transforms` already defaulted `store=True` (the Maya side now matches it).

- **2026-07-30 — Freezing an instanced object no longer touches the DAG: `XformUtils.freeze_instanced_group` bakes the shared geometry in place, replacing the fork/re-link design that displaced geometry and broke per-instance shading on a production scene.** User-reported and reproduced against the real file: the previous approach forked a group's shape, let the op freeze it, then re-linked the fork onto every sibling — and adding/removing DAG instance edges renumbers `instObjGroups`, so the re-link failed with `Connection not made … MAT_…SG.dagSetMembers[n]`. Because the re-link ran *before* the matrix compensation, a mid-way failure left siblings carrying baked geometry with un-compensated matrices — geometry visibly out of position. It also littered the scene with `__uninst_tmp` shapes (the reported file had accumulated **116**, one shape reaching **15 parents** through nested `…__uninst_tmp…__uninst_tmp…` names). Nothing in the new path edits a DAG edge: a `parentOnly` duplicate — a shapeless stand-in Maya *will* freeze — yields the exact baked delta `B = pre_local · post_local⁻¹` (pivots included), the shared shape's points are transformed by `B` once (every member sees it), the master takes the stand-in's frozen channels, and each sibling is compensated `L → B⁻¹·L` with world pivots re-pinned. Measured on the reported 4-member group: **every member drift 1.4e-7, per-instance shading unchanged, instancing intact, master fully frozen**. `preserved_instances` / `PreservedInstances` / `_restore_instance_group` are **removed** — the "run any op on a forked master" contract cannot be honored safely, and the concrete operation replaces it.
  - **The shared-intermediate blocker is gone as a concept.** `makeIdentity` refuses to freeze past a shared orig shape, but the stand-in never runs `makeIdentity` on the real object, so a shared intermediate stops mattering — no `delete_history` consent needed for the preserve path. `_authoring_shapes` picks what to bake: construction history (`polyCube1` → `inMesh`) is fine (point writes land on the tweak and survive re-evaluation), a shape downstream of a `geometryFilter` is skipped in favour of its orig shape, and **orphaned intermediates are ignored** — dead history remnants, common in imported/scanned assets, whose member set may not even match the visible shape's.
  - **`fix_non_orthogonal_axes` now decides per group instead of always preserving.** Baking shared points fixes every member only when they all need the same correction; on the reported group just 1 of 4 was skewed, so preserving would have pushed that shear onto the other 3 (measured). A divergent member is detached on its own — the unflagged members keep sharing, untouched — and forks are renamed after their transform (`vdat533Shape`) instead of keeping the `__uninst_tmp` scratch name, which is what let the wreckage accumulate across runs.
  - **End-to-end on the reported scene**: previously "0 frozen, 1 skipped" plus `Cannot freeze below transform vdat533 … multiply-instanced child polySurfaceShape21`, object unfixed. Now the scene reports **0 skewed transforms remaining, 0 of 193 shapes displaced, 0 shading changes, and no new `__uninst_tmp` nodes**.
  - **Group membership comes from the shapes a bake actually writes to** (`_instance_group_members`), not from every shared shape. Deriving it twice was a silent-drop bug: an orphaned intermediate can be shared more widely than the visible shape, so unrelated objects counted as "handled" and were removed from the freeze without ever being touched. A group the bake *skips* is likewise no longer claimed — it falls through to the normal loop and gets reported as an instanced skip.
  - **Driven / multi-path members are triaged across the whole group.** A driven sibling would have its compensation overwritten on the next evaluation (displaced against baked geometry) and a driven master rejects the frozen channels outright, so which member the caller passes must not change the answer — checking siblings only meant a skipped first member simply retried with the driven one as master and raised.
  - `test_xform_utils.py` gains per-instance-shading, shared-intermediate, driven-sibling and orphan-sharing regressions; suites 165/165 across node_utils/xform_utils/diagnostics. blendertk's context manager is made private (`_preserved_instances`) so the public surfaces stay mirrored.

- **2026-07-30 — Freeze/fix on an instanced object with construction history was a permanent no-op ("0 frozen, 1 skipped" + "Cannot freeze below transform … multiply-instanced child"), because the four helpers disagreed about what "instanced" means.** User-reported, reproduced in mayapy. `cmds.instance` shares **every** shape, including the intermediate (orig) shape a deformer creates — and `makeIdentity` refuses to freeze while *any* child shape is multiply-instanced, intermediate or not (verified directly: forking only the visible shape still yields "Cannot freeze below transform q_src due to multiply-instanced child q_srcShapeOrig"). But `get_instances` counted intermediates while `uninstance` and `preserved_instances` both passed `noIntermediate=True`, so they forked the visible shape only, left the orig shape shared, and freeze skipped the object *forever*. New SSoT **`NodeUtils.get_instanced_shapes(node, intermediate=True)`** — the shape-side counterpart of `get_instances` — now backs the fork decision, the freeze skip test, and `freeze_transforms(instance_strategy=…)` alike, so detection and forking can no longer drift apart. This also silently broke the documented `uninstance(objs, freeze=True)` "engine-safe finish" on any object carrying history.
  - **A shared orig shape must not simply be forked** — measured: the deformer reads it per instance, so dropping one transform's instance edge invalidates that index and the *remaining* instances evaluate to an **empty mesh**. (Caught by probe before shipping; the naive version fixed the target and destroyed its sibling.) Baking the history away first is the safe route and preserves every member's appearance, so both `uninstance` and `preserved_instances` gained an opt-in **`delete_history`**: with it the group forks and freezes normally *and keeps its instancing*; without it the orig shape is left shared and reported. `fix_non_orthogonal_axes` gained the same passthrough, and when it is blocked it now says exactly that once — the doomed `makeIdentity` fallback is skipped when the node is still shape-shared, instead of stacking Maya's warning on top of a reason already known. End-to-end on the reported topology: default → refuses safely, zero drift; `delete_history=True` → fixed, instancing retained, both members drift 0.0.
  - Also fixed: `_fork_instanced_shape` looked the duplicate up with `noIntermediate=True`, so forking an intermediate source reported "duplicate produced no shape node"; a fork of an intermediate is now re-flagged intermediate so it can't render on top of the visible shape. And `_restore_instance_group` half-restored when the wrapped op deleted a forked shape (relink skipped, but sibling matrices still compensated — moving geometry that never migrated); it now aborts that group coherently and reports.
  - `delete_history` is the gather's only destructive step, so it now runs **last** — it was ordered ahead of the transform-level-instancing and driven-sibling checks, so a group rejected by either had already lost its history despite being reported as skipped and untouched. It also no longer claims the whole group when history deletion collapses the sharing outright: the siblings are independent at that point and get evaluated on their own iteration instead of being dropped from the operable set. The flag is likewise threaded through the two paths that ignored it (`instance_strategy="uninstance"`, and `fix_non_orthogonal_axes`' uninstance fallback), where opting in used to fail silently.
  - `test_node_utils.py` +6, `test_xform_utils.py` +1 — 172/172 across node_utils/xform_utils/diagnostics.

- **2026-07-30 — Instanced objects no longer force a choice between "skipped" and "instancing destroyed": `NodeUtils.preserved_instances` keeps instance groups intact across destructive ops, and `freeze_transforms` / `fix_non_orthogonal_axes` ride it.** User-reported: the non-orthogonal-axes fix failed on a scene with multiple instanced objects — the old path permanently uninstanced every flagged member one at a time, and `freeze_transforms` just skipped instanced objects outright. The new context manager forks only the first targeted member of each group (the *master*), yields it for the wrapped op, and on exit — including when the block raises — re-links every sibling to the master's post-op shape with its local matrix compensated by the master's baked delta (`B = pre_local · post_local⁻¹`, sibling `L → B⁻¹·L`), so the instance topology AND every member's world geometry survive. Op-agnostic: `B` is *measured* off the master's matrices, not assumed, so a pure shape edit degenerates to `B = I`; the one contract is that the wrapped op bakes any master matrix change into the shape (the freeze contract). World rp/sp pivots are re-pinned after the matrix write (probe-verified beforehand that `xform -os -matrix` is exact under non-zero pivots, and that the pivot re-pin is stationary). Restore is per-group best-effort and **never leans on the undo queue** — the instance-edge surgery is `MFnDagNode.removeChild`/`MDagModifier` API work, invisible to `cmds` undo — with members held as `MObjectHandle`s, since DAG paths go stale the moment an edge is removed. Groups that can't be preserved are triaged up front and reported untouched: transform-level instancing (per-path compensation is impossible on a shared node), referenced nodes, driven sibling channels, overlapping multi-shape groups. `uninstance`'s duplicate/graft/removeChild block is extracted into the shared `_fork_instanced_shape` — one fork implementation for both. `freeze_transforms` gains `instance_strategy="skip"|"preserve"|"uninstance"` (default unchanged) mirroring `connection_strategy`; `fix_non_orthogonal_axes` gains `instance_strategy="preserve"|"uninstance"` **defaulting to preserve** — one master freeze clears a shared-skew group in place (the duplicated-instance case), and only members whose own divergent skew survives the compensation — or is introduced by it; shared points cannot satisfy differing corrections — are forked off and baked individually. `test_node_utils.py` +6, `test_xform_utils.py` +4, `test_diagnostics.py` +3 — all failing pre-implementation; scoped run 164/164.

- **2026-07-30 — `libpyside: Failed to disconnect … from signal` console spam from the Reference Manager header and the Channels table (reported against the Blender twin; identical code here).** Two defects in idempotent signal re-wiring — mandatory in both panels because the QWidget outlives the slots instance across a reload. (1) **Blanket `signal.disconnect()` on a signal with nothing attached**: PySide reports that through `warnings.warn`, not an exception, so the surrounding `except (RuntimeError, TypeError)` never suppressed it and every first call printed the line — the header's `refresh_requested`, and channels' four `cellScrub*` / `cellWheelScrolled` bindings (which, unlike the `cellChanged` / `itemSelectionChanged` / `customContextMenuRequested` blankets beside them, have no owner but us). Both now drop only their own prior connection, tracked per (widget, key). (2) **The tracked-disconnect primitive had the same hole**: `_rewire_signal` dropped its stored `QMetaObject.Connection` through the signal-*instance* `disconnect()`, which expects a slot — handed a Connection it can't match (the reload case: PySide breaks the binding when the old slots instance is collected) it warns and returns rather than raising, so the `except` was dead code and the warning would simply have come back wearing a Connection repr. Verified against live PySide6, then switched to the static `QObject.disconnect()` — the API that actually takes a Connection — with a falsy (already-broken) Connection skipped outright. `test_reference_manager.py` 51/51, `test_channels.py` 92/92, `test_channel_box.py` 8/8.

- **2026-07-30 — Blender-scene import robustness: the FBX import no longer inherits the user's sticky import preset, and UDIM sets resolve to a real tile instead of a file-less warning.** (1) The FBX plugin's import options are global and sticky — whatever the last interactive FBX import set (mode, axis, constraints) silently shaped `BlenderSceneImport._import_fbx`'s `cmds.file` call. Verified in a fresh mayapy that a poisoned `FBXImportMode` persists across calls, and that `FBXResetImport` restores the factory `merge` — which *updates animation on same-named pre-existing scene nodes*, so the import now resets AND pins mode to `add` (the same default `FbxUtils._DEFAULT_IMPORT_OPTIONS` chose), mirroring the conversion template's `FBXResetExport` rule; both calls are best-effort so a plugin build missing either command can't block the import. The reset is a new **`FbxUtils.reset_import()`** primitive (the import twin of the export side's reset, beside `reset_takes`), and `FbxUtils.import_scene` — the canonical importer, which had the same gap — now calls it before applying options, so sticky options *outside* the applied dict (scale factor, axis conversion) can't leak in either; the bridge's `_import_fbx` delegates to FbxUtils instead of hand-rolling the plugin-load + MEL. `test_fbx_utils.py` +1 (poisoned mode + `options={}` isolates the reset). (2) A `.blend` material's UDIM/UVTILE image carries a `<UDIM>` token in its filepath — never an on-disk file — so the conversion template's manifest either dropped it or (all-tiled materials) emitted the misleading "packed or needs relinking" warning; `_resolved_image_file` now globs the tile set (`glob.escape`d, so bracketed paths survive) and manifests the first existing tile, logged as flattened — one real texture beats none, and the authoritative FBX geometry is unaffected. Also: `convert` / `import_scene` / `bake` / `bake_scene` expand `~` (`expanduser`) beside the existing `$VAR` expansion. `test_scene_import.py` +2 → 26 (sticky-state reset pinned against a real FBX round-trip in the suite's mayapy; template pins for the tile tokens).

- **2026-07-30 — Game Shader: a dropped packed map's uncovered channels now come back as wired loose maps.** Rides pythontk's coverage-aware `filter_redundant_maps` (see its changelog): when a set carries an MSAO/ORM beside loose Metallic/Roughness but no separate AO, the AO channel is extracted to a real file beside the sources and connected to `TEX_ao_map` — previously dropping the packed map silently cost the network its AO. `_resolve_map_conflicts` now consumes the filter's decision report directly (reasons come from the SSoT instead of being reconstructed by diffing), and extracted maps appear in the per-map table with an `extracted from <packed>` note. `test_game_shader.py` +1 end-to-end (build from `Base_color + Metallic + Roughness + MSAO`, assert the recovered AO file drives the AO slot).

- **2026-07-30 — `MatUtils.find_unassigned`: the complement of `find_by_mat_id`, for "which objects did I forget to shade".** Maya has no true no-material state for renderable geometry — new meshes join `initialShadingGroup`, so they report the default shader (`standardSurface1` on 2025+, `lambert1` before it) rather than nothing — while geometry pulled out of every shading engine reports nothing at all and renders black. Both read as "unshaded" to a user, so the helper returns both, with `include_default=False` narrowing to the orphaned case. Object-level like `find_by_mat_id`, and deliberately skipping *partially* assigned meshes (some faces on a real material, the rest default): their unshaded faces are a different question. Takes the same `objects` pool convention — components resolve to their object (`objectsOnly`) and groups/transforms walk down to their shapes (`dag=True`) — and a pool that resolves to nothing returns `[]` rather than falling through to an argless `cmds.ls` scene scan. Drives tentacle's materials "No Material" select mode. `test_mat_utils.py` +7.

- **2026-07-30 — Game Shader died mid-batch on any texture set carrying both an opacity map and an AO map, and quietly wired two textures into the same slot when a set carried both a packed map and the maps it packs.** User-reported from the panel (`RuntimeError: setAttr: No object matches name: MAT_VDATS_decals.use_ao_map`, killing sets 3-of-3 onward). A StingrayPBS node's slots come from the ShaderFX graph loaded into it, and `Standard_Transparent.sfx` — loaded whenever the set has an Opacity map — exposes **no `TEX_ao_map` / `use_ao_map` at all** (verified against the live 2025 presets: the opaque graph has both, the transparent graph neither). `connect_stingray_nodes` wrote those plugs unconditionally. Every plug write now goes through `_wire`, which probes the slot first (`_has_attr`), reports a graph-specific gap as a skipped map instead of an exception, and creates no file node when the slot is absent; packed maps connect the channels the graph does have and report the rest. A failing set no longer aborts the batch — `create_network` isolates each set and marks it `Failed` in the summary table. `test_game_shader.py` +5, four of which error pre-fix with the reported message.
  - **One source per slot.** The dedup only dropped packed-vs-packed conflicts (ORM beats MSAO beats Metallic_Smoothness), so a set of `Metallic + Roughness + MSAO + AO` — the shape Substance emits — wired all four into the same three slots: last write won, the losers stayed in the scene as stray file nodes, and because packed maps connect the *children* (`TEX_ao_mapR/G/B`) while simple maps connect the parent, both survived on the same slot. `_resolve_map_conflicts` now hands the decision to **`ptk.MapFactory.filter_redundant_maps`** — the registry's `replaces` / `config_key` rules that Mat Updater already used, so the two tools can't drift and the packed-vs-separate direction follows the requested workflow (`mask_map` / `orm_map` / `metallic_smoothness` / `albedo_transparency`). mayatk keeps only what's its own: reporting each dropped map with the reason as a `–` row in the per-map table, and collapsing same-type duplicates (`_Mixed_AO` + `_AO`) to the first. `_wire` also clears a slot's parent *and* child inputs before connecting, making a re-run idempotent.
  - Mat Updater's **Selected Objects** scope already covered material nodes selected directly in the Hypershade/Outliner (`get_mats` passes them through) — verified, and the tooltip and its empty-selection messages now say so instead of implying geometry only.
- **2026-07-30 — `MatUtils.enable_viewport_opacity`: wire a material's opacity map into the slot its shader type honors.** New primitive for the case where the map exists but the viewport shows the material opaque — the material predates the map, or was built from a shader graph with no opacity slot. Finds the opacity source in the shading network, or (`search_disk`, default on) beside the material's other textures on disk, then drives the right slot per shader type: alpha into `opacity` for standardSurface / aiStandardSurface / StingrayPBS, `geometryOpacity` for OpenPBR, inverted through a `reverse` into `transparency` for the classic shaders. StingrayPBS materials are switched to the transparent ShaderFX graph first (their opacity slots don't otherwise exist), snapshotting and restoring the network `loadGraph` would wipe. Takes materials **or** objects, and reports per material (`enabled` / `already enabled` / `no opacity map` / `unsupported: …`). Ships with `get_mats_by_scope` (selected / visible / scene — the scope primitive material tools kept re-deriving), `find_opacity_source`, `set_transparency_algorithm` (named Viewport 2.0 modes), and `connect_to_channels` — the compound-slot fanout `game_shader` had privately, now one implementation for both. `ensure_transparent_graph` moves from `OpacityMaterialMode` to `MatUtils` (same graph swap, one caller updated). `test_mat_utils.py` +10.

- **2026-07-29 — "RizomUV exited cleanly but did not modify the FBX": the round-trip's temp files were fixed `%TEMP%` names, so any concurrent bridge run silently hijacked the one in flight.** User-reported from the panel. `_prepare_script_file` wrote `riz_uv_script.lua` and `export_path` defaulted to `rizomuv_exported.fbx` — one name each, shared by every process: a second Maya, the Blender twin (byte-identical names), and this repo's own test suite, whose `test_selection_preset_requires_select_objects` leaves a 55-byte body at exactly that path (which is what was sitting there, timestamped to the minute of the failure, alongside test-chunk artifacts from a suite run minutes earlier). RizomUV **re-reads the `-cfi` script after launch** — the same mtime-watch behaviour the one-way send flow already had to design around — so a mid-run overwrite means it never reaches `ZomSave`: reproduced by replacing the script ~1s after launch (`rc=0, saved=False`, verbatim the reported failure; other timings hang until the bridge's timeout instead). Both payloads now come from **`ptk.TempArtifacts`** — the existing primitive for exactly this ("write a temp payload for another process"), whose docstring already named "the rizom-style fixed-name pattern" — under the `"scoped"` policy, which is this flow's literal shape: the run blocks until RizomUV exits, so a clean run deletes both while a **failure keeps them**, which is what makes the new error's "open the script in RizomUV's Script Editor" advice actionable. Allocation age-sweeps same-prefix leftovers, so a crashed run can't accumulate either. Ruled out first, each against the real 2020.1 install and the FBX left behind by the failing run: Lua rendering for every preset, the user's exact panel values (rotate 180 / recursion 1 / 4096 / mix on, read out of `HKCU`), executable resolution, the POSIX `-cfi` argument, and a second RizomUV instance being open — plus a full production round-trip in a fresh `mayapy`, which passes end to end. `test_uv_rizom_bridge.py` +5 → 32, including one that drives `_execute_uv_script`'s no-save branch through a stubbed `AppLauncher.run` (the branch only fires on failure, so a scope error there would ship silently) and one pinning that a caller-assigned `export_path` is never deleted.
  - **The one-way send flow moves onto the same primitive** (`detached` policy — the honest one there: the session may outlive Maya and never signals completion, so nothing may delete its files; allocation age-sweeps instead). That deletes three hand-rolled helpers (`_make_send_tag` / `_make_send_fbx_path` / `_make_send_script_path`) whose tag was `time.time_ns()` — which `TempArtifacts` documents as *not* unique enough on its own, Windows' ~15ms clock resolution being coarser than two back-to-back sends, so two fast clicks could mint one tag and produce the very cross-send clobber the helper existed to prevent. Filenames are unchanged (`<stem>_send_<tag>.fbx` / `riz_send_<tag>.lua`), and the send flow's previously unbounded temp footprint — every send left an FBX + Lua behind forever — is now age-swept.
  - **A reused bridge re-allocates.** `cleanup()` untracks what it removed, so caching the paths across runs would leave run 2's payloads untracked and never cleaned — and the panel keeps one bridge for the whole session. `_release_temp_payloads` drops only the paths the store actually removed, which is what leaves an explicitly assigned `export_path` (never tracked) untouched. Verified live in a fresh `mayapy`: two consecutive round-trips through one bridge, each allocating and cleaning its own pair.
  - **The error also promised a diagnostic that cannot exist.** It said to "enable debug logging to see the Lua traceback"; RizomUV emits *nothing* on stdout/stderr in `-cfi` mode (confirmed across ~20 runs), so no log level could ever surface one. `_no_save_diagnosis` replaces it: it names the script and FBX, **compares the script still on disk against what the bridge wrote** — so a clobber names itself instead of costing an investigation — and points at RizomUV's Script Editor as the only place a failing line is visible. Also recorded in `rizom_headless_probe.py`'s findings block: degenerate input UVs (every coordinate collapsed onto one point) crash 2020.1 outright (`0xC00000FF`), while no UVs at all, a renamed UV set, two UV sets, and a NURBS surface in the selection are all safe.
  - **Known gap, unchanged here: a face / UV-shell selection is silently widened to the whole mesh.** `get_transform_node` strips the component suffix, so the bridge exports and repacks the entire object — verified in a fresh `mayapy` (`faceCube.f[0:5]` → all 24 faces repacked; other objects untouched, so the scope is per-object). Honouring a sub-object scope needs either a faces-only export or Rizom-side island selection; `UvUtils.pack_uvs` (xatlas) is the path that does support it today.

- **2026-07-29 — `UvUtils.pack_uvs` (xatlas) only accepted whole objects; it now packs a face / UV-shell selection too.** User-reported: with Method set to xatlas, the Pack tool did nothing on a component selection. The cause was input resolution — `_UvPackInternal.run` fed everything through `_AutoUnwrapInternal._resolve_meshes`, which resolves *to* mesh transforms and returns `[]` for a component string, so a face selection raised `ValueError: No mesh objects to pack.` before the engine ran (reproduced in a fresh `mayapy`). Replaced by `_resolve_scope`, which maps the input to `[(mesh, faces or None), ...]`: an object entry packs whole, a component entry (faces, UVs, verts, edges — so a shell selection) is widened to the faces it touches and packs only that region, and an object entry *wins* over components of the same mesh so a leftover component selection can't silently narrow the pack. `_uv_arrays` takes the face scope and **compacts** its arrays to just the UVs those faces reference, returning the `uv_ids` map the write-back addresses them through — so the engine never sees, and no `polyEditUV` can reach, a UV outside the scope. Face indices are read via `MFnSingleIndexedComponent.getElements()` rather than by parsing `cmds.ls(flatten=True)` strings (a shell routinely covers tens of thousands of faces). The density pre-pass and the returned `PackUvsResult.targets` are scoped the same way, so `preserve_3d` neither measures nor rescales the untouched part of the map, and a caller's density readout reports what actually packed. `_restore` now reads whole-mesh positions + `getUvShellsIds` directly instead of going through the (now-compacted) `_uv_arrays` — untouched shells solve to the identity and are skipped, so it also stopped paying for a triangulation pass it never used. `test_uv_utils.py` +6 (`TestPackUvs`): face scope moves exactly the scoped UVs and parks nothing else, shell scope into UDIM 1002, object-wins-over-components (both input orders), mixed object+component in one engine call, component-pack undo, and instance collapsing — 4 verified failing against the pre-fix resolution (the others guard against over-narrowing and double-writing, which passed before).
  - **Instances collapse for free, and the triangulation loop got ~33% faster.** Two things probed rather than assumed while reviewing the above. (1) Components selected on two instances of one shape *must not* become two scope entries — they share a UV set, so the engine would reserve space for the same UVs twice and then write two transforms onto them. No dedupe code was needed: `_resolve_meshes`' `cmds.ls(..., dag=True, type="mesh")` reports an instanced shape by its FIRST path, so every sibling canonicalizes to the same transform however it was named (verified for component/component, component/object and object/object pairs; now pinned by test, because it is the *same* `cmds.ls` quirk `get_neighbor_shell_bounds` documents as a hazard, and a future change there would silently reintroduce the double write). (2) Scoping needed random access to a face's UV ids, which first arrived as numpy indexing — and numpy element access boxes every face-vertex as an `np.int64`. Timing the loop alone over a 102k-face plane: 198ms materializing `starts`/`uv_ids` as plain Python ints, 296ms through numpy arrays, 300ms for the sequential walk being replaced — so the scoping rewrite ends up faster than the code it replaced instead of 1% slower. Also confirmed by probe (both were candidate bugs, neither was): `getAssignedUVs` carries one count per polygon *including* unmapped ones, so the face-index offsets and the scope clamp stay aligned on a partially-mapped mesh; and non-mesh components (curve CVs) drop out of the batched face conversion rather than raising.
- **2026-07-29 — Shell Xform's `Distribute` left no gap between shells; it now uses the panel's existing tile spacing.** `tb006` handed `texDistributeShells` a hardcoded `0` for its `$spacing` argument, so a distributed row came out butted edge to edge — every shell touching its neighbour, which bleeds across its own seams at render time and cannot be packed without a repack pass. The gap is now `_border_margin()` (`ptk.MathUtils.uv_tile_margin`, 1/512 at every map size), the same clearance the move pad's shell snap (⌖) parks against and `Gather to Tile` insets by, so the panel speaks with one spacing value instead of three behaviours. Probed in a fresh `mayapy` against `texDistributeShells` directly (spacing 0 / margin / 0.25 / 0.5 → gaps 0.0 / 0.001953 / 0.25 / 0.5) before wiring it up. `test_shell_xform.py` +3 (`ShellXformDistributeTest`): U and V, verified failing against the pre-fix `0`, plus a third that overrides `_border_margin` — the first two still pass if the value is re-hardcoded to today's constant, so only that one proves the slot reads the panel's spacing (verified failing against a deliberate hardcode). **Not** mirrored to Blender: `btk.distribute_uv_shells` implements a different algorithm — it evenly spaces island *centers* between fixed endpoints rather than re-flowing them into a row — so it has no inter-shell gap to respect.
  - **Shell Xform's `Straighten` and `Distribute` were not undo-chunked.** Found while fixing the above: both drive MEL procs that walk the selection *one shell per command* (`texDistributeShells` moves each shell with its own `polyEditUV`; `texStraightenUVs` loops shells and edge loops, and `tb005` fires `texStraightenShell` on top), so one button press cost as many presses of Ctrl+Z to revert. Both now carry `@CoreUtils.undoable`, matching `tb008`/`_rotate_uvs` beside them and closing a silent parity gap — the Blender twin already wrapped both. The remaining slots need no chunk: `move_to_uv_space` is a single `polyEditUV`, and `mirror_uvs` / `gather_to_udim` are decorated in the engine.

- **2026-07-29 — `m_frame` gains a timed step cycle and clip fitting: `m_frame(steps=2, adjust_clipping=True)`; `CamUtils` gains `get_view_state` / `set_view_state` / `fit_camera_clipping`.** The old macro cycled three hard-coded fit factors per element type and ended by framing *wider* — there was no way back to the view you were working from, and the state lived in a MEL global. Now the first press frames at the ideal working distance (the per-element factors, kept as `FRAME_FIT_FACTORS`), each further press within `FRAME_STEP_TIMEOUT` (2 s) steps *into* the selection, and the press after the last step puts the camera back exactly where it started — snapshot on entry, restore on exit, via the new `CamUtils` view-state pair. Pausing collapses the cycle (`ptk.StepToggle`: a stale press just goes back), and re-selecting restarts it rather than stepping deeper into what you just left — after a pause that also re-homes the cycle (`StepToggle.began_cycle`), so "back" can't teleport the camera to a view from minutes ago. `steps=1` is a plain frame/restore toggle; more steps start a little further out (`StepToggle.scales`). `adjust_clipping` runs `fit_camera_clipping`, which fits the near/far planes to the framed selection's depth range plus a quarter-diagonal of slack to orbit in — **widening only**, so it is a no-op once nothing clips, and the original planes come back with the camera. Also fixed: with nothing selected the macro framed nothing at all — `@CoreUtils.selected` returns early on an empty selection, so the documented frame-everything branch was dead code (the macro now resolves the selection itself). `BoundingBox` gained a `corners` property (the 8 min/max combinations) — `adjust_camera_clipping` and the new fit both needed it and each had rebuilt it inline. `test_macros.py` +8, `test_cam_utils.py` +8, `test_core_utils.py` +3.

- **2026-07-29 — Shell Xform's snap toggle becomes a tri-state (off / grid / **shell**); `UvUtils.get_neighbor_shell_bounds` added.** "Move to the next shell" is a *snap mode*, not a fifth scope — it answers the same question the grid mode does (snap to what?) and composes with every scope — so the option-box button became a 3-state `ActionOption` cycle rather than the combo gaining an item. Icon **and** tint change per state (grey ▦ off / blue ▦ grid / amber ⌖ shell): two enabled modes are easy to confuse by colour alone, and the off state has to read as inert. Colours come from `ptk.Palette.status()` so they track the theme. In shell mode the arrows park the selection one border margin from the nearest neighbouring shell in that direction, skipping gaps too small to fit, and fall back to the grid rule whenever nothing lies ahead — the scope is ignored, since the neighbour sets the distance. New `UvUtils.get_neighbor_shell_bounds(objects)` supplies the pool: every non-intermediate mesh whose **current UV set name** matches the input's (a lightmap-set mesh is not a neighbour even where its UVs occupy the same numbers), minus the input's own shells. Shells are excluded by API shell id, not bounding box — stacked shells share a box and would exclude each other — and the exclusion is keyed on the shape's **MObject**, not its DAG path: an instanced shape has one path per instance while `cmds.ls` reports only the first, so a path key missed any selection made through a second instance and leaked the input's own shell back as its own neighbour (shell snap then parked a shell against itself, hopping it by its own height). Caught by probe, regression-tested. Persistence moved to a fresh `shell_xform_move_snap_mode` key; the old one held a bool that would restore as a state index. `test_shell_xform.py` +11 → 20 (tri-state ordering/distinctness, park, scope-independence, grid fallback, self-exclusion, out-of-lane, walk-past); `test_uv_utils.py` +5 (input exclusion both ways, alone, UV-set scoping, partial exclusion on a two-shell mesh, no-UV input). Blender twin at parity.

- **2026-07-29 — The export sidecar generalized into a scene-data record: `.{stem}.scene_data.json` = hierarchy diff baseline + `data_export` snapshot, one JSON per export.** The `.hierarchy.json` sidecar carried only the diff-check baseline, while the metadata shipping *inside* the FBX (`shot_metadata`, `fbx_takes`, the audio/lightmap/shadow/emissive channels) had no readable copy outside the binary. The manifest is now format v2 with two sections under one contract — an externalized copy of exactly what shipped: `hierarchy` (sorted namespace-stripped DAG paths + SHA-256; consumed solely by `check_hierarchy_vs_existing_fbx`, and the hash still covers only the paths, so metadata churn can never false-positive the hierarchy check) and `data_export` (`DataNodes.dump(decode=True)` of the carrier at export time — never `data_internal`, whose contract is scene-private state that must not export). `HierarchySidecar` → **`SceneDataSidecar`** (`scene_data_sidecar.py`, registered as `mtk.SceneDataSidecar`; the old module stays as an import shim for one release), with a new `read_data()` accessor and a single migration entry point `migrate_legacy()` promoting v1-named files — including orphaned `.prev` backups, so the corrupt-manifest fallback protection survives the rename — and per-version manifests of either naming (current naming wins a version tie). The write gate widened symmetrically (`write_scene_data_sidecar`, was `write_hierarchy_manifest`): previously the manifest was only maintained when the hierarchy check was in play; now any export whose carrier shipped content leaves the record — so GLB-only deliverables, where FBX user properties may not survive conversion, always keep a readable metadata copy. "Shipped" is literal: the snapshot is gated on the carrier actually being in the export set, so a scene whose hidden carrier was excluded (e.g. 'selected' mode with the `export_data_node` task off) records nothing rather than claiming channels the FBX never got. A data-only change still rolls the old manifest to `.prev`, so the previous export's full record stays diffable; v1 flat manifests remain readable through a section shim. `test_scene_data_sidecar.py` (was `test_hierarchy_sidecar.py`) 49/49 incl. v1-compat / migration / hash-isolation; `test_scene_exporter.py` 83/83 — its hand-written v1 manifests now exercise the migration organically.

- **2026-07-29 — Scene Exporter's "Missing Texture" check reported maps that never ship: the HDR skydome's, and the file nodes orphaned by its own duplicate-material cleanup.** Reported live: "I have exclude hdr checked and yet I still get `[ERROR] Missing Texture: university_workshop_8k_file -> …/hdr/machine_shop_02_8k.hdr`", followed by ~20 duplicate lines for `<map>`, `<map>1`, `<map>2`, `<map>3`. `check_valid_paths` was the last texture task still scanning `cmds.ls(type="file")` **scene-wide** — every sibling (`check_texture_file_size`, `resolve_invalid_texture_paths`, `check_absolute_paths`) had already moved to the export scope. So `exclude_hdr` correctly dropped the `aiSkyDomeLight` from `self.objects` and the check flagged its texture anyway, and `reassign_duplicate_materials` — which deletes the duplicate *shader* but leaves its upstream `file` nodes behind as orphans — manufactured a fresh batch of false positives on every run. The check now iterates `_get_export_file_nodes()`, the shared helper whose docstring already promised "exactly the textures that will ship". Entries are also grouped by path, so one map shared by several file nodes logs one line listing them rather than one line each. `test_scene_exporter.py` +6 — 3 of them verified failing against the pre-fix implementation.
  - **`MatUtils.resolve_path` gains `search=True`, and the two validity gates pass `search=False`.** Replacing the check's hand-rolled lookup with `resolve_path` fixed one bug and would have introduced another: it fixed `<UDIM>` paths being reported missing wholesale (the literal token was compared against disk), but `resolve_path` is a *repair* primitive — its last two fallbacks hunt for the texture under `sourceimages`, by relative path and then **by bare basename**. That is right for `resolve_invalid_texture_paths`, which writes the result back; it is wrong for a validity gate, where a node pointing at a stale directory would match a same-named file it does not reference, read as valid, and ship broken anyway. `search=False` keeps env-var expansion, `workspace(expandName=...)` and UDIM handling — precisely how Maya itself resolves the stored path — and drops the hunt. `check_valid_paths` and `check_texture_file_size` (which would otherwise size-probe the wrong file) both use it. `test_mat_utils_resolve_path.py` +2, asserting both branches in one test so the default's hunt is proven, not assumed.
  - **`_get_export_file_nodes()` is now cached** beside `_cached_materials`. It is a `listHistory` over every export material and has three callers per run (`resolve_invalid_texture_paths`, `check_valid_paths`, `check_texture_file_size`) — the third being new here. Both caches derive from the same source, so they are cleared together through a single `_invalidate_material_caches()`; a lone `_cached_materials = None` (what `reassign_duplicate_materials` used to do) would have left the file-node cache pinned to deleted materials. `test_scene_exporter` runtime 15.1s → 7.7s.

- **2026-07-29 — `aiMultiply` / `bump2d` showed up as *materials*, because reading a node's role off its raw classification string reads its *draw override* instead.** `ArnoldBridge._setup_nodes` created its two helpers with a hardcoded `cmds.shadingNode(..., asShader=True)`, and `-asShader` parks ANY node type in `defaultShaderList1` — exactly what `cmds.ls(materials=True)` reports. So the helpers surfaced in Hypershade's Materials tab and in every materials list built on that query (tentacle's materials combo among them). All three bridge nodes now go through `NodeUtils.create_render_node`, which derives the flag from the type's classification, instead of hardcoding it at the call site.
  - New **`NodeUtils.get_classification_tokens(node_type)`** is the single place that parses `cmds.getClassification`: it drops `drawdb/` (viewport draw override) and `swatch/` tokens, whose paths embed misleading substrings — Arnold registers nearly every `ai*` node as `drawdb/shader/surface/arnold/genericShader` regardless of what it does, so `aiBump2d` (role `utility/shader`) read as a surface shader. A type classified *only* by its draw override (`adskMaterial` is just `drawdb/shader/surface/adskMaterial`) falls back to that path with the prefix stripped, so it keeps a role instead of losing one.
  - `create_render_node` now matches those tokens rather than raw strings. Diffed old-vs-new across all 1036 classified node types in a live session: 88 types change, every one narrowing to the role the node itself declares (`aiBump2d`/`aiColorCorrect`/… asShader→asUtility, `lightInfo` asLight→asUtility, `place3dTexture` as2DTexture→asUtility), **zero** widening to `asShader`. None of the 5 types this package actually creates through it (`StingrayPBS`, `aiMultiply`, `bump2d`, `file`, `reverse`) are affected; `aiSkyDomeLight`'s category is corrected from `surfaceShader` to `defaultLight` on the non-fast path.
  - Belt-and-braces for scenes already polluted by the old bridge: **`get_scene_mats(exclude_utility_nodes=True)`** (new, default on) drops nodes claiming a `utility/` / `texture/` / `math/` role and no shader role — conservative on purpose, so an unclassified custom-plugin shader is still a material. The shader test runs first because real shaders can carry "utility" deeper in the path (`StingrayPBS` and `surfaceShader` are both `shader/surface/utility`, and a naive "contains utility" rule would delete them from the list).
  - **Texture Path Editor: "Exclude Arnold Nodes"** header toggle (off by default). An Arnold preview shader owns a *dedicated* file node per texture, so every bridged material contributed a duplicate row for the same image. This also surfaced a gap in `get_file_nodes` itself: it only read a shading group's `surfaceShader` / `volumeShader` / `displacementShader` slots, so a bridge shader — which drives `aiSurfaceShader` — was invisible, and its textures were reported with an **empty Shader column**, as unowned orphans. (The filter appeared to work anyway, because `create_render_node` hands the bridge shader its own member-less shading group; "Delete All Unused Materials" removes exactly those, and the feature silently stopped working — caught by a test that deletes it.) Shader discovery now also picks up any *other* shader-classified node feeding the same shading group, so renderer-specific slots work without naming each one. Backed by **`get_file_nodes(exc_classification=...)`**, which drops file nodes used *exclusively* by matching shaders — a texture shared with a non-Arnold shader is kept, and an orphan file node (no shader at all) is always kept, since surfacing those is the panel's job.
