The toggle also narrows the *all* scope, so Normalize / Set Directory / Resolve Missing never touch rows the panel is hiding, and a row is never labelled with a shader the user asked to hide. `test_mat_utils.py` +1, `test_texture_path_editor.py` +3, `test_arnold_bridge.py` +2.
  - Also new: **`get_scene_mats(exc_classification=...)`**, dropping materials by classification pattern — `"rendernode/arnold*"` hides Arnold shaders, `"rendernode/redshift*"` Redshift's. `get_connected_shaders`' private copy of the shader test now calls the shared predicate. `test_mat_utils.py` +2, `test_node_utils.py` +3, `test_arnold_bridge.py` +1.

- **2026-07-29 — `test_toolbag_helpers.py` was dead on arrival: 45 of its 54 tests errored on `AttributeError`, unnoticed.** Found while running the mock suite for the change below. The Toolbag helpers moved off the module root onto a `ToolbagHelpers` class (the workspace's no-root-level-functions rule) and the test's `import _toolbag_helpers as helpers` was never re-pointed — a module-level lookup imports fine and only fails at *attribute access*, so collection stayed green and nothing flagged it. `helpers` now binds to the class. `test/mock_tests` 208/208 (was 45 failed / 163 passed).
- **2026-07-29 — The "mayatk panels get a hide button" rule is now a *default* the UI Browser can override, not a hardcode that silently won.** `MayaUiHandler.apply_styles` overrode `style["header_buttons"]` to `("menu","collapse","hide")` for anything tagged `mayatk` — a fixed button tuple, which is a value a user preference has no way to outrank. That was one of the three reasons the UI Browser's window-persistence setting had no effect in tentacle (uitk's changelog has the full diagnosis). The override is replaced by **`default_persistence(ui)`**, uitk's new per-window default hook: mayatk-tagged UIs declare `PERSISTENCE_STICKY` and everything else (native-menu wrappers included) falls through to the base `PERSISTENCE_TRANSIENT` — byte-for-byte the same defaults as before, but now the bottom of a precedence chain the browser's global setting and its per-window "Open as" override sit above. Panels that opt into a pin button in their slot's `header_init` are unaffected: the handler leaves a self-configured header alone unless the user made an explicit choice, and then swaps only `pin`/`hide`, so a panel keeps its `refresh` button. `mock_tests/test_maya_ui_handler.py::DefaultPersistence` (+4, incl. a guard that the `apply_styles` override stays gone).
- **2026-07-29 — 25 panels declared fixed-vertical: they can no longer be stretched into dead space above the footer.** Follow-through from the tentacle sweep (live report: "still trouble with deadspace in the shell xform window… we only fixed the tentacle window files"). Every `.ui` was loaded and classified by whether its trailing spacer is the SOLE vertical absorber — i.e. nothing else can use extra height. For those 25 (`shell_xform`, `bevel`, `bridge`, `mirror`, `naming`, `snap`, `curtain`, the `duplicate_*` family, `hdr_manager`, `lightmap_baker`, `arnold_bridge`, `image_to_plane`, `render_opacity`, `image_tracer`, `shadow_rig`, `wheel_rig`, `curve_to_tube`, `color_id`, `exploded_view`, `audio_clips`, `shots`, `dynamic_pipe`, …) the spacer is now `sizeType=Fixed` and each group box declares `vsizetype="Fixed"`, so Qt's own `QLayout.maximumSize()` is finite and the window locks to its content instead of relying on uitk's container see-through. Seven of them also carried Qt Designer's untuned **20x40** spacer — pinned as `Fixed`, that would have become a permanent 40px band, so those hints are tuned to the 10px norm (guarded workspace-wide by `m3trik/scripts/check_ui_spacers.py`). `dynamic_pipe` needed the group declaration specifically because its `QGridLayout` is opaque to the content walk. Panels with genuinely growable content (`scene_exporter`, `reference_manager`, `smart_bake`, `game_shader`, `mat_updater`, `shader_templates`, `telescope_rig`, the bridges, …) are deliberately untouched and stay resizable. Verified per panel through the real loader: finite native cap, footer gap at the ~10px norm, and collapse/expand cycles round-tripping exactly.

- **2026-07-29 — Shell Xform gains `Gather to Tile`, and its snap now respects the tile border padding.** `UvUtils.gather_to_udim` moves shells sitting outside a target UDIM into it — each stray keeps its sub-tile position via a whole-tile offset (no repack, which is too expensive for a move pad), clamped inside the shared border margin. With no explicit UDIM the target is decided by majority vote over the shells' own tiles, so the bulk of a selection stays put and only the strays travel. The move pad's grid snap (▦) lands shells one margin *inside* each grid line rather than on it, so a snapped shell no longer bleeds across a tile seam; the margin is applied by snapping the unpadded anchor and adding it back, since padding the result instead stranded the reverse press on the margin it had just added and read as a dead arrow. Both routes share one rule via `ptk.MathUtils.uv_tile_margin`, which also replaces the open-coded half-gutter in `_uv_pack`; the target-tile vote lives in `ptk.MathUtils.majority_tile`. `get_uv_shell_sets` gains `whole_shells=False`: it groups only the *input's* faces by shell, so a shell-level op run on a face subset translated that fragment and **tore the shell in half** — gather opts into the expansion (matching the Blender twin's `_target_islands`, which was already whole-island), and the narrow default that face-level callers rely on is unchanged and now test-guarded. The expansion re-walks the mesh once, skipped when the input already covers it.
- **2026-07-29 — The Unity Bridge offers to install the optional `unitytk` rather than failing to import.** `_unity_bridge.py` imported `unitytk` at module scope, so a user without the optional package (`pip install mayatk[unity]`) hit an ImportError when the module was merely loaded — before the panel existed to explain it, and with no way to act on it. The import is now deferred behind `UnityBridge._deliverer_cls()`, and the panel's `make_bridge` calls `ensure_optional_package("unitytk", feature="Unity Bridge")` (see uitk's changelog): prompt, install, proceed — decline and the panel reports no engine instead of raising. `list_delivery_modes` became a classmethod so it resolves the deliverer the same lazy way. Install goes through `mayapy`, which shares Maya's site-packages, so it is importable in the running session. `test_unity_bridge.py` 12/12.

- **2026-07-28 — `test_reference_manager`'s fake Switchboard gained the `tooltip` namespace the tooltip migration made load-bearing (2 errors → 0).** `ReferenceManagerController._folder_structure_preview` moved off a direct `from uitk…tooltip_mixin import TooltipFormat` onto `self.sb.tooltip.placeholder_preview(...)` in the same pass that migrated 83 modules. Production is correct — the Switchboard really does own that namespace now — but this suite drives the controller through a hand-rolled `MockSB`, which had no `tooltip`, so both `TestFolderStructurePreview` cases raised `AttributeError` the moment the preview was built. The suite is `GUI_REQUIRED` (it native-crashes headless and defers to the GUI pass), so the breakage surfaced only on a full `--all` run rather than at import or collection. `MockSB` now carries the **real** `TooltipNamespace` rather than a stub — the tests assert on actually-rendered HTML (`&lt;scene name&gt;` escaping, the typo note, every placeholder's meaning), so a stub would have made those assertions pass while proving nothing — imported lazily so the module still imports without a Qt binding.

- **2026-07-28 — `pack_uvs`: the xatlas engine's own flags audited; "Rotate Shells: off" now actually holds.** No signature change — the fixes are upstream in `pythontk.UvPack` (see its changelog: hull-axis rotation leak, every `PackOptions` field pinned, a non-monotonic scale-search bug, brute-force made a competing candidate instead of an unconditional post-pass). Two consequences here. `rotate=False` previously still handed shells to the engine's arbitrary-angle hull-axis pre-rotation, so the option did not do what it said; it does now. And `rotate=True` may apply an arbitrary-angle turn where that packs tighter — already handled by the write-back, whose per-shell similarity solve has always been angle-general and reflection-aware. Verified in fresh headless Maya sessions across 3 scenes (cube with all edges cut, 4 hard-surface primitives, 4 organic primitives) × 4 coverage modes × brute on/off: **no failures and no regressions in any of the 24 combinations, gains to +8.3%.** The write-back itself was audited against the engine's own output while chasing a suspected fidelity loss and is faithful (delivered/engine UV area 0.9994–1.0000) — the loss was in the search, not the transfer. Docstring corrected: the gutter/cell description was accurate but `rotate`'s was not. `test_uv_utils.py` 83/83.
- **2026-07-28 — Smart Bake: no dead space between the collapsed output group and the footer.** Two causes, both fixed. The bulk (~145 px) was a uitk `MainWindow.adjust_height_by` defect that double-counted the collapse delta — see [uitk's CHANGELOG](../uitk/CHANGELOG.md); every panel with a `CollapsableGroup` + trailing spacer + footer was affected. The residual 5 px was local: `smart_bake.ui`'s `verticalSpacer` was Qt Designer's default `Expanding`, which `SizeGripMixin._layout_content_max` treats as the layout's surplus absorber and therefore reports the vertical axis as unbounded — so with the group collapsed the window max never locked to the content and the surplus left by the first expand (the `sizeHint` fallback overpredicts the group height while `_expanded_height` is still unknown) had nowhere to go but the gap. The spacer is now `Fixed` at height 0: the *expanded* axis is still unbounded (the `QTextBrowser` inside the group is genuinely growable, so the log area still stretches with the window), while the *collapsed* state is fully height-fixed, so the content-max sync snaps the window flush. Measured offscreen through the real Switchboard load: collapsed gap 147 px → 2 px (pure layout spacing), window height 431 → 286 with `maximumHeight` locked at 286 collapsed and free when expanded. `test/mock_tests/test_smart_bake_panel.py` 9/9.
- **2026-07-28 — Shell Xform's move pad gains a scope combobox + a snap toggle; `UvUtils.get_uv_bounds` added.** The four arrows were hardwired to ±1 tile, and the tooltips already promised a step that did not exist. `cmb_move_scope` (row 0 of `gridMove`, arrows moved to row 1) selects Tile / Half Tile / Quarter Tile / Selection Bounds — the last stepping by the selection's own UV bbox, so shells tile edge-to-edge. The combo ships **no static item list**: `cmb_move_scope_init` builds it from `_MOVE_SCOPES` with the step as each item's *data*, so a label and the distance it means cannot drift apart (the `.ui` and a lookup table would have been two sources for one list). Snap rides along as an option-box toggle (`grid` icon, persisted under `shell_xform_move_snap`) rather than a fifth combo item, because it composes with every scope; on, the arrows land the selection's lower-left corner on that scope's grid instead of offsetting relatively — the half-tile-to-half-tile case that prompted this. The toggle is cached on the slots at `*_init` so `_move` never reaches into option-box internals (and stays drivable from a Qt-free harness). New `UvUtils.get_uv_bounds(objects) -> (u_min, v_min, u_max, v_max)` backs both the size-derived scope and the snap anchor. **`move_to_uv_space` no longer filters the component conversion by `fromFace`** — that silently dropped a UV / edge / vertex selection, making the move a no-op for those and desyncing the caller's bounds from what actually moved. New `test_shell_xform.py` (10 slot-layer tests: scope steps, axis/sign via opposite-arrow cancellation, snap on/off, UV-component selection, empty-selection warning) — mayatk had no coverage of this panel's slots at all; `test_uv_utils.py` +6 (fractional step, UV-component input, bounds, bounds-track-a-move, **multi-object union**, non-poly input → None). Because the combo is now populated at init, that population is load-bearing — covered by new `test/shell_xform_ui_check.py`, a Qt-but-not-Maya check run under the workspace `.venv` (mayapy + offscreen Qt segfaults on a QMainWindow, so it cannot live in the standard runner; the non-`test_` name keeps it out of discovery). `get_uv_bounds` deliberately does **not** flatten its component conversion — `polyEvaluate` reads ranged components, and flattening would materialize one string per UV on every arrow press. Blender twin at parity.

- **2026-07-28 — `UvUtils.pack_uvs` placement redesigned onto fixed-page packing: coverage boxes now tile with square cells the engine fills edge-to-edge.** Rides `ptk.UvPack`'s new fixed-page mode (see pythontk's changelog for the mechanism and the aspect-waste measurement that motivated it). The coverage box is tiled with near-square cells along its long axis — Full/Quarter = one cell, the halves = two stacked — the engine packs one square page per cell at its real pixel size (`map_size × short-side`, so the gutter is exact pixels now, not approximate), and each island lands in its page's cell. Measured on the reported case (cube, all edges cut), fill of the allowed box before → after: **Full 0.50 → 0.68 (0.80 brute), Half-U 0.69 → 0.72, Half-V 0.25 → 0.72, Quarter 0.49 → 0.77** — u3dLayout's range on the same content is 0.55–0.96, so the two methods are now trade-offs rather than a working one and a broken one. Also measured while re-evaluating the whole parameter surface: u3dLayout's own weak case is the tall Half-U box (0.547), `-mutations` does **not** help it (0.549) but the rotation search does (0.709) — recorded in the Pack tool's Rotate Max tooltip. Fixed-page scale is texel-quantized, so per-shell relative scale can deviate ~0.5% (sub-pixel; the preserve-3D test tolerance now says so). `test_uv_utils.py` gains the fill-floor regression: all four coverage options must keep the cut-cube above 0.6 fill with zero rejections and zero spill.
- **2026-07-28 — Blendshape Animator's prefix field hid the very placeholder it was describing.** Its tooltip read "Name prefix for the new blendShape node (becomes `<prefix>_BS`)" — but Qt parses tooltips as rich text and `<prefix>` isn't a tag, so it was swallowed and the hint rendered "(becomes `_BS`)". Now `&amp;lt;prefix&amp;gt;` in the `.ui`, which reaches Qt as a literal `<prefix>`. Found by the new `m3trik/scripts/check_tooltips.py` gate (see m3trik's CHANGELOG), which now sweeps every tooltip in the workspace.
- **2026-07-28 — All 42 panels now reach the rich-text tooltip DSL through the Switchboard instead of importing a uitk mixin module by path.** `from uitk.widgets.mixins.tooltip_mixin import TooltipFormat` appeared in 42 hand-written modules here (and 41 in blendertk) — reaching past uitk's public surface into a mixin's file path, which is exactly the kind of coupling the `sb.QtCore` / `sb.QtWidgets` passthroughs exist to avoid. uitk now exposes the DSL as `Switchboard.tooltip` (see its CHANGELOG), so every call site is `self.sb.tooltip.fmt(...)` and the imports are gone — including the three `try: … except ImportError: TooltipFormat = None` guards (plus their `if TooltipFormat is not None:` branches in `arnold_bridge` and `lightmap_baker`), which existed to keep a headless import clean and are moot once nothing is imported. The rewrite was mechanical and verified rather than eyeballed: it only converted files where *every* usage sits in a class that demonstrably carries `self.sb`, and the result was re-checked by AST — 251 call sites across both packages, 0 outside a Switchboard-injected class, 884 consumer modules still parsing, no surviving `TooltipFormat` reference. **One site deliberately keeps the import:** `Preview._format_op_error` is a `@staticmethod` on a helper constructed with widgets and callables but no switchboard; it is already guarded and degrades to plain text, so routing it would mean contorting a pure formatter to reach an instance it doesn't have. Registries are unaffected (the name was never public API here).
- **2026-07-28 — Panel tooltip pass: Emissive Groups' 13 tooltips restructured through `self.sb.tooltip`, and the Mirror / Cut On Axis option text says what the values actually do.** The Emissive Groups panel explained itself in run-on sentences that front-loaded the qualifier ("Mask Texture only. Masks are chunky — 512 usually suffices…", "Vertex Color only. Bake even when…"), so the reader hit the gate before learning what the control was. All 13 are rebuilt with the uitk DSL — title = the control's label, body = what it does, the gate demoted to a `note` — using the new `self.sb.tooltip` Switchboard passthrough (see uitk's CHANGELOG), which also lets the module drop its `try: from uitk…tooltip_mixin import TooltipFormat / except ImportError: TooltipFormat = None` guard and the `if TooltipFormat is not None:` branch around the header help: the panel is a uitk slots class, so the guard could never fire. Content fixes along the way: the **Encoding** combo now states the 4-group cap — as a `note` on the combo, not on the Vertex Color bullet, because it comes from `RegionMaskManifest`'s RGBA `SLOT_CHANNELS` and therefore binds *both* encodings; **Remove Group** says the slot is *retired* in bold (the load-bearing contract), **Make Weights Keyable** splits the two DCCs' transports into a real section and names itself as the one authoring action that creates the export carrier, and **Remove Keyable Weights** replaces shouty `INCLUDING` with markup. `.ui` text: **Mirror**'s `chk007` Instance tooltip gains the disabled-Bounding-Box-(center)-pivot restriction it was missing (it was in the header help only) and names the bake path (Un-Instance, then freeze scale); **Cut On Axis**'s four weighted-spacing fields stop being placeholders, their text taken from a **measured** run of `ptk.ProgressionCurves` through `_cut_offsets` rather than inferred from the names — which caught the first draft stating Bias exactly backwards (`weighted` lerps toward `x**curve` as bias rises, so **above** 0.5 bunches cuts toward the *start*, below 0.5 toward the end) and mis-describing two curves (Ease In-Out bunches at *both* ends; Smooth Step behaves like an ease-out, not a symmetric S). Bias also notes it is inert while Curve is 1, since `base_curve` then equals `x`. **Discrepancy left standing, deliberately:** `toggle_weight_ui` disables the Curve field for `smooth_step` on the grounds that it "uses neither", but `ProgressionCurves.smooth_step` demonstrably consumes it (`smooth_x ** (weight_curve / 4)` — measured: curve 1 / 2 / 6 give visibly different spacing). The tooltip therefore says Curve is *disabled for* Linear and Smooth Step (true of the control) rather than *ignored by* them (false of the engine); enabling it is a UI-behavior change needing a live-GUI pass, and the deliberate `/4` softening suggests the exclusion may be curation rather than an oversight. Text and structure only — no widget names, defaults or logic touched; the twin blendertk files get the identical edit (parity sweep 0 deltas).
- **2026-07-28 — `UvUtils.pack_uvs` bug-fix pass: ordinary meshes were being rejected and then left dumped on top of the pack.** Reported as "xatlas leaves a lot of space between shells"; measured against `u3dLayout` on the same content, the engine was actually fine (91% of u3dLayout's coverage, 99% with brute force) — the space was ours. **Two defects, both mine.** (1) **The residual tolerance was tighter than the engine's own precision.** `6e-05` ("a quarter-pixel at 4K") vs. a measured noise floor of `2.1e-04` across cube/cylinder/sphere/torus/cone/pipe/prism/helix at padding 0-8, so common meshes — a plain sphere among them — failed their similarity check. Now `1e-03`, chosen from the measurement: rigid moves land under `2.1e-04`, a genuinely unreproducible one misses by `1e-01`+, three orders apart, so the threshold sits with 5x margin over noise and 100x under a real failure. (2) **A rejected mesh was not restored.** The Preserve-3D pre-pass rewrites UVs *before* packing, so "left untouched" was a lie — a rejected mesh kept its equalized-but-unpacked layout and sat on top of the packed ones (the reported symptom: total UV area 3.09 vs u3dLayout's 0.66, UVs spilling to -0.05..1.04). The pre-pass state is now snapshotted per mesh and any rejection is put back through the same per-shell similarity machinery in reverse (the density pass is itself a per-shell scale about the shell's bbox center, so the restore is exact — verified to 8e-9). **Root cause of the remaining rejections, now named honestly in the error text:** Maya UV shells are VERTEX-connected while xatlas charts are EDGE-connected, so a shell pinched to another at a single shared UV point is two charts to the engine; it packs them apart *without duplicating the shared vertex*, which no rigid per-shell move can reproduce. Those meshes are rejected, restored, and told to split/merge at the pinch or use the Standard method. Also verified and dismissed while hunting this: the engine's own chart decomposition never splits a shell (chart count matched Maya's shell count on every primitive tested, and feeding one xatlas mesh per shell changed nothing), and the fan triangulation is sound (zero triangles span a shell, `getAssignedUVs` alignment exact). `test_uv_utils.py` gains the two regressions: five common primitives all pack inside the tile with zero rejections, and a pinched mesh is rejected *and* restored while its healthy neighbor still packs. Two follow-ups from the critique pass: the snapshot reads UV positions through a new `_uv_positions` (which `_uv_arrays` now builds on, so there is still one reader) instead of the full extraction — the snapshot never needed the per-face triangulation loop, and skipping it is **24x cheaper** on a 15k-UV mesh (23.6ms → 1.0ms, values identical), paid once per mesh per pack; and a shell the engine placed only one UV of is now translated rather than silently skipped (the solve degenerates to a pure translation, which is exactly right for a lone point), with a snapshot/live UV-count mismatch during restore now reported instead of silently doing nothing.
- **2026-07-28 — `UvUtils.pack_uvs`: pack existing UV shells with the external xatlas engine (`ptk.UvPack`), fully undoable.** The pack-only counterpart of `auto_unwrap`, and an in-process round trip — no OBJ files: the current UV set goes out as plain arrays (`MFnMesh.getUVs`/`getAssignedUVs`, fan-triangulated per polygon over the assigned UV ids), xatlas packs it, and the result comes back as **per-shell similarity transforms applied with `cmds.polyEditUV`** rather than a UV-table rewrite — `MFnMesh.setUVs` would bypass undo, while the polyEditUV route makes the whole pack one undoable edit (probe-verified: `-angle` + pivot rotates, negative `-scaleU` mirrors, and undo restores byte-exact). Each shell's least-squares solve is **reflection-aware** — live packing caught xatlas *mirroring* a chart (det < 0, a perfect reflected similarity; the same liberty `u3dLayout` takes, and it can also 180°-turn shells even with `rotate=False`) — and every solve is validated against a quarter-pixel-at-4K residual tolerance *before* any shell of that mesh moves, so a mesh either packs whole or reports `(mesh, reason)` and stays put. Placement mirrors the Pack tool's native semantics: UDIM anchor + fractional coverage box + the same `calculate_uv_padding` gutter/margin rules, with the engine's content-sized atlas fitted uniformly (aspect-true) into the box. `preserve_3d=True` (default) equalizes per-shell texel density first via `set_texel_density` — the `-preScaleMode 1` equivalent, exact because xatlas preserves relative input scale (`False` = Preserve UV). Mesh resolution reuses `_AutoUnwrapInternal._resolve_meshes` (selection fallback, instance dedupe, input order). The engine check runs before the scene is touched; missing package → RuntimeError with the pip command. Live-verified end-to-end (placement exact, density ratios exact to 7 decimals, undo exact); `test_uv_utils.py` gains `TestPackUvs` (4 cases, real engine, skip-clean without it). **Also new: `UvUtils.udim_to_tile`** — delegates to the extracted `ptk.MathUtils.udim_to_tile` (the `1001 + u + 10*v` rule), replacing the inline tile math here and in the tentacle Pack slot, same delegation pattern as `calculate_uv_padding`.
- **2026-07-28 — `UvUtils.auto_unwrap`: external-engine auto-unwrapping (Ministry of Flat / BFF), and Cut Cylinder now picks its own seam algorithm.** `auto_unwrap(objects, method="hard"|"organic", …)` exports each mesh to OBJ, unwraps it with `ptk.UvUnwrap`, and transfers the UVs back — one undo chunk, one scratch namespace, each mesh's UVs snapshotted so a failure rolls that mesh back and the loop continues (`AutoUnwrapResult` reports `succeeded` / `failed` pairs; the executable is resolved *before* the scene is touched, so a missing engine can't leave a half-run). Because both engines return the input topology unchanged, the transfer is a component-space `transferAttributes` — exact, and no triangulation anywhere. Layout is per engine: Ministry of Flat packs its own islands but into a rectangle that overruns the tile, so only its scale is fitted (`_fit_uvs_to_tile`); BFF only flattens, so it gets the full `_pack_shells` pass, extracted from `unwrap_cylinder` (which now shares it rather than carrying a second copy). **New `detect_seam_algorithm`** replaces the interim UI algorithm picker: a closed genus-1 body (torus) and a mesh whose open ends don't encircle the fitted revolution axis (a bend) both need the topology engine; everything else takes the axis path. `SEAM_ALGORITHMS` gains `"auto"`, now the default for `cut_cylinder_seams` / `unwrap_cylinder`. **Bug fixed in `snapshot_uv_sets`** (pre-existing, and it silently affected the RizomUV bridge too): `polyUVSet -create` makes the new set *current*, so every UV edit performed after taking a snapshot landed in the backup and vanished when it was discarded — the original set is now restored as current, with a regression test. Verified against both real engines in Maya (topology preserved, UVs inside 0-1, no residue beyond ordinary construction history); test_uv_utils.py 70 green headless.

- **2026-07-28 — Scene Exporter: `Set Linear Unit` and `Set Workspace` actually reach the FBX now.** Both used the `set_`/`revert_` pair, which `TaskFactory` fires when `run_tasks` returns — *before* the export write, a limitation its own docstring spells out ("only mutations the export itself doesn't depend on may be reverted this way"). But Maya's FBX plugin stamps the file's unit from the working unit at write time (proven: the same cube exported under `cm` vs `m` yields different files) and resolves project-relative texture paths against the *active* workspace, so the revert landed first and both tasks were inert — the unit combo did nothing, and `Set Workspace` could hand the writer back the old project after `convert_to_relative_paths` had just made the texture paths relative to the new one. Both now stage their restore via `TaskFactory.stage_deferred_restore` — the counterpart primitive added to pythontk beside the revert machinery it corrects — which `perform_export`'s outer `finally` drains through `run_deferred_restores()` on every exit path; they return `None` so the too-early pairing stays disarmed, and `revert_linear_unit` / `revert_workspace` are removed. blendertk hit the identical defect (there it also hid a time-misaligned animation export), which is why the seam lives upstream rather than twice.
- **2026-07-28 — Runner: the GUI-pass wait is now progress-aware, so a slow box can no longer be denied a badge forever.** `wait_for_results` polled the results file for progress but enforced a flat wall clock, and the default was `max(600, 30 * modules)` — under the real cost of a GUI module (one took ~6 minutes). A full run was killed at **13/15 modules with four seconds to spare**; the two casualties then recorded as `TIMEOUT`/NOT RUN, which correctly blocks the badge update — so a machine that is merely slow could never publish a number at all. The loop now ends on a *stall* (no module finished in `idle_timeout`, default 600s) with `timeout` demoted to a hard ceiling (`max(1800, 90 * modules)`); the timeout message names which one fired. Net effect both ways: a healthy-but-slow pass runs to completion, and a genuinely hung one is detected in 10 minutes instead of waiting out the full ceiling.

- **2026-07-28 — Badge fix: skipped tests were counted as passed.** `_aggregate()` computed `"passed": tests - failures - errors`, folding every skip into the badge total — and mayatk skips a lot (the `GUI_REQUIRED` registry defers whole modules out of the headless mayapy pass, and `@skipIfBatch` gates more). The badge therefore advertised a number no other package's badge meant, and a module going fully skipped *raised* nothing but also cost nothing, so silent coverage loss was invisible. Now `tests - failures - errors - skipped`, matching every sibling and the standard: [m3trik/docs/TEST_BADGE_STANDARD.md](../m3trik/docs/TEST_BADGE_STANDARD.md). `update_readme_badge` also drops its hand-rolled shields/regex/anchor block for `ptk.StatusBadge`, and the never-called `parse_test_results` (which had the same skip bug) is deleted. Expect the next full run's badge to land below the previous 4069 by exactly the skip count — that drop is the correction, not a regression.

- **2026-07-28 (keyable weights) — Emissive Groups: group weights are now optionally keyable in-DCC and the keys ride the FBX to Unity.** New `EmissiveGroups.make_weights_keyable(names=None)` adds one keyable 0-1 float per group on the `data_export` carrier (`emissiveGroup_<name>`, seeded from the group default) — the RenderOpacity attribute-mode idiom, but model-global, and it is the ONE authoring action allowed to *create* the carrier (keyable is inherently export-facing; plain authoring still leaves clean scenes untouched, test-pinned). `key_weight(name, value=None, frame=None)` keys the plug (None = current value / time; auto-makes the group keyable); `remove_keyable_weights()` strips attrs **and animation** while leaving groups intact; `remove_group` deletes its attr too; `set_default` follows through to an *un-keyed* attr only (once keyed, animation owns the value). The registry records each attr in the manifest (`ptk.RegionGroup.attr`) so Unity's importer knows what to rebind — and unlike RenderOpacity's shared `opacity` name, every group attr is unique, so Unity's root-flattened custom-property import stays unambiguous and needs **no visibility dual-key workaround**. Panel: table context menu gains a Keyable section (Make Weights Keyable / Key Weights @ Current Frame / Remove Keyable Weights; slots stay spliced-identical with blendertk, parity 0 deltas). `validate()` also gains an orphan-carrier-attr warning — an FBX *reimport* restores the keyable attrs but not the registry (`data_internal` never rides the FBX), the attr-side twin of the existing orphan-set warning; on relink, `make_weights_keyable` re-marks a reimported (non-keyable) attr keyable at the *plug* level, and the default-follows sync skips a plug with ANY incoming connection (`connectionInfo isDestination` — an expression-driven weight previously made `set_default` raise on the connected setAttr). Verified: `test_emissive_groups.py` +11 incl. a real FBX export→import proving the keyed curves and the manifest `attr` record survive (the exact transport the Unity rebinding rests on). Docs: `docs/emissive_groups.md` keyable-weights section. **Release note: publish pythontk first** (`RegionGroup.attr` / `set_attr`).
- **2026-07-28 — `PlayblastExporter.encode_sequence` warns when the muxed audio track is effectively silent, instead of shipping a silent movie without comment.** Live verification against a production training scene (C130H FCR speedrun) proved the capture/encode/mux pipeline artifact-free — 2,700 frames across four ranges, mp4 frame-exact vs the PNG capture, audio offset sample-accurate (measured 866.6666 s vs 866.6667 s expected) — but also surfaced a faithful-yet-surprising outcome: `resolve_sound_node` returns the timeline's ACTIVE sound node (Maya-native semantics), and in scenes carrying per-module composite tracks an export range outside that node's audible span produces a valid mp4 whose AAC track is pure digital silence (`apad` fills it). The encode now probes the finished file with ffmpeg `volumedetect` (new private `_audio_peak_db`; threshold `_SILENT_PEAK_DB` −60 dB — calibrated: digital silence measures −91.0 dB, real content −7.5 dB) and logs a warning naming the muxed source so the user checks the timeline's active sound node rather than discovering the silence downstream. Warning-only by design: a silent-but-present track can be temporally correct, so behavior is never altered. Tests in `test_playblast_exporter.py`: `_audio_peak_db` discriminates generated silent/loud WAVs (and returns None for unmeasurable input), and the encode path emits the warning via a patched probe.
- **2026-07-28 (same-day follow-up) — Emissive Groups: authoring no longer creates the export carrier, the panel stopped clobbering uitk's own table wiring, the help tooltip renders, and the slot bookkeeping moved upstream.** Four fixes from a critique pass plus live-Blender reports. **(1) Scene hygiene:** `add_group` / `set_default` / `remove_group` called `refresh_export_metadata`, which *creates* the `data_export` node — so merely authoring groups stamped an export carrier into a scene that had never been baked or exported. New `_refresh_export_if_published` only refreshes a manifest that already exists (the export preparer regenerates it at export time anyway), keeping the carrier out of clean scenes while never shipping stale data; +2 tests pin both halves. **(2) The panel's `_wire_table_signals` used a blanket `signal.disconnect()`** — which does not merely warn when nothing is attached (`libpyside: Failed to disconnect (None) from signal "cellScrubStarted(int,int)"`, reported live), it also **tears out the widget's own internal connections**: uitk's `TableWidget` wires `cellChanged` to its `_on_cell_edited` in its constructor, so every panel re-init silently disabled that. The previous binding is now tracked on the widget and disconnected *by reference*, which also keeps the receiving slots instance alive while the widget points at it. **(3) The header help tooltip passed `sections=[("Notes", "<one long string>")]`** — `TooltipFormat.fmt` renders `sections` as `(title, [items])` and iterates the second element, so a string became one `<li>` **per character**. Caveats moved to `notes=` (what it exists for) and the section now carries a real list contrasting the two encodings. **(4)** Slot/retirement/manifest bookkeeping now delegates to the new `ptk.RegionGroupRegistry` (see pythontk's changelog) instead of a private copy; behavior and the registry schema are unchanged. Verified: engine 26 headless + panel 20 in a launched GUI Maya. One panel test also had to stop asserting the option box's factory spinbox values — uitk persists widget state to QSettings, so those depend on run history; it now pins types and ranges (and that `cmb000` is a real `QComboBox`, the earlier `menu.add("ComboBox", …)`-yields-a-QLabel bug).
- **2026-07-28 — New Emissive Groups tool (`mat_utils/emissive_groups.py` + panel): author named face groups whose emissive regions Unity toggles/dims independently off ONE all-on emissive map.** `EmissiveGroups` (classmethod engine; state lives in the scene) + `EmissiveGroupsSlots`/`emissive_groups.ui` (auto-discovered). Scene-data contract per the data-nodes conventions, nothing created until used: membership = bare per-group `objectSet`s (`emissiveGroup_<name>`); slots/defaults/encoding = one `emissive_groups` JSON channel on `data_internal` (cleared when the last group + retired slot are gone); the export manifest is regenerated onto `data_export.emissive_groups` before every FBX export via a new `_KNOWN_PRODUCERS` entry (`refresh_export_metadata`, the ShadowRig pattern) and rides the FBX as a user property for unitytk's importer. Slot stability is a contract: slots are assigned once and retire on group removal — never auto-reused (engine scenes key against them); `compact_slots()` is the explicit binding-breaking reclaim. Two bake encodings (shared model = pythontk's new region-mask engine): `bake_vertex_colors()` writes the `emissiveGroups` RGBA color set per-face-vertex (hard group boundaries; whole-mesh zeroed first so re-bakes can't leave stale membership; refuses foreign color sets without `force` — Unity imports a single color stream) and `bake_mask()` harvests each group's UV triangles (OpenMaya, per-face triangulation) into a `<scene>_EMask.png` + manifest sidecar via `ptk.RegionMaskPacker`. `validate()` flags empty groups, overlaps, orphan sets, and foreign color sets. Verified: `test_emissive_groups.py` (new, 24) green in fresh mayapy; `check_cmds_syntax` PASS. Its `TestFbxRoundTrip` pins the two assumptions the whole Unity hand-off rests on and that no Maya-side state can prove — a real export→import shows the color set surviving with exact per-channel values, and the manifest surviving as a `data_export` user property (found via probe first, then promoted into the suite rather than left as scratch). Cross-package SSoT doc: [`docs/emissive_groups.md`](docs/emissive_groups.md) (wired into the docs hub). **Release note: publish pythontk first.**
- **2026-07-28 — Emissive Groups panel built to house style, plus its own GUI-pass test module; exposed in tentacle (both DCCs).** The panel is uitk-native rather than stock-Qt: a `TableWidget` (Group / Slot / Weight / Faces) whose **Weight** column is scrub- and single-click-editable — MMB-drag adjusts the weight like a Maya channel-box field via the widget's `cellScrub*` signals (150 px per 0-1 sweep, clamped), typing works the same cell — with a context menu (Select Members / Remove Group / All On / All Off); `LineEdit` + `PushButton` add row; `Remove / Select / Validate`; and ONE **Bake** button (`tb000`) whose option box carries the encoding combo, mask resolution, padding, and the force-over-foreign-color-set toggle — replacing the earlier two-bake-buttons-plus-groupbox draft. Table setup follows the established `tbl000_init` contract (one-time construction behind `is_initialized`, signals re-wired unconditionally because the QWidget outlives the slots instance, `refresh_on_show`); help text via `TooltipFormat.fmt` on the header. New `test_emissive_groups_panel.py` (19 tests) covers what the engine tests structurally can't — the `.ui` parsing and its custom widgets resolving, the option box building, table config (columns / scrub / single-click-edit columns), every menu entry having a same-named slot method, both bake paths through the option box, weight edit + scrub + clamp + unparseable-revert, and that a fresh slots instance re-binds the persisted table. It is **GUI-only** and registered in the runner's `GUI_REQUIRED`: loading the panel's uitk `TableWidget` hard-crashes mayapy in batch (exit 9 / 0xC0000409, the same native crash that gates `test_sequencer`) — confirmed by probe before gating. Tentacle exposure: `("Emissive Groups", "b027", …)` under `_TOOLS_ITEMS["Materials (scene)"]` + a `b027` launcher in **both** `slots/maya/materials.py` and `slots/blender/materials.py`; no tentacle `.ui` edit is needed (the Tools list renders from that table). Parity sweep: emissive_groups 0 deltas, materials.py unchanged at 3 triaged.
- **2026-07-27 — `NodeUtils.replace_with_instances` preserves each target's apparent rotation (live report: "instances don't keep their original rotation, even with Center Pivot checked"), and Center Pivot / Delete History actually run when Freeze Translate is off.** Two defects. (1) Placement relied solely on `matchTransform`, which copies the target's transform *channels* — a target whose rotation was ever frozen (orientation baked into the mesh, rotate channels zeroed) came back at the SOURCE's orientation (repro'd in mayapy: a 4×2×1 cube target rotated 90° and frozen returned as 4×2×1 instead of 2×4×1). Each target is now compared against the source with AutoInstancer's `GeometryMatcher`; identical meshes are placed by geometric registration (`rel_mtx * target_world`, the exact convention `_replace_member_with_instance` uses), which lands the shared shape on the target's world geometry regardless of what its channels say. Non-identical targets keep the `matchTransform` fallback, where the `retain_bbox_*` options still apply (an exact registration already reproduces the size, so it skips them). On the registration path the instance's pivot is centered when `center_pivot` is set, else copied from the target's world pivots. (2) The pre-pass called `freeze_transforms(translate=freeze_transforms, ...)` — when the Freeze Translate checkbox was off, `translate=False` still counted as an explicit channel flag with nothing to freeze, so the helper returned before its centering/history steps and **Center Pivot / Delete History were silent no-ops** unless Freeze Translate was also on. The no-freeze case now centers pivots and deletes history directly. Critique pass added a third fix: **the replacement now takes over the target's exact name** — the rename ran while the target still lived as a sibling, so Maya auto-suffixed (`my_wall` came back as `my_wall1`); the delete now precedes the rename. Probe-verified edge cases pinned by tests: a mirrored (negative-scale) identical target reproduces its exact world matrix through the registration path (negative determinant included — though which *channel* carries the mirror follows the decomposition, not the target's own channels), and a frozen-rotation target under a rotated+translated parent lands exactly (world-space set composes with the reparent). Six failing-first/pinning tests in `test_node_utils.py` (frozen-rotation preservation incl. world center + still-an-instance, rotated-parent composition, mirrored world-matrix, name retention, center-pivot-without-freeze, target-pivot retention with center off) — 46/46 headless. Blender's mirror swaps `obj.data` on the target in place (channels and name survive by construction), so no blendertk change.
- **2026-07-27 — `DisplayMacros.m_cycle_display_state` (F2) no longer dies on a group whose children aren't all surfaces.** Live report: cycling display on a selected group raised `RuntimeError: No surfaces selected` from the very first call. The macro expands a group to its leaf children (`NodeUtils.get_unique_children`) and then probed the cycle position with `cmds.displaySurface(sel[0], q=True, xRay=True)` — but probed in mayapy, `displaySurface` accepts *only* nodes with a mesh/nurbsSurface/subdiv shape below them and raises on anything else, so a single locator / joint / curve / camera among the children aborted the macro before it touched a thing. Worse, `get_unique_children` returned `list(set(...))`, so **which** child got probed was non-deterministic — the same group could work one press and fail the next. Three fixes: (1) the children are split into surfaces and non-surfaces, the state is probed from a *surface* when the selection has one (a non-surface can't report x-ray, so probing it stranded the cycle on the visible/templated legs), and the x-ray leg applies to the surfaces only while template/visibility still reach every leaf; (2) a selection with nothing to x-ray at all (a group of locators) skips that leg — Visible → Templated → Hidden → Visible — instead of appearing dead under the key; (3) `setAttr` on `.template`/`.visibility` goes through `Attributes.set_plug`, so one keyed or constrained child can't abort the cycle for every sibling after it (`RuntimeError: … is locked or connected`) — which needed a fix in `set_plug` itself: it guarded `objExists` and *locks* but not **connections**, so a keyed/constrained plug still raised straight through it. It now skips a non-settable plug in both modes; `force` deliberately doesn't help, because unlike a lock, clearing a connection is destructive and a caller asking to set a value hasn't asked to break a rig (the same reasoning behind `fix_non_orthogonal_axes`' opt-in `break_connections`). `NodeUtils.get_unique_children` gets two fixes of its own: it de-dupes through a dict rather than a set, so the de-duplication **no longer scrambles the order** `ls`/`listRelatives` handed back (hierarchy order, for a group) — callers keying decisions off the first element (this macro; `CoreUtils.build_mesh_similarity_mapping`'s tie-breaks) get a stable answer instead of a per-run coin flip — and it resolves **components to their owning node**, stripping the suffix *before* the `ls` so a dense `f[0:9599]` selection no longer flattens into 9600 names just to collapse back to one, since "the children of a face" is meaningless and a live face selection previously came back as `|pCube1.f[0]`, a name no node-level command accepts (pressing the same display/smooth-preview keys in component mode died on `ValueError: No object matches name: |pCube1.f[0].visibility`; `m_smooth_preview` shared that break). The surface-type tuple the macro filters on turned out to be inlined **six** separate times across mayatk (`_display_utils`' `_RENDERABLE`, `_edit_utils`, `mat_utils` ×2, `task_manager`'s `geometry_types`, and the new site) — all the same set for the same reason; it now has one home, `NodeUtils.SURFACE_TYPES`, and every site references it. Six failing-first tests in `test_macros.py` (mixed group doesn't raise, full 4-step cycle returns to visible, non-surface children still hide, all-non-surface group still cycles, component selection cycles its object, locked-visibility child doesn't abort siblings) + three in `test_node_utils.py` (order preservation, component resolution, `set_plug` vs. a connected plug) — full suite green.
- **2026-07-27 — The non-orthogonal fix is now connection-preserving: it freezes ONLY rotate/scale (translate connections are never at risk), and driven rotate/scale channels are skipped by default instead of silently disconnected.** Follow-up to the entry below, from the live question "can we fix without breaking connections, or restore them after?" — probed in mayapy, the answers are structural: (1) **translation never contributes to axis skew**, so the old `t=1` in the freeze was pure blast radius — dropping it means point constraints and position anim curves (the most common connection kind) survive completely untouched, verified: fix runs with a connected `.translate`, connection intact, skew cleared, geometry unmoved. (2) **Restore-after-freeze is provably impossible for rotate/scale drivers**: reconnecting re-drives the channel the freeze just zeroed — measured 0.79 units of double-transform WITH the skew back — and under a varying driver the skew is time-varying, so no static bake exists; instead of a lying "restore", `fix_non_orthogonal_axes` now pre-scans rotate/scale/shear for incoming connections (`get_non_orthogonal(detailed=True)` reports them in a new `"driven"` key), **skips driven objects with a warning naming the driver plugs**, and only disconnects under the new explicit `break_connections=True`. (3) **The freeze/unfreeze contract survives the fix**: when an object carries stored bake history (`store_transforms`), the fix composes its freeze into it first, so a later `restore_transforms` returns the full expected values (pinned: panel-freeze 25° + fix-freeze 10° → restore = 35°). The store/restore system itself was audited clean — it deals in values, never connections, and never ran in the fix path. VDATS re-verified: 20/20 fixed (0 driven), drift 2.4e-7. +4 tests in `test_diagnostics.py` (translate-connection survival, driven skip + detailed reporting, break_connections opt-in, store/restore composition) — 35/35 headless.
- **2026-07-27 — `freeze_transforms`' disconnect/delete strategies now actually find per-axis blockers.** Pre-existing bug surfaced by the probe above: the internal blocker scan queried only the compound plugs (`node.rotate`), but a compound `listConnections` does NOT see child-plug connections — and anim curves and constraints connect per-axis (`ctrl.rotateZ -> node.rotateZ`), so for the most common connection form `connection_strategy="disconnect"` found no blockers and silently skipped the node instead of disconnecting and freezing. The scan now queries the compound AND its children (both directions of the asymmetry are probe-pinned: a compound-level connection is equally invisible to a child query). Failing-first test in `test_xform_utils.py` — 67/67 headless.
- **2026-07-27 — `TransformDiagnostics` detects the shear FBX actually complains about: new `get_non_orthogonal` measures the *world* matrix, and `fix_non_orthogonal_axes` runs on it.** The FBX plug-in's "Non-orthogonal matrix support" warning fires on the evaluated (composite) matrix, but `get_sheared` reads only the transform's own `shear` attribute — so the most common production case, an object under a non-uniformly scaled **and** rotated ancestor, read `[0,0,0]` local shear while its world axes were badly skewed (repro: 0.78 axis-pair cosine on a clean-local child) and the fix pass never touched it. `get_non_orthogonal(objects, tolerance, detailed)` measures the max axis-pair cosine of the world matrix (non-uniform scale alone stays orthogonal — no false positives) and with `detailed=True` returns `{node: {"skew", "shear", "cause": "shear"|"inherited"}}`; `get_sheared` remains as the local-only primitive, and now reads `getAttr .shear` instead of `xform -q -shear` (which printed a "Cannot query absolute shear" warning per node — unusable noise on a scene-wide scan). `fix_non_orthogonal_axes` detects via the new check, sorts offenders **top-down** and re-checks before each freeze (freezing an ancestor commonly clears its descendants — they're skipped, not frozen twice), and skips referenced nodes with a warning instead of failing mid-pass. Verified against a live production scene (VDATS_module.ma, the scene behind the screenshot'd FBX warning "(20)"): all 20 offenders found (old check also found these — all were local-shear — plus the constructed inherited cases it misses), fixed with zero remaining and max world-space vertex drift 2.9e-7; the FBX round-trip that previously corrupted geometry by up to **0.031 units** re-imports at **0.000000** drift after the fix. +6 tests in `test_diagnostics.py` (inherited detection incl. the get_sheared blind spot, cause/skew reporting, no false positives on clean hierarchies, geometry-preservation, top-down single-freeze chains, dry-run) — 31/31 headless. The skew measurement itself lives in the new `ptk.MathUtils.max_axis_skew` (extracted so blendertk's mirror shares it instead of copying it — see pythontk's changelog); `_matrix_skew` is a thin delegate feeding the world matrix's axis rows in. Exposed on the tentacle scene header (both DCCs) — see tentacle's changelog. **Release note: publish pythontk first.**
- **2026-07-27 — RizomUV bridge: Edge Spacing / Tile Margin are gone as knobs — the pack gutter is now DERIVED from the same padding rule the Maya pack operation uses.** The panel exposed two hand-dialed spinboxes (`PACK_SPACING` 0.004, `PACK_MARGIN` 0.002) that had no relationship to `UvUtils.calculate_uv_padding`, the rule `u3dLayout` packing feeds from (`shellSpacing` / `tileMargin` in the Pack UVs slot and `unwrap_cylinder`). A Rizom round-trip and an in-Maya repack therefore landed on different gutters, and re-packing a Rizom result silently reflowed the layout. Both tokens now come off `Parameters.derived_values()` — island spacing = the normalized padding (1/256 of the tile), tile margin = half of it, exactly the pack op's ratio — and are folded in **after** the user values in `render_context`, so a stale `PACK_SPACING` left in a saved JSON preset can't win. The value is map-size-invariant by construction (`(size/256)/size`), so it is stable whatever Resolution is set to: 4 px at 1024, 16 px at 4096, always the same fraction of the tile. `ZomPack`'s `MarginSize` / `SpacingSize` / `PaddingSize` (and the ≤2021 vs ≥2022 field-name split) are untouched — only where their numbers come from changed. `UvUtils.calculate_uv_padding` now delegates to the new `ptk.MathUtils.calculate_uv_padding` (the formula moved to the bottom of the stack so blendertk's mirror shares it instead of copying it — see pythontk's changelog); the Maya-side signature, name and results are unchanged. Tests in `test_uv_rizom_bridge.py`: the keys are absent from `PARAMS`/`defaults()` yet still substituted in the constructed script, the derivation matches `calculate_uv_padding` at two map sizes, and a stale preset value loses (27/27, plus `test_uv_utils` 45/45 over the delegation). **Release note: publish pythontk first.**
- **2026-07-27 — `EditUtils.detach_components` works across multiple objects (live report: "RuntimeError: Doesn't work with multiple objects selected"), and its vertex path stops ignoring its argument.** (1) `polyChipOff` refuses a component list spanning more than one object, so Polygons ▸ Detach died outright on a multi-object face selection. Faces are now grouped per object (`Components.map_components_to_objects` — the same fix `UvUtils.cut_uv_edges` needed for `polyMapCut`) and chipped object-by-object; `polySeparate`, which refuses multi-object input for the same reason, likewise runs per source object. A source that can't be separated (e.g. every one of its faces chipped off, leaving one shell) warns and is skipped instead of aborting the whole call. (2) **Selection after the fact is now the detached piece(s), reliably.** The old code selected `result_objects[-1]`, but `polySeparate`'s output order is *not* stable — probed in Maya 2025: the extraction comes **first** when chipped in place and **last** when duplicated, so the previous behaviour happened to be right only for `duplicate=True` and handed back the leftover body otherwise. The body is now identified by face count (captured before the chip-off: the source's count, less the chipped faces when not duplicating) and everything else is selected; when the count can't single out a body — exactly half the faces chipped, or a source that already had loose shells — no guess is made and every piece stays selected. (3) The vertex branch ran `mel.eval("polySplitVertex")`, which reads `cmds.ls(sl=True)` and therefore **silently ignored an explicit `components` argument**, splitting whatever the user happened to have selected; it now calls `cmds.polySplitVertex` on the components it was given. Three failing-first tests in `test_edit_utils.py` (multi-object separate incl. the resulting selection, multi-object in-place, vertex-path argument) — 55/55 green.
- **2026-07-27 — `NodeUtils.uninstance(objects, freeze=True)` folds the transform bake in as an optional second step; `freeze_transforms` drops the `uninstance` param it briefly had.** Breaking the link and baking the transform are two halves of one job — un-instancing alone leaves the object's *transform* untouched, so a mirrored instance still carries the negative scale that exporters object to, and the bake can only run once the shape is unique. One call now does both, and there is exactly one way to spell it. `freeze_transforms` keeps its long-standing skip of instanced objects and points at `uninstance(freeze=True)` in the message. Note the bake applies to every object *passed*, not just those that needed forking — an already-unique object still gets baked, which is what the caller asked for (pinned by a test in both engines).
- **2026-07-27 — New `Preview` hook `prepare_operation(objects)`: a one-shot precondition that runs at `enable`, OUTSIDE any contract.** Some operations need scene state in place that must not participate in rollback. Mirror's case: forking an instanced shape inside `perform_operation` is unreversible (rollback deletes the forked shape as a *created* node, and the preserved-original restore only fires for originals that were **deleted** — the transform survives, so it is left empty). Running it once at enable keeps every later refresh/rollback cycle clean, so instanced input is handled automatically instead of being refused. A failing hook aborts the enable — if the precondition can't be met, previewing isn't safe. Documented in the module's contract list next to the "don't delete pre-existing nodes" rule it exists to satisfy. Tests in `test_preview.py`: runs once (not per refresh), the full instanced-mirror round-trip (both objects intact after cancel — pre-hook this emptied them), and the abort-on-failure path.
- **2026-07-27 — DATA LOSS fix: previewing a mirror on an instanced object left BOTH objects shapeless on cancel.** `EditUtils.mirror` always breaks the instance link, but doing that inside `perform_operation` violates Preview's documented contract ("do not delete pre-existing nodes -- deletions cannot be reversed"): the fork replaces the transform's pre-existing shape, rollback deletes that forked shape as a *created* node, and the preserved-original restore never fires because it only triggers for originals that were **deleted** — the transform survives, so the object is simply emptied. Verified in mayapy against a non-instanced control that round-trips cleanly (6 → 12 → 6 faces). The Mirror panel now breaks the links in a `prepare_operation` hook (above) — once, at enable, outside the contract — so instanced input just works and cancel restores both objects intact. Instance *output* (`chk007`) is exempt: it only ever adds an instance, never forks one. The engine keeps its unconditional fork for direct API callers, where no rollback is involved.

- **2026-07-27 — BREAKING: `EditUtils.mirror` drops the `uninstance` parameter — mirroring now ALWAYS breaks the instance link first, and the panel's Un-Instance checkbox (`chk005`) is gone.** There is no safe way to mirror a shared shape: separate mode fed `polySeparate`, which consumes the source transform and took every sibling instance with it (measured: both objects deleted); the merge modes survived but silently rewrote every other instance's geometry, which nothing in the UI could make obvious. Rather than expose a switch between "destroys your data" and "surprises you", the engine handles it under the hood. Migration: drop the argument — `mirror(objs, uninstance=True)` → `mirror(objs)`; there is no longer a way to request the shared-shape edit (mirror the shape they all share if you want that). The panel's symmetrize path (bounding-box center → `cut_along_axis`) breaks the link unconditionally too. Tests updated to the new contract: a merge-mode mirror leaves sibling instances untouched and leaves each transform owning its own shape.

- **2026-07-27 — DATA LOSS fix: `EditUtils.mirror` in separate mode (`mergeMode=-1`) destroyed an instanced source AND every sibling instance.** Separate mode routes through `polySeparate`, which consumes the source transform; on a *shared* shape that took all of its parents with it. Repro'd in mayapy: mirroring one of two instances left **neither** object in the scene (`objExists` False for both) — the whole selection silently vanished, and `uninstance=False` (the panel default) is exactly the path that hit it. The separate path now always forks a unique shape first, whatever the caller passed: there is no way to honor "keep the instance link" there that doesn't destroy data. The merge modes are deliberately left alone — they edit the shared shape in place so every instance picks up the mirror together, which is the point of instancing, not damage (measured: source and sibling both 6 → 12 faces). `uninstance` therefore stays a meaningful option *for the merge modes*, and the tooltip/help now say so. Regression tests in `test_edit_utils.py` cover both halves of that split.

- **2026-07-27 — `XformUtils.freeze_transforms` gains `uninstance=False`: the generic "bake this transform into the geometry even though the shape is shared" switch.** Freeze has always *skipped* instanced objects (baking into a shared shape would rewrite every sibling's geometry — a deliberate guard, verified: source untouched, "0 frozen, 1 skipped"). That left no supported way to bake an instanced object at all, so each tool that needed one would have had to hand-roll uninstance-then-freeze. `uninstance=True` forks the shape first and then bakes, which is the engine-safe finish for anything carrying transform state on a shared shape — a mirrored instance from the new `EditUtils.mirror_instance` becomes independent geometry with a **positive** scale and correct outward normals (Maya's freeze reverses the winding itself; no `polyNormal` conform needed). One parameter on the canonical helper rather than a bespoke "bake mirror" op, so every future caller with the same need inherits it. Tests in `test_xform_utils.py`: the default skip leaves BOTH halves untouched; `uninstance=True` bakes the scale away, makes the shape single-user, and moves neither half.

- **2026-07-27 — Mirror gains an **Instance** output (`chk007`): the mirrored half is a linked copy sharing the source's shape, not new geometry.** Until now the panel only ever built geometry (`polyMirrorFace`), and its one instance-related control — *Un-Instance* — went the other way, breaking links before mirroring. New `EditUtils.mirror_instance(objects, axis, pivot, use_object_axes)` instances each object and multiplies the copy's world matrix by the plane's reflection, so the mirror lives entirely in the transform (negative determinant) and the halves stay live — edit either, both follow. Verified in mayapy: the shape really is shared (2 parents), the copy lands reflected, and normals stay outward on both halves (Maya transforms them by the inverse-transpose; VP2 flips the winding for display). Documented tradeoff: a mirrored instance can't have its normals corrected per-copy — `opposite` is a *shape* attribute shared by every sibling — so reach for `mirror` when the halves must diverge or when baking to an engine that dislikes negative scale. The pivot resolution `mirror` did inline is now the shared `_EditUtilsInternal._mirror_pivot_point` (both paths resolve the plane identically; the duplicated `manip`/`baked` branches collapse into one), joined by `_reflection_matrix`. Panel: Instance grays out Merge Mode / Delete Original / Un-Instance (nothing for them to act on) and rejects the Bounding Box (center) pivot with a clear message, since symmetrize cuts geometry. Tests in `test_edit_utils.py` (shared shape + reflection + negative determinant, object-pivot on a rotated object, invalid-axis raise); the existing mirror suite still passes after the extraction (50 green).

- **2026-07-27 — `NodeUtils.replace_with_instances` can retain each target's apparent size: new `retain_bbox_scale` (uniform) + `retain_bbox_per_axis` (non-uniform) options.** `matchTransform` copies a target's scale *channels* only, so a target whose size lives in its geometry (frozen/baked scale, or simply a bigger mesh) came back at the source's size — the instance was correct but visibly wrong. `retain_bbox_scale=True` rescales each new instance so its **world bounding box** matches the object it replaced, via the existing `XformUtils.match_scale(..., average=True)`; uniform because world boxes are axis-aligned, so a per-axis ratio would misfit any instance rotated off-axis. Instances are *not* limited to uniform scale (each transform owns its own scale channels), so `retain_bbox_per_axis=True` fits each axis independently — measured through the new private `NodeUtils._local_bbox_size` (the **shape's** object-space box: `MFnDagNode.boundingBox` on the *transform* silently bakes in that node's own rotation, which produced a box-correct but proportion-wrong fit in the failing-first test). Values that can't be reproduced degrade instead of corrupting: an axis with no extent on either side (flat target vs. solid source) keeps its scale rather than collapsing to zero, and a node with no single shape (group / multi-shape) falls back to the uniform fit. Runs before the target is deleted and before re-parenting. **Mirroring is never introduced or cancelled**: bounding-box extents are unsigned, so every ratio is positive and each channel keeps the sign `matchTransform` copied off the target — which matters because a negative-determinant instance can't be corrected per-instance anyway (`opposite` is a *shape* attribute, shared by every sibling; real mirroring belongs in `EditUtils.mirror`/`polyMirrorFace`, which builds correctly-wound geometry). Tests in `test_node_utils.py`: uniform retention, a rotated 1:2:4 per-axis fit (world box *and* local proportions), the degenerate-axis guard, and mirror preservation in both modes.
- **2026-07-26 — `UvUtils.set_texel_density` no longer aborts on degenerate shells: native per-shell reimplementation replaces the MEL delegation.** Live report: Set Texel Density raised `texSetTexelDensity.mel line 56: Division by zero` — the MEL scales each shell by `density/currentDensity` with no guard, so ONE collapsed/unmapped shell (zero UV or surface area) killed the whole operation. The method now implements the UV Toolkit "Set" semantics natively: input UVs are grouped per object and per shell (`MFnMesh.getUvShellsIds`), each group's current density is measured from its faces (`polyEvaluate` world/UV areas), and the group scales about its own 2D bounding-box center (`polyEditUV` pivot scale) — a shell whose density can't be measured is *skipped* and summarized in a single warning instead of aborting everything. Partial face/UV selections still scale just those components, like the toolkit. Also gone: the MEL path's select/restore dance (and its `$gMainProgressBar` dependency). Returns `(scaled, skipped)` counts. Repro'd failing-first in mayapy; regression tests `test_set_texel_density_skips_degenerate_shells` + `test_set_texel_density_multiple_objects` in `test_uv_utils.py`.
- **2026-07-26 — New `UvUtils.cut_uv_edges`: multi-object UV cutting (`polyMapCut` refuses component lists spanning objects).** Live report from tentacle's Cut Hard Edges: `cmds.polyMapCut(hard_edges)` with edges from several objects raises "Doesn't work with multiple objects selected" (repro'd failing-first in mayapy). The new helper groups the edges per object via `Components.map_components_to_objects` and cuts object-by-object, returning the grouped dict. Regression test `test_cut_uv_edges_multiple_objects` (asserts one cut per object and that every object's shell count actually increased).
- **2026-07-26 — Cylinder unwrapping gains a topology-driven seam algorithm (`algorithm="topology"`) for bent tubes and toruses.** `get_auto_seam_edges` assumes a straight revolution axis (PCA), which breaks on elbows, swept pipes, and closed toruses. The new `UvUtils.get_topology_seam_edges` derives the cuts from smooth-region topology instead: faces are segmented into smooth regions (authored hard/soft shading when present — coplanar hard edges ignored so triangulated flat caps don't shatter — else a dihedral threshold), region borders are cut like the axis algorithm's hard creases, and each region then contributes exactly the opening cuts its own surface topology needs — tube regions (≥ 2 boundary loops, counted from the border-edge graph, with disks recognized via the Euler characteristic) get one lengthwise cut per extra boundary (an edge-loop walk that follows the surface, so a bent tube's seam runs straight down the tube; Dijkstra shortest-edge-path fallback for non-quad topology), closed regions get one edge loop plus the crossing edge ring when torus-like (a torus unrolls to a single rectangle; a sphere splits in two), and caps/flat steps get none. Key subtlety, found probing: Maya primitives author **every** edge hard, and a coarse tube's sweep-direction faceting reaches ordinary crease thresholds (the test torus facets past 45° — the default angle — so a raw dihedral test shattered it into random bands). Mirroring the axis algorithm's axial-edge exemption topologically: only *ring-direction* edges (chained across quad-opposite edges from a boundary rim or a non-quad cap border) are crease-eligible at the user threshold; sweep-direction edges are exempt below an unambiguous ~60° cap — which also keeps boxy all-quad closed shapes (cube: 90°) splitting per-face as before. `cut_cylinder_seams` / `unwrap_cylinder` gain the pass-through `algorithm` parameter (default `"axis"`, unchanged behavior; unknown values raise). Tests: `TestTopologySeamAlgorithm` in `test_uv_utils.py` — capped-cylinder parity with the axis algorithm, half-torus elbow → one strip with a duplicated-UV seam, closed torus → loop+ring opening, unknown-algorithm ValueError. Full UV stack green headless (118 tests across the 5 UV modules).
- **2026-07-26 — `XformUtils.world_align_pivot` now honours component selections instead of dropping to object mode.** The op opened with `cmds.select(resolved_transforms, replace=True)`, so running it with faces/verts/edges selected replaced the selection with the owning transform — kicking Maya out of component mode — and the `pivot_type="manip"` branch returned *without* the restore the other branches did, losing the selection outright. It never needed the selection at all (every `xform`/`manipPivot` call addresses nodes by name), so the select/restore dance is gone: the selection is now left untouched and component mode survives. Components are also no longer merely collapsed to their object — a new internal `_group_components_by_transform` (one masked `filterExpand` classifies the whole selection; components stay unexpanded so a dense `f[0:5000]` selection costs one entry, not 5000) maps each owning transform to the components it owns, and the pivot lands on those components' bounding-box center: per owning transform for `pivot_type="object"`, the combined center for `"manip"`. A whole-object selection is unchanged (its own rotate pivot, mean across objects). `mode="get"` gains a `"components"` key, and the op is now `@CoreUtils.undoable` (aligning N objects was N×2 undo entries — harmless when the object path was a positional no-op, not once it actually moves pivots). Two traps the critique pass caught: (1) the component mask set was seeded from the full `Components.component_mapping`, which includes the *parametric* types (curve/surface parameter points, knots, ranges, trim edges, isoparms) and the rotate/scale **pivot handles** — the former have no position `exactWorldBoundingBox` can measure, the latter are manipulators rather than geometry, and both would have been "centered on" as if they were real components; the mask set is now only the types that carry a world position. (2) Passing that filter is necessary but not sufficient — `exactWorldBoundingBox` signals "nothing to measure" with an *inverted* sentinel box (min +1e20 / max -1e20) instead of raising, and averaging it yields exactly (0, 0, 0), so a NURBS surface-face selection (a legitimately-masked type) silently yanked the pivot to the world origin. A new internal `_component_center` returns None on that sentinel and the caller falls back to the object's own rotate pivot. Note `XformUtils.get_bounding_box` carries no such guard — its `"center"` for an unmeasurable component set is a silent (0, 0, 0), which is worth auditing separately. Also fixed while in here: `_resolve_transforms` used `listRelatives(path=True)` for its shape→parent branch, which yields the *shortest unique* name while the transform branch yields long paths — so a selection holding both an object and one of its own components produced `"|pc"` and `"pc"`, two entries the de-dupe couldn't merge and callers couldn't match (it also silently double-processed that object in `bake_pivot`); now `fullPath=True`, matching the documented "de-duped long paths" contract. Nine new tests in `test_xform_utils.py` cover mode preservation (both pivot types), the component-center position, multi-object component selections, the `get` payload, the unchanged whole-object path, the unmeasurable-component fallback, non-geometry mask rejection, and the de-dupe.
- **2026-07-25 — BREAKING: removed the flat convenience wrappers `mtk.import_blender_scene`, `mtk.bake_blender_scene`, and `mtk.auto_instance` — call the classes instead.** These hand-written module-level functions duplicated public surface the encapsulation standard reserves for classes (root `CLAUDE.md`); the implementation already lived on `BlenderSceneImport` / `AutoInstancer`. Migration: `mtk.import_blender_scene(p, **kw)` → `mtk.BlenderSceneImport().import_scene(p, **kw)`; `mtk.bake_blender_scene(p, **kw)` → `mtk.BlenderSceneImport().bake_scene(p, **kw)`; `mtk.auto_instance(nodes, **cfg)` → `mtk.AutoInstancer.run_once(nodes, **cfg)` — a new `@classmethod` that keeps the one-shot ergonomics *and* drops the 20-parameter signature `auto_instance` duplicated from `__init__` (config now flows through `**config`). Internal callers (`reference_manager`) and tests migrated. Verified under mayapy: `test_auto_instancer` (54) + `test_scene_import` (24) + `test_reference_manager` (51), 0 failures. blendertk mirrors this removal.
- **2026-07-25 — DCC-send bridge panels: fixed parameter show/hide after the params-class refactor.** The Blender- and Unity-bridge slots handed the shared `BridgeSlotsBase` the bare `parameters` module as `params_module`, but the refactor moved `referenced_keys`/`defaults`/`render_context` onto the `Parameters` class (leaving only `PARAMS` at module scope). Opening either panel or switching template then raised `AttributeError` from `_refresh_param_visibility`. Now return `Parameters` (which re-exposes `PARAMS` as a class attribute), matching the already-correct marmoset/substance slots. Also: the RizomUV panel's version-gated visibility now expands `__…__` include blocks *before* stripping unsupported knobs, so on pre-2022 Rizom it hides gated rows instead of surfacing knobs the send path silently drops. Verified under real Qt (`test_uv_rizom_bridge`, 25 tests).
- **2026-07-25 — Reference Manager: referencing the currently-open scene now takes ONE click (matching Blender).** Clicking the reference icon of the open scene closed it (new empty scene) but required a *second* click to actually reference it. Cause: `_toggle_reference_at_row` closes the scene then adds the reference, but its *unblocked* `item.setSelected(True)` fired `itemSelectionChanged` → `handle_item_selection` (Maya's selection→reference sync, which Blender has no equivalent of). The just-closed row's name item is still flagged non-selectable, and `setSelected` on a non-selectable item fires the signal yet leaves the item un-selected — so the sync saw the freshly-added reference as a stale-selection diff and removed it. Fix: block the table's signals around the programmatic `setSelected` calls in both toggle branches, matching every other selection-mutating method here (`sync_selection_to_references`, `unreference_all`). Verified in mayapy (the close+reference primitive works in one pass) and by a real-Qt regression test driving the actual `_toggle_reference_at_row` + `handle_item_selection` (fails without the fix: reference clobbered → `[]`). blendertk needs no change — it has no selection→reference sync, which is why it was already one-click.
- **2026-07-25 — Reference Manager: icon columns pinned right, no dead space on resize; NOTES moved to the middle.** The panel never set a stretch column, so with the `TableWidget` default (Interactive, no stretch-last-section) resizing the window left dead space to the right of the last column, and the icon action columns weren't pinned to the edge (NOTES sat rightmost). Now FILES and NOTES use `QHeaderView.Stretch` (FILES fills when NOTES is hidden; the two share the free space when NOTES is shown), and NOTES is moved to the visual middle via `header.moveSection` — visual-only, so every logical column index (actions on 1/2/3, NOTES on 4) is untouched. The three icon columns keep their Fixed square sizing and now sit at the right edge. blendertk mirrored. Live DCC confirmation owed (geometry-only; not headless-testable against the mock table).
- **2026-07-25 — Reference Manager: the Folder Structure field's hover tooltip is now self-documenting *and* live.** Hovering `txt_subfolder_structure` shows the field's purpose, every supported placeholder with **its meaning** (`{scenes}` / `{name}` / `{workspace}` / `{suffix}` — listed even when the current pattern uses only some), each one's value resolved against the current workspace + open scene, the real absolute save directory, worked examples, and a live `{scene}`-typo warning — built on the new uitk `TooltipFormat.placeholder_preview` + pythontk `StrUtils.resolve_placeholders`. (The help text is folded into the bound tooltip because `tooltip.bind` replaces the static `setToolTip` — so the instructional content that used to live there is preserved, not lost.) The `{scenes}` file-rule lookup shared by Save and the folder-structure filter is unified behind `_scenes_folder()` (the two inline `cmds.workspace(fileRuleEntry="scene")` copies removed). Blender mirror updated in lockstep; panel parity unchanged (0 untriaged). **Needs published pythontk + uitk.**
- **2026-07-25 — Option-box declutter: folded standalone action icons into their existing dropdown menus.** Reference Manager `txt000` (Root Directory) now offers **Set Directory…** as the first row of its option menu instead of a separate folder icon (fires the `b000` slot; the pin/recent-directory button is untouched). Audio Clips `cmb000` folds its browse icon into the **Tracks** menu as **Add Tracks…**, and `tb001` (Key Audio Event) folds its two icons into the menu as **Select Carrier Node** / **Sync Audio to Timeline**. Behaviour is unchanged — only the presentation moved from icon buttons to labeled menu rows. Blender mirror updated in lockstep; parity ledgered in `tentacle/docs/parity_map.py`.

- **2026-07-25 — Reference Manager: no spurious "unsaved changes" prompt after opening a scene via the panel.** Opening a reference-bearing scene through the Open icon applies the referenced files' edits during `cmds.file(open=True)`, which leaves Maya's scene `modified` flag set even with no user edits; an immediate close (second Open click) or reference toggle then falsely prompted "unsaved changes — close anyway?". `open_scene` now clears the load-time dirty flag (`cmds.file(modified=False)`) right after its own open — a genuine subsequent edit re-sets it, so the discard guard still protects real work.
- **2026-07-25 — Reference Manager: fixed the REAL launch crash — action columns were registered before the table's columns existed.** The panel still hard-crashed Maya at launch after the prior `_rewire_signal` fix; a self-driven fresh GUI Maya with an in-process `faulthandler` produced the actual native stack: settings-restore fires `chk_filter_suffix` → `refresh_file_list` → lazy `tbl000_init` → `_wire_table_signals` → `TableActions.add` → `_apply_sizing` → `QHeaderView.setSectionResizeMode` on **column 1 of a still-0-column table** — a native access violation on Maya's Qt 6.5 (the prior restructure moved `_wire_table_signals` ahead of the one-time `setColumnCount(5)`; the `.venv`'s newer Qt tolerates the out-of-range call, which is why every offscreen suite passed — the known venv-newer-than-Maya masking). Fix: `tbl000_init` runs the one-time construction (`setColumnCount` et al.)
