BEFORE `_wire_table_signals`, and uitk's `TableActions._apply_sizing` gained a fail-soft out-of-range guard so the bug class can't hard-crash any DCC again (see uitk CHANGELOG). **Verified in a live GUI Maya**: full panel launch (get → show → refresh → deferred timers) completes with the process alive; blendertk mirror reordered identically. Note: the prior entry's crash attribution (blanket `disconnect()`) was a real hazard but was NOT the reported launch crash — this was.
- **2026-07-25 — Reference Manager: fixed a live-Maya crash, made the Open icon a toggle, and enforced open/reference mutual exclusion (mirror of the blendertk fix).** (1) **Crash**: the prior turn's `_wire_table_signals` re-wire blanket-`disconnect()`ed `customContextMenuRequested` and the item delegate's `closeEditor`, stripping the table's OWN internal handlers — the widget wires `customContextMenuRequested` → `_show_context_menu` in its `__init__` (right-click menu) and Qt wires `closeEditor` for the edit lifecycle, so nuking them broke the context menu and left a dangling editor on the next `clear()` (a live crash). Now re-wired through a new `_rewire_signal(widget, signal, slot, key)` that stores the per-(widget, key) `QMetaObject.Connection` and drops only this panel's own prior connection — reload-safe without touching internal wiring (mechanism proven under the blendertk `.venv`). (2) The **Open icon is a toggle**: clicking it on the currently-open scene **closes** it (new empty scene via the new `ReferenceManagerController.new_scene` → `cmds.file(new=True, force=True)`, guarded by an unsaved-changes confirmation) instead of re-opening. Currentness is filepath-authoritative (`_is_current`: a native row's own file, or a foreign `.blend` row's deterministic `_foreign_scratch_path` bake), so a second Open on a baked-open scene closes it. (3) **Open and Reference are mutually exclusive**: opening a *referenced* file makes it the open scene — loading replaces the whole session, so the reference (a reference node in the previous scene) is discarded for free, no explicit un-reference; referencing the *open* scene first closes it (a scene can't be referenced into itself). A close (`file -new`) fires `NewSceneOpened`, not the subscribed `SceneOpened`, so the close paths explicitly `refresh_file_list()`. New slot helpers `_current_scene_path` / `_foreign_scratch_path` / `_is_current(path, current=None)` / `_confirm_discard_unsaved` / `_close_scene` / `_find_reference_for_path`; `open_scene`'s foreign scratch path is DRY'd through `_foreign_scratch_path`; `update_table`'s Open-column currentness routes through the single `_is_current` source of truth. Structurally mirrored from the `.venv`-verified blendertk panel; **live-Maya verification owed** (no mayapy in this session). Registry regen'd (`new_scene` on the controller).
- **2026-07-25 — Package-wide DAG long-name hardening: ~55 `cmds.*` call sites that returned/keyed on ambiguous short names now use full DAG paths.** `cmds.ls`/`listRelatives`/`filterExpand`/etc. return non-unique **short** names by default, so any code that re-passed one into another `cmds` call, keyed a dict/set by it, or string-compared it silently operated on (or collapsed, or deleted) the wrong object when two nodes shared a leaf name under different parents. Swept every ecosystem-relevant module (`node_utils`, `core_utils/components`+`scene_audit`, `xform_utils`, `edit_utils`, `rig_utils`, `uv_utils`, `anim_utils`, `env_utils`, `nurbs_utils`, `display_utils`, `cam_utils`, `light_utils`) adding `long=True`/`fullPath=True`/`path=True`, keying caches by long path or UUID, and dropping `.split("|")[-1]` truncation that re-introduced ambiguity after Maya had already disambiguated. Three were genuine **data-loss** risks — `DuplicateRadial`/`handoff_export` could `cmds.delete` the wrong same-named object, and `AnimUtils.copy_keys`/`paste_keys`/`transfer_keyframes`/`set_keys_for_attributes` could cross-apply keyframes between same-leaf-named objects. Notable specifics: `NodeUtils.get_shape_node`/`get_history_node` shapes; `XformUtils._manip_cache` re-keyed by long path (was leaking a cached manip pivot onto the wrong object across calls); `Naming.rename` hardened with **UUID** re-resolution (immune to both duplicate names and intra-batch reparenting); the blendshape tween creator re-resolves each dup from `cmds.parent`'s post-rename return via a new `_parent_to_group` helper; `SceneAnalyzer._build_material_caches` leaf-fallback only trusts a unique match. **Public return-format change** (leaf-tolerant callers unaffected; noted here for consumers): `get_joint_chain_from_root`, `get_visible_geometry`, `clear_stored_transforms`, `has_stored_transforms`/`copy_keys` dict **keys**, `find_objects_in_namespace`, and `ShadowRig`/`ExplodedView` self-references now surface full DAG paths. Dict-return `keyed_by_original` contracts (`DuplicateLinear`/`DuplicateRadial`) preserve the caller's reference as the key and resolve long only for the cmds ops. Two audit suggestions were rejected on verification: `polyListComponentConversion` has **no** `fullPath` flag (disambiguate via the wrapping `cmds.ls(long=True)` instead), and `_scene_import._transforms` keeps short names to preserve documented **blendertk parity** (the real fix is at the import source, out of scope). Signatures unchanged (a speculative `get_shape_node` param was dropped), so no API-registry regen. Full suite **124/124 headless modules PASS, 0 failures/0 errors**; the affected tests updated to leaf-compare where a return format legitimately changed.
- **2026-07-25 — `XformUtils.transfer_pivot` gains a `mirror` option — optionally transfer a *mirror* of the source pivot.** New `mirror: str = ""` keyword (accepts `"x"` / `"y"` / `"z"`, case-insensitive; empty = unchanged direct copy) reflects the transferred pivot across the world axis-plane through the origin, for when the target is a mirrored copy of the source. The translate/scale pivot **position** is reflected (the mirror-axis component negated); the rotate pivot **orientation** is conjugated by the reflection (`S · R · S`) so the mirrored frame stays a valid right-handed rotation — the world-space path sets the target's world orientation to the mirrored source frame (instead of `matchTransform`) and rides the existing `rotateAxis` relocation, while the object-space path negates the two non-mirror-axis `rotateAxis` components. An invalid axis warns and falls back to no-mirror. Regression coverage in `test/test_pivot_transfer_scenarios.py` (position reflection + a +30°→−30° Z-rotation mirror across X). AST-verified; live-Maya run owed (no Maya in this session).
- **2026-07-25 — Reference Manager: opened foreign (.blend) rows, folded Import (convert) into Unlink and Import, expanded the Include Types row, and dropped the `(Blender)` origin tag — full parity with the blendertk changes.** (1) **Open** on a foreign (`.blend`) row now bakes the scene to a `.ma` (`_bake_foreign_path` → headless Blender + mayapy) and opens a throwaway copy as a new scene, the mirror of blendertk's `_open_foreign_as_new`; the scratch copy keeps the cached bake (reused for referencing) unedited. Consequently the Open icon on a foreign row is now clickable + neutral `#555555` (matching the reference column) instead of the near-invisible greyed-out `unavailable` state (the open `unavailable` state was removed). (2) The separate **Import (convert)** context-menu item (`btn_import_scene`) is removed; **Unlink and Import** (`btn_unlink_import`) now covers both cases — a linked reference (native *or* a foreign row's bake, resolved via `_bake_source_key`) is made local, and a not-yet-referenced foreign row is converted + imported via the headless-Blender bridge. (3) The header **Include Types** row now uses `justify="expand"` (full-width equal slots) instead of `justify="between"`. (4) Table names no longer append a `(Blender)` origin tag (the extension distinguishes foreign rows; the unrelated `(External)`/workspace tag is unchanged). `test_update_table_foreign_row_toggles_like_a_native_row` updated (open col now `"default"`). `tentacle/docs/parity_map.py` re-ledgered (`btn_toggle_reference`→`row_toggle_reference`, `btn_unlink_import`→`row_unlink_import`, the `btn_import_scene`/`row_import` entry retired); `compare_panel_surface.py --panel reference_manager` green.
- **2026-07-25 — Reference Manager: hardened the panel against re-entrant `*_init` (duplicate menus) and row actions going dead after a reload, plus guarded foreign-scene Open — mirror of the blendertk fix.** `tbl000_init` is now split into a one-time guarded block (table config + context-menu `QPushButton` construction — building these twice was the duplicate row-menu-entries bug, traced in blendertk to a nested reentrant `init_slot` call before the framework stamps `is_initialized`) and a new `_wire_table_signals`, called **unconditionally** on every `tbl000_init`. This matters because `__init__` builds a fresh `self.controller` every time (`self.controller = ReferenceManagerController(self)`), so a widget-outlives-its-slots-instance reload leaves a NEW controller — if the action-column states / `itemDoubleClicked` / `itemSelectionChanged` / `customContextMenuRequested` / `itemChanged` / the item delegate's `closeEditor` / the `register_menu_action` handlers only wired inside the one-time block (guarded on the PERSISTED widget's `is_initialized`, already `True`), every row icon click and context-menu action would silently stay bound to the OLD, orphaned controller — the exact bug class `channels`' `_wire_table_signals` exists to prevent, mirrored here (`TableActions.add` / `register_menu_action` are dict-keyed and idempotent by design; only the raw Qt signals need an explicit disconnect first). `header_init` gets the identical split for its own one-time widget construction vs. the `refresh_requested` connection. `open_scene` short-circuits a foreign (`.blend`) path with a guard message before handing it to `cmds.file`. The header **Include Types** row spans the full menu width (`Menu.add_row(justify="between")`). Structurally mirrored from blendertk (Qt-level regression coverage lives there under the offscreen `.venv` harness; this side is AST- and read-verified — no live Maya available in this session).
- **2026-07-25 — RizomUV bridge phases 1/2/4: padding + scale-preservation knobs, auto-cut quality upgrades, true-instance dedupe, and a DRY shared pack block.** Every Lua candidate was probed against the installed 2020.1 first (results recorded in `rizom_headless_probe.py` + the upgrade plan's evidence table). **Phase 1**: new **Edge Spacing** (`SpacingSize` ≤ 2021 / `PaddingSize` ≥ 2022) and **Tile Margin** (`MarginSize`, ungated) params — the version split rides a new inline `-- @min/@max_rizom_line:` gate in `strip_unsupported` (keyed on the line, so one param renders under two field names); the mislabeled Pre-scale / Layout Scale enums are corrected to their real `Scaling.Mode` / `LayoutScalingMode` meanings with a "maintain scale between objects = Keep + Keep" tooltip. The quadruplicated group+pack+placement recipe is extracted to `templates/pack_block.lua`, pulled in via a new `Parameters.expand_includes` `__PACK_BLOCK__` directive (standalone-line-only, so it can't clobber comments — a bug caught in testing) — so pack/unwrap_hard/unwrap_organic now also get UDIM/coverage placement for free. **Phase 2**: `unwrap_hard` gains `Auto.ReWeld` + `Auto.BooleanUnoverlap` (gated ≥ 2022, probed to crash 2020.1); `unwrap_organic` gains always-on `Auto.SkeletonUnoverlap` + a new **Fit Cones** toggle (both probed safe, FitCones has real effect); new **`unwrap_hybrid`** preset (SharpEdges + QuasiDevelopable in one pass, preset-gated ≥ 2022). **Phase 4**: `process_with_rizomuv(skip_instances=True)` collapses true DAG instances to one representative per shared shape before export (reuses `NodeUtils.filter_duplicate_instances`; the transfer propagates via the shared shape) — no more wasted per-instance solves. Effect verification of the ≥ 2022-gated fields, plus `STACK_DUPLICATES` for non-instance duplicates and a texel-density scaling mode, remain owed (see [docs/rizom_bridge_upgrade_plan.md](docs/rizom_bridge_upgrade_plan.md)). Tests `test_uv_rizom_bridge.py` 25/25 (+8: include expansion, per-version padding/ReWeld gating, new params, instance dedupe); headless probe PASS (6 cases). Mirrored to blendertk.

- **2026-07-25 — RizomUV bridge: target-UDIM + fractional-tile packing, pack-into-empty-space preset, preset-level version gating.** The `pack` preset gains **Target UDIM** (1001–1100) and **Tile Coverage** (Full / Half U / Half V / Quarter) — applied as a post-pack `ZomDeform` affine, probe-verified on Rizom 2020.1 (row-major 3×3 `Transform`; identity placement skips the call; new probe case `pack_udim_quarter` asserts the layout lands in UDIM 1012's bottom-left quadrant via the probe's new `check_bounds` assertions). New **`pack_into_existing`** preset packs `select_objects=`' islands into the gaps of the existing layout (`ZomSelect` island-group names → `ZomPack WorkingSet="Visible&Selected"`, `Scaling.Mode=0` + `LayoutScalingMode=0` so existing shells and texel density stay put); `RizomUVBridge.expand_by_materials` expands a selection to every mesh sharing its material(s), and `process_with_rizomuv(select_objects=)` renders the exported `__RZTMP` names into the script. Because island-subset selection is a **silent no-op on 2020.1** (probed: group `Names`/`IDs` variants dead; `ZomIslandProperties`/`ZomItemNames` access-violate), the preset ships behind a new **preset-level gate**: an `-- @min_rizom: 2022.2` header marker (`Parameters.preset_min_version`) hides it from the panel combo and makes the bridge refuse loudly below the gate. Live verification on a ≥ 2022.2 install is owed — full phased plan in [docs/rizom_bridge_upgrade_plan.md](docs/rizom_bridge_upgrade_plan.md). Tests `test_uv_rizom_bridge.py` +7 (marker parsing, token references, gate refusal, select-token guard, name mapping, material expansion) — 18/18; headless probe PASS (6 cases).

- **2026-07-25 — Duplicate Grid: per-axis Spacing X/Y/Z with optional lock, dimension min 1, and reset on every field.** The single uniform *Spacing* field is replaced by per-axis *Spacing X/Y/Z*, each laid out directly under its *Dimensions* field; each spacing field carries an option-box **lock** toggle — lock two or more and a change to one shifts the others by the same delta. Dimension counts now clamp to a minimum of 1 in the UI (invalid `0` is locked out; the engine still accepts negative counts for programmatic callers). The three Dimensions fields are now the uitk `SpinBox` (not native `QSpinBox`): the native trigger-adjacent wrapper churned during the option-box wrap and silently dropped its reset button; the stable uitk widget keeps it (matches the ecosystem convention of uitk widgets over native Qt). `DuplicateGrid.duplicate_grid`'s `spacing` now accepts a float (uniform, unchanged) **or** an `(sx, sy, sz)` tuple, via `_normalize_spacing`. Every spin box (dimensions + spacing) gets a reset-to-default button. Tests `test_edit_tools_duplicate.py` (+2: per-axis spacing lattice, scalar↔uniform-tuple). Verified live in Maya (options present on all six fields, lock linking, per-axis lattice). Relies on the uitk reset-default + option-box-wrap fixes (see uitk CHANGELOG).

- **2026-07-25 — Combine & Separate gain an `uninstance` guard; bake-pivot instance report verified NOT reproducible.** Live report: combining/separating (and reportedly baking pivots on) instanced geometry made instances vanish. Reproduced the destructive cases in a fresh headless Maya (never a live session): **`polyUnite` combining 2 of 3 instances silently deletes the unselected 3rd**, and **`polySeparate` on an instanced mesh silently deletes its sibling instance** — the shared shape is the hazard. `EditUtils.combine_objects` and `EditUtils.separate_objects` gained `uninstance=False`; when set they fork each input to a unique shape via the existing `NodeUtils.uninstance` (same helper `EditUtils.mirror` already uses) before the destructive step, so siblings outside the selection survive. Defaults are a no-op, so existing callers are unchanged; tentacle wires both to a default-ON "Uninstance" checkbox. **Bake-pivot: no defect found.** `XformUtils.bake_pivot` operates only on the transform (`zeroTransformPivots`, `rotate`/`move`), never the shared shape — probed both headless and in a launched GUI Maya with the Move tool active, a custom manip pivot (mode 6), and pivot-edit mode engaged, across single- and multi-selection, plus `transfer_pivot(bake=True)`: instances stayed alive, shape stayed shared, positions unchanged (no collapse). No uninstance/reinstance control added to pivot bake (it would guard nothing; nothing to reinstance). Tests: `test_group_combine.py` +2 and `test_separate_objects.py` +2 (uninstance preserves the sibling; characterization that the unguarded path deletes it) — 24/0 in the scoped mayapy run. Registry regenerated. blendertk twins (`combine`/`separate`) parity is owed.
- **2026-07-25 — Cut on Axis gains Spacing + interpolation weighting; pivot resolver double-checked and locked in.** Two-part request. (1) **Pivot report ("get-pivot helper returns the manip pivot for the object pivot") — verified NOT reproducible; resolver is correct.** `manipPivot` is a GUI-only manipulator override, so this can't be exercised under mayapy — driven fresh-GUI-Maya probes (never reusing a session) proved `XformUtils.get_operation_axis_pos`/`get_operation_axis_matrix` honor each pivot type: with a custom manip at (2,0,0) and the rotate pivot at (5,0,0), `object` → 5 and `manip` → 2 (distinct); the full `CutOnAxis.perform_cut_on_axis(pivot="object")` tool path cuts at 5, not 2; and the `_manip_cache` does not go stale after a manip reset. The only case where `object` and `manip` coincide is the common one — an *uncustomized* manip reports the default origin, so `manip` **deliberately falls back to the object rotate pivot** (a documented prior fix), making the two identical until you drag a custom pivot. Locked in with `TestPivotRetrieval` (object=rp, object follows an edited rp while center stays at the bbox center, manip-uncustomized falls back to rp) plus a `@skipIfBatch` GUI test asserting a custom manip diverges from object — 0 skipped/0 failures in the launched-GUI pass. (2) **Feature: cut distribution control.** `EditUtils.cut_along_axis` + `CutOnAxis.perform_cut_on_axis` gained `spacing` (`0` = legacy even-fill of the axis; `>0` fixes the span to `spacing*(cuts-1)`) and DuplicateLinear-style `distribution`/`weight_bias`/`weight_curve` interpolation, backed by a new pure helper `EditUtils._cut_offsets`. It drives `ptk.ProgressionCurves` on a **symmetric** parameter `t∈[0,1]` (centered at 0.5) rather than DuplicateLinear's 1-based `(i+1)/N` normalization, so `"linear"` reproduces the historical centered even-fill **exactly** (byte-for-byte: cube length 10, 3 cuts → offsets −2.5/0/+2.5) while non-linear modes bias interior density toward one end with endpoints pinned at ±span/2; the delete side now anchors on the extreme cut (`max`/`min`) so a non-monotonic curve still deletes from the outermost cut. The panel adds a Spacing spinbox (`s002`), an Interpolation combo (`cmb001`, the 7 shared modes), and Bias/Curve spinboxes (`s003`/`s004`) enabled per-mode by `toggle_weight_ui` (mirrors DuplicateLinearSlots); grid reordered Pivot→Amount→Offset→Spacing→Interpolation→Bias/Curve→Delete→Mirror. Defaults (`spacing=0, distribution="linear"`) are a no-op, so existing callers are unchanged. Tests: `test_edit_tools_geometry.py` — `TestCutOffsets` (pure-math: even-fill preserved, spacing gap, single-cut-on-pivot, empty, non-linear endpoints/interior) + geometry `test_spacing_controls_span` (spacing fixes xmax after delete) + `test_distribution_runs_and_cuts` (all modes cut) — 57/0 headless and 57/0 in the GUI pass; full slots→perform_operation UI wiring (combo populate, `s000-4` connect range, weight toggle, spacing cut) verified live in a launched GUI Maya. blendertk twin (`edit_utils/cut_on_axis`) parity is owed. Registry regenerated.
- **2026-07-25 — Bevel preview "adjusting Segments undoes my work" solved: `polyBevel3`'s silent no-op surfaced as a real error, and canceling a preview re-selects the original selection.** The live report ("preview is undoing a step it shouldn't; the operation fails and the extra undo loses work") was NOT a Preview-engine defect — a full headless repro plus a fresh-GUI-Maya probe driving the real bevel panel (real `s001` spinbox signals, real `add_reset_buttons` wrap) proved the hermetic refresh cycle correct at every segment value: no bevel stacking, pristine restore on cancel, user construction history intact, undo queue byte-for-byte intact (an earlier "queue flushed" reading was an artifact of calling `cmds.undo()` through the command port). The real mechanism: **`polyBevel3` "succeeds" while producing NO topology change** when the width×segments combination can't fit the adjacent geometry (repro: a Y-flattened cube bevels at seg 1–3 and silently no-ops at seg ≥ 4; thin-sliver faces no-op at every count). Inside a refresh that silence is destructive UX — the rollback removes the visible bevel, the "successful" re-run applies nothing, the bevel simply vanishes with the checkbox still checked, and the natural recovery Ctrl+Z eats real pre-preview work. `Bevel.bevel` now compares per-object vert/edge/face counts around each `polyBevel3` call and raises a curated `OperationError` ("Bevel produced no change on: …" + why/what-to-lower) listing only the failing objects — every object is processed first, so healthy meshes in a multi-object selection keep their bevel; the Preview message pipeline turns it into a readable popup instead of a silent vanish. Separately, `Preview` gained `reselect_on_disable=True`: unchecking Preview re-selects the enable-time selection (components included — most ops like `polyBevel3` clear it), suppressed via `stateWithoutFlush` so the cancel still leaves zero undo-queue trace; opt out per-panel with `reselect_on_disable=False`. blendertk twins unaffected by design: `bmesh.ops.bevel` clamps (`clamp_overlap=True`) instead of silently no-oping, and its snapshot rollback already restores selection+active. Tests: `test_edit_tools_geometry.py` +3 (silent-no-op raises w/ failing-object name, fitting segments don't raise, multi-object partial reporting) — 44/0 headless; `test_preview.py` +4 reselect tests (op-cleared object selection, component selection, opt-out, no undo-queue leak) — 35/0 in the GUI pass. Empirical repros kept in `test/temp_tests/repro_bevel_segment_refresh.py` + `polybevel_segment_failure_scan.py`. Self-critique round then surfaced a **pre-existing** grouping bug the fix sat on: `Components.map_components_to_objects` keyed by leaf name (namespace stripped), so two same-leaf-name objects (`grp1|pCube1` + `grp2|pCube1`) merged into ONE group — `polyBevel3` rejects multi-object component lists outright ("Doesn't work with multiple objects selected", probed), so beveling (or bridging) across duplicate-named objects was already broken, and every key consumer had grown a resolve-from-values workaround. **Keys are now the node strings exactly as `cmds.ls` disambiguates them** — one key per real object, cmds-safe (no consumer used keys as node names anymore; the workarounds' stale comments trimmed in `set_edge_hardness` + tentacle `normals`). The collision test rewritten to pin the new contract (`test_map_components_to_objects_collision_keys_stay_unambiguous`), plus `test_duplicate_leaf_names_bevel_independently` — red-first via the probe. `components` + `edit_tools_geometry` + `bridge` headless green.
- **2026-07-25 — `EditUtils.detach_components`: headless mode-detection fixed.** Found via its (then-failing) `center_pivot` feature tests: the vertex/face branch decision read `cmds.selectType(q=True, ...)`, which returns **None in mayapy standalone** (no UI) — every headless call silently fell through to the generic `DetachComponent` branch and returned None. The branch now derives from the actual components (`filterExpand` masks 31/34), with the selection-mask query kept only as a fallback — more correct in GUI too, since the component masks aren't mutually exclusive. (The `center_pivot` semantics themselves belong to that feature's own entry.) `edit_utils` green headless at time of writing (47/0 with the in-flight feature work).
- **2026-07-25 — `EditUtils.get_similar_mesh`: tolerance and metric checkboxes actually work now (backs tentacle's Select ▸ Similar).** Three defects found probing the live report ("select similar isn't working correctly"): (1) **Metric flags passed as `False` silently forced an exact all-stats match.** `cmds.polyEvaluate(obj, uvcoord=False)` treats a False flag the same as no flag and returns the FULL stats dict; that dict then hit the exact `==` equality branch inside `ptk.are_similar` — so any unchecked option-box metric (the tentacle slot passes every metric explicitly, False for unchecked) required *every* polyEvaluate stat to match exactly, overriding both tolerance and the user's checkbox choices. Falsy flags are now dropped before evaluation. (2) **The self-filter never fired.** `_get_scene_polygon_transforms` returned `filterExpand`'s short names while the query object was long-named, so `m != obj` always passed and the original object matched itself despite `inc_orig=False`. The helper now re-resolves through `cmds.ls(long=True)` (`filterExpand`'s own `fullPath` flag returns shapes, not transforms — probed); `get_similar_topo`'s obj resolution long-names the same way so its `m != obj` still pairs up. (3) **The documented no-kwargs default ("compare all flags") compared nothing** — empty property lists are trivially similar, so every mesh in the scene matched. A real default metric set now applies (vertex/edge/face/uvcoord/triangle/shell/area/worldArea; `boundingBox` deliberately excluded — it's position-dependent, so an identical mesh that was merely moved would never match). Regression test `test_get_similar_mesh_tolerance_and_unchecked_flags` (failing-first via mayapy probe: identical twin matches at tolerance 0, original excluded, subdivided cube excluded at 0 and included at 100); `edit_utils` 44/0 headless.
- **2026-07-25 — UvDiagnostics: non-manifold UV find/repair added (backs the Unfold "Repair + Retry" fix).** `u3dUnfold` rejects non-manifold **UVs** with the same "non-manifold" error as bad geometry, but they are invisible to `polyInfo -nonManifoldVertices` and untouchable by `polyCleanup`'s nonmanifold flag ("No items found to cleanup") — such UV topology can't even be authored with Maya's own tools (it arrives via imports/legacy files; the repro needed `MFnMesh.assignUVs` with a same-direction shared UV edge). Probed repairs that do NOT work, in order: `polyClean -cleanUVs`, `polyForceUV -unshare` (per-face and whole-mesh), iterative `polyMapCut`, history flush — the corrupt sharing survives all of them. The one reliable repair is `polyMapDel` on the affected faces followed by `polyAutoProjection` (fresh planar layout for those faces only; the rest of the mesh's UVs untouched). New public API: `UvDiagnostics.find_non_manifold_uvs(objects)` → `{shape: [map components]}` via `polyInfo -nmuv`, and `UvDiagnostics.repair_non_manifold_uvs(objects)` → `{"total", "fixed", "remaining"}`. Tests `TestNonManifoldUvs` (failing-first): find on corrupt/clean meshes, repair-to-zero, and unfold-succeeds-after-repair; full `test_uv_diagnostics` 35/0 headless.
- **2026-07-25 — `NodeUtils.replace_with_instances`: `ValueError: More than one object matches name` on scenes with duplicate names fixed.** The op resolved objects and their parents by **short name** (`cmds.ls(...)` / `listRelatives(target, parent=True)` with no path flags), so when a target's parent shared a non-unique short name (e.g. two `polySurface13` under different groups) the reparent step `cmds.parent(instance, objParent[0])` couldn't disambiguate and raised. The stale `except RuntimeError` never caught it (it's a `ValueError`), and widening the catch would have silently misparented instances instead. Fixed at the source: objects now resolve via `cmds.ls(..., long=True)` and parents via `listRelatives(..., fullPath=True)`, so every DAG reference is unambiguous; output leaf names are unchanged (final rename still uses `CoreUtils.short_name`). Regression test `test_replace_with_instances_duplicate_parent_names` (failing-first: reproduced the exact `dup` ambiguity, and asserts each instance lands under its target's *own* full-path parent, not merely a same-named one); `node_utils` 31/0 headless.
- **2026-07-25 — Curve to Tube: twisted/mangled polygon tubes on curves with non-uniform parameterization fixed red-first.** `CurveToTube._sample_centerline` stepped the curve uniformly in **parameter** space (`pointOnCurve` over `minValue→maxValue`), and every polygon-build decision hangs off those 200 samples — the RDP ring placement, the end-tangent anchors, and the outward-normal check. On a curve whose knot vector is skewed (lopsided `attachCurve`, detach, imported CAD, knot edits) uniform parameter steps map to wildly uneven geometric steps: in the repro (same CVs, skewed knots) the max/median sample-spacing skew was **331×** with one 8.7-unit gap, so RDP never saw the geometry inside the gap — the swept path cut the corner and the sweep frame swung across one giant span, producing flipped faces (8/48 inward) and cross-sections up to 1.9× radius off the curve. This was the "sometimes" in the twisted-tube report: freshly drawn curves (uniform knots) were immune, edited/imported ones weren't — and it's why rebuilding the curve first appeared to fix it. Now sampled uniformly by **arc length** (`MFnNurbsCurve.findParamFromLength` → `getPointAtParam`, world space), which is parameterization-proof — equivalent to a `rebuildCurve` for sampling purposes with zero scene side effects (no source-curve mutation, live drivers untouched). Skew on the repro curve drops to 1.09×, zero flipped faces, deviation back within the RDP tolerance. Caveat unchanged: **NURBS** output extrudes along the source curve directly (no resampling), so a badly parameterized curve can still twist a NURBS tube — inherent to `cmds.extrude`; rebuild there if it matters. blendertk's twin is unaffected (native curve bevel, no RDP sampling). Test `test_polygon_immune_to_skewed_parameterization` (failing-first: 331× skew + corner-cut/flip symptom checks at radius > RDP tolerance, since a radial check at radius *below* the allowed RDP deviation reads legitimate corner-cutting as flips); `curve_to_tube` 26/0 headless.

- **2026-07-25 — Un-freeze keeps the pivot and can walk the hierarchy.** `XformUtils.restore_transforms` no longer loses the object's pivot: each object's world-space rotate/scale pivot is captured before the restore and re-anchored afterwards (`_apply_clean_local` still zeroes the pivot attrs during the clean channel write so `makeIdentity`'s leftover pivot-translate offsets can't fold into translate; `cmds.xform`'s default `-preserve` then rebuilds the compensation, so the object doesn't move). It also gained `traverse=False` — the restore-side mirror of `store_transforms(traverse=True)` / `freeze_transforms(freeze_children=True)`: descendants are collected and restored top-down (targets long-name-resolved and depth-sorted) so a whole hierarchy un-freezes from one root call. Self-critique then caught (via a mayapy probe, ~4.8-unit vertex drift) a pre-existing hierarchy bug the one-click traverse would have amplified: the single-pass restore read each object's world points/pivots AFTER its ancestors were already restored, so child geometry re-absorbed the ancestors' restored transforms (channel values were always correct — only geometry/pivots drifted; invisible to the transform-only assertions of the older chain test). The restore is now **two-phase**: phase 1 plans every node and snapshots world shape points + pivots before ANY write (each node's final world derived from its parent's computed final world, top-down), phase 2 applies. `_shift_shape_points` was split into `_get_shape_points_world` / `_set_shape_points_object` (shared `_shape_fn` dispatch) to support the split; restoring a hierarchy across separate calls remains subject to cross-call staleness — documented on the docstring (use one call or `traverse=True`). Pinned by four new `test_xform_utils` cases (pivot round-trip incl. no-displacement vertex check, rotated-hierarchy vertex/pivot preservation, traverse chain restore, traverse=False leaves descendant bake history); module 55/0, `test_channels` 92/0, `test_freeze_restore_locator_rig` 26/0 in fresh mayapy.

- **2026-07-25 — Substance Painter reimport works end-to-end: new Painter-side `substance_rpc` plugin + four bridge bugs fixed.** The reported failure — "Reimport launches a new Painter and nothing reloads" — had two independent causes. (1) **No transport existed:** stock Painter binds no RPC port (`--enable-remote-scripting` is a no-op, verified 2026-05-18), so the reimport/render/bake_lighting templates were dead on arrival. The bridge now ships a self-contained Painter Python plugin (`substance_rpc/plugin_src/substance_rpc` — registry/server/main-thread marshaller vendored from the marmoset_rpc pattern) serving pythontk's `{op, kwargs}` HTTP wire on port 8090 (`SUBSTANCE_RPC_PORT`), with ops `mesh.reload` (the reimport primitive — `substance_painter.project.reload_mesh` with `MeshReloadingSettings`, async status via `mesh.reload_status`), `project.info`, `js.evaluate` (routes legacy-JS `alg.*` template bodies through `substance_painter.js.evaluate` — render/bake_lighting transport unblocked; their JS symbol names remain unverified), `system.eval/ping/list_ops/version`. `Installer` (new, on the pythontk `PluginInstaller` strategy) resolves Painter's plugins folder (`SUBSTANCE_PAINTER_PLUGINS_PATH` env, then known-folder Documents) and the bridge auto-installs on every send (`ensure_rpc_plugin`, idempotent). `PainterRpcClient` rewritten as a `pythontk.RpcClient` subclass (the JSON-RPC 2.0 envelope client is gone — it spoke a protocol nothing served). (2) **Targeting bug:** the template's `TARGET_INSTANCE="current"` never constrained routing — the slot's default `target="auto"` fell through to a fresh bare launch (no `--mesh`, 60 s RPC wait, nothing happens). `_preflight` now narrows a default "auto" to the template's declared target, and `_resolve_connection` probes the default RPC port after a registry miss (`SubstanceConnection.attach`) so "current" finds a Painter across Maya restarts or one the user launched by hand; `SubstanceConnection.open()` always wires the RPC client (a plugin-served instance is reachable regardless of launch flags — previously an `import`-launched Painter could never be reused). (3) **Path drift:** the reimport FBX must overwrite the exact file Painter's project was created from; every export now records its path in the scene's `fileInfo` (`substance_bridge_last_fbx`, saved with the scene) and reimport (`REUSE_RECORDED_EXPORT`, new template field) writes to the recorded path instead of re-deriving it — surviving Maya restarts, Output-Dir drift, and the PID-keyed temp-fallback dir. (4) **Graceful degradation:** new `NO_CONNECTION_HINT` template field — when a hint-declaring template can't reach Painter, `_deliver` keeps the produce result (the FBX overwrite already happened), logs the manual steps (Edit ▸ Project Configuration ▸ Select), and returns `delivered: False` instead of `None`. Templates gain structured `RPC_OPS = [("op", {kwargs})]` (dispatched before `RPC_SCRIPT`; string kwargs get `__KEY__` substitution, non-strings stay typed); reimport uses `mesh.reload` — not the legacy `alg.mesh.reimportMesh` JS. Tests: `test_substance_bridge.py` 85/0 (+11: target narrowing, port discovery, RPC_OPS parsing/rendering, hint fallback), `test_substance_connection.py` rewritten for the new wire 7/0, new `test_substance_rpc_plugin.py` 12/0 (real plugin server + faked `substance_painter`, full reimport transport end-to-end). Mirrored 1:1 in blendertk (scene custom property instead of `fileInfo`). **First-run caveat surfaced everywhere the user looks:** a newly-installed Painter plugin can sit disabled until ticked once in Painter's **Python** menu (Python ▸ Reload Plugins Folder picks it up without a relaunch; Painter remembers the choice) — this is the usual reason a first reimport does nothing. It's documented in a new [`substance_rpc/README.md`](mayatk/mayatk/mat_utils/substance_bridge/substance_rpc/README.md) (canonical, with the ops table + manual install), and consistently in the install-success log, the RPC-timeout error, the reimport template's `NO_CONNECTION_HINT`, and the panel Help. Live-Painter verification of an actual mesh reload still owed (needs an interactive Painter session).
- **2026-07-25 — Mirror re-centers the result's pivot when the operation calls for it.** `EditUtils.mirror` gained `center_pivot=True`: merge modes weld both halves into the same transform, so its pre-mirror pivot ended up off to one side of the combined result — it now re-centers on the merged bounding box (`xform centerPivots`). In separate mode (`mergeMode=-1`), `separate_mirrored_mesh` centers the **new** half on its own bbox instead of copying the source's pivot onto it (the old `preserve_pivot` path left the mirrored object's pivot floating over the *original* object — "nowhere useful"); the surviving original half is not touched by us (polySeparate already centers it). The Bounding Box (center) symmetrize path inherits this via `mirror(mergeMode=1)`. `center_pivot=False` restores the legacy pivot. `edit_utils` 43/0 headless.
- **2026-07-25 — `UvUtils.set_texel_density` delegates to Maya's native `texSetTexelDensity`; `get_texel_density` batches its area evaluation.** The setter's Python loop — `get_uv_shell_sets` + per-shell `get_texel_density` (which itself ran two `cmds.polyEvaluate` calls **per face**) + bbox-center `polyEditUV` — is replaced by one `texSetTexelDensity <density> <mapSize>` call (the UV Toolkit "Set" op), which performs the identical per-shell about-center scaling natively. Selection is set/restored around the call; no-input now warns instead of silently no-oping. `get_texel_density` (still used by the pack-stats readout and the Get button) drops the per-face loop for a single aggregate `polyEvaluate(faces, worldFaceArea/uvFaceArea)` pair — two calls total regardless of face count — and also drops the `filterExpand` flatten (ranged components feed the aggregate calls directly) and gains an explicit empty-input guard (`polyListComponentConversion` falls back to the *current selection* when handed nothing, so `get_texel_density([])` with something selected would silently have measured the selection). Public surface and behavior unchanged (per-shell scaling to target density — blendertk's documented per-object divergence note still stands). `uv_utils` 38/0 headless, incl. the set→get round-trip; `check_cmds_syntax` validates the MEL proc against the live registry.
- **2026-07-25 — `UvUtils.transfer_uvs_to_similar`: fan one source mesh's UVs out to every geometrically similar mesh.** New public method backing tentacle's UV ▸ Transfer UV's scope option box. Unlike `transfer_uvs` (one-to-one group pairing), this takes a single source and a candidate pool (explicit list, or the whole scene when `candidates=None`), keeps the candidates that score ≥ `tolerance` (default **0.9** — deliberately stricter than `transfer_uvs`' 0.1, since a fan-out accepts *every* candidate over the threshold rather than picking a mutually-best match) on the existing bbox-volume + vertex-count similarity metric, and transfers via the exact-pairs path (`match_by_similarity=False`). Instance-aware by construction: the source's true instances are excluded (shared shape — their UVs already match), and candidate instance groups collapse to one representative via `NodeUtils.filter_duplicate_instances` (the shared shape covers the rest). Validates the source resolves to exactly one polygon mesh (`ValueError` otherwise); returns the target transforms transferred to. Tests +3 in `test_uv_utils.py` (scene-scope fan-out skips dissimilar geometry + true instances, candidate pool restricts the search, invalid-source contract) — `uv_utils` 38/0 headless. Registry regenerated.
- **2026-07-25 — RackBuilder: geometry layer fixed (its tests had never run) + test file moved to the canonical test dir.** `test_rack_builder.py` sat in the package-internal `mayatk/mayatk/test/` — a location `run_tests.py` never discovers and where its own `mayatk.test.base_test` fallback import can't resolve — so the geometry tests had never executed. Moved to `mayatk/test/` alongside every other test, which immediately surfaced two real bugs: (1) `_box` and `_punch_rail_holes` unpacked two return values from `cmds.polyCube` / `cmds.polyCBoolOp`, but with `constructionHistory=False` both return only `[transform]` (no history node) — every geometry build raised `ValueError` (6 errors); (2) `test_every_part_is_under_the_group` expected 5 direct children, forgetting the enclosure the builder always adds per its documented design (plinth + header + 2 default side panels) — corrected to 9 with the itemization spelled out. `rack_builder` now 14/14 under mayapy.
- **2026-07-25 — Shader Templates: dropped the redundant `widget.setEditable(False)` calls inside `rename_template_safe`.** The handler only ever runs from inside `ComboBox.on_editing_finished` — edit mode has already exited by then, so the calls were no-ops at best; before today's uitk emission gate (see uitk's CHANGELOG), the "name unchanged" branch's call **re-entered the emission and recursed to RecursionError** (repro-confirmed widget-side) — committing a rename without changing the name could crash Maya. uitk now hard-blocks the re-emission at the widget level; these call sites — the original crash vector — are removed so the pattern isn't copied elsewhere. blendertk's twin never had them.
- **2026-07-25 — New `edit_utils/rack_builder.py` (`mtk.RackBuilder`): parametric EIA-310 (19″) equipment-rack generator driven by a validated `RackSpec` (JSON).** A rack cabinet is a rectilinear object fully specified by a published standard (EIA-310-D / IEC 60297) — panel width, rack-unit pitch, and mounting-hole pattern are constants, so it is the worst case for image-to-mesh (marching cubes rounds every edge and fills every bay) and trivial to generate exactly. The cabinet is described declaratively by `RackSpec` / `BaySpec` / `OccupantSpec` (pythontk `SchemaSpec` subclasses using the new `nested=[…]` list-of-nested support — **requires pythontk ≥ the 2026-07-25 release**), so a rack is data (author/validate/document as JSON), not code. `RackBuilder(spec).build()` emits geometry — mounting flanges, per-occupant faceplates + chassis bodies stacked bottom-up by U, base plinth, top header, optional side panels — composing existing primitives (`Primitives`, `DuplicateGrid`) rather than re-implementing them; hero-only `punch_holes` arrays the standard 0.5/0.625/0.625 in hole column via `DuplicateGrid(mode="combine")` and one `polyCBoolOp` difference. All spec dimensions are real-world millimetres emitted at `1 unit = scene_unit_mm` (default 10 → cm), so output is metrically exact and rescales cleanly. Constants live on `EIA310` (single source of truth — no magic literals). The build runs in one `CoreUtils.undo_chunk` and never touches pre-existing nodes. Registered on the lazy root (`RackBuilder`, `RackSpec`, `BaySpec`, `OccupantSpec`, `EIA310`). Tests: `test_rack_builder.py` — schema layer (constants, list-of-nested occupant validation with indexed paths, skeleton self-validity, round trip) is pure-Python and verified in sandbox; geometry layer (single-group return, part counts, blank-has-no-body, metric panel width, bay X-offset, non-disturbance of existing geometry) runs under mayapy. Example spec: `comfyui/examples/racks/DA_2.json` (VDATS DA-2 bench rack). **Follow-ups:** `RackBuilderSlots` (Switchboard UI, deferred — untested Qt), and API registry regen (`generate_api_registry.py mayatk pythontk`).
- **2026-07-23 — Fix: RizomUV UV-transfer failed on namespaced objects (`No object matches name: ...`), plus a redundant/riskier re-match dropped for the bridge's already-verified pairs.** (1) **Bug:** `CoreUtils.build_mesh_similarity_mapping` keyed its returned mapping on `short_name` (namespace **and** DAG path stripped), but `UvUtils.transfer_uvs` feeds those values straight into `cmds.transferAttributes` — for any object living in a namespace (e.g. the RizomUV bridge's `ns:mesh_55__RZTMP` re-import), the stripped name is unresolvable by `cmds`, so both the batch transfer and the pairwise fallback failed for every namespaced pair. Switched to `leaf_name` (DAG path stripped, namespace preserved). Regression test `test_build_mesh_similarity_mapping_preserves_namespace` (failing-first against the old `short_name` behavior, confirmed red then green). (2) **Design flaw found reviewing the same call path:** `_rizom_bridge.py`'s `_transfer_uvs_and_cleanup` already builds exact, name-verified `(imported, original)` pairs via `_export_name_map`, but handed them to `transfer_uvs`, which re-derives its own pairing from scratch via geometric (bounding-box + vertex-count) similarity against the *entire* other list — redundant, and risky: a known-correct pair whose similarity score fell under `tolerance` was silently dropped, and two pairs of near-identical geometry (duplicate/mirrored parts, common in this asset pipeline) had no exclusivity guard against cross-wiring. `transfer_uvs` gained `match_by_similarity: bool = True` (default preserves prior behavior for every other caller); the bridge now passes `match_by_similarity=False` to apply its already-known pairs directly, skipping the similarity search entirely. Regression tests `test_transfer_uvs_match_by_similarity_false_uses_exact_pairs` (tolerance set above the maximum possible similarity score to prove the exact-pairing path never consults it) and `..._length_mismatch_raises`. No public-surface break (additive parameter); `API_REGISTRY.md`/`API_INDEX.md` regenerated. `core_utils` 28/0, `uv_utils` 35/0 headless; full suite 3892 tests, 0 new failures (2 pre-existing, unrelated `test_reference_manager` Qt errors); `test_uv_rizom_bridge` 12/0 live-Maya GUI pass confirms the bridge call-site end-to-end.
- **2026-07-20 — Reference Manager: Include Types row replaces two checkboxes, and a foreign (.blend) row now references like any other.** (1) **Include Types:** the header's "Hide Binary Files (.mb)" and "Include Blender Scenes (.blend)" checkboxes are gone, replaced by a single horizontal **Include Types** row (`chk_include_ma` / `_mb` / `_fbx` / `_blend`, via the new `uitk` `Menu.add_row`) — the same control, same objectNames, on both panels, so hiding .mb is just unchecking "mb". `.fbx` is newly listable and references natively via the FBX plugin. Classification is explicit (`NATIVE_EXTENSIONS = .ma/.mb/.fbx`, `FOREIGN_EXTENSIONS = .blend`), and `WorkspaceManager` gained a `SCENE_FILE_TYPES` hook so the workspace cache scans the whole native superset once — an Include toggle only re-filters, never re-scans disk. (2) **Foreign reference parity:** the green import-only state is gone. A `.blend` row's reference icon now bakes the scene to a cached `.ma` (headless Blender → FBX → headless `mayapy` → `.ma`) and references *that*; clicking again un-references it, and display overrides work — full behavioral parity with a native row, with only Open staying `unavailable`. Maya references FBX natively, so the bake is deliberate **symmetry** (both panels reference a cached *native* scene, so the referenced-file surface behaves identically whichever DCC the row came from), not a capability gap. "Import Scene" stays in the row menu as the local-copy path. (3) **New converter API:** `BlenderSceneImport.bake_scene()` / `mtk.bake_blender_scene()` (accepts `.blend` **and** `.fbx`, which skips the Blender hop entirely), `bake_source()` for the inverse lookup, a `templates/_bake_scene.py` headless-mayapy template that replays the conversion's texture manifest through the *existing* `_apply_texture_manifest` engine (no second copy of that logic — the parent's `sys.path` is passed into the child so mayatk is importable there), and `find_scenes(extensions=…)`. Conversion + bake are cached independently on source **and** template identity; the caching/promotion machinery moved to the new shared `ptk.CachedArtifact`. (4) **Bug fixed while wiring it:** `handle_item_selection` diffed the selection against *every* live reference, so a bake-backed reference — whose row is intentionally non-selectable and whose path is the cache artifact, never the selection — would have been silently removed on the next selection change. Tests +6 in `test_reference_manager.py`; `parity_map` entries for the retired `chk_hide_binary` and the two now-name-identical header operations removed; sweep passes 0 untriaged.
- **2026-07-19 — Reference Manager: header regrouped into Naming / Filter-Display / Operations with clearer tooltips, + cross-DCC Blender-scene import.** (1) **Header reorg (this is the reference blendertk now mirrors 1:1):** everything used to sit under one "Naming:" separator; the filter / hide / notes toggles now live under a distinct **Filter / Display:** separator, **Save To Workspace** moved beside the Naming fields it consumes, and the case-style combo switched from `"Case: X"` labels to bare `ptk.StrUtils.set_case` tokens (`_format_name` already stripped the prefix, so this is display-only and matches blendertk). Tooltips clarified throughout. (2) **Cross-DCC import (reverse of blendertk's Maya-scene listing):** a new **Include Blender Scenes (.blend)** header toggle (+ a row context "Import (convert)…", `btn_import_scene`) also lists the workspace's .blend files as import-only rows — the reference icon becomes an **import** action, open + display show `unavailable`, and the name is non-selectable (tagged "(Blender)") so the selection→reference sync can't try to reference a .blend; `_sync_reference_icons` skips them so the import icon isn't clobbered. The import routes to `mtk.import_blender_scene` (the existing headless-Blender→FBX bridge). Converter API extended with `BlenderSceneImport.find_scenes()` (the discovery half of the import — reuses the same `SUPPORTED_EXTENSIONS` the importer accepts; delegates to `ptk.FileUtils.get_dir_contents`). Tests added to `test_reference_manager.py` (`_is_foreign` classifies .blend only; `update_table` renders a foreign row import-only + non-selectable) — **40/40**. Registry regen deferred to a clean-tree pass.
- **2026-07-19 — Fix: `ScriptConsole.show` must not shadow the dockable-mixin `show`.** The encapsulation sweep turned `env_utils.script_output`'s `show` facade into a `ScriptConsole` **classmethod** — but `ScriptConsole` is a `MayaQWidgetDockableMixin` QWidget whose `setVisible()` calls `self.show()` internally (the virtual `setVisible` is invoked by C++ and dispatched back to Python by shiboken). A `show` classmethod shadows the mixin's instance `show`, so `self.show()` re-enters `show_console()` instead of running the real `QWidget.setVisible` — the widget's visibility path is silently replaced by a workspace-control facade (a latent correctness bug; a classmethod cannot stand in for the instance method Qt/Maya call). Removed the `show` classmethod (entry points are the collision-free `show_console` / `toggle`; `toggle` now forwards to `show_console`); the first-creation path already calls `MayaQWidgetDockableMixin.show` explicitly. Regression guard `test_show_is_not_a_shadowing_classmethod`. Verified live in a fresh GUI Maya (real quit→relaunch `uiScript` restore, floating + docked, with the tentacle `TclMaya` startup): the console restores, mirrors, and lays out correctly. NB: this removes a real footgun but was **not** reproduced as the cause of a specific restore break in isolated testing.
- **2026-07-19 — Full source-level encapsulation: no module-level functions.** Completed the sweep so **no module has root-level functions** (except exec-templates + the vendored marmoset plugin). Module-level helpers folded into `_<Class>Internal` bases — the pre-existing `_AnimUtilsMixin` / `_XformUtilsInternals` / `_MatUtilsInternals` were normalized to the singular `_AnimUtilsInternal` / `_XformUtilsInternal` / `_MatUtilsInternal` convention with the loose module helpers merged in; stray public functions across ~56 modules (audio `batch`/`compositor`/`migrate`/`nodes`/`segments`, `hierarchy_sync`, `bake_session`, `geometry_matcher`, `preview`, `components`, the shots subsystem, the substance/marmoset bridges, and no-class config modules now wrapped in classes) relocated into their classes. `env_utils.script_output` `show`/`toggle` facades became `ScriptConsole` **classmethods** (over `show_console`) — no module functions. Fixes along the way: forward-ref annotations stringified / `from __future__ import annotations` where a moved fn referenced a later-defined class (`RestoreResult`, `AudioSegment`, `_BatchContext`); a module constant (`MACRO_HOTKEY_SET`) moved above its class; multi-hop re-export chains (`detect_shot_regions` via `_shots`→`_shot_manifest`; `list_template_modes` via `_marmoset_bridge`) routed through the owning class; `mock.patch` targets updated to the class attribute. **No public-surface change.** Import-clean across every submodule; **353 + 71 audio tests**, 0 failures.
- **2026-07-19 — Encapsulation: module-level utils + `style_setter` helpers moved into their classes (mirror of the blendertk sweep).** `short_name` / `leaf_name` / `as_strings` / `get_bounding_box` (→ `CoreUtils`), `get_translation` / `get_object_matrix` / `set_object_matrix` (→ `XformUtils`), and the four `ui_utils.style_setter` `def fn` + `staticmethod(fn)` helpers (→ `StyleSetter`) are now real class methods instead of module-level functions; the 26 cross-module importers were rewritten to `Class.fn` (scope-aware). **No public-surface change** — `mtk.short_name` and `mtk.CoreUtils.short_name` are the same object under `"*"` (the roots stay wildcard-exposed). `CLAUDE.md` "common helpers" table updated to the new class homes. Import-clean across every submodule; **307 tests** (core / xform / node / components / edit / cam / naming / duplicate / matrices) 0 failures.

- **2026-07-18 — `rig_utils.telescope_rig` overhaul: cycle-free for any segment count, world-space driver, validation-first builds, axis option, bundle + teardown.** (1) **Bug (correctness-critical):** interior segments were carried by helper locators point-constrained to their *neighboring segments* — which themselves hung under the adjacent helper locators — a genuine DG dependency cycle for any build beyond three segments (57 cycling plugs on a 5-segment build; Maya's evaluation became order-dependent). Interiors are now point-constrained directly to the base/end locators with graded weights (`i/(n-1)`), each weight applied at creation so the maintained offset preserves the build pose; cycle-free for any count, and the helper locators (plus the destructive `cmds.parent` of user geometry under them) are gone entirely. (2) **Bug:** the `distanceBetween` driver read local `.translate` — a parented locator measured the wrong distance (10.0 vs a true 14.14 in repro); now `worldMatrix[0] → inMatrix1/2`. (3) **Bug:** every aim constraint used `upVector` parallel to `aimVector` (degenerate solve, unpredictable roll); up is now a world axis orthogonal to the aim axis, with `world_up_type` exposed. (4) **Bug:** the coincident-locator check ran *after* building — a refused build left the distance node, six constraints, and re-parented segments in the scene. All validation (including per-channel settable pre-flight and `0 < collapsed_distance < initial`) now runs before the first node is created, and an unexpected mid-build failure rolls back what it made. (5) **Bug:** driven keys used default tangents/infinity, so extending past the build pose tore the rig open (scale froze while the gap grew); keys are now spline-tangent (an end key's "linear" tangent degenerates to flat, which silently defeats linear post-infinity — probed live) with linear post-infinity (scale tracks `distance/initial` exactly, verified 1.6 at 16/10) and constant pre-infinity (clamps at the collapse ratio). Build-pose scales other than 1.0 are preserved as the baseline rather than snapped. (6) **New:** `aim_axis` parameter ("x"/"y"/"z", signed) drives aim vectors, the driven scale channel, and the off-axis lock set — exposed in the panel as an Aim Axis combo (`cmb_axis`, ledgered `pending` for the blendertk twin in `parity_map.py`). (7) **New:** builds return a `TelescopeRigBundle` (exact node names, driven plugs, recorded anim curves, locks, original scales) and `teardown()` removes precisely what was built, restoring locks/scales — later user keys on the same plugs survive. Locking is now scoped to the only rig-breaking free channels (off-axis scales) instead of a blanket `except: pass` sweep. `test_rig_utils.py` +6 failing-first regression tests (cycle-free 5-segment distribution, world-space driver, scale tracking incl. clamp, clean-refusal, axis remap, teardown); mock suite rewritten for the new node graph and the stale-skipped `TelescopeRigSlots` tests revived (8/8).
- **2026-07-18 — `anim_utils.shots` improvement pass: behavior-applier error masking fixed, ambiguous-name key edits hardened, audio event pairing unified onto `AudioUtils`, dead surface removed.** (1) **Bug (correctness-critical):** `apply_to_shots` probed its injected callables by *calling* them inside `except TypeError`, so a genuine TypeError raised **inside** a modern applier was misread as "legacy 4-arg signature", silently re-invoked the applier with reduced arguments (dropping `source_path`/`anchor_override` — an audio entry then no-opped) and reported the entry as **applied**. Signature support is now probed once via `inspect.signature(...).bind` (no call made; un-introspectable callables assume the modern contract), and an internal TypeError is recorded in `failed` like a RuntimeError instead of aborting the batch. `test_shot_manifest_behaviors.py` +4 (legacy-signature compat, internal-TypeError → failed-not-retried, source_path-only applier keeps its source in the audio pass, exists_fn TypeError propagates unmasked — the latter three failing-first). (2) **Bug:** `ShotSequencer.scale_object_keys` passed the stored (possibly short) name straight to `cmds.scaleKey` — a same-leaf-name duplicate in the scene raised *"More than one object matches name"*; now resolved through `_disambiguate_matches` (prefers the match carrying anim curves), and `move_object_keys` upgraded from `matches[0]` to the same policy. (3) **Bug (UI):** the manifest's scene-discovered "additional objects" row passed 5 cells to a 6-column tree, printing the literal word "scene" under the Behaviors header. (4) **DRY:** the audio on/off pairing state machine lived twice (`AudioUtils.read_events` + the sequencer's batched all-tracks scan, byte-for-byte drift risk) — extracted as `AudioUtils.pair_on_off_events`, both consumers rewired; new `AudioUtils.is_registered(raw)` collapses the `has_track(normalize_track_id(x))` idiom; marker-dict serialization, manifest fps fallback, `leaf_name` splits, and `_provide_sub_rows`' duplicate curve→attr loop each unified to one site. (5) **Dead code removed:** `ShotSequencer.from_current_range`, the never-passed `prevent_overlap` param + `_push_overlapping_objects`, manifest module facades `resolve_duration`/`_audio_placeholder_dur`, controller `browse_csv`, and the sequencer slots' unreachable `spn_gap` branch (widget lives on the Shots settings panel, not the sequencer UI). (6) Import-failure `print()`s → module loggers; `expand_missing`'s stdout dump → logger + footer count; segment-collector per-curve swallows now debug-log. `apply_audio_clip`'s stale `end`-is-a-hard-cap doc corrected (grow-only contract). **Mirrored in blendertk** (dead `browse_csv` + `prevent_overlap` path removed); registries regenerated for both.
- **2026-07-18 — `core_utils.diagnostics` improvement pass: 4.3k-line `scene_diag` split three ways, repair helpers return structured results, uninstancing DRY'd onto `NodeUtils`.** (1) **Module split (SRP):** `scene_diag.py` mixed the scene-repair helpers with the entire audit engine; now `audit_records.py` holds the Maya-free data contract (`AuditProfile`, per-asset records, `SceneReport`, `SceneInfoSection`), `scene_audit.py` holds `SceneAnalyzer`, and `scene_diag.py` keeps only `SceneDiagnostics`. Every root name (`mtk.SceneAnalyzer`, the record classes, the `mtk.Diagnostics` aggregate) is unchanged — `DEFAULT_INCLUDE` now points at the new modules. (2) **`TransformDiagnostics`:** `fix_non_orthogonal_axes` delegates uninstancing to `NodeUtils.uninstance` (the hand-rolled duplicate/delete/rename destroyed the original transform node), returns the fixed transforms (dry-run returns the would-fix list — the blendertk mirror already did both), and gained `tolerance`/`quiet` params; new `get_sheared` detection primitive backs it. (3) **`MeshDiagnostics.get_ngons`:** `objects=None` falls back to the selection and empty input raises `ValueError` (same contract as `clean_geometry`) instead of erroring inside `cmds.select`. (4) **`SceneDiagnostics`:** `fix_unknown_plugins` / `cleanup_scene` now return what they removed; deleted the never-called `_fix_color_management_globals` and the `locals()`-introspection reporting. (5) **`AnimCurveDiagnostics`:** collapsed the `repair_corrupted_curves` → `_repair_corrupted_curves` pass-through left over from removed legacy wrappers. (6) **Audit engine cleanups:** `generate_report`'s heaviest-textures pass builds one path→materials reverse map instead of rescanning every material per path (was O(paths × materials × textures)); dead per-texture bpp block dropped from `_analyze_material` (compressed estimates were always computed scene-scope in `generate_report`); redundant local `time`/`math`/`re` imports hoisted, and the pyflakes-dead locals (`count`, `raw_total_verts`/`raw_total_slots`, the superseded `slot_stats` dict) plus the never-called `_group_records` deleted. `get_ngons` also returns flattened face components now (matching `clean_geometry`'s granularity). Tests: +6 — shear return contract incl. the instance/uninstance path, `get_ngons` selection fallback + validation, repair-helper return shapes — 86/0 headless across the four affected modules; registry regenerated.
- **2026-07-18 — `ShadowRig` improvement pass: namespaced/duplicate-name targets fixed red-first, full-teardown `delete_rigs()` API, failed builds roll back, multi-plane bake batched into one pass.** (1) **Bug:** `_name_base` used the raw target name, so a namespaced target (`char:Box`) minted rig nodes inside the namespace and — nastier — a texture filename carrying a `:`, which NTFS silently writes as an **alternate data stream** on `sourceimages/char` (the PNG "saves", no file appears); a path-qualified target (duplicate leaf names → `|grp|Box`) crashed `create()` outright (`|` is illegal in the name flag; the texture save also raises `OSError`). The base now runs through `short_name` (path + namespace stripped). (2) **New teardown API** `ShadowRig.delete_rigs(planes=None, delete_textures=False)` + instance `delete()`: hand-deleting the `*_shadow_grp` orphaned the whole support graph — shader, SG, file/place2d/opacity-mult nodes, expression, decomposeMatrix pair, and the contact locator parented under the target. `create()` now stamps every support node it built onto the plane as a `shadowRigMembers` multi-message manifest (captured by set-diff around the build, so the ShaderFX graph's stock file nodes ride along; rename-proof), and `delete_rigs` removes manifest + connection-resolved live nodes + the enclosing group (only when named `*_shadow_grp` and holding nothing else — never a user's own parent), optionally the PNG on disk, then republishes (clears) the `shadow_metadata` channel. Works on live and baked rigs; targets and the shared source locator survive; pre-manifest rigs still lose plane/group/expression/dm via connections. (3) **Rollback:** a failed `create()` (e.g. mesh-less target) used to leave the just-built source/locator/plane behind — the build is now wrapped, deleting every created node + any half-written texture before re-raising; a non-transform node squatting the source name raises a clear `ValueError` up front instead of failing mid-build with a cryptic `xform` error. (4) **Perf:** `bake_planes` ran one `bakeResults(simulation=True)` — a full timeline replay — *per plane*; now one batched call over every live plane's plugs. (5) **Referenced rigs** (self-critique round): a referenced shadow plane made `bake_planes`/`delete_rigs` error mid-batch trying to delete reference-owned nodes (bake's hole was pre-existing) — both now skip it with a warning ("tear down / bake in the source file"), pinned by a real save→reference round-trip test. Also from the critique round: the expression/decomposeMatrix resolution shared by bake + delete deduplicated into `_live_rig_nodes`; `_link_members` moved inside `create()`'s try so a manifest-stamping failure also rolls the build back; Slots' axis/mode index maps promoted to class constants (`_AXIS_MAP`/`_MODE_MAP`, blendertk idiom); the redundant lazy `leaf_name` import dropped. Tests: `test_shadow_rig.py` +7 — five failing-first (namespaced, path-qualified, delete live+baked+`delete_textures`, rollback set-equality, source-name conflict) + batch-bake and referenced-skip coverage — 17/0 headless. **Mirrored in blendertk** (`delete_rigs`/`delete`, `shadowContact` pointer prop, + its own red-first overlapping-selection dedup fix — see its CHANGELOG); registries regenerated for both packages.

- **2026-07-18 — Tube Rig improvement pass: vertical-hose auto-bend fixed red-first, dense-mesh centerline 33× faster, bind nodes rig-prefixed, strategy dispatch opened up.** (1) **Bug:** `setup_auto_bend` drove the mid group's `translateY`, and translation runs in *parent* space (world-aligned) — on a vertical hose the mid control slid ALONG the tube axis instead of bowing outward, making auto-bend a silent no-op on any non-horizontal tube (probe-measured: max perpendicular offset 1.31 = pure volume bulge, zero bow). A chord-frame orient group (`_frame_rotation` of start→end; X down the hose, Y its stable perpendicular — Z-up fallback keeps vertical hoses defined) now sits above the bend group, so the driven `translateY` bows perpendicular at any orientation; horizontal rigs are unchanged (chord frame ≈ identity). Hierarchy: `Follow → ORIENT → AutoBend → Offset → Control`. blendertk's `_add_auto_bend` shares the defect class (drives `delta_location` Y; its own comment scopes it to "Z/X-aligned" tubes) — left for a live-Blender pass. (2) **Hygiene:** the driver-curve bind left default-named debris (`skinCluster1`, `bindPose1/2`) that name-based sweeps and multi-rig scenes can't attribute — `skin_curve_to_drivers` now names its cluster `<rig>_curve_skinCluster` (and returns it), and new `SkinUtils.name_bind_pose` renames a cluster's dagPose (`cmds.skinCluster` honors `name` for the cluster but never the pose); `SkinUtils.bind` applies it automatically (`<name>_pose`), covering the mesh bind for every caller including `bind_to_curve`. A rebuild-leak probe came back clean (teardown leaves zero rig nodes — the suspected orphan-dagPose leak does NOT exist since teardown deletes the joints too), so only naming changed. (3) **Perf:** `get_edge_loop_centers` dropped its per-edge `polyListComponentConversion` + per-vertex `pointPosition` round-trips (and the up-front all-edges string materialization) for one `MFnMesh` handle — loop topology via `getEdgeVertices`, world positions via a single `getPoints` — folding `_loop_is_closed` into the new `_loop_topology` (unique-edge tuple doubles as the seed-independent visited-set key): 0.66s → **0.02s** on a 201-loop tube, identical output. (4) **DRY:** the thrice-duplicated `closestPointOnMesh` create/connect/try-finally scaffolding (`_complete_cap_ends`, `estimate_radius`, `_refine_centers`) collapsed into a context manager — promoted in the critique round to public **`Components.closest_point_probe`** (core_utils) when the sweep found a fourth instance of the pattern in `Components.get_closest_vertex`. (5) **OCP:** `build()`'s if/elif strategy chain is now the `TubeRig.STRATEGIES` registry (name → `TubeStrategy` subclass; unknown names raise `NotImplementedError` listing valid keys) — new strategies register instead of editing `build`. (6) **Pre-existing bug found by that sweep, fixed red-first:** `Components.get_closest_vertex` fed WORLD-space query positions into a `closestPointOnMesh` whose `inputMatrix` was never connected (LOCAL space) — on any transformed target it returned the wrong vertex (probe-verified: a box at x=10 rotated 180° about Y returned its FAR side, world x=12, instead of the near side at 8; reachable from production via `Snap.snap_to_closest_vertex`). Routing it through `closest_point_probe` supplies the `worldMatrix → inputMatrix` hookup; identity-transform callers (and the `freeze_transforms=True` path) are unchanged. Tests: +3 failing-first (`test_vertical_hose_auto_bend_bows_perpendicular`, `test_build_nodes_carry_rig_prefix`, `test_get_closest_vertex_on_transformed_object`); tube_rig 51/0, skinning 40/0, components 52/0, snap 4/0 headless.

- **2026-07-18 — Blendshape Animator improvement pass: cross-setup data-loss closed (scene-global tween queries now setup-scoped), tag-based `from_existing` target detection, and the tween-creation core deduplicated.** All fixed red-first (10 new tests). **Cross-setup safety:** `Targets.find_all_targets` gains optional `blendshape=`/`base_mesh=` filters (leaf-name tolerant), and every per-setup consumer now scopes: `Creator.get_existing_weights` (setup B's weight 0.5 was silently offset to 0.501 because setup A held 0.5 — blendertk's twin already scoped this; mayatk never got the fix), `Applicator.apply_tweens(None)` (bulk-apply grabbed other setups' tweens and cross-applied them), `finalize_for_export` (**deleted other setups' tween meshes** — proven live), `cleanup_topology_mismatches`, `recover_animation`, `diagnose_topology_issues`, the slots tree/tooltip, and `Targets.update_all_references` (a recovery on one setup stomped every tween's tags scene-wide; now scoped by base mesh, which survives the rebuild). **`from_existing`:** tween targets are excluded via their `isInbetweenTarget` tag instead of the brittle name heuristic (a custom `name_prefix` — which the UI exposes — made the animator adopt a tween as its target mesh); name patterns remain only as a legacy-untagged fallback, and the chosen geometry is normalized to its transform (was sometimes bound to a shape or tween name). **Creator:** duplicated create sequences collapse into `_ensure_group`/`_duplicate_at_weight`/`_add_inbetween` (mirroring blendertk's factoring; the facade's `_process_existing_inbetweens` reuses `_add_inbetween`); `create_frame_based_tween` gains `group_name`/`name_prefix` params (defaults unchanged); weights outside (0,1) are skipped with a warning (Maya accepted a meaningless 1.5 in-between live); `find_nearby_weight` finally honors its `tolerance` parameter (was dead); `tag_tween_mesh` is table-driven; the tween group is created lazily and only after a successful attach (no stray empty group when a weight is skipped or the defensive duplicate-weight discard fires). Self-critique probe, pinned by test: on Maya 2025 an untagged in-between occupying a requested weight does NOT raise 'Weights must be unique' — the add silently **replaces** the slot, so the batch completes and the tool's tagged tween adopts the weight (the catch remains as defense for older Maya). **Facade:** `edit_weight_based`/`edit_frame_based`/`edit_apply_tweens` are `@CoreUtils.undoable` (one Ctrl+Z per batch) and pass `group_name`/`name_prefix` through; `recover_animation` no longer drops a tween recorded at frame 0 (`if t.target_frame` → explicit None check; negative ranges make frame 0 legal); the duplicated delete-tweens + empty-group blocks collapse into `Targets._delete_targets`/`Targets._delete_empty_groups`. **Slots:** `b001` routes through the facade methods (single code path, undo chunk, SSoT defaults) and honors the group/prefix overrides in Frame mode too (frame tweens from the UI now default to the `morph_ib` prefix; API default stays `tween_f*`); the context-menu delete uses the shared helpers so it also cleans up an emptied tween group. Tests: `test_blendshape_animator.py` +10 (all failing-first) — module 22/0; adjacent `anim_utils` 131/0, `static_analysis`, `maya_ui_handler` green. Registry regenerated. blendertk's twin already has the scoping/factoring; its `find_nearby_weight` shares the dead-tolerance quirk — left for a live-Blender pass.

- **2026-07-18 — `ScriptJobManager` hardening: shared-widget cleanup bug fixed, protected shared jobs, `suppressed()` context manager, visible listener errors.** (1) **Bug:** `connect_cleanup` tracked connected widgets by `id(widget)` alone, so a second owner registering cleanup on the *same* widget (e.g. two tools sharing one panel) was silently dropped — that owner's subscriptions leaked on widget destroy; now tracked per `(widget, owner)` pair (idempotent per pair, independent per owner). (2) **Bug:** `suppress()` recorded unknown/OM tokens unconditionally (dict leak; OM callbacks can't be gated anyway) — now guarded to live scriptJob subscriptions, and `_suppressed` is a set (matches blendertk). (3) **Bug:** if `cmds.scriptJob` creation raised, `subscribe` left orphan bookkeeping (subscription recorded with no backing job) — the job is now ensured before recording. (4) The shared jobs are created `protected=True`, so a stray `scriptJob -killAll` from another tool can't silently kill the central dispatcher (SJM's own `_kill_job` already uses `force=True`, which removes protected jobs). (5) New `suppressed(*tokens)` context manager replaces the manual suppress/try/finally/resume dance around scene-mutating code (skips `None` tokens; tokens already suppressed on entry stay suppressed on exit; suppression is **counted** — suppress increments / resume decrements — so nested and overlapping blocks compose, closing a self-review-caught race where a prior block's still-pending deferred resume un-silenced a currently-open block on the same token) — and with **idle-aware resume**: live GUI probing proved Maya runs event scriptJobs only at idle, so an event raised inside a synchronous block dispatches *after* the block exits, and an immediate resume lets it escape (observed live); interactive sessions therefore resume via default-priority `cmds.evalDeferred` — the queued dispatch registered first, so it runs (silenced) before the resume, proven live end-to-end (probe: swallow=0, resumed, next event fires); `lowestPriority=True` was tried first and **starves** (the resume never ran in 5+s of idle — token stuck suppressed, subscription dead), while batch sessions resume immediately (scriptJobs never dispatch there; a deferred callback might never run). This also means the pre-existing manual suppress→mutate→resume idiom never actually silenced the trailing dispatch in a GUI session — the render_opacity slots (both DCCs) are migrated to `suppressed()`. (6) Listener exceptions and OM registration failures now log at WARNING with traceback instead of DEBUG (a central dispatcher swallowing subscriber bugs invisibly); dead `cmds is None` guards removed (top-level import can never leave it None) and the `status()` docstring corrected (`om_callbacks` rows carry `cb_id`). Mirrored 1:1 in blendertk (same `connect_cleanup` bug existed there; `suppressed()` + warning-level errors added). Tests: mock suite +9 failing-first (40/0; full mock run 196/0), blendertk headless suite +5 checks (PASS under real Blender 5.1); protected-flag + real-dispatch behavior verified against a live GUI Maya via a fresh `MayaConnection` instance.
- **2026-07-18 — `Controls` fix + parity sweep: builtin-preset lockout and `combine()` world-position loss closed red-first; `shapes()` + `circle`/`square`/`cube` presets added (blendertk parity).** (1) `_register_builtin_presets` guarded on `if cls._PRESETS`, so calling `register_preset()` with a custom preset before the first `create()` permanently blocked every builtin (`create("diamond")` → *Unknown control preset*); now guarded by a dedicated `_builtins_registered` flag, and `create()`/the dynamic-preset metaclass register unconditionally (cheap after the flag) instead of wrapping registration in a swallowed `try`. (2) `combine()` merged shapes with `parent -r -s` into a transform matched to the **first** source's xform — local CVs unchanged means every *other* source snapped to that pivot, silently losing its world placement; each source is now parented under the combined transform (world preserved) and frozen so its offset bakes into the CVs before the shape merge. (3) Parity with `btk.Controls` (whose tentacle menu already offers shapes mayatk lacked): new `shapes()` classmethod (sorted preset names, for UI combos/validation), new `circle` (`sections=` kwarg) and `square` flat presets — the most standard rig controls, previously absent — and a `cube` alias for `box`. Also: `_match_transform` drops the parentConstraint+scaleConstraint create/delete dance for `cmds.matchTransform` (locked-scale fallback matches position/rotation only), failed `parent=` parenting in `create()`/`combine()` warns instead of passing silently, and the metaclass gains `__dir__` so dynamic presets show up in tab-completion/`dir(Controls)`. Self-critique round: `combine(delete_sources=False)` finally honors its contract — the shape merge *moves* shapes, so the flag had always left the "kept" sources as gutted husks (and the bake step would additionally have reparented/frozen them); sources are now duplicated up front and the duplicates consumed, leaving originals fully intact (shapes + placement, failing-first test), which let `_merge_curve_shapes` drop its now-always-True `delete_sources` param (sources are simply consumed; documented), and a failed placement-bake warns instead of passing silently. Tests: `test_controls.py` +8, all failing-first for the three bugs (registry-lockout repro, combine bbox spans both sources, keep-sources intact) — controls 25/0, tube_rig 49/0, remaining 34/0. Registry regenerated (`Controls.shapes` added). Follow-up: tentacle's Maya Create▸Control list is hand-curated and doesn't yet offer Circle/Square (Blender's does).: panel feedback restored via logger DI, one texture-conversion pass per restore, dead shading-group path removed.** (1) `GraphSaver`/`GraphRestorer` reported through `print`/`cmds.warning`/a detached stdlib logger (`ShaderTemplateManager`), so none of the user-relevant restore feedback — which textures resolved to which map slots, what's missing — ever reached the panel's redirected text widget (the widget handler lives on the slots' own LoggingMixin logger). Both classes now take an injected `logger` (facade passes it through: `save_template`/`restore_template` gain an optional `logger` kwarg, defaulting to the old stdlib logger for library callers), and the slots pass `self.logger`. Pinned by `test_logger_injection_receives_feedback`.
