(2) `_restore_node` built a fresh `TextureProcessor` per file node, discarding its per-instance conversion cache — a derived map shared by several slots (e.g. an ORM pack feeding metallic/roughness/AO) was recomputed per node; the processor is now created once per `restore_graph`. Its in-memory-PIL fallback also replaced a hand-rolled `%TEMP%` writer (orphaned `generated_*_<id>.png` files) with the processor's own `save_map` — generated maps now land beside the source textures with standard naming/optimization. (3) Removed `GraphRestorer._handle_shading_group`: `create_render_node(create_shading_group=…)` already creates and wires the SG from the saved `connected_to_shading_engine` metadata; the extra path was unreachable for every stock surface type (`"shader" in node_type` matches none of them) and would have double-created an SG if it ever fired. Also: restored attributes are copied before texture-path fixups (no more mutating the loaded YAML config in place), and all remaining root-`logging` calls unified onto the instance logger. Self-critique hardening: `load_yaml` rejects a template that parses to a non-dict (previously `AttributeError` on `.values()`), generated-map `output_dir` is `abspath`-anchored (a bare relative texture filename would have sent conversions to the CWD), the slots' Delete/Open-file menu actions guard an empty template combo (`os.path.exists(None)` raised `TypeError`), and rename shares `b002`'s name sanitizer, rejects empty names, no-ops on an unchanged name (focus-out fired a spurious "already exists" error), and drops the combo out of edit mode afterward (it stayed editable forever). Pre-existing test debt fixed in `test_shader_templates.py`: `test_partial_restoration_and_attributes` had three vacuous PyMel-migration blocks (`hasattr`/`getattr` on node-name *strings*, always falsy) so its attribute-restore and Base_Color-connection assertions never executed — rewritten on `attributeQuery`/`listConnections` (with compound-children fallback) and passing, proving the StingrayPBS re-apply path; the file's unused auto-injected `_pm_*` shims removed. Tests 13/0 headless. **Registry regen deferred** (tree mid-WIP; the `logger` kwargs are the only public-surface change).

- **2026-07-18 — SmartBake fix sweep: four bugs closed red-first, plus perf/cleanup.** (1) `delete_inputs` treated the inherited-visibility bookkeeping (`inherited_visibility` ancestor curves + `inherited_visibility_plugs` plug strings) as driver inputs — it errored trying to `delete()` a plug string and **deleted the parent's own visibility animCurve**, destroying animation that was never baked (`_mute_driver_nodes` already skipped these; the delete loop now does too). (2) Non-restorable sessions (`delete_inputs` base-layer mode) still stashed the child's vis curve, but `restore()` refuses such sessions — the locked `__smartBakeStash` nodes leaked into the scene permanently; the vis pass now records/stashes only for restorable sessions. (3) An object baked by BOTH the vis pass and the standard channel pass lost its `"v"` entry — phase 2 assigned `result.baked[obj]` instead of merging, mis-reporting and starving the optimize-keys fallback. (4) `_get_objects()` listed every joint twice (`ls(type="transform")` already includes joints — joint derives from transform); now deduped. Perf: the per-joint O(joints×handles) IK-chain scan is skipped entirely when the scene has no ikHandles. Cleanup: dead `_type_cache` removed, `backup_file` typed `Union[bool, str, None]`, `_mute_driver_nodes` takes `Dict[str, BakeAnalysis]`, inline `collections`/`AnimUtils` imports hoisted/deduped, `result.skipped` deduped. Tests: `test_smart_bake.py` +3 (dup query, vis+transform channel merge, destructive-vis stash-leak + parent-animation survival) — all failing-first; module 60/0, slots 10/0.

- **2026-07-18 — Hierarchy-sync path helpers delegate to pythontk's new `HierarchyPath` (no behavior change).** The module-level `clean_hierarchy_path` / `get_clean_node_name_from_string` / `format_component` in `_hierarchy_sync.py` were verbatim re-implementations of path primitives pythontk kept private; they now delegate upstream (`HierarchyPath.strip_namespaces` / `clean_namespace(leaf())` / `clean_namespace`) with signatures and all ~15 call sites unchanged, and `tree_utils.py` swaps its pokes at the `HierarchyMatching._clean_namespace` / `HierarchyIndexer._join_hierarchy_path` privates for the public `HierarchyPath` API (the upstream shims remain, so released mayatk builds keep working against new pythontk). `get_clean_node_name` keeps its Maya-specific `cmds.ls` fallback untouched. **Requires the pythontk release that ships `HierarchyPath` — publish pythontk first.**

- **2026-07-18 — `PlayblastExporter` overhaul: capture-once / encode-many target registry replaces the ad-hoc variations API (breaking).** The old surface (`create_playblast`, `export_variations` + per-variation dicts, cached `start_frame`/`end_frame`, the `tentacleEnable*` optionVar coupling) is gone; the new one is four primitives + one orchestrator: `capture_sequence` (numbered frames, **real scene frame numbers**), `capture_still` (exact single file via `completeFilename`), `capture_movie` (native `avi`; the QuickTime-era `qt`/MOV formats are dropped — dead on modern Windows Maya), `encode_sequence` (ffmpeg via `ptk.VidUtils.compress_video`), and `export(output_dir, name, targets=…)` planning over a `TARGETS` registry (`ExportTarget` dataclass: mp4/mov/png·jpg·tif·tga_sequence/still/avi/arnold; UIs build pickers from `available_targets()`). `export` captures the viewport **once** per needed image format — every encode target rides the same lossless PNG capture (the old path re-captured per variation, and MP4 meant a giant uncompressed AVI intermediate), sharing it with a requested `png_sequence` deliverable or cleaning a `_png_tmp` dir (`keep_frames` to retain). Per-target failures land on `ExportResult.error` (dependents of a failed capture are isolated; independent targets still run). **Bugs fixed:** encoded outputs from ranges not starting near frame 0 (ffmpeg `start_number` — needs today's pythontk, **publish pythontk first**); "still" now captures one frame (the old preset wrote the whole range); camera override retargets only the captured panel (`editorPanelName`) instead of rewriting *every* model panel; `render_with_arnold` returns only files the run wrote (mtime/pre-existence snapshot — stale prefix-matching frames were returned as new) and maps the `ai_translator` to the real extension (`jpeg`→`jpg` etc.); frame ranges resolve at call time (`resolve_frame_range(mode, …)` with playback/animation/current/custom) instead of being frozen at construction; tif sequences validated (missing from the old extension map); debug `print` → `LoggingMixin`. New: `include_audio` attaches the timeline's active sound (`resolve_sound_node`; native `sound` flag for AVI, ffmpeg mux with frame-offset→seconds conversion for MP4/MOV), quality now also drives the encode CRF, and `scene_fps`/`scene_name` are static queries. Post-review hardening: `capture_sequence` pre-cleans prefix-matching frames left by an earlier/wider run (they passed the frame-count check and ffmpeg reads a printf pattern contiguously past `end`, encoding stale frames into the movie); `_collect_frames` sorts numerically (lexicographic misordered across a padding boundary, "10000" < "9999"); plugin load reuses `EnvUtils.load_plugin`; `export()` strips owned keys from `**overrides` (duplicate-kwarg TypeError) and warns when a camera override can't apply (no model panel in batch). Tests rewritten (`test_playblast_exporter.py`, 21 tests — registry/planner with mocked primitives + real mayapy captures: offset-range MP4 frame-count, single-file still, stale-frame cleanup, scene-fps duration, Arnold stale-file regression) — all pass headless, 0 skips. Consumer migrated: tentacle `rendering.py` (see its CHANGELOG).

- **2026-07-18 — Live-Maya contract test for the shared workspace format: `test/test_workspace_mel.py` (3 tests).** blendertk now reads/writes/creates Maya project workspaces through the new zero-dep `pythontk.file_utils.workspace` codec; this suite proves the contract against the real runtime, both directions: Maya **opens** a codec-written project and resolves its file rules — a foreign (Blender-side) `blenderScene` rule included — and Maya's own rewrite (`workspace -saveWorkspace`, after a Maya-side `fileRule` addition) **preserves** that foreign rule while the codec parses Maya's output back; `ptk.Workspace.find_containing` also agrees with `cmds.workspace -q -rootDirectory` about which project owns a saved scene. No mayatk production change — `cmds.workspace` stays the in-Maya authority for the active project (the codec matters downstream, where there's no Maya to ask). Deferred follow-up: listing `.blend` scenes in the Reference Manager / WorkspaceManager tables and routing opens through `import_blender_scene` (needs a live-GUI pass).

- **2026-07-18 — `auto_instance` can now report *why* matching meshes weren't instanced** (object simplicity / count), for the Duplicate slot to surface in its message box + console. `AutoInstancer` gains a `last_run_summary` dict — `matched_groups` (viable groups of ≥2 identical meshes), `instanced_groups`/`instances_created`, `simple_groups` (identical but below `MICRO_TRI_THRESHOLD` → combined, not instanced), `kept_separate_groups` (flagged individual / non-static), `micro_threshold`, and per-group `details` — populated in `_process_groups` (bookkeeping only; no change to the instancing decisions). New static helpers `AutoInstancer.default_summary()` (the shape) and `AutoInstancer.format_summary(summary, output_count)` (ASCII human text, so a non-UTF-8 console `print` can't raise). The `auto_instance(...)` wrapper takes `return_summary=False` (default → bare list, backward compatible); `True` → `(created, summary)`. Tests: `test_auto_instancer.py::TestAutoInstancerRunSummary` (+5). Also fixed a **pre-existing headless failure** in the same suite: `_assign_new_material` used `cmds.hyperShade(assign=…)`, a GUI command that silently no-ops under mayapy / `--background` (the `connectWindow.mel` errors) so objects stayed on the default shader and `combine_by_material` mis-merged — now assigns via a shading-group + `sets forceElement`.

- **2026-07-17 — `MayaUiHandler.__init__` registers `UiUtils.dispatch_log_link` into uitk's new log-link registry** (`uitk.bridge.register_log_link_handler`), so uitk no longer hard-imports mayatk for log-panel node actions (dependency inversion — see the uitk CHANGELOG). Registration is at the handler init point (runtime, not module import — no import side effects) and idempotent. Wiring pinned by `test_maya_ui_handler.py::TestMayaUiHandlerLogLinkRegistration`: the registration is `try/except`-guarded in `__init__` (never block UI startup), so the test exercises the exact import path to catch silent drift if `UiUtils.dispatch_log_link` ever moves.

- **2026-07-17 — Duplicate Grid 'Combine' no longer crashes when the selection carries non-mesh objects.** `DuplicateGrid._combine` gated `polyUnite` on the raw copy count (`len(objects) > 1`), but `polyUnite` demands at least *two polygonal* objects — so a selection mixing a mesh with a locator/curve/empty-group (only one poly copy reaching the unite), or a pure non-mesh selection with count > 1 (zero poly copies), aborted the whole preview/commit with the cryptic `Invalid selection : polyUnite needs at least 2 polygonal objects` (live report from a preview refresh). `_combine` now partitions the copies via a new `_is_polygonal` predicate (descends so a group-of-meshes still counts, matching how `polyUnite` gathers inputs): ≥2 meshes → unite them (non-mesh copies discarded with the holder, as before — combine's one-mesh contract); exactly 1 → lift-and-rename (unchanged 1×1×1 path); 0 → nothing to merge, so it falls back to grouping the copies (`<source>_grid_grp`, like Unique mode) and warns instead of crashing. Tests: `test_edit_tools_duplicate.py` +2 (`test_combine_with_non_mesh_in_selection_does_not_crash` — mesh+locator 1×1×1 yields one named mesh; `test_combine_pure_non_mesh_falls_back_to_group` — locator-only 3×1×1 yields a 3-child group, no fabricated mesh); 47/0. blendertk's mirror (`_join_copies`) already guards the <2 case but would still raise on a pure non-mesh combine (`object.join()` poll fails on a non-mesh active) — left for a separate live-Blender pass since it's a shared helper.

- **2026-07-17 — Hierarchy Sync: 'Filter Cameras' / 'Filter Lights' diff options — exclude all cameras / all lights from the comparison.** Two checkboxes in the Diff Options combo beside Filter Mesh Objects, read by both `tb001` (Diff button) and `b012` (menu action) and threaded through `HierarchySyncController.analyze_hierarchies` → the engine's existing `filter_cameras`/`filter_lights` params. Fixes a latent no-op: type filtering via `should_keep_node_by_type` now matches on each shape's *inherited* node-type chain (`cmds.nodeType(inherited=True)`), so the base class `light` catches concrete shapes (`pointLight`, `directionalLight`, `spotLight`, …) which `cmds.nodeType` never reports as a bare `light` — `filter_lights` previously matched nothing. Maya's default cameras (persp/top/front/side) remain always-excluded regardless. Test: `test_hierarchy_sync.py` +1 (`test_filter_cameras_and_lights`: real camera + point light — on → both excluded, off → both extra); 305/305.

- **2026-07-17 — Hierarchy sidecar: a deleted or corrupt manifest now compares against its `.prev` backup instead of silently passing.** Follow-up to a design re-check of sidecar-vs-in-scene baseline storage (sidecar upheld: the baseline is metadata about the *export artifact* — shared across scenes, users, and DCCs — while an in-scene store only persists if the scene is saved after export and can't be read cross-DCC; its durability advantage is illusory). The one real gap: `write_manifest` has always preserved the outgoing baseline as `.prev` on every content change, but `compare()` treated a missing manifest as an instant pass — so the exact loss cases the backup exists for (cleanup scripts, cloud-sync pruning of dotfiles) skipped the drift check. New `HierarchySidecar._load_manifest` tries the manifest then falls back to `.prev` (also covering a corrupt/partial manifest write); `read_manifest`/`compare` route through it, and `check_hierarchy_vs_existing_fbx` distinguishes the cases — no baseline at all keeps the existing "manifest will be created" notice, manifest-missing-but-backup-present proceeds with a visible "compared against its .prev backup" message (the manifest self-heals on the next export since the check ran). Worst case is unchanged (neither file → one uncovered export, visible notice, re-baseline); intact manifest always wins over `.prev`. Mirrored 1:1 in blendertk. Tests: `test_hierarchy_sidecar.py` +6 (`PrevFallbackTest`: deletion fallback match + drift detection, corrupt-manifest fallback, `read_manifest` fallback, no-files pass, manifest-wins-over-prev — also the file's first direct `compare()` coverage), `test_scene_exporter.py` +1 (task-level fallback notice); scene_exporter 75/0.

- **2026-07-17 — USD phase 2: the Blender-scene import bridge gains a USD intermediate (`import_blender_scene(via="usd")`), live-verified A/B against the FBX route.** New `templates/_import_scene_usd.py` exports the .blend via `wm.usd_export` (kwarg-tolerance loop across Blender versions; `export_textures_mode="KEEP"` so the artifact references ORIGINAL texture files — 5.1's copy-default wrote refs into scratch `textures/` that cleanup then deleted, observed live; `merge_parent_xform` + empty `root_prim_path` for flat FBX-parity structure), and `BlenderSceneImport` routes per `via`: template selection, per-route cache key (template identity), `.usd` artifact, native mayaUsd import — **no texture manifest**, materials arrive as real UsdPreviewSurface conversions. Live e2e (`scene_import_live_e2e.py`, USD leg added): linked Base_Color textures arrive natively with zero manifest rebuild, two-material per-face split survives as GeomSubsets. Known route asymmetry, asserted in the e2e: UNLINKED/packed images (`Normal_OpenGL`/`Metallic_Smoothness` transported by the FBX route's manifest-by-filename) are NOT carried by USD — it exports the actual node network; pick the route per scene. FBX stays the default.

- **2026-07-17 — Hierarchy Sync: 'Hide Ignored' header toggle — hide ignored items from the trees instead of only dimming them.** A `chk_hide_ignored` checkbox in the header menu (beside Dry Run / Log Level); when on, the controller's `_hide_ignored` flag makes `apply_ignore_styling` call `setHidden(True)` on every ignored item (direct *and* inherited-via-ancestor) rather than just striking it through, and clearing it reveals them again. Folded into the single existing tree-walk in `apply_ignore_styling` (no second pass); the styling method now runs one uniform loop (the empty-ignored-set early-return is gone) so visibility always reconciles even as the ignored set changes. Mirrored 1:1 into blendertk (parity sweep: 0 prop deltas). The shared hide logic is locked by a Qt renderer test (blendertk `test_hierarchy_tree_utils.py`, +5 checks: hide-off dims/keeps-visible, hide-on hides direct+inherited but not un-ignored, toggle-back reveals).

- **2026-07-17 — USD support: `env_utils/usd.py` (`mtk.UsdUtils`) over the native `mayaUsd` runtime, + a USD fast path in the Blender-scene import bridge.** The USD sibling of `FbxUtils` (same module shape and verbs; mirrored by `btk.UsdUtils`): `load_plugin` (mayaUsdPlugin), `export` via `cmds.mayaUSDExport` with interchange-quality defaults (registry shading converted to `UsdPreviewSurface`, instances kept instanceable, transform+shape merged), and `import_scene` through the `cmds.file` *USD Import* translator so imports get the same active-namespace isolation the `.ma`/FBX paths have. A `.usdz` destination exports a temp text layer and packages it self-contained via `pythontk.UsdzPackager.from_layer` (textures pulled in-package). `BlenderSceneImport.import_scene` now short-circuits `.usd*` sources to this native import — no headless Blender launch, cache, or manifest involved (USD is what the FBX round-trip's texture-manifest machinery approximates; materials survive the hop natively). Verified headless: `test_usd.py` (12 tests — usd/usda/usdz export, spec-valid packages, namespace-isolated round-trip, fast path proven with a bogus Blender path) + `test_scene_import.py` still green.

- **2026-07-17 — Renamed the `hierarchy_manager` tool → `hierarchy_sync`. "Manager" undersold a tool that imports a reference scene/FBX, diffs the current scene's structure against it, and *reconciles* the differences (create stubs / reparent / fuzzy-rename fix / quarantine + object Pull) — i.e. it **syncs a scene to a reference**.** Module dir `env_utils/hierarchy_manager/` → `env_utils/hierarchy_sync/`; files `_hierarchy_manager.py` / `hierarchy_manager_slots.py` / `hierarchy_manager_ui.py` / `hierarchy_manager.ui` → `hierarchy_sync_*`; engine class `HierarchyManager` → `HierarchySync` (and `HierarchyManagerController` → `HierarchySyncController`); panel discovery key + `.ui` → `hierarchy_sync`; `test_hierarchy_manager.py` → `test_hierarchy_sync.py`. `ObjectSwapper` and `HierarchySidecar` keep their names — the sidecar is descriptively named and is really the **SceneExporter's** export-drift baseline (the interactive tool only borrows its two stateless formatting helpers, `get_top_level`/`count_descendants`), so it stays put rather than inherit the tool name (a later move to `scene_exporter/` is the cleaner home, deferred). Mirrored 1:1 in blendertk + tentacle (Scene submenu label + `marking_menu.show("hierarchy_sync")` for both the Maya and Blender scene slots). Verified: `hierarchy_sync` 304/0 (+20 extended-deferred), `hierarchy_sidecar` 23/0, `scene_exporter` 74/0, and `mtk.HierarchySync` / `HierarchySyncController` resolve in a fresh mayapy. **Registry/parity regen deferred** — a module-path rename must regenerate `API_REGISTRY` / `PARITY_SURFACE` / `API_SHADOWS`, and the tree is mid-WIP, so those run in the next clean-tree refresh (which already owes the FBX-refactor surface entry below).

- **2026-07-17 — FBX reference import rebuilt on native namespace isolation: `FBXImporter` sheds ~970 lines of manual per-node namespace-moving, 6 re-find fallbacks, and live-scene object shelving; the reusable FBX-import primitive now lives in `FbxUtils.import_scene`. Two pre-existing regressions fixed along the way — every `.ma/.mb` import through `NamespaceSandbox` was silently broken, and the entire extended real-scene test suite never ran.** The old `FBXImporter` imported the FBX at root and then hand-renamed every transform, material and shading engine into the sandbox namespace — because the FBX translator ignores `cmds.file(namespace=…)`. That drove a huge, fragile apparatus: `_move_objects_to_namespace`, six fallback strategies to re-find the moved nodes, and `_handle_import_name_conflicts`/`_restore_renamed_objects`, which *temporarily renamed the user's own root-scene objects* to dodge import clashes (a genuine data-safety liability). Probed live on Maya 2025: the translator **does** honor the *active* namespace — `cmds.namespace(set=…)` before `cmds.file(i=True, type="FBX")` isolates the whole import (transforms **and** materials/shading engines) under that namespace natively, exactly as `.ma/.mb` imports already are, and root-scene objects are never touched (clashes become impossible, so the shelving has nothing to work around). `FBXImporter` is now a thin delegator over the new **`FbxUtils.import_scene(file, namespace=…)`** — the general, headless-testable FBX-import counterpart to `FbxUtils.export`, owning plugin load + import options (applied best-effort and quietly through `set_fbx_options`, since import setters mix syntaxes — `FBXImportMode -v add` vs bare `FBXImportUpAxis y` — and some don't exist, e.g. there is no `FBXImportMaterials`) + the active-namespace-scoped `cmds.file` call, restoring the prior active namespace even on failure. Verified against the real 207 MB `C5_AFT_COMP_ASSEMBLY.fbx`: the `MA-vs-FBX` diff baseline is byte-identical (0 missing / 2 extra / 0 reparented). **Regression #1 (`.ma/.mb` import, shipped-broken):** the April PyMel→cmds migration replaced `pm.importFile(…)` with `cmds.file(…)` in both `MayaImporter` methods but dropped the `-i` flag `pm.importFile` implied, so every Maya-scene reference import failed with *"-returnNewNodes can only be used with -open/-import/-reference"* and returned `None`. Restored `i=True`; the `MA-vs-MA` baseline (48/4/1) passes again. **Regression #2 (dead test suite):** every `@skipUnlessExtended` real-scene test guarded its fixtures with `cmds.objExists(str(<path>))` — which tests for a *Maya object*, never a file on disk, so it always skipped — several also referencing a bare `real_scenes_dir` that raised `NameError` first; the whole extended regression suite (C5/C17/C130/C5M) had never executed an assertion. Guards corrected to `Path.exists()`; the suite runs. **Tests:** `import_scene` round-trip + namespace-isolation + active-namespace-restore + missing-file coverage in `test_fbx_utils` (17/0); a permanent `TestImportRoundTrip` in `test_namespace_sandbox` locks **both** regressions (a synthetic `.ma`/`.fbx` round-trip through `NamespaceSandbox` — the MA case returns `None` without the `i=True` fix; the FBX case plants a colliding root object and asserts it survives untouched) (26/0). Removed two dead-code tests that drove the now-deleted `_move_objects_to_namespace` directly (their edge cases — per-node rename ambiguity on same-name parent/child, nested-namespace stripping — are structurally impossible under native import and are covered end-to-end by the C5/C17 real-scene tests). `test_hierarchy_manager` 304/0 (+20 extended-deferred). **Self-review hardening:** `_create_unique_namespace` now disambiguates against the live scene — its `int(time.time())` name is only 1-second resolution, so two same-second imports collided and the second wiped the first's temp namespace — and the FBX import path clears a reused namespace before importing (matching `MayaImporter`, so a stale import can't inflate the re-queried transform list). Registry regen deferred (tree mid-WIP; `import_scene` is the only public-surface addition).

- **2026-07-17 — Hierarchy Manager audit fix-sweep: two animation data-loss paths closed, `skip_animated` honors its documented contract, repair passes stop starving each other, and the post-PyMel stale-string handles are gone (nodes tracked by UUID).** Full-file audit (core + slots + sidecar + tree helpers) with every confirmed finding fixed red-first; 306/0 module tests (GUI pass) + 23/0 sidecar. **Animation safety:** (1) `_transfer_anim_curves` is now **all-or-nothing** — destination attrs are pre-flighted and mid-transfer failures roll back; previously a partial transfer followed by "preserve existing" left the moved curves on the replacement the caller then deleted, permanently destroying the existing node's animation (the EC-10 test masked it by asserting existence, not keys — now it asserts both; `test_transfer_partial_success` re-pinned to the new contract). The transfer also aligns `rotateOrder` when rotation curves move and detaches (rather than deletes) replacement curves that other plugs share. (2) `quarantine_extras(skip_animated=True)` now also checks **animated ancestors** — the docstring/tooltip always promised "extras parented under an animated object are left in place" but the implementation only checked the node itself, so attached props were silently detached from their motion. (3) `fix_reparented` gained the same `skip_animated` guard (wired to the repair checkbox): keyed/driven/expression/motion-path nodes are skipped with a visible note (their curves evaluate in parent space — moving them changes world motion); constraint-only nodes still move (Maya rewires `parentInverseMatrix`). (4) `_has_animation_data` blind spots closed: motion paths, `pairBlend`s, constraints connected from outside the node's children, and **shape-level animation** (keyed camera focal length etc.) — descendant sweeps now include shapes. (5) `_safe_merge_delete` preserves nodes that are **referenced** (delete always errored into the fallback), have an **ambiguous short name**, or **drive animation elsewhere** (driven-key driver / constraint target / expression input — deleting them silently broke other nodes). **Correctness:** repair order is now renames → stubs → **reparent → quarantine** (quarantining an extra parent stranded its reparented child on a stale path); the now-empty old parent is no longer deleted when the **reference expects it** (stub churn) and its cleanup failures aren't mislogged as failed reparents; stub/parent-chain creation uses `createNode(parent=)` so a root-level name clash can't auto-suffix the chain (`B`→`B1` corrupted the rebuild); dry-run stub previews skip already-existing paths; namespace-stripping collisions (`ns:GRP` vs `GRP`) are tracked and repairs refuse the ambiguous paths instead of coin-flipping; `_detect_reparented` confirms shape-type compatibility before pairing leaf-name matches (a missing mesh no longer pairs with an unrelated same-named empty group, which `fix_reparented` would then move); `_resolve_node` falls back to a live cleaned-path scene lookup when the recorded path went stale. **Pull integration:** nodes are tracked by **UUID** across rename/reparent — the old string handles went stale after `_cleanup_namespaces` renamed the root, so *every* live pull exited through the exception fallback with a spurious "integration failed" warning (tests couldn't see it: their assertions string-sliced the input); a new namespace guard force-strips pulled nodes (unlocking if needed) so nothing pulled can remain in a `temp_import_*` namespace for the post-pull cleanup to delete. **Locked stubs** no longer break quarantine, Delete Selected, or drag-reparent (unlock/relock around each). **Slots/UI:** multi-item drags and batch renames survive nested selections (UUIDs + deepest-first + descendant-path patching); drag-reparenting an animated node warns; `tb002`'s ignore filter compares tree paths (leaf names never matched — ignored items were pulled anyway); tree-item extraction prefers the stored DAG path over the ambiguous leaf name; recent-scenes settings coerce QSettings' bare-string round-trip; `b017` runs in an undo chunk; `_apply_diff_options` shed ~600 lines of debug scaffolding into three small helpers (same selection/expansion behavior). **Removed (breaking):** `ObjectSwapper.push_objects_to_scene` — it silently performed a *pull* (imported the target file into the current scene); its entire support chain (`NamespaceSandbox.import_to_target_scene`) was an unimplemented stub that returned success, and it now fails honestly instead. Also: iterative path-map traversal (deep hierarchies no longer hit the recursion limit), the type-filter map uses `cmds` instead of dead PyMel calls (`inc_types`/`exc_types` classified everything "unknown"), `analyze_hierarchies` failures reset stale diff state, sidecar manifests write temp-then-swap (a failed write can no longer eat the baseline) and `HierarchySidecar.rename` migrates base-stem sidecars too, and `tree_renderer`'s import guard that was pasted *inside* the module docstring is repaired. ~40 new regression tests, all failing-first where mayapy could run them.

- **2026-07-17 — `run_tests.py` is now HEADLESS-BY-DEFAULT: chunked fresh-mayapy execution with automatic crash attribution/resume and one GUI pass for the modules that genuinely need a GUI session — full `--all` drops ~50 min → ~18 min, badge-verified green (3747/0).** The in-session harness moved out of the runner's giant command-port payload string into the shared `test/_suite_driver.py`, so the headless (`maya.standalone`) and GUI (command-port) paths run identical per-module logic (real-maya-module snapshot/restore, shots-prefs sandbox, per-module scene reset, per-module wall time). Modules run in chunks of ≤12 per FRESH mayapy process (one long standalone session accumulates native state and hard-crashes); `STARTED/FINISHED/INIT_DONE/DONE` progress markers let the orchestrator attribute a native crash to its module, resume the chunk's remainder in a fresh process, and defer the offender to the GUI pass — proven live: 8 modules crash-deferred on the first full run and all passed GUI-side. The `GUI_REQUIRED` registry seeds the known set (14 modules: Qt-widget crashers like hierarchy_manager's reference-tree population — re-confirmed crashing ALONE in a fresh process — sequencer/shot_manifest's event-loop callbacks, and batch-incompatible-by-design suites: `hotkeySet` raises under batch, `displayRGBColor` queries return None, native-menu wrapping needs the GUI menu bar, maya_connection asserts interactive-mode detection). New flags: `--gui` (force the old port path), `--no-gui-pass`, `--chunk-size`, `--jobs` (concurrent chunks, standalone inits staggered against license-checkout races), `--module-timeout` (rolling headless hang detection, default 900s), `--mayapy` (discovery otherwise delegates to `pythontk.AppLauncher.resolve_app_path` — no hand-rolled scan). Results gain a SLOWEST MODULES section (top: test_maya_connection at ~360s of deliberate dead-port retry tests — future trim candidate). **Badge integrity:** the badge now updates only on a complete `--all` run where every in-scope module produced results — closes the twice-bitten scoped-run badge clobber, and modules that never ran are listed under `NOT RUN` instead of silently vanishing. **Per-test speedup:** `MayaTkTestCase.tearDown` no longer re-wipes the scene (setUp wipes, and the driver resets between modules) — halves the ~7.5k per-run `file(new,force)` calls; new `base_test.skipIfBatch` marks interactive-only tests (applied to `test_align_pivot_to_selection`; `snap3PointsTo3Points` is GUI-sourced MEL). Two real fixes shaken out by the first headless full run: `test_channels.test_keyframe_connection` now pins `currentTime(5)` — a fresh batch scene lands on frame 1 where a key sits, and `classify_connection` correctly promotes to `keyframe_active` (companion test added for the promote path); and **`PlayblastExporter.scene_name` requires a file extension before trusting `cmds.file(q, sceneName)`** — batch reports a phantom extensionless `…/untitled` path for an unsaved scene (GUI reports `""`), so batch callers got `"untitled"` instead of the documented `"playblast"` fallback (same rule SceneExporter already learned). Also: `--no-wait` no longer force-closes the Maya it just launched (the old finally-shutdown killed fire-and-forget runs unless `--keep-maya`). Verified: full `--all` = 3747 tests / 0 failures / 0 errors in 17.8 min (headless bulk at seconds-per-module + 14-module GUI pass); registry regenerated (no public-surface change).

- **2026-07-17 — uitk restructure sync: deep imports of `uitk.widgets.mixins.{settings_manager,state_manager,icon_manager,preset_manager}` → `uitk.managers.*`; case-broken `.ui` custom-widget headers corrected.** uitk moved its standalone service modules out of `widgets/mixins/` into `uitk/managers/` (+ `uitk/themes/` for `StyleSheet`) — path sync only, no behavior change (details in uitk's CHANGELOG). Also `duplicate_*.ui`, `channels.ui`, `reference_manager.ui`, and `curve_to_tube.ui` referenced `uitk.widgets.combobox` / `uitk.widgets.table_widget` — casing/underscore variants of the real `comboBox` / `tableWidget` modules that only resolve on a case-insensitive filesystem; corrected to the real module casing.

- **2026-07-17 — Retired `mat_utils/mat_transfer.py` + `mat_utils/shader_remapper.py` (breaking removal); `DEFAULT_INCLUDE` bare keys → full dotted paths.** The two modules were referenced by no production code anywhere in the monorepo, absent from `DEFAULT_INCLUDE` (so `mtk.MatTransfer` / `mtk.ShaderRemapper` never resolved), and carried empty stub docstrings — parked WIP from the pre-`mat_manifest` shader-normalization cluster; their tests retired with them. `ShaderAttributeMap` stays (`mat_manifest` composes it). The include dict's 15 legacy bare-basename keys (`"_anim_utils"` …) are now full dotted paths (`"anim_utils._anim_utils"`) — the resolver matched bare keys by module *basename* against every module in the package walk, so the dotted form removes that basename-collision ambiguity; the wildcard **values** are retained deliberately and now documented in place: the wildcard AST scan publishes each class's public methods as package attributes (328 method-level names, e.g. `mtk.get_parent` → `NodeUtils.get_parent`), a surface the explicit name-list form cannot express. Verified: resolver-surface snapshot before/after byte-identical (130 classes / 328 methods / 16 submodules); full suite green.

- **2026-07-17 — Structure cleanup: `audio_utils/segments/` flattened to `segments.py`; the bridge-slots base module renamed to match its class.** The `segments` package held a single real module (`discovery.py`) behind an `__init__` re-export shim, so it's now the flat module `audio_utils/segments.py` — the consumer import path (`from mayatk.audio_utils.segments import collect_all_segments`) is unchanged, only the internal `segments.discovery` layer is gone. `ui_utils/maya_bridge_slots.py` → `maya_bridge_slots_base.py`: the file holds `MayaBridgeSlotsBase` (restoring the file↔class naming convention), and the old name was character-identical to blendertk's `env_utils/maya_bridge/maya_bridge_slots.py` — a *panel* slots file, not a base — so the same filename meant two different things across the mirror pair; all five bridge-slot subclass imports updated (blender/unity/marmoset/substance/rizom). `__init__.py`: dead trailing note lines removed and the `env_utils.devtools` include moved into the Environment group it belongs to. Registry regenerated (path-only moves, identical signatures); parity sweep exit 0. Audit notes from the same pass: `mat_utils/mat_transfer.py` and `mat_utils/shader_remapper.py` are on the published registry surface and test-covered but referenced by no production code and absent from `DEFAULT_INCLUDE` — left in place pending a keep-or-retire call.

- **2026-07-16 — Native-menu wrappers are now named `<key>_menu`, not the bare menu key — 'render' and 'create' were shadowing `QWidget.render()` / `QWidget.create()` on the window that hosts them** (live report: `[render] [register_widget]: objectName 'render' shadows MainWindow.render(); not binding it as an attribute`). `MainWindow.register_widget` binds every registered child's objectName as an attribute on the window, and `get_menu` named the `EmbeddedMenuWidget` after its mapping key — so for exactly the two keys that collide with a `QWidget` method, uitk's shadow guard warned and skipped the convenience binding (before that guard existed, the same line would have clobbered the method outright, the failure mode the `move`-named `CollapsableGroup` hit). Nothing read the objectName — lookups go through `self.menus[menu_key]`, and `add_ui` takes the window name separately (the *window* stays `render`, so marking-menu target resolution is untouched) — so the rename is contained to the registration handle. Regression `TestNativeMenuObjectName` drives the real `get_menu` (Qt/Maya boundary stubbed, per the file's existing pattern) over **every** `MENU_MAPPING` entry rather than the two that collide today, since a new mapping entry is the likely way this comes back; red-first, it reported `{'create': 'create', 'render': 'render'}`. `maya_menu_handler` 18 + `native_menu_window` 9 + `maya_ui_handler` 6 + `ui_utils` 1 green. Mirrored in blendertk, where the colliding keys are 'render' and 'window'.

- **2026-07-16 — Pre-push review sweep on this change set.** The scene-import manifest now also carries **`scene_materials`** — every material name on an exportable object, textured or not — and the Maya side excludes those exact spellings from the clash-rename suffix match: an *untextured* sibling ("Mat2" beside textured "Mat") has no manifest entry, so the existing entries-only guard couldn't protect it, and the suffix path would read it as "Mat renamed to Mat2" and repaint its faces with Mat's rebuilt textures (narrow — needs a literal digit-suffixed sibling; Blender's `.001` encodes to `FBXASC046001` and can't false-match). Older manifests lack the key and keep the entries-only behavior. Pinned by `test_suffix_match_never_steals_an_untextured_scene_material`. Also: `maya_collision_checker(ignore=…)` got its owed test (predicate suppresses the bound command; a non-matching predicate leaves the report intact), and the pre-existing `test_smart_bake_panel.py` failures (4) are repaired — the Jul-13 checkbox→combobox migration (`chk_override_layer` → `cmb_bake_layer`, 9c49d9b) never updated the mock test. The rewritten tests follow the deterministic-state rule: combo items/default and the Delete-Inputs gating are pinned through the handler, and the signal *connection* is asserted via `isSignalConnected` — because signal *delivery* under the venv's newer offscreen Qt proved intermittently dead in this harness (connection reports connected, handler doesn't fire; reproducible flip-flop across identical runs) while the wiring itself is correct.

- **2026-07-16 — New `mtk.import_blender_scene` / `BlenderSceneImport` (`env_utils/blender_bridge/_scene_import.py`): import a `.blend` into the current Maya scene via a headless-Blender FBX round-trip — the pull-direction mirror of blendertk's `btk.import_maya_scene`.** A fresh `blender --background --factory-startup` (session-safety rule; factory startup also skips the user's addons) runs the underscore-hidden `templates/_import_scene.py`, which exports a full-fidelity FBX (`path_mode='ABSOLUTE'` so temp-dir-relative texture paths can't go missing — the mirror of the Maya side's workspace fix; per-kwarg tolerance drops a renamed exporter parameter and retries) plus a `.manifest.json` sidecar carrying every textured material's original image files — the metallic/roughness/AO and packed maps (`Metallic_Smoothness`/`MSAO`/`ORM`) FBX's classic material model has no slot for. Maya imports the FBX (`cmds.file` + `returnNewNodes`) and rebuilds each manifest entry natively through the **`GameShader` engine** (standardSurface; packed-map config flags enabled per detected `ptk.MapFactory` type — MSAO's flag is `mask_map` per the registry SSoT), then swaps at the **shading-group level** by transferring set members (`forceElement`), which preserves per-face/multi-material assignments — the Maya analogue of blendertk's slot-level swap — with an object-level fallback when the importer renamed the material, replaced networks orphan-purged, and file-less entries surfacing as **named warnings** (the rule the mirror direction learned from a live production report). Same conversion-cache design as the twin (scene+options+template identity key; scratch-then-promote so a killed partial can't poison it; live: 2.1s → 0.2s on a re-import). One empirical find pinned by tests: Maya's FBX importer spells illegal Blender name chars as **`FBXASC###`** (`dotted.001` → `dottedFBXASC046001`, leading digit encoded too — probe-verified live), not underscores; `_fbx_safe_name` models that for matching, `_maya_safe_name` keeps rebuilt nodes readable. Tests: `test_scene_import.py` (23 — template hygiene, FBXASC matching, real-node member-transfer/purge/fallback orchestration with the Blender run stubbed) + the gated `scene_import_live_e2e.py` (**16/16** in a real Blender 5.1 → mayapy round-trip: dotted-name primary path, per-face preservation on a two-material cube, shared material rebuilt once across two meshes, packed Metallic_Smoothness wired with smoothness→roughness inversion, named missing-texture warning, cache + temp hygiene). Exposed in tentacle's Maya scene submenu as Import ▸ "Import Blender Scene". **Review-pass hardening (both red-first):** (1) the manifest collector now iterates `bpy.context.scene.objects`, not `bpy.data.objects` — the FBX exporter writes only the active scene, so a multi-scene .blend produced entries nothing Maya-side could match; (2) the clash-rename suffix match is sibling-safe — exact importer spelling wins, and a suffix candidate is rejected when it is ANOTHER manifest entry's exact target ("M_test", renamed by the importer, must not empty "M_test2"'s shading groups and demote that entry to the object-level rescue path — pinned by a log-path assertion, since the end state looks identical either way).

- **2026-07-16 — `apply_bindings` FIXED: a macro whose chord was still bound but whose `runTimeCommand` had been deleted was skipped as "already applied" — leaving a hotkey that ERRORS on press, permanently, because the startup applier never healed it.** Deleting a runTimeCommand does not release its chord (documented in `set_macro`'s multi-bound note), so the split state (binding → vanished nameCommand) arises from Maya's own Hotkey Editor deletions or any teardown, and today it surfaced live: `TestApplySavedMacros.test_applies_active_user_preset` failed against real-prefs state carrying exactly that split. The idempotency no-op check (`_key_bound_to`) now also requires the runTimeCommand to exist before skipping; a healthy converged session behaves identically (no "Saving hotkeys…" churn regression — the idempotency tests now stage the command's existence, as a truly converged macro always has it). Failing-first regression: `test_apply_rebuilds_missing_runtime_command_when_chord_still_bound` (reproduces the split state in a fresh Maya, asserts the re-registration). `test_macros` 75 + `test_hotkey_collisions` 26 green.
- **2026-07-16 — `live_hotkey_map` FIXED: the shift modifier was silently dropped for non-letter keys — a rebind/clear then released the WRONG chord, leaving the real binding live.** Surfaced by the Macro Manager live verification (assign `Ctrl+Alt+Shift+F9` → the table read back `Ctrl+Alt+F9`; the subsequent clear targeted that phantom chord and the real binding survived). Root cause in `hotkey_collisions._ks_modifier`: Maya 2025's 7-element `assignCommand` keyString stores shift as the **last** element, not index 4 as assumed — probe-verified live (`ctl+alt+sht+F9` → `["F9","1","1","0","0","0","1"]`; `sht+i` → glyph `"I"` + trailing `"1"`). Letter keys masked the bug (shift+letter is also encoded as the upper-case glyph, which `keystring_to_token` reads); function/named keys had no fallback. Affected every `live_hotkey_map` consumer, including the retired panel — not a regression from the editor move. Failing-first regression: `TestKeystringToToken.test_shift_function_key`; end-to-end re-verified in a fresh live Maya (assign → correct cell text → clear → actually unbound).
- **2026-07-16 — Macro Manager panel RETIRED — the UI is now the unified uitk `ShortcutEditor` over the `Macros` controller (`Macros.show_editor()`).** The bespoke `edit_utils/macro_manager` panel (`.ui` + ~600-line slots) was a fourth parallel binding editor, re-implementing the shortcut editor's capture delegate, conflict display, filtering, and preset row. It now launches the ONE editor through uitk's new `RegistrySwitchboardFacade`: categories render as the editor's group combobox (opens on the flat all-macros view; the combo filters — the old `cmb000`'s role), the header-menu preset row fronts the exact same `PresetStore` that `apply_saved_macros` applies at startup (`.active` sidecar, shipped `default`, format unchanged), and per-row reset restores the shipped-default binding. **Functional upgrade:** assignments are now checked against Maya's *entire* live hotkey map via `maya_collision_checker` — with the "Assign & free Maya binding" flow — where the old panel only detected macro-vs-macro clashes; the checker gained an `ignore=` predicate so managed `m_*` commands aren't double-reported (the editor's built-in checker already lists those with a clear option). **Deliberately dropped:** in-UI recategorization — the `*Macros` mixin is the documented category SSoT, and presets still carry + apply custom `cat`s; a custom category simply renders as its own group. New Qt-free provider glue on `MacroManager` (`editor_categories`, `get_editor_registry`, `apply_editor_binding`, `export_bindings`, `import_bindings` — the last two moved from the panel slots) with only `show_editor` importing uitk lazily; `edit_utils/macro_manager/` remains solely as the shipped-presets anchor. tentacle `preferences.b011` now calls `show_editor` (both DCCs, branch-free). Tests: `test_macros` 74 (6 new `TestEditorGlue`), `mock_tests/test_macro_editor_window.py` replaces the retired panel window test; `test_macro_manager_slots.py` deleted (its export/import regressions carried into `TestEditorGlue`).
- **2026-07-16 — New `Macros.m_grid` (grid-only toggle, bound to `0`); `m_grid_and_image_planes` FIXED — it silently did nothing in any scene without image planes.** The grid half was unreachable: `cmds.grid(toggle=…)` sat *inside* `for obj in cmds.ls(exactType="imagePlane"):`, so an empty image-plane list — i.e. most scenes — skipped the loop body entirely and the macro (on `F1`) toggled nothing at all. The image planes also **led** the grid (grid state was derived from each plane's `displayMode`, last-one-wins across multiple planes), diverging from blendertk's counterpart, which has always had the **grid lead**. Since blendertk mirrors mayatk's *behavior*, that was a live parity break with Maya on the wrong side. Fixed by extracting the toggle into a first-class `m_grid()` that flips `cmds.grid(q=True, toggle=True)` and **returns the applied state**; `m_grid_and_image_planes` now calls it and syncs `displayMode` (2/0) to that return, so the grid leads and the planes follow — no re-derivation, no drift, and the two macros compose (pressing either leaves the other coherent, since both read live grid state). Probe-verified in mayapy first, rather than assumed: `cmds.grid(query=True, toggle=True)` returns a real `bool`, set/read round-trips, `inViewMessage` survives standalone, and the no-image-plane no-op reproduced (`grid before=True after=True`). New `TestGridMacros` (4 tests) written **failing first** — 2 failures (grid untouched with no planes; grid didn't lead) + 2 errors (no `m_grid`) — now green. `m_grid` needs no registration plumbing: `list_available_macros` discovers it by the `m_` prefix and `macro_category` derives "Display" from the defining `DisplayMacros` mixin, so smoke-invocation and `set_macro` registration picked it up automatically. Bound to `0` in the shared `m3trik` preset (both DCC stores byte-identical); `F1` keeps grid+image-planes. `test_macros` **68/68** (was 64/4).

- **2026-07-16 — `PersistentMenu` / `EmbeddedMenuWidget` re-homed to uitk (`uitk.widgets.embeddedMenu`); `maya_native_menus` re-exports them.** Pure-Qt classes with nothing Maya-specific inside — blendertk's harvested chord-menu wrap needs the same widget pair (blendertk cannot import from mayatk; uitk is the shared layer below both). Public surface unchanged: both names still import from `mayatk.ui_utils.maya_native_menus`.

- **2026-07-16 — Script Output: the hand-rolled hover-focus `enterEvent` is gone; the shared widget owns it.** `ScriptConsole.enterEvent` (focus the dialog on mouse-over, so Ctrl+C reaches the console without clicking in first) was the only copy of that behavior in the ecosystem, which left the Blender console without it. It moved into `uitk.ScriptOutput` as `focus_on_hover` (default on — see uitk's CHANGELOG), so both DCC consoles now get it from one place. Behavior here is unchanged-to-better: the dialog's layout has zero margins and the output fills it, so the hover area is identical, and focus now lands on the output **widget** directly rather than on the dialog that would delegate it there anyway. Maya also keeps its `app_wide_copy=True` safety net (Ctrl+C works even when Maya intercepts the shortcut or the widget lacks focus), so this is additive. No public API beyond the removed override; `test_script_output.py` exercises the widget, not the override, and is unaffected.

- **2026-07-14 — Substance/Marmoset bridge: temp-folder fallback for a missing Output Dir.** Both bridges opt into the new shared `uitk.BridgeSlotsBase.TEMP_OUTPUT_FALLBACK`: when neither a typed value nor the scene/workspace default resolves (an unsaved scene), the panel falls back to a self-cleaning temp folder instead of erroring, so the FBX + baked maps hand-off proceeds. The Marmoset help text's stale "(required)" is dropped. Unity's bridge is unaffected (it still requires a real project Assets dir). No engine change.

- **2026-07-13 — Test hygiene: two stale test groups that shipped red are green again (no product change).** A full-suite run after the Channels/parity changeset surfaced 6 failures the committed badge understated (it was generated before these test-touching changes were exercised). (1) `test_smart_bake_slots` ×4 stubbed the migrated-away `chk_override_layer` checkbox; updated the stub/override to the `cmb_bake_layer` two-item combo (the SmartBake bake-target is a combo now, not a checkbox — the production migration itself was correct; only the stub lagged). (2) `test_scene_exporter` ×2 asserted `_optimize_keys_enabled`, an internal flag the shots→pythontk `TaskFactory` migration dropped; rewritten to spy on the actual `optimize_keys` task dispatch (the feature is intact — `task_manager.optimize_keys` → `AnimUtils.optimize_keys`). Full suite now **3743 passed / 0 failed** (was 3737/6); badge refreshed. Test-only + badge — no shipped-code change.

- **2026-07-13 — Channels: the footer *Shape* button works after *History*, so Shape↔History is a real toggle.** Live report: with a transform selected, pressing the footer History icon selected the construction-history node (correct), but pressing Shape next warned "No shape nodes found" — you had to reselect the transform in the viewport to get back to the shape. Root cause: the controller's `get_shape_nodes` only did `cmds.listRelatives(node, shapes=True)`, which returns nothing for a DG history node (it has no shape children). Fix: `get_shape_nodes` now delegates to the canonical `NodeUtils.get_shape_node`, which resolves the shape from a transform, a shape, **or** a history node (via its `listHistory(future=True)` → parent → recurse branch) — removing a downstream duplicate of the upstream helper (DRY). `get_history_nodes` already resolved history from both a transform and a shape node consistently (same top-of-stack node either way), so it's unchanged; the two now round-trip cleanly in both directions without losing the target mesh. Signatures unchanged (no registry regen). TDD: `test_channels.py` +3 (`test_resolves_shape_from_history_node` — the exact regression; `test_resolves_shape_from_shape_node`; `test_shape_history_toggle_round_trip`), plus a stacked-mesh check; all fail before the fix and pass after, verified in a fresh mayapy standalone (simple cube → `polyCube1`, extruded mesh → `polyExtrudeFace1`, empty group → clean empty list). blendertk has no analogue (a Blender object owns its mesh directly — no DAG shape/history split; ledgered in `tentacle/docs/parity_map.py`), so Maya-only. **Review-pass hardening:** `NodeUtils.get_shape_node` resolves via `listRelatives` *without* `fullPath`, so `get_shape_nodes` now re-resolves its result through `cmds.ls(..., long=True)` — restoring the full-DAG-path guarantee the pre-delegation code had, so the footer *Shape* select can't raise "More than one object matches name" in a scene with duplicate short names.

- **2026-07-12 — Channels: the single/multi-object toggles are color-coded from session start and can no longer drift apart.** Two fixes land together. (1) The root cause of "state icons start theme-colored until first clicked" was upstream — uitk's first theme sweep wiped explicitly-colored state icons (see uitk's CHANGELOG, same date); the filter / invert / target option-box actions here need no code change to benefit. (2) The compact-footer target button is rewired onto the centralized mechanism: it now passes the same state dicts as the txt001 option-box action (shared via a new `_target_toggle_states()`) to `footer.add_action_button(states=...)`, so it renders color-coded and mode-correct at creation — the manual `_update_compact_btn_state` re-tint dance (which was never even called at startup) and `_on_footer_compact_btn_clicked` are deleted. `_on_toggle_single_object` now syncs *both* toggle widgets to the controller via a new `_sync_target_toggles()` (the option-box action is kept as `self._target_action`; uitk's `add_action` now returns it), which closes the old drift where toggling via the footer left the hidden option-box action stale — the leaving-compact "reset the mode so the button happens to match" workaround is replaced by routing through `_on_toggle_single_object(False)` (which also refreshes the table to the restored mode; the old direct reset never refreshed). Mirrored 1:1 in blendertk (`compare_panel_surface.py --panel channels`: 0 untriaged, summary unchanged).

- **2026-07-12 — Channels: *Compact View* and *Auto-fit Window* now default ON.** Both header-menu checkboxes ship checked. Auto-fit was already pull-based (read on every refresh), so its default flips for free; compact view is push-only (applied from its `toggled` signal), and persistent-state restore only re-emits `toggled` when the stored value differs from the construction default — so a fresh install would show the box checked but leave the effect unapplied. Added `_sync_compact_default()` (a Type-column-style pull reconcile) called from `header_init` and at the top of `_refresh_table`, so the checked-by-default compact state actually applies on first show. Users who previously toggled either option keep their stored preference (restore still wins). Mirrored 1:1 in blendertk (`compare_panel_surface.py`: 0 deltas).

- **2026-07-12 — Channels: *Select Shape Node* / *Select History Node* moved from the header menu to footer icon buttons.** The two selection actions are now always-visible icon buttons in the footer (a `cube` icon for Shape, a `history` icon for History), sitting to the left of the compact-mode target-toggle button (footer action buttons fill left-to-right toward the size grip, so they're added before it). The header menu's "Selection" section is gone; the handlers (`_hdr_select_shape` / `_hdr_select_history`) are unchanged, just rewired. Help text updated to point at the footer. blendertk's channels has no analogue (a Blender object owns its mesh directly — no DAG shape/history split), so this is Maya-only; the now-orphaned `hdr_select_shape` / `hdr_select_history` entries were removed from `tentacle/docs/parity_map.py` (channels **and** a pre-existing stale copy under reference_manager) — both panels' parity summaries unchanged (`compare_panel_surface.py`).
- **2026-07-12 — Channels: clicking anywhere in an enum row's value cell now opens the dropdown, not only over the (often collapsed) combobox.** Live report: the value cell of an enum attribute was mostly dead space — the dropdown opened only when the click hit the small combobox at the left of the cell. The embedded combobox is created with `AdjustToContents` and self-resizes to its content width after the table's layout (leaving the rest of the wide value cell inert; see the uitk entry for the reproduction). The Channels value column now opts into uitk `TableWidget`'s new `set_cell_widget_click_columns([COL_VALUE])`, so a click anywhere in an enum value cell is forwarded to the combobox and opens its popup — a no-op when the combo already spans the cell, so no double-open. No change to numeric/text value cells (they still open the inline editor / MMB-scrub / wheel-scrub) since forwarding only fires for cells that host a widget.
- **2026-07-12 — Shot Sequencer: opening a shot no longer crashes with `More than one object matches name` when a shot references a non-unique object name.** Live report (traceback): opening a shot in the sequencer raised `ValueError: More than one object matches name: <LOC>` from `SegmentKeys.collect_segments` → `cmds.listConnections`. Root cause: `ShotSequencer._shot_nodes` validated a shot's stored object names with `cmds.objExists`, which returns `True` even for a **non-unique** short name (one whose leaf collides with a second scene node after a duplicate / import / namespace merge). The ambiguous short name passed validation unchanged and blew up the moment it reached any `cmds` query that demands a unique node, taking down the whole sequencer rebuild. Fix: `_shot_nodes` now resolves each stored name through `cmds.ls(..., long=True)` — non-existent names are dropped (as before), uniquely-resolving names keep their stored form (so display/matching is unaffected), and a **non-unique** name is disambiguated to a single long DAG path via a new shared `_disambiguate_matches` (prefer the anim-curve-bearing match — the node a shot actually animates — else the first), so no ambiguous name ever reaches the segment collector. The identical disambiguation policy that was inlined in `_reconcile_stale_paths` now calls the same helper (DRY); a self-critique pass caught that the consolidation had referenced `self` inside that `@staticmethod` (latent `NameError` on its multi-match branch) and corrected it to the class-qualified call. TDD: `test_sequencer.py` +3 — `test_shot_nodes_disambiguates_nonunique_name`, `test_collect_object_segments_survives_nonunique_name` (reproduces the exact traceback), and `test_reconcile_resolves_ambiguous_leaf_to_animated` (covers the `_reconcile_stale_paths` multi-match branch that harbored the `self` bug); all three failed before the fix and pass after, verified in a mayapy standalone (fresh scene per test). Track labels are already leaf-shortened for display, so the resolved long path introduces no UI change.

- **2026-07-12 — Review fixes on the engine-migration change set (audio take-scoping edge, dependency floor, test-infra hygiene).** (1) **`AudioClips` take-scoping no longer invents a phantom `(0, 0)` take**: `_published_takes` now drops any `fbx_takes` entry missing its `name`, `start`, or `end` (previously only name-less entries were filtered, and `_scope_events` defaulted a missing range to `(0, 0)` — a malformed take silently swallowed frame-0 events into a clip that doesn't exist). `_scope_events` also skips range-less takes outright, so direct callers get the same guarantee. When *no* published entry carries a usable range the bake now falls back to unscoped (`clip: ""`) — consistent with the existing unreadable-channel handling — instead of dropping every event. TDD: `test_audio_clips_export` +2 (`test_scoping_skips_takes_missing_range`, `test_scoping_falls_back_unscoped_when_no_take_has_a_range`); both failed before the fix and pass after, each run in a fresh port-mode Maya. (2) **`pyproject.toml` pythontk floor → `>=0.8.85`** — the version that ships the shots/manifest engines, `process_stream`, `TaskFactory`, `Weights`, and `RailSurface` this package now imports; `>=0.8.84` would let pip resolve a pythontk without them and break `import mayatk` at the first engine import (release order stays pythontk-first via `push.ps1`; the floor just makes the requirement honest). (3) **`run_tests.py` shots-prefs sandbox now cleans up after itself** — the `mkdtemp` dir is `atexit`-removed in the test-host process instead of leaking one `shots_prefs_test_*` directory per run. (4) **API registry regenerated once against the pre-refactor baseline** so `API_CHANGES.md` records this change set's real public-surface diff — a second same-day refresh had already advanced the JSON baseline, overwriting the narrative with "no changes". Known follow-up (deferred, generator-owned): the registry does not index re-export facades, so still-importable names like `plan_respace`, `ShotBlock`, `parse_csv`, `load_behavior`, and `MappingSpec` no longer appear in `API_INDEX.md` even though the mayatk import paths remain public API.

- **2026-07-12 — Curtain drape engine vendored in-package: `edit_utils/_curtain_drape.py`.** `CurtainDrape` (the pure pleat/fold/catenary/crease displacement the curtain tool drapes with) moved down from pythontk, which keeps only the general primitives it composes — the new `ptk.RailSurface` rail→(u,v)-grid primitive plus `Polyline`/`MathUtils`/`BandLimitedNoise` (a single tool's domain math isn't a pythontk engine; see pythontk's CHANGELOG same date). `CurtainMesh` now subclasses the local class — same parameters, same drape, no behavior change. The file is a code-identical twin of `blendertk.edit_utils._curtain_drape` (neither DCC can import the other), guarded against drift by extapps' `test_vendor_sync.py` (`TestCurtainEngineDccSync`). New `test/test_curtain_drape.py` (12, moved from pythontk) pins the drape math; pure Python, so it runs under plain pytest as well as the in-Maya runner. **Verified:** drape tests 12/12 under pytest; fresh mayapy standalone smoke 8/8 — `CurtainMesh` subclasses the LOCAL twin, builds a 1625-vert curtain whose vert count and bbox match the engine drape exactly, and `ptk.CurtainDrape` no longer resolves.

- **2026-07-12 — Shots system migrated onto the shared pythontk engine (`core_utils/engines/shots`) — mayatk now runs the same core blendertk shipped on.** Every module that duplicated engine logic became a thin Maya adapter/facade; the public module paths and names are unchanged, so slots, tests, and tentacle need no edits. The engine was line-audited against the Maya code being removed before the swap (it had never run in production here): the extraction was faithful, and the two real gaps found — the audio track-path fallback in `compute_duration` and the audio-grow duration semantics/mock-seam in `_compute_plan` — were closed upstream as injection seams (`resolve_source_fn`, `_audio_grow_duration`; see pythontk's CHANGELOG same date) rather than by re-duplicating code here. Per module: **`_detection`** keeps Maya acquisition (`resolve_to_transform`, `SegmentKeys`, `_filter_flat_objects`) and delegates the clustering/boundary math to `ptk.cluster_segments_by_gap` / `boundaries_from_key_entries`; **`_shots`** keeps `MayaScenePersistence` (DataNodes carrier, undo-safe writes, SJM subscriptions) and subclasses `ptk.ShotStore`, overriding only the scene hooks (`_scene_fps`, `has_animation`, `detect_regions`, `assess`, `publish_export_view`, `_schedule_flush` = `evalDeferred` coalescing, FBX export-preparer registration); **`_shot_plan`** is a pure re-export (and `plan_reorder` is now available locally); **`_shot_apply`** supplies the Maya writer strategies (`_batch_move_keys` envelope-slop keyframe shifts, `_shift_audio_range` audio-margin shifts) to the engine's three-phase park/order/land walk, wrapped in one audio batch with the hoisted track-list filter and dirty-track marking exactly as before; **`behaviors`** keeps the Maya appliers (`apply_behavior` dual-key opacity/visibility, `verify_behavior`, `apply_audio_clip`, `apply_to_shots`) over the engine's pure loading/schema/`resolve_keys`, with `compute_duration` binding Maya's audio measurement + track-path fallback into the pure summation; **`mapping`** and the behavior schema are facades (built-in templates now ship with the engine — the three shipped `.yaml` behaviors were verified semantically equal to the engine's `.json` twins and deleted; `BEHAVIOR_FORMAT.md` regenerated from the engine spec); **`range_resolver`** injects the Maya `duration_fn`; **`_shot_manifest`** subclasses `ptk.ShotManifest`, overriding the fps/audio-measure/name-resolution/anim-walk/assess/apply/rewire hooks and keeping the lazy package imports so the established `...behaviors.*` mock seams still intercept the inherited planner (`from_csv` also overridden so the default store is the Maya one, not the engine base's). **Behavior changes (deliberate):** user behavior/mapping templates now live in the cross-DCC store (`user_config_root()/shots/manifest_behaviors|manifest_mappings/`, JSON) shared with Blender — no real user files existed in the old `mayatk/shot_manifest_*` locations (only TemplateSet scaffolding), so no data migration; cross-scene detection prefs moved from `QSettings("uitk","shots")` to the engine's shared JSON store with a one-time legacy-QSettings migration on first access (`ShotStore._restore_user_prefs`); and `_audio_placeholder_dur` now treats a registered-but-unprobeable audio track (path on file_map but the file missing/unreadable, so `audio_duration_frames` can't measure it) as *unresolved* — the step gets the 30f grow-to-clip placeholder instead of fit-mode sizing (the old `has_track and get_path` check accepted a dead path as resolvable; probe-based resolvability means the shot still grows to the real clip length once the file materialises). **Test infra:** the runner now sandboxes the engine's prefs JSON (`_prefs_dir_override`) — the suite had started writing the user's real (cloud-synced) `shots/prefs.json`, the same leak class as the old always-on QSettings writes; `TestCrossScenePrefs` rewritten to the new contract (+1 case: prefs file wins over legacy QSettings) and the behaviors custom-template fixture moved to JSON. Verified green module-by-module during the swap and with a finishing full run. The scene-exporter `TaskManager` now subclasses `pythontk.TaskFactory` (the shared reflection-based task/check pipeline, formerly vendored in both DCCs). Blendshape `creator`/`slots` use `pythontk.Weights` (morph weight math, formerly `blendshape_animator.weights`). `substance_bridge/connection.py` sheds its bundled stream machinery — `OutputStream`/`ProcessReader`/`LogTailer` now come from `pythontk.core_utils.process_stream` (the app-agnostic mechanism); the Painter-specific `SubstanceConnection` shell, `substance_rpc` client, `templates/`, and the whole Marmoset engine stay vendored here **by design** (app-specific code fails pythontk's app-agnostic charter), kept code-identical with blendertk's copies by extapps' `test_vendor_sync.py` guard. Verified in a fresh Maya: `TaskManager` subclasses the shared `TaskFactory`; `Weights` resolves to pythontk.

- **2026-07-11 — `DataNodes` gains a read/inspect side: `dump()` + `format_dump()`.** The node had per-channel writers/readers (`set_`/`get_internal_string`, `set_`/`get_export_string`) but no way to see everything a scene actually carries. `DataNodes.dump(decode=True)` reads *every* user-defined attribute off both `data_internal` and `data_export` and groups them by node — `{node: {attr: value}}` — best-effort JSON-decoding **string** values (producers store JSON blobs; plain wire strings pass through) while keeping **non-string** channels as-is: notably the audio tool's per-track `enum` attrs (`AudioClips.ensure_track_attr`), which a string-only filter would have silently dropped. New producer channels appear automatically (nothing keyed to the well-known constants); empty string channels are skipped. `format_dump(decode=True)` is the one-call pretty-JSON text form for the console (`print(DataNodes.format_dump())`) and the viewer dialog — `default=str` guards the rare non-JSON-native value — returning `""` when neither node carries a channel so callers can treat falsy as "no scene data". Powers tentacle's new Scene ▸ Scene Metadata button (see tentacle CHANGELOG). Tests: `test_data_nodes.py` +8 (`TestDump`: grouping, JSON decode on/off, non-JSON passthrough, cleared-channel skip, hidden per-track enum kept + serialized, empty-scene contract, `format_dump` round-trip) — 31/31 under a fresh mayapy standalone. blendertk mirrors the same names (its CHANGELOG). Registry regenerated.

- **2026-07-11 — `audio_manifest` v2: versioned JSON with per-clip scoping (was a flat `"12:footstep 24:jump"` wire string).** The old scene-global format had no clip key, so a multi-take FBX (Shots) made Unity inject *every* audio event into *every* AnimationClip at the same clip-relative offset — the only export channel that wasn't versioned JSON, and the only one that produced wrong output when combined with takes. `AudioClips.prepare_for_export` now emits `{"version": 2, "events": [{"clip", "frame", "name"}, …]}`: it reads the published `fbx_takes` channel (loose coupling via the carrier — the same JSON `apply_takes_from_node` realizes, so scoping is consistent-by-construction with the clips that actually ship) and assigns each event to every take containing it, `frame` rebased to that take's start; events outside every take are dropped with a warning (they could never fire in any clip); with no takes, events ship unscoped (`clip: ""`) rebased to `playbackOptions min` as before. JSON also lifts the v1 tokenizer's no-spaces/no-colons restriction on event names. **`FbxUtils.run_export_preparers` now runs known producers in canonical `_KNOWN_PRODUCERS` order (shots → audio → shadow) before other preparers (registration order)** — previously session-hook order was registration order, so audio could bake against stale takes when its track was authored first. **`AudioUtils.bake_manifest` → `bake_events`**: the primitive now returns `[(frame, label), …]` raw int scene-frames (rebasing is caller policy); the legacy string builder had no remaining production caller and was deleted per the dead-code rule. Consumer: unitytk's `AudioEventController.cs` parses v2 (brace-detected) and injects each event only into its named clip, keeping a legacy parser for pre-v2 FBX — see unitytk's CHANGELOG same date. Docs: `data_nodes.md` (v2 schema + scoping rules), `shot_export_unity.md` (canonical hook order). Tests: `test_audio_utils_phase2f` (`TestBakeEvents`, +frames-are-ints), `test_audio_clips_export` (JSON envelope asserts + new take-scoping case incl. outside-all-takes drop), `test_fbx_export_preparers` (+`test_known_producers_run_in_canonical_order`) — 44/44 scoped; `test_scene_exporter`'s channel-agnostic entry-count summary needed no change.
- **2026-07-10 — Color ID panel: fixed swatch sizing + tightened grid layout.** The 12 color swatches gain explicit min/max sizes and the grid gets explicit margins/spacing, so the palette lays out consistently instead of stretching with the window. `.ui`-only — no widget objectName, signal, or slot changes (Color ID slots untouched); mayatk/blendertk paired, parity sweep clean.

- **2026-07-10 — Macro preset re-apply no longer flushes prefs on every launch ("Saving hotkeys…" spam), and rebinds release the old chord.** User report: every tentacle launch printed the full `savePrefs` block (`userRunTimeCommands.mel`, every `userHotkeys_<set>.mel`, `userNamedCommands.mel`) even with nothing changed. Root cause: the launch-time `apply_saved_macros()` → `apply_bindings()` re-created every runtimeCommand and unconditionally `cmds.savePrefs(hotkeys=True)` regardless of whether the live registry already matched the preset. Live-diagnosed in a fresh GUI Maya against the user's real prefs with a read-only diff probe: 20/22 preset entries already matched — only two ever "changed", and they changed **forever** because the preset had been re-chorded (`m_shading` 4→ was live on F11, `m_object_selection` sht+q→ on F9) and `set_macro` only *adds* a binding (deleting the runtimeCommand doesn't release its hotkey), so the command stayed **multi-bound**, `assignCommand` kept reporting the stale chord, and the diff re-applied + re-saved every launch without converging. Fixes: (1) `apply_bindings` diffs each entry against the live registry (`get_current_bindings`, normalized key + category), applies only genuine differences, and flushes prefs only if ≥1 binding changed (empty preset → no registry scan). (2) On a rebind (`live_key` present, key mismatch) it releases the stale chord via `clear_hotkey(name, key=live_key)` before `set_macro`, so the command converges to a single chord — except when the stale chord is itself a target key of the same preset (a chord swap/chain), where a sibling binding re-claims it and clearing would clobber that. **Verified end-to-end in a fresh GUI Maya (user prefs, savePrefs counted):** first apply released F11/F9, bound 4/sht+q, flushed once; second apply — and a simulated next launch on the now-converged prefs — applied nothing and never called savePrefs (VERDICT: SILENT NO-OP). Also hardened the harness: a new `_NoPrefsFlush` test mixin blocks `cmds.savePrefs` for the integration cases (`TestApplyBindings`/`TestApplySavedMacros`) — the test Maya runs against the developer's real prefs (no `MAYA_APP_DIR` sandbox), so those tests had been flushing throwaway F7/F8/F9 hotkeys into `userHotkeys_*.mel` (the same seeding that created the user's stale chords). Tests: `test_macros.py` +4 (`TestApplyBindingsIdempotent`: rebind-clear, no-op-no-save, unbound-binds-without-clear, chord-swap-doesn't-clobber-sibling), resilience test now mocks savePrefs — 63/63 scoped in a fresh port-mode Maya. blendertk mirror needs no change (no savePrefs; its keymaps don't persist and are re-applied each session by design).

  **Follow-up (same day) — the spam still recurred; caught the real trigger by instrumenting the user's actual `userSetup.py` (a stack-logging `savePrefs` wrapper, since synthetic repros came up silent).** The stack showed `apply_bindings` *was* the caller after all — and the deeper cause: a macro command bound to **several** chords (e.g. `m_object_selection` on `sht+q` **plus** `sht+f`/`sht+g`, extras the user/editor added) collapses to a **single, non-deterministically-chosen** entry in `live_hotkey_map` (one `assignCommand` entry per name command). When that entry happened to be an *extra* chord rather than the preset key, the `normalize(live_key) == normalize(key)` diff spuriously failed → cleared one extra, rebound, `savePrefs` — on some launches but not others, which is exactly why it looked random and "came back". Fix: new `Macros._key_bound_to(name, key)` queries the **target** chord directly (`cmds.hotkey(k, q, name) == f"{name}Command"`) — deterministic, unaffected by how many other chords the command carries — and `apply_bindings` uses it for the match test. A macro whose preset key is already live is now a true no-op regardless of extra chords. **Verified: the real instrumented `userSetup` deferred `TclMaya` startup logged 0 `savePrefs` / 0 macro edits across 3 consecutive fresh launches (was intermittent before).** Tests: +1 (`test_multibound_target_present_is_noop` — live_hotkey_map returns an extra chord, target is bound → no-op/no-flush; would have re-applied + flushed pre-fix); idempotency tests now stub `_key_bound_to` — 64/64 scoped. Note: extra chords aren't auto-pruned (Maya's API can't enumerate all keys for a command), but they no longer trigger saves; prune via the Hotkey Editor if unwanted.

- **2026-07-10 — Adversarial-review pass: preview rollback, shadow rig, bakers, RizomUV, port probe, skinning DRY.** **Preview** — `CleanupContract.record_modification` now snapshots a *missing* attr as a sentinel and rollback DELETES it: canceling a shadow-rig preview in a scene whose `data_export` carrier pre-existed (Shots/Audio) left a stale `shadow_metadata` channel behind (the missing-attr getAttr raised and the snapshot was silently skipped; +test). **Shadow rig** — `b002` no longer falls back to baking (destructively de-rigging) EVERY scene plane when a non-empty selection merely contains none; `find_shadow_planes` walks descendants by fullPath and dedupes (duplicate transform leaf names crashed `attributeQuery`); `_world_bbox`/`_gather_world_meshes` exclude intermediate (Orig) shapes, so a posed skinned target no longer measures or rasterizes its bind pose; the rig name base is uniquified against existing rigs (a second multi-target "combined" rig deleted the first's decomposeMatrix nodes out from under its expression and overwrote its PNG); the default StingrayPBS material routes alpha THROUGH the fade multiplier (the expression-driven rise fade previously drove a dangling `opacity_mult` and had no visible effect); `chk_combine` tooltip describes the actual behavior. **Lightmap baker** — the atlas instance dedupe keys the shape's UUID (a DAG-path key is unique per instance path, so it never fired: two `cmds.instance` transforms both got rects and the shared UV set was remapped twice, compounded); the panel forwards preset `gi_depth`/`gi_samples` to the bake (the "preview" tier's single bounce actually bakes 1 bounce from the panel now — previously only `from_preset` API callers got preset GI); `pack_atlas` stamps `uvRect` on the marker the moment it remaps UVs (a crash — or a direct-API caller who never reached commit — left silently remapped UVs invisible to `revert_lightmap` and the pre-bake guard) and `commit_lightmap` carries an existing marker rect forward; intensity is applied to the files only after at least one marker resolves (a commit recording nothing no longer mutates EXRs a retry would re-scale); the new GI params moved AFTER `baker` (positional-caller compat); a missing committed UV set warns instead of silently publishing uvIndex 1. **Texture baker** — a `shader=` override with Arnold unavailable now ABORTS loudly instead of silently baking the real materials via convertSolidTx (albedo×lighting would have been committed as "lighting-only" and the engine composites albedo twice);
