batch mode falls back per-object on namespaced shapes (RTT's filename mapping is ambiguous — ":" is illegal on Windows) and warns when a multi-shape transform claims only its first map; batch failure still fires the final progress tick. **RizomUV** — `_export_name_map` keys the name `cmds.rename` actually returned (a stale `*__RZTMP` survivor made Maya uniquify the rename and silently skip that object's UV transfer on re-import); Lua comments point at the real probe path; probe scratch dir `mkdir(parents=True)`; `_export_objects` docstring is FBX-only. **maya_connection** — `get_available_port` fails OPEN to the legacy connect probe when the installed pythontk lacks `is_port_bindable` (wheel lag): failing closed read every port as busy and killed port-mode launching outright. **Skinning** — the DCC-agnostic falloff resolver and clamped-B-spline basis math hoisted to `pythontk.MathUtils` (`resolve_falloff_profile` re-exported; one implementation for the blendertk mirror per the generic-helpers-upstream rule); `skin_mesh` unbinds via `SkinUtils.unbind` instead of deleting the cluster (no more orphan dagPose accumulation across re-binds); `_control_path` returns the post-reparent path for group-less controls; a bare `run_tests.py` now covers `test_skinning`. Stale `build/` artifacts removed. Full suite green (3725; re-run after the pass).

- **2026-07-10 — Reference Manager: stop double-populating the workspace combo on open (fixes the duplicated "No workspaces in ..." warning).** On startup the combo was populated twice — once by `cmb000_init` and again by `txt000_init → update_current_dir()`. The second call fired because `update_current_dir`'s change check compared `os.path.normpath(text)` (backslashes, no trailing sep) against `current_working_dir`, which comes from Maya's `cmds.workspace(q=True, rd=True)` (forward slashes + trailing separator) — so the directory read as "changed" on every open even when unchanged, triggering a redundant `_update_workspace_combo()` and a second warning for workspace-less folders. Fixed by normalizing both sides of the comparison (`os.path.normcase(os.path.normpath(...))`). Genuine directory changes still repopulate. Tests: `test_reference_manager.py` +3 (`TestUpdateCurrentDirNormalization`).
- **2026-07-10 — Tube skinning: smooth B-spline basis weights (cubic default) — bends spread across neighboring joints instead of hinging at each station.** Live report: the rigged tube "does not deform smoothly". Root cause was the parametric solver's shape, not its accuracy: `CurveWeights.solve` weighted every vertex to at most the **2 bracketing joints**, with each ring at a joint station pinned 100% to that joint — so a bent chain deformed as near-rigid segments with a visible hinge crease at every joint (the smoothstep profile is C1 in the weight field but concentrates the blend mid-segment, making the plateaus worse). `solve` now takes `degree` (default 3, clamped to `len(joints)-1`) and evaluates a **clamped B-spline basis** over the joint arc-length stations (knot-averaged interior knots via de Boor; Cox-de Boor evaluation) — C2-continuous weights spanning up to `degree+1` joints, which is exactly how the driving spline IK curve itself deforms, so bends and twists spread smoothly down the tube. All prior invariants hold: ring-uniform by construction, rows sum to 1 (partition of unity, pinned exactly), caps + end stations clamp fully to the end joints (`degree+1` end-knot multiplicity — constrained ends still track their anchors 1:1). Degree 1 keeps the legacy pairwise behavior with `profile` shaping (and 2-joint chains — anchor rigs — clamp to it automatically, so their 0.5/0.5 midpoint contract is unchanged). `bind_to_curve` passes `degree` through and now defaults `max_influences` to `degree+1`; every TubeRig bind (one-click, Step 3 `bind_joint_chain`) inherits the smooth basis via `skin_mesh`. Same-day critique pass: `constrain_end_with_falloff` no longer passes `source_influence` to `apply_falloff` — the pairwise anchor↔end-joint blend zeroed every other influence inside the falloff radius and snapped back to the multi-joint row at the boundary (a weight-field discontinuity — a crease — exactly in the constrained end's blend zone, made more visible by the spread weights); the anchor weight's remainder now redistributes across the vertex's existing influences (skinPercent semantics), continuous at the boundary and smooth-basis-preserving (SkinUtils' pairwise mode itself is unchanged + still tested). Tests: `test_skinning.py` +3 (`test_quadratic_basis_values` — exact Bézier values at the mid ring, `test_influence_support_bounded_by_degree`, `test_smooth_basis_spreads_station_rings` — interior station rings must NOT be rigid while end stations stay pinned) with the legacy ≤2-influence case now explicit `degree=1`; `TestTubeRigSkinning`/`TestStepOneClickParity` bounds updated to the cubic ≤4. Registry regenerated.

- **2026-07-10 — Macro hotkeys now actually land: `set_macro` guarantees an editable hotkey set (new `ensure_editable_hotkey_set`), and `apply_bindings` is per-entry resilient + persists to prefs.** User report: "when I manually set a preset, the hotkeys do nothing" and preset bindings never survived a session. Live-diagnosed in a fresh GUI Maya against the user's real prefs: the active hotkey set at boot was the locked factory `Maya_Default` (a stray `Maya_Default_Duplicate` existed but wasn't current) — the active preset `m3trik` existed while **zero** `m_*` bindings were live, so a binding either raised out of `cmds.hotkey` or landed in a set that isn't current at boot; either way "the hotkey does nothing". Fixes: (1) new `hotkey_collisions.ensure_editable_hotkey_set()` (public; builds on the existing `_is_hotkey_set_editable`) — when the locked factory set is current, switch to the well-known user set `mayatk` (`MACRO_HOTKEY_SET`), creating it once sourced from the current set, exactly what Maya's own Hotkey Editor prompts to do; `set_macro` calls it (best-effort — headless has no hotkey-set registry) before binding. (2) `apply_bindings` wraps each entry in try/except — pre-fix, one raising chord aborted the whole preset apply mid-loop (killing both the manual load and tentacle's startup apply) — and flushes `cmds.savePrefs(hotkeys=True)` afterwards, since a `cmds.hotkey` edit lives only in memory until a *clean* exit (same rationale as `_unbind_maya_hotkey`). Ruled out by probe before fixing: `commandLanguage` was NOT the bug (Maya 2025 defaults runTimeCommands created from Python to `"python"`; invoke verified headless). **Verified end-to-end in the live GUI Maya (user prefs):** `set_macro("m_group", key="alt+9")` auto-switched to the `mayatk` set, `alt+9` → `m_groupCommand` in the live `assignCommand` registry, and MEL-invoking the runTimeCommand (the hotkey's exact path) executed the macro; session cleaned up, prefs never written. Tests: `test_macros.py` +4 (`TestEnsureEditableHotkeySet` ×3 mocked locked/reuse/editable paths; `TestApplyBindingsResilience`) — 59/59 scoped. Companion blendertk fix same day (its own "hotkeys do nothing" cause: unregistered dispatcher operator); tentacle startup apply shipped same day (see those CHANGELOGs). Registry regenerated.

- **2026-07-10 — RizomUV bridge: auto-unwrap presets do a true re-unwrap (weld-first), organic switches to Mosaic segmentation, and an engine bug-fix pass — every Lua change live-verified against headless RizomUV 2020.1.** The review was probe-driven: a new standalone harness (`test/rizom_headless_probe.py`, no Maya needed) renders each preset through the real wrapper/substitution path against generated OBJ meshes and runs the installed Rizom `-cfi`, asserting exit 0 + file rewritten + UVs changed — the same gate any future Lua edit must pass (2020.1 access-violates on unknown fields, so nothing ships unprobed). **Presets:** (1) `unwrap_hard`/`unwrap_organic` previously only *added* cuts on top of the incoming layout (the wrapper loads XYZUVW+UVWProps, so existing island borders always survived ZomCut) — measured: a 6-island cube stayed 6 islands through "auto unwrap". Both now weld ALL existing seams first behind a new `WELD_SEAMS` knob (default on; off restores the old additive behavior), probe-verified (24→18 vt on the seamed cube). (2) `unwrap_organic` no longer keys off `SharpEdges` — dihedral detection is useless on smooth meshes (its own header admitted the shared 39° default was wrong for it) — and instead drives island creation from Mosaic segmentation (`QuasiDevelopable`, new `DEVELOPABILITY` knob 0–1, default 0.5), keeping Pipes/Handle cutters + StretchLimiter; the full combination probes clean on 2020.1. **Parameters:** `MIX` was labeled "Mutations", colliding with `PACK_MAX_MUTATIONS`'s label — two indistinguishable widgets in the panel; now "Mix". New label-uniqueness regression. **Engine (`_rizom_bridge.py`):** (a) the public `script_path` getter crashed with `AttributeError` after setting raw Lua content (`_prepare_script_file` stored a `str`; the getter calls `.as_posix()`) — it now always stores a `Path`; (b) two originals sharing a leaf name under different parents collapsed to one `_export_name_map` key, cross-wiring the UV transfer back onto the wrong mesh — duplicates now get an indexed suffix (`name_<i>__RZTMP`), one unique key + destination per export (regression extends the existing collision test); (c) custom scripts carrying their own ZomLoad/ZomSave bypassed BOTH version-stripping and placeholder substitution (raw `__KEY__` tokens went to Rizom); the passthrough now runs after both; (d) `.obj` export was a trap — the setter accepted it but the exporter always wrote FBX data, producing a file Rizom can't parse; export_path is now honestly FBX-only and the dead OBJ import branch is deleted; (e) the version parser required digits adjacent to "RizomUV" and returned `(0,0)` (silently hiding every gated knob) on newer `RizomUV VS RS 2022.2`-style install dirs — extracted to `_parse_rizom_version` (year-anchored, handles all known naming variants, unit-tested); (f) the hard 120s Rizom timeout (dense meshes exceed it) is now `RizomUVBridge(timeout=)`, default 600; (g) the impossible src/dst length-mismatch branch and its duplicated pairwise fallback collapsed; cleanup (delete imports, drop the `RizomUVImport` namespace — now a class constant — restore selection) runs in a `finally` so a failed transfer can't leak import nodes; (h) script file I/O pinned to UTF-8; texture collection dedupes order-preserving (docstring claimed unique paths; the code didn't). Suite: `test_uv_rizom_bridge` 12 green (was 5) in a fresh port-mode Maya; probe PASS on all presets incl. the weld-off Lua branch. blendertk mirror same day (dedupe + header-button objectName; the round-trip pipeline itself remains unported/ledgered there).

- **2026-07-10 — Tube Rig: constrained hoses now behave naturally (mid-control follow groups + auto-bend on by default).** Live report: after One-Click (spline defaults) + Add End Constraints, "the tube still moves away from its end constraints." Probe-measured: the tube ends were actually glued (gaps ≤0.24 under carry/stretch/lateral) — the failure was the **body**: the mid control stays nailed to its build position, so carrying both anchors +5 moved the tube middle only **1.18** (and compression accordioned dead-straight, end gap 0.78). Two fixes: **(1)** intermediate spline controls now ride between the end controls via a `<rig>_follow_N_GRP` above their offset group — point-constrained by arc position (mo=True), generalized to N-point layouts — so posing/carrying the ends carries the whole hose while the controls stay hand-animatable on top; **(2)** the spline preset enables auto-bend by default and the `autoBend` attr's default is now 0.5 instead of 0 (an "enabled" auto-bend was a silent no-op until the animator discovered the attribute) — compression bows the hose out instead of accordioning it. Re-measured: carry mid **5.00/5.00** exact with 0.00 end gaps; compression bows (max 2.69) with the end gap down to 0.34 (the falloff blend zone — reads as sleeve softness). Regression-locked in `test_tube_rig.py::TestHoseNaturalBehavior`.
- **2026-07-10 — Preview/CleanupContract: rollback now restores snapshotted STRING attrs (silent no-op fixed).** The attr-snapshot restore loop called a bare `setAttr` for non-double3 values — Maya raises on string attrs without `type="string"`, and the loop's per-attr `except` swallowed it, so any string channel recorded via `contract.record_modification` was silently left at its mutated value after rollback. Surfaced by the Shadow Rig preview (which snapshots the `data_export` `shadow_metadata` channel before rebuilding); fixed with an explicit string branch. Regression-locked Qt-free in `test_preview.py::TestCleanupContractAttrRestore` (string restore + scalar-path guard), verified in mayapy standalone.

- **2026-07-10 — Shadow Rig → Unity is now true plug-and-play through the standard export pipeline: a `shadow_metadata` carrier channel (Scene Exporter / `data_export`) + a unitytk import template that finishes the setup engine-side — verified end-to-end against a real licensed batch Unity.** Follow-up to the same-day overhaul below, closing the hand-off gap that made "export to Unity" a manual chore: Unity had no way to recreate the unlit-transparent shadow material (its importer maps StingrayPBS to a lit "Autodesk Interactive" material). Integrated via the ecosystem's established custom-data pattern, NOT a bespoke export path: new `ShadowRig.refresh_export_metadata()` publishes `{"version", "planes": [{name, texture, intensity}]}` onto the shared `data_export` carrier (at create/bake, and registered in `FbxUtils._KNOWN_PRODUCERS` so `run_export_preparers` — the Scene Exporter's pre-export refresh — always ships a current channel, clearing it in plane-less scenes). The record's texture is resolved from the material network's **opacity chain** (stingray `.opacity`/`TEX_mask_mapX`, or standardSurface via the `*_opacity_mult`) — `listHistory` doesn't traverse a ShaderFX node's inputs, and the loaded Stingray graph carries three stock IBL preset file nodes a material-wide search wrongly matches (found via a live mayapy probe). `create()` also captures `self.group` post-parent (blendertk parity). **Unity side (unitytk):** new `ShadowPlaneController.cs` template — consumes `shadow_metadata` off the carrier exactly like the lightmap channel (records join renderers by GameObject name), rewires the imported material **in place** to a pipeline-correct unlit-transparent shader (Built-in `Unlit/Transparent`; URP Unlit + transparent surface; HDRP best-effort) bound to the silhouette PNG (a project asset resolved by the recorded filename), turns off shadow casting/receiving + light/reflection probes on the plane, pins `*_shadow` PNG imports (`alphaIsTransparency`), and removes stale markers on a channel-less re-export. The integration test exposed two real Unity API traps, both documented in the template: the default ImportViaMaterialDescription pipeline never calls the legacy `OnAssignMaterialModel`, and it overwrites `OnPostprocessMaterial` edits after the fact — only an `OnPostprocessModel`-time in-place mutation survives (all three approaches tested against a live batch Unity). **Verified:** unitytk `test_shadow_plane_integration.py` 3/3 on a licensed batch Unity: real `ShadowRig.create` + `bake` + `FbxUtils.run_export_preparers` (exercising the producer registration) in mayapy → group + carrier exported → imported prefab reads back `Unlit/Transparent` with the silhouette bound, cast/receive/probes off, `ShadowPlaneController` attached, PNG pinned, baked keys arriving as an AnimationClip; mayatk `test_shadow_rig` 10/10 (channel publish/refresh/clear + known-producer registration); blendertk mirror 53/53 (see its CHANGELOG); parity sweep exit 0; registries regenerated.

- **2026-07-10 — Lightmap Baker: Atlas by Material is now fully standalone — the atlas rect is repacked into the lightmap UVs at pack time, so the export is plug-and-play in any engine (no unitytk, no scaleOffset binding).** Previously the atlas kept every object's lightmap UVs at 0-1 and published the rect only as `scaleOffset` metadata; nothing but unitytk's Unity editor helper ever applied it, so the atlas was engine-wrong without that script (per-object mode never had the coupling). `pack_atlas` now applies each object's area-weighted rect directly to its lightmap UV set (`_transform_lightmap_uvs`, exact-affine `polyEditUV` scale-about-origin + translate) — the FBX carries atlas-ready UV2, the industry-standard packed-atlas layout, and the manifest publishes an identity scaleOffset. The remap is fully non-destructive: `commit_lightmap(uv_rects=)` records the applied rect on the marker (`uvRect`, revert bookkeeping only — never published), `revert_lightmap` inverts it back to the original unit-square layout, and a new pre-bake guard in `bake_fused` (`_restore_atlased_uvs`) restores + strips a stale remap so a direct-API re-bake can't bake into the shrunken layout or compound the rect. Degrade paths stay engine-correct: an object with no lightmap UV set (or a failed edit) keeps its own per-object map with an identity rect (warned); instanced transforms — which share one UV set and can't have per-instance rects once UVs are baked — dedupe globally to the first transform's rect (warned; global rather than per-group, since per-instance shading could land two instances in different groups and double-remap the shared set); and each material group packs independently (`_pack_group` + per-group guard), so a group-level failure falls back to that group's per-object maps — sources intact, no UVs moved — instead of poisoning other groups or the commit (a source map is only deleted after its object's UV repack succeeded, and UVs only remap after the atlas file is safely on disk). `scale_offsets` remains on `commit_lightmap` for legacy manifests; the panel no longer passes it. Tooltips/help/docstrings rewritten engine-agnostic: unitytk's `LightmapMetadataController.cs` is now described as what it is — an optional one-file convenience that auto-binds Unity's *native* lightmap slots (a Unity platform necessity for native binding, not a mayatk dependency); without it the maps work via plain UV2 sampling. Verified headless (60/60 `test_lightmap_baker`, incl. new: exact affine UV-bounds remap, no-lightmap-set fallback, group-failure per-object fallback with UV-bounds unchanged, uvRect marker + identity manifest, revert restore round-trip, idempotent bake guard, forward+invert transform identity). Mirrored in blendertk (`uv_rects` param + marker, 28/28 live headless; `pack_atlas` port stays ledgered).

- **2026-07-10 — Naming panel: Strip Chars `Trailing` checkbox → `Leading`/`Trailing` combo (`cmb002`); restored the header pin/auto-hide window behavior; Find/Rename fields no longer persist between sessions.** (1) The Strip Chars end-to-strip is now an explicit two-item choice instead of a boolean — default `Trailing` preserves the prior checked-by-default state; `tb002` derives `trailing = currentText() == "Trailing"`. Engine `strip_chars` unchanged. (2) `header_init` was missing the `config_buttons("menu", "collapse", "pin")` call every sibling gesture-scoped `edit_utils` panel has (mirror / cut_on_axis / bridge), so the naming window had fallen back to the default hide button; restored the pin button + auto-hide-on-key-release. (3) `txt000`/`txt001` (Find / Rename) now set `restore_state = False` so their text starts empty each session instead of restoring the last search/rename string. Mirrored in blendertk; parity sweep exit 0.

- **2026-07-10 — Wheel / Shadow / Telescope rig panels: tooltips brought up to the Tube Rig `fmt` standard (single-source, structured, per-widget).** Follow-up to the 2026-07-09 Tube Rig pass, applied to the three remaining quick-rig tools. Each slots class now owns a `_init_tooltips()` (called from `__init__`, the same pattern as `tube_rig.py`) that builds every option/action tooltip with the uitk `fmt` helper — titles, bodies, `steps`, `rows`, `sections`, and `notes` — so hover help reads consistently across all four rigs and states exactly what to select and in what order. **wheel_rig**: consolidated tooltips that were scattered across three sources — raw-HTML `b000` string in `__init__`, plain-text `.ui` properties, and plain menu/option-box strings — into `_init_tooltips()` (`txt000`, `s000`, `cmb000` axis-mapping rows matching the runtime combo, `chk010`, `b000`); the header `World Space` menu toggle and the `Get Wheel Size` option-box button now use `fmt` too. **shadow_rig**: nine `.ui` plain tooltips migrated to `fmt`, and the two previously-undocumented buttons (`b000` Create Shadow, `b001` Reset to Defaults) gained tooltips; mode/axis descriptions align with the header help. **telescope_rig**: added the missing per-widget tooltips (`spin_collapsed`, `btn_build`) and fixed the header's double-numbered selection list (nested manual numbering inside an `<ol>` → a `sections` block + clean `steps`). The now-redundant `<property name="toolTip">` entries were stripped from `shadow_rig.ui` (7) and `wheel_rig.ui` (7) — including the dead ones on wheel_rig's runtime-deleted `chk000`/`cmb001` and its `.ui`-vs-`__init__` double-set `b000` — so Python is the single source of truth (the compiled `_ui.py` twins regenerated to match; `tube_rig`'s `.ui` already carried none). No behavior change: verified by rendering all 39 `fmt`/`kbd` calls across the four modules to non-empty HTML and an AST parse of every slot + regenerated `_ui.py`.
- **2026-07-10 — Tube Rig: 'Add End Constraints' actually moves the tube ends now (constraint routed through the end controls).** Live report: the utility did nothing (or errored) regardless of anchor selection order. Probe-reproduced per mode — the chain joints are the wrong constraint target on *every* built rig: **spline** joints are IK-driven, so the direct `parentConstraint` was silently overridden (the old "constrain the ikHandle instead" special case was doubly dead: its effector-parent comparison never matched the end joint, and a spline ikHandle's translation is ignored by the solver anyway — block deleted); **anchor** joints already carry point/orient constraints to their controls, so the second driver raised `Object is already connected` (the raw error the user hit); **FK** joints blended 50/50 against their control's constraint (measured: exactly half the anchor's motion). `constrain_end_with_falloff` now routes the constraint through the rig's start/end **control** (new `_end_control` resolver: live build records first, then builder naming conventions for post-restart rigs, ambiguity narrowed to the rig's group; `mo=True` so an off-end anchor doesn't snap), falling back to the direct joint constraint only for bare chains with no controls. `b004` additionally auto-assigns each anchor to its **nearest tube end**, so anchor selection order no longer matters (tooltip updated). Verified per mode: moving an anchor now carries the tube end, its joint, and its control 1:1 while the opposite end stays pinned. Regression-locked in `test_tube_rig.py::TestEndConstraints` (three tests, one per pre-fix failure signature). Critique pass on the same day: `b004` now rejects selections that don't expand to a ≥2-joint chain (picking the END joint of a spline/FK chain previously constrained that one joint from both anchors), and the Step-2 duplicate-controls precondition delegates to `_end_control` (its hand-rolled check tested `<rig>_start`, a name the builders never produce — leftover anchor controls went undetected and re-runs stacked duplicates).

- **2026-07-10 — Shadow Rig realism + Unity-usability overhaul: projected ground anchor, objectHeight-proportional stretch, rise fade, light-view silhouette, world-space light reads, one-click bake, shared rasterizer (cv2 dependency dropped).** Full-module critique-and-improve pass on `rig_utils/shadow_rig.py`, mirrored 1:1 to blendertk the same day. **Realism (the expression math, both modes):** (1) the plane now hangs off the **projected ground anchor** — where the light ray through the contact point hits the ground (`S = L + (C−L)·(Ly−G)/(Ly−Cy)`, clamped) — so a jumping/aerial target's shadow slides away from the light instead of staying glued under it (degrades exactly to the old behavior while grounded); (2) stretch is now **proportional to the target's measured height** (`scale = 1 + objH·ratio/size` — the old `1 + ratio` implicitly assumed every object is as tall as its own footprint, under-stretching poles and over-stretching slabs), with the hardcoded 4.0 clamp promoted to a keyable `maxStretch`; (3) new keyable `fadeHeight`: opacity fades out as the contact rises off the ground (blob-shadow/penumbra convention; default 2× objectHeight — the old fade only reacted to the *light* dropping); (4) the silhouette default (`axis='auto'`, plus explicit `'light'`) is now rendered **as seen from the light's horizontal bearing** (u-axis `(dz,0,−dx)`, v = up — the physically correct ground-shadow shape) instead of a light-agnostic widest-dimension side view, falling back to the old heuristic only when the light is overhead. **Bugs:** the expressions read the light via `.translate` — LOCAL space, so a parented/grouped source silently warped the shadow from the wrong position; both light and contact now come through `worldMatrix→decomposeMatrix` (pinned by `test_light_world_space`). Bounding-box measurement now uses **mesh shapes only** (`_world_bbox`): `exactWorldBoundingBox(targets)` included the rig's own contact locator (parented under the target before the plane is measured), inflating `objectHeight`/footprint by the locator's display size — blendertk's empty-skipping bounds never had this. The build-vs-expression ground-offset mismatch (0.01 vs 0.005, a visible first-eval snap) is unified on `GROUND_OFFSET`; `create()` finally exposes `ground_height` (the ctor supported it; the factory hardcoded 0). The dead `_create_silhouette_fallback` (referenced nonexistent `self.target`; zero callers) is deleted. **DRY / packaging:** the hand-rolled triangle rasterization + contact-falloff block (~150 lines duplicating `pythontk.ImgUtils.rasterize_silhouette`, extracted for the Blender port but never consumed back) is replaced by the shared rasterizer (world tris still gathered per-instance via om2), killing the module's top-level `import cv2` — an undeclared hard dependency that broke module import in a clean env (PNG now saved via Pillow, a guaranteed transitive dep) — and unifying falloff output across DCCs. **Unity usability:** new `bake(start, end)` / `bake_planes()` / `find_shadow_planes()` — bakes the expression-driven channels to keyframes over the playback range and deletes the live rig nodes (expression + decomposeMatrix), replacing the manual Edit ▸ Keys ▸ Bake Simulation step; surfaced as a **Bake to Keyframes** button (`b002`) on the panel (selected planes, or all). Help text/docstrings now state honestly that the opacity fade is shader-side (FBX carries no material animation into Unity) and that orbit mode is the right choice for animated lights (stretch mode's silhouette mirrors when the light crosses sides — documented limitation). Slots: resolution parse hardened (try/except, blendertk parity); prints → `LoggingMixin` logger; unused imports (`CoreUtils`, typing) removed. New `test/test_shadow_rig.py` (8 tests: build/attrs, evaluated stretch/anchor/rise-fade math against hand-derived reference values, world-space light via a moved parent group, orbit rotation/scale, bake round-trip incl. no-op re-bake, plane discovery) — registered in the runner defaults; suite green in a fresh port-mode Maya. Parity sweep exit 0; registries regenerated. Building a tube rig when nodes sharing its names already existed elsewhere (a second rig, a re-run without teardown, debris) failed mid-build with *"No object matches name"*. Root cause was in the shared `Controls.create`: it parents the control under its offset group but discarded the reparent return, so `ControlNodes.control` pointed at the pre-parent path — which stops resolving the instant a same-named control exists (Maya returns a path-qualified name to disambiguate). `Controls.create` now re-captures the control after each move and resolves both control and group to their **shortest unambiguous** name (bare leaf when unique — unchanged for the common case and its contract tests — full DAG path only under collision; new `Controls._addressable`). Because tube_rig reparents the control's group *after* creation (re-staling the ref), its three control builders (`create_spline_drivers`, `create_fk_controls`, `create_anchor_controls`) now re-derive each control's path from its group via `_control_path`. Regression-locked in `test_tube_rig.py::TestNameCollisionSafety` (spline/fk/anchor each built over pre-seeded colliding control names). Verified: two same-named rigs now build cleanly across all three strategies.
- **2026-07-10 — Lightmap round 2 (probe-driven): RTT premultiply fix, native per-shape white card via `-shader` (batched, 7.45×), end-to-end composite invariants, denoise ruled out with evidence.** Every change this round was gated on a fresh-mayapy measurement probe. **(1) RTT premultiplies RGB by texel coverage** (measured: island-edge texels carry `alpha ×` the true lighting — raw/interior ratio 0.502 at mean alpha 0.511; ÷alpha recovers 0.971) — every map shipped to date had coverage-darkened island borders that dilation then smeared into the gutters. `_dilate_lightmap` now un-premultiplies partial texels before dilation (`rgb = α·L̂`, so the division is bounded by scene radiance, not a noise blow-up; unit test pins it). **(2) `arnoldRenderToTexture -shader` applies per shape being baked** (measured: batch-baking a grey floor + red wall with a white override left the wall RED during the floor's render — floor bounce R/G ≈ 22,000 — while the wall's own map read the card's exact 1/π): that is a **native, batched per-object white card**. `bake_separated` now passes a true-white (Kd 1) lambert as the `-shader` override instead of swapping materials — identical physics to the per-object carding shipped yesterday (the red-bounce e2e still pins it), **zero scene mutation** (shading is never reassigned at all), and it batches. **(3) Batch mode**: `TextureBaker.bake(batch=True)` bakes the whole selection in ONE RTT call — the per-object loop re-translates the scene N times; measured **7.45× (151.6s → 20.3s)** on 8 objects in a 40-object scene. Batch requires unique shape leaf names (RTT names files by shape leaf) and falls back to the per-object loop with a warning otherwise; `bake_separated` batches by default (`batch=False` restores per-object cancellation granularity). The material-swap machinery (`_apply_white_card`/`_restore_white_card`, the per-object aggregation loop) is deleted. **(4) End-to-end composite invariants** now pin the pipeline the way Unity consumes it: `test_fused_equals_effective_albedo_times_separated` (fused bake ≈ effective-albedo × lighting-only bake per channel, ±3% — exactly the engine's `albedo × lightmap` composite) and `test_atlas_samples_back_through_scale_offset` (sampling the atlas at `uv·scale+offset`, flip included, returns each object's own texels). **(5) Denoise: closed with evidence** — OIDN wired to `defaultArnoldRenderOptions.imagers[0]` produces bit-identical RTT output (imagers are ignored by the bake driver), `noice.exe` needs variance AOVs RTT can't emit, and no standalone OIDN binary ships: the supported noise lever is the preset-driven `gi_samples`. Also: `MayaConnection.get_available_port` now delegates to the new `pythontk.NetUtils.is_port_bindable` (SSoT for the bind-vs-connect probe semantics, same fix applied to extapps' `PainterConnection`).

- **2026-07-09 — MayaConnection: bind-based port availability + configurable launch wait (zombie-squatted port made every runner launch hang).** Root-caused from three consecutive `run_tests.py` launch failures: a hung (Not Responding) test Maya held a **bound-but-not-listening** socket on 7002 — `get_available_port`'s connect-based probe read that as "free", each new Maya's `commandPort` then silently failed to bind, and the runner waited out its timeout on a port that could never open (orphaning a fully-started Maya each time). The availability probe now **binds** the candidate port (`bind()` succeeds only if a new Maya could bind it too); `_is_port_free` deliberately stays connect-based — its call site asks the different question "is a session *listening* here to attach to". Also: the hardcoded 180s launch wait (cold laptop starts exceed it) is now 360s, overridable via `MAYATK_PORT_TIMEOUT`. Regression: `test_maya_connection.py::test_get_available_port_skips_bound_but_not_listening` (squats an ephemeral port without listening, asserts it is skipped).
- **2026-07-09 — Lightmap pipeline quality pass: true-white per-object card, pinned GI render settings, real RTT flag surface, NaN-safe half-float EXR, atlas gutters, applied intensity, honest uvIndex.** A full review of the bake→FBX→Unity chain (with fresh-mayapy measurement probes) found two systematic quality bugs and a set of contract gaps, all fixed with pixel-value regressions. **(1) The "white card" was an 80% grey card** — `_apply_white_card` set `lambert.color` but not `.diffuse` (Maya default 0.8), so every lighting-only map baked ~20% dark (measured ratio 0.8006); the card now pins `Kd = 1`. **(2) The card is applied per object, not all-at-once**: `bake_separated` bakes each object in its own pass with only that object carded, so indirect light carries the real neighbor materials — correct bounce energy, correct color bleed (Phase 0's accepted +13% all-white over-bounce is gone, which mattered doubly because…), and emissive/transparent neighbors now contribute/shadow correctly during everyone else's bake. **(3) GI is a scene render setting, not an RTT flag** — the bake ran at whatever `defaultArnoldRenderOptions` held (mtoa scene default: 1 bounce, 2 diffuse samples, the single biggest quality lever left dangling, and non-reproducible between scenes). `TextureBaker` gained `render_settings=` (snapshot→set→restore around the bake; unknown attrs skipped), `LightmapBaker` gained first-class `gi_depth`/`gi_samples` dials, and the presets now carry them (preview 1/2, quest 2/4, desktop 3/6). **(4) RTT's real flag surface**: `format=`/`all_uvs=` are not registered flags (probed `cmds.help`; mtoa 5.4.5 silently tolerates them) and output is always `<shape>.exr` — the fake flags are gone, the Arnold path pins the effective format to EXR (a `file_format="png"` request previously glob-missed every output), and a multi-shape transform's file is now matched by shape leaf name instead of `sorted()[-1]`. **(5) Every shipped EXR goes through one `_write_lightmap_exr` policy**: NaN→0 / ±inf + fireflies clamped to half-max 65504 (one bad ray no longer spreads through dilation or atlas resize, and can't become `inf` in the half encode), written half-float (halves disk + Unity import). **(6) `pack_atlas` rects are inset by a resolution-scaled pixel gutter** (freed borders dilate-filled from an *exact* placed-rect coverage mask — new pythontk `inset_atlas_rects`/`atlas_pixel_rects` primitives, see pythontk CHANGELOG) so mips/bilinear taps can't bleed between neighbors; the published scaleOffset is the inset content rect, so sampling stays exact. **(7) `commit_lightmap(intensity=)` is now real**: Unity's native lightmaps have no per-map multiplier (unitytk parsed the field and ignored it), so a non-1.0 intensity is applied into the texels at commit, once per unique file (atlas-shared files included); default 1.0 = Maya-render parity (Arnold bakes radiance `albedo·E/π`; pass `math.pi` to match Unity-native light conventions instead). **(8) The manifest publishes the lightmap set's real UV index** (warns when ≠1 — Unity only samples uv2 — instead of hardcoding a `1` that hid mis-ordered sets), warns on duplicate leaf names (Unity's join key, first-match-wins), and the bake warns once on a light-less scene (silent black bake). Also: `bake_separated` normalizes inputs to long names up front (a short-name input previously missed the stem dict and fell back to the leaf name) and aggregates `on_progress` across its per-object passes (same tick contract, cancellable). Suite: `test_lightmap_baker` + `test_texture_baker` 67 green headless, including two new Arnold pixel-value regressions that would have caught both measured bugs — a dark-albedo plane under a perpendicular intensity-1 directional must bake to ≈1/π, and a red-wall/grazing-light scene must show *red* bounce on the floor (per-object carding) that a `gi_depth=0` baker kills (settings pinning). Unity-side consumer hardening is in unitytk's CHANGELOG same date; blendertk mirror notes in blendertk's.

- **2026-07-09 — Tube Rig: step-by-step operations unified with One-Click, proportional control sizing, polished tooltips.** The step buttons and the one-click build previously diverged: One-Click ignored *Reverse Direction* and any user edge selection (both honored only by Step 1), Step 2 ignored *Auto-Bend*, silently built a legacy RP-solver IK in FK mode, and refused Anchor mode outright, and Step 3 bound geodesically while One-Click bound parametrically. The strategies are now thin compositions of shared `TubeRig` step methods the slots call directly — `resolve_centerline` (single source for extraction params), `create_anchor_joints`, `create_spline_controls` / `create_anchor_controls` / `create_fk_controls`, and a `bind_joint_chain` that solves the same ring-uniform parametric weights as One-Click (geodesic only as fallback) — so parity holds by construction. Each step now validates its preconditions with actionable messages (mesh selected last, exact anchor joint count, existing controls, *bind-before-falloff*), re-running Bind replaces the mesh's skinCluster, and Anchor mode disables its irrelevant options (squash/auto-bend) while Auto-Bend gates on the 3-control spline layout. **Proportional sizing**: new `TubePath.estimate_radius` (median interior centerline→surface distance, cap-plane samples excluded) drives `TubeRig.resolve_sizes` — controls always scale to the measured tube radius, and *Joint Size* defaults to Auto (half tube radius). All step/option tooltips rebuilt with the uitk `fmt` formatting (explicit what-to-select-in-what-order steps) and moved out of the `.ui` into the slots class. Regression-locked in `test_tube_rig.py` (`TestStepOneClickParity`, `TestProportionalSizing`).
- **2026-07-09 — StyleSetter ships a `Maya` style: the factory-restore counterpart to `Blender.json` the theme combo was missing.** The tentacle preferences theme combo (`cmb003` → `mtk.StyleSetter.list_templates()`) offered only `Blender` — once applied, there was no path back to Maya's look (Maya has no native named-preset selector to fall back on, unlike Blender's Themes dropdown; the bespoke backup/restore was removed 2026-07-05 by request). New `styles/Maya.json` restores the factory values for exactly the keys the Blender style overrides (background/backgroundTop/backgroundBottom, dormant grid + polyEdge palette slots) — same targeted-overlay scope, never a bulk factory reset. Values captured empirically from a Maya 2025.3 GUI launched with a **virgin `MAYA_APP_DIR`** (true factory prefs): notably, `displayRGBColor(resetToFactory=True)` does NOT restore `backgroundTop`/`backgroundBottom` (a first capture on the user's prefs returned the persisted Blender overlay for those keys), so the factory-prefs launch is the only reliable source. **Verified live** (fresh virgin-prefs Maya GUI): `list_templates()` → `{Blender, Maya}`; apply `Blender` then `Maya` returns every key to factory exactly (6/6 checks). Regression-locked in `test_style_setter.py` (rewritten: the shipped-set assertions now expect both styles, a key-surface contract test pins Maya.json as an exact restore counterpart of Blender.json — same rgb keys + display_color name/state entries — and a Blender→Maya round-trip asserts every key lands on the style's stored values; the suite's hygiene snapshot also now derives the keys it captures/restores from the style files themselves, fixing a pre-existing gap where `backgroundTop`/`backgroundBottom` leaked mutated). Suite green GUI-connected (9/9, part of a 63/63 run).

- **2026-07-09 — Test fix: `TestSelectionMacros` now skips under batch/standalone instead of false-failing.** Its four tests assert `cmds.selectMode(query=...)`, which returns `False` regardless of the actual mode under mayapy standalone (known quirk) while passing GUI-connected — the class docstring even claimed "work headlessly". A class-level `skipIf(cmds.about(batch=True))` documents the constraint; verified both ways (GUI: 4/4 pass; mayapy: 4 skipped, suite OK).

- **2026-07-09 — Macro Manager: the shipped `default` preset is now all-unbound (no macro hotkeys out of the box).** The previous shipped default carried the author's full 22-chord binding set, which stomped Maya hotkeys on any "Reset to Default". New contract: `default` = every macro unbound; loading it (header "Reset to Default", per-row reset) clears macro hotkeys, and `apply_saved_macros()` with no active preset registers nothing — bindings are opt-in via user presets (save your set once; the `.active` sidecar re-applies it thereafter). Mirrored in blendertk the same day. Tests updated to the new contract: `test_shipped_default_is_all_unbound`, `test_default_registers_nothing_when_no_active_preset`, and a new `test_applies_active_user_preset` covering the startup path that now matters (active user preset → applied).

- **2026-07-09 — Tube rig: end joints no longer hook toward an opening vertex on open-ended tubes.** Live report (same S-pipe as below): `TubePath._complete_cap_ends` — designed for capped tubes, where the closest surface point past the end is the on-axis cap centre — appended RIM vertices verbatim on open tubes: an angle-cut opening projects past the end ring's centroid plane, satisfying the along-tangent check, and with no cap to hit the closest point sits one wall-radius off-axis. Both end joints curled to the rim. The appended end centre is now the hit's **projection onto the tangent axis** — the hit still tells how far the surface really extends (so open tubes whose boundary rings evade loop detection keep full-length coverage, per the pre-existing `test_open_tube_ends`), but the recovered end stays on-axis for caps and open/angled rims alike (a radial-rejection variant fixed the hook but broke that coverage — projection satisfies both). Verified on the reporting user's mesh (ends terminate on-axis at the openings) and regression-locked with a synthetic open, angle-cut tube (`test_open_angled_tube_ends_stay_on_axis`).

- **2026-07-09 — Test infra: the Maya-mock machinery no longer sabotages real-Maya suite sessions (three stacked leaks).** (1) `test_shot_manifest` assigned `_cmds.undoInfo = MagicMock()` on the real shared module at nine sites with no restore — every module running later in a full-suite session inherited a fake `undoInfo`: undo chunks silently stopped opening and `cmds.undo()` reverted only single commands (`test_skinning.test_set_weights_undoable_undo` failed exactly this way under `--all` while passing scoped). All sites now route through a `_mock_undo_info` helper (`patch.object` + `addCleanup`). (2) `conftest.py` injected `sys.modules["maya.cmds"] = mock_cmds` (and `maya.api.OpenMaya`) **unconditionally** — in a fresh test-Maya session where nothing had imported `cmds` on the Python side yet, its hasattr-guarded attribute loop then bound the mock onto the **real `maya` package attribute** (which `import maya.cmds as cmds` resolves through *before* `sys.modules`), silently giving every subsequent test module a MagicMock `cmds` (`test_skinning` reported 8F/26E from mock artifacts whenever the shot modules ran first). Injection now only happens when a real `maya.cmds` is not importable — and the import probe itself closes the fresh-session window by populating the package attribute properly. (3) `run_tests.py`'s per-module cleanup restored `sys.modules` but not the parent-package **attributes**, and its snapshot silently skipped keys not yet imported (unrestorable forever after); it now imports the core Maya modules before snapshotting, restores package attributes, and evicts fake entries for unsnapshotted keys. Diagnosed by class-level bisection inside the port-Maya runner after standalone repros exonerated `sys.modules` poisoning alone (the parent-attr import path shadows it).

- **2026-07-09 — Tube rig: auto-joints no longer collapse into a ring on user-modeled pipes (edge-loop orientation + polySelect closure quirk).** Live report: on a modeled S-pipe the auto-joint stage produced a handful of joints clumped at one bend, silently. Two stacked causes in `TubePath.get_edge_loop_centers`. (1) The walker seeded from `all_edges[0]` **assuming it was circumferential** — on user-modeled pipes e[0] is commonly longitudinal (polyCylinder just happens to number a rim edge first), which transposes the loop/ring traversal so every "cross-section centre" is a longitudinal-strip centroid: a small ring of points around the mesh centroid instead of a path down the tube. The seed is now the first CLOSED loop found (closure = cross-section), skipping edges covered by rejected loops so the scan is bounded by loop count; open arcs are also filtered per-station. (2) The closure test itself must count **unique** edges: `polySelect -q -edgeLoop` signals a closed loop by repeating the seed edge at the end of the returned list (e.g. `[1, 175, 136, 97, 58, 1]`), so a naive `#verts == #edges` check reads every closed ring as open and the whole edge-loop path silently defers to the sampler fallback. Verified against the reporting user's actual mesh (imported from the Maya clipboard into a fresh standalone session): 4 clumped joints → 20 cross-section centres tracing the full S-curve. Regression-locked by `TestEdgeLoopOrientation` with a synthetic `MFnMesh` tube whose edge 0 is deterministically longitudinal, asserting the edge-loop path directly (a sampler fallback also spans the tube, so the dispatcher alone can mask the bug).

- **2026-07-09 — Precision auto-skinning: new `SkinUtils` + `CurveWeights` (`rig_utils/skinning.py`); TubeRig rewired onto parametric arc-length weights with dual-quaternion binding.** `SkinUtils` is the general skinning surface: `bind`/`unbind` with the full skinCluster arg map (closest/hierarchy/heatmap/geodesic bind methods, classic/DQS/blended skinning — heatmap/geodesic auto-fall back to closest-distance when the bind fails without a GL context; geodesic itself verified working headless in mayapy 2025), batched API 2.0 weight I/O (`get_weights`/`set_weights` via `MFnSkinCluster` on a **physical-index contract** — `influenceObjects()` order, never logical plug indices — with an undo-safe `skinPercent` route and old-weights return for manual restore), sparse `set_vertex_weights` with skinPercent-style redistribution, `prune_weights`/`normalize_weights`/`set_max_influences` (actually re-weights each vertex to its top-N; Maya alone doesn't), `set_skinning_method`, `copy_weights`/`mirror_weights`, `deformerWeights` XML `export_weights`/`import_weights`, distance-based `apply_falloff`, and `add_delta_mush`. `CurveWeights.solve` is the tube-precision centerpiece: vertices project to arc length along a curve/centerline (new `NurbsUtils.get_arc_lengths`/`get_curve_length`; `MFnNurbsCurve` closestPoint→findLengthFromParam) and blend between bracketing joint stations (smoothstep default) — **ring-uniform by construction, ≤2 influences, caps clamp to the end joints**. All three TubeRig strategies now hand their centerline/IK curve to `skin_mesh` → `SkinUtils.bind_to_curve` (parametric + DQS; geodesic+DQS when no curve is available), `bind_joint_chain` (UI "Bind Joint Chain") binds geodesic+DQS instead of closest+linear, and `constrain_end_with_falloff`'s per-vertex `skinPercent` loop became one batched `apply_falloff` call with identical linear math. Quality is regression-locked in the new `test_skinning.py` (36 tests) + `TestTubeRigSkinning` (3): under a 90° end twist, identical parametric weights collapse a mid ring to ~0.71 radius with classic linear blending but hold ~1.00 under DQS (candy-wrapper metric), and the old raw closest-distance bind strands mid-tube twist near 0° where ~45° is due (dead-twist metric) — the two failure modes this rewrite eliminates. 65 tests green across both suites.

- **2026-07-09 — Tube rig: group/multi-shape selections resolve to the mesh; user-typed rig names no longer abort joint creation; fallback centerline reaches the tube ends.** Three live-reported "Create Joints does nothing / joints not along the tube" fixes. (1) Selecting the **group** containing the tube (common outliner pick), or a transform carrying a leftover second mesh shape, crashed `get_edge_loop_centers` with `polySelect`'s cryptic *"This command requires at least 1 argument…; found 0"* — `polyListComponentConversion` silently expands a group's descendants to edges, but `polySelect` drops non-mesh transforms. New module-level `_resolve_mesh_shape` (transform / shape / component / group → first non-intermediate mesh shape, warning on multiples) now anchors `TubePath.get_centerline` (which raises a clear `ValueError` when nothing resolves — an empty group previously died deep in the fallback with `"The source attribute 'None.outMesh' cannot be found"`), `get_edge_loop_centers` (loop queries and components now run on the full shape path), and `TubeRig.__init__` (`self.mesh` = the mesh transform even from a group pick, so skinning binds the mesh; plain transforms still accepted — b002 builds a `TubeRig` from a joint after a restart). `create_joints_from_tube` message-boxes the `ValueError` (b000 already catches). (2) `TubeRig.__init__` sanitizes the rig name (`Naming.strip_illegal_chars` + leading-digit guard): a name like `hose-01` or `my rig` from the panel's free-text field crashed `generate_joint_chain`'s stale-sweep `cmds.ls` pattern before any joint was created, and names Maya auto-sanitizes on `createNode` (leading digit, `*`) mismatched that pattern so reruns accumulated duplicate chains. (3) `TubePath.get_centerline` now applies `_complete_cap_ends` to the sampler fallbacks (surface-normal / bounding-box), not just the edge-loop path: on triangulated tubes (imported/booleaned geo, which have no quad loops) the sampled end estimates were pulled ~one radius inboard by the cap planes, leaving ~20% of the tube unrigged (bent triangulated coverage 70%→91%, straight 80%→100%). (4) The step workflow (b001 'Create Joints' → b002 'Create Controls') could never look the rig up from a selected joint: `get_tube_rig` pre-resolved through `NodeUtils.get_transform_node` — a *list* for joints, defeating `for_node` — and only `build()` registered the rig group, which the step workflow never calls. `for_node` now receives the raw node, and group registration lives in the `rig_group` property (where the group is materialized; `build()`'s duplicate removed), so b002 reuses the b001 rig instead of silently spinning up a second one with a mangled name. b001 also no longer shows a success box after a failure box. Not mirrored to blendertk: its `TubePath` is the intentionally-divergent point-cloud slice (ledgered), Blender names are freeform, and its slots pre-filter mesh objects. +6 regression tests (`test_group_selection_resolves_to_tube`, `test_multi_shape_transform_uses_first_shape`, `test_meshless_input_raises_cleanly`, `test_rig_name_with_illegal_characters`, `test_triangulated_tube_fallback_covers_ends`, `test_for_node_after_create_joints_only`): 26 total, all green.

- **2026-07-09 — Gesture-scoped tool windows: seven panels pin/auto-hide on `key_show` release; UV Transform renamed "Shell Xform".** `reference_manager`, `color_id`, `exploded_view`, `bridge`, `cut_on_axis`, `mirror`, and `shell_xform` now configure a `pin` header button in `header_init` instead of inheriting `MayaUiHandler`'s blanket `"mayatk"→hide` default, so — like the tentacle scene panels — they auto-hide when the marking-menu activation key is released (drag the header to pin one open). No other change is needed: `MainWindow.request_hide` already gates auto-hide on the presence of a pin button, and `setup_lifecycle` already wires `key_show_release → request_hide` for every standalone window. `reference_manager` keeps its `refresh` button. Separately, the `shell_xform` panel's visible title (Header text + help-tooltip title) is now **Shell Xform**; the module id / registration name stays `shell_xform` (so `marking_menu.show("shell_xform")` is unchanged).

- **2026-07-09 — Tube rig repaired: rebuilds, anchor placement/stretch, twist, end coverage, edge-selection centerline.** A full audit of `rig_utils/tube_rig.py` (post-pymel-migration regressions, live-verified in mayapy) found and fixed seven functional bugs: (1) rebuilding on an already-rigged mesh crashed on joint-name collisions — `build()` now tears down the previous rig first (new `TubeRig.teardown()`: mesh skinCluster, rig group, and `<rig_name>_*` DG utility strays); (2) anchor (piston) joints/controls landed at 1/3–2/3 of the tube instead of its ends, and explicit joint counts left ~36% of the tube unrigged — both from interior-only centerline sampling; `TubePath.get_centerline` now always prefers edge-loop centres (samplers are fallback-only, seeded end-to-end), skips the degenerate single-edge "loops" capped tubes produce at their boundary rings (their off-axis midpoints, radius ≈ cos(π/sx), also leaked anchor stretch into world axes via diagonal joint frames), and recovers the true cap centres via closest-point completion (`_complete_cap_ends`); (3) anchor stretch scaled world-X regardless of tube direction — anchor joints are now oriented X-down-the-tube (baked to jointOrient) so the distance-driven `scaleX` runs along the tube; (4) advanced twist was dead — the up locators were point-constrained (never rotating), so `dWorldUpType=4` read static matrices; they now parent under the start/end controls; (5) `get_centerline_using_edges` returned raw edge vertices (on the tube *wall*) — edge midpoints are now refined onto the axis via the shared opposing-hit helper (`_refine_centers`, which steps surface-coincident seeds inward along the cpom normal); (6) joint/control-based rig lookup always missed (`_instances` only keyed the mesh UUID) — `build()` now also registers the rig group, and the new `TubeRig.for_node()` resolves anything under it (slots delegate to it; the ancestor walk starts from the raw node because `NodeUtils.get_transform_node` returns a *list* for joints); (7) falloff anchoring (`constrain_end_with_falloff`) silently skipped its skin weighting — strategies now record the mesh skinCluster via the new `TubeRig.skin_mesh()` (replaces three copy-pasted bind blocks), and the method falls back to resolving the skinCluster from the constrained joint, derives geometry from it, and batch-reads vertex positions. Also: the ik logic curve is built through edit points (the CV hull shortfall dragged end joints ~0.16 off the centerline); spline drivers place the mid control and tangents on the *path* (not the chord) with end frames from local tangents (shared `_frame_rotation` helper replaces matrix math duplicated across `AnchorStrategy`/`create_spline_drivers`); `b002` honors the num-controls spinner and squash/volume checkboxes and wires `main_control` (its stretchFactor/volumeFactor attrs previously never appeared on that path); anchor mode no longer advertises an unimplemented volume toggle; dead `create_start_end_locators`/`create_rig_from_joints` removed; the bbox-slice sampler fetches vertex positions once instead of per-slice; `_parent_to` warns instead of swallowing parenting failures. Tests: `test_tube_rig_cleanliness.py` folded into a new behavioral `test_tube_rig.py` (13 tests — end placement, rebuild, twist response, stretch axis, falloff weights, node lookup; every new test failed against the old implementation). **Round 2 (live-Maya reports, same day):** the `rig_group` property returned `None` after an undo or manual delete left the cached path stale (`cmds.parent → 'No object matches name: None'`) — it now revalidates with `objExists` and recreates, and returns the stored name directly instead of laundering it through `NodeUtils.get_transform_node` (which returns a *list* for some node types); leftover joints sharing the rig's `<rig_name>_jnt_*` prefix anywhere in the scene made short names ambiguous (`'More than one object matches name: …_jnt_2'` — `cmds.parent` renames on sibling clash and the code kept the stale short name) — `generate_joint_chain` now sweeps the prefix before creating and tracks the long paths `_parent_to` returns; chains created via the standalone "Create Joints" step were left world-aligned — `generate_joint_chain` now auto-orients by default (X aims at child, Y up, end joint zeroed; `orientation=None` default replaces `[0,0,0]`), and the FK/spline strategies' duplicate manual orient blocks were removed. +7 tests (stale group, rerun, debris names (spline + anchor), auto-orientation, bent-tube accuracy via baked `nonLinear` bend — interior points on-axis within 4% of radius — and capless-tube end coverage): 20 total, all green. **Review hardening:** `get_edge_loop_centers` builds its edge-component strings from the full DAG path (was the leaf name — ambiguous when another mesh shares the short name); `_refine_centers`/`_complete_cap_ends` moved their `closestPointOnMesh` `connectAttr` wiring inside the cleanup `try` so a failed connect can't leak the temp node.

- **2026-07-08 — Post-review hardening (round 3): parked-shot key corruption on shared curves fixed.** The collision-cycle parking shipped in round 2 corrupted keys whenever cycle members shared an object (and audio windows unconditionally): the timeline-last envelope's `+INF` sentinel let a later park/ordered window sweep already-parked content into a second shift, and `cmds.keyframe`'s default `option="move"` clamped parked keys at the first neighboring key on the same curve instead of teleporting past it — mayapy-reproduced by the new `TestParkedKeysSingleShift` (a shared-object B/C cycle landed B's keys at 79−ε instead of 40/49). Two-part fix in `_shot_apply.apply`: when any shot is parked, every move window's `+INF` envelope end is capped at `_content_top(plan.moves) + 1` (a new `_shot_plan` helper — the same high-water mark `_park_offset` clears by `+1000`, so cap and park zone can never disagree; also avoids Maya's frame→tick precision loss at 1e9-magnitude ranges), and the park/land phases pass `option="over"` (`_batch_move_keys(over=True)`; ordered phase-1 moves keep the clamping default — their topological order guarantees no crossings). Also in this pass: scene-exporter `_get_revert_method` now documents the revert-timing constraint (reverts fire before the export write, so only mutations the write doesn't depend on may pair with a revert — mirrors blendertk's factory); `shell_xform.ui`'s `move` group renamed `move_grp` (shadowed `QWidget.move` — uitk's MainWindow guard skips the binding and warns on every panel load; renamed identically in the blendertk twin); `pythontk>=0.8.80` dependency floor (the `AssemblySorter`/`match_clouds`/`nn_query` APIs the auto-instancer delegates to shipped in 0.8.80); `.gitattributes` pins `*.bat text eol=crlf` (a Linux CI wheel build otherwise ships the package-manager mirror LF-only, which cmd.exe can mis-scan on goto/call labels); `straightenShell` local → `straighten_shell` (PEP 8); a stray in-place regeneration of the `wood_MSAO.png` test fixture reverted.

- **2026-07-08 — Shots-system full review, round 2 (`anim_utils/shots/` + the uitk sequencer widget): six-way adversarial audit (90 findings), two CRITICAL data-loss paths closed, persistence holes plugged, collision-cycle respace implemented, and the uitk↔mayatk signal contract audited 34/34.** Follow-up to the same-day optimization pass, this time covering the uitk widget package too (see uitk's CHANGELOG for the widget-side half). **CRITICAL fixes:** (1) a selected-keys CSV build restricted `build_steps` to the resolved subset but still passed `remove_missing=True`, so building one region **deleted every other built shot from the store** (store removals aren't in Maya's undo queue — unrecoverable); removal is now gated on building the full step list. (2) `fit_shot_to_content`'s outer-content probe was an unbounded `cmds.keyframe(obj)`, attributing keys owned by OTHER shots (shared objects) to the shot being fitted — a routine "Move to Shot" could extend the shot over its neighbor and the follow-up implicit extend rippled every upstream shot's keys; the probe now skips frames owned by any other shot (gap fade-tails still count; pinned by `TestFitShotSharedObjectClamp`). **Respace collision cycles (mayapy-reproduced crash):** a uniform-gap apply where one shot moves forward while another moves backward formed an ordering cycle and `plan_respace` raised `ValueError` out of the Apply Gap button; `_plan_sequence` now returns the cycle members as `MovePlan.parked` with a computed `park_offset`, and `_shot_apply.apply` runs a three-phase park → ordered-moves → land pass (the temp-parking design `move_shot_to_position` already used) — `TestRespaceCollisionParking` pins plan partition and final bounds. The same vacate-destination-first ordering now governs `slide_shot`/`set_shot_start` (forward moves ripple downstream BEFORE moving the pivot, so shared-object curves can't be shifted twice), and `move_shot` collapsed onto `slide_shot` (its hand-rolled per-shot ripple loop was the last non-plan-backed ripple). **Persistence holes (edits silently lost on save/reload):** engine boundary mutators (`fit_shot_to_content`, `slide_shot`, `move_object_in_shot`, `resize_object`, `resize_shot`, `set_shot_duration`, `move_shot_to_position`) and `_shot_apply.apply` never marked the store dirty — Trim/Move/resize survived the session but reverted on reopen while the moved keys stayed; all now `mark_dirty()`. Same class: gap lock/unlock (serialized but never dirtied), the manifest's "skipped"/"patched" rebuild branches (metadata/description/objects were direct attribute writes — a behaviors-only CSV edit rebuilt clean and saved nothing; now change-detected `update_shot` calls), `store.source_csv`, controller-assess `object_status` writes, and assess's scene-object merge. `batch_update` now flushes an events-less dirty batch (pin/hide/gap-lock mutators don't notify, so exit skipped the write), and `update_shot` clamps inverted bounds (`end < start` from the end spinner) to zero-duration. **Reproduced resolver bug:** `resolve_to_transform` treated only exact-`nodeType == "transform"` nodes as owners, so a keyed **joint** resolved to its parent (its keys were left behind on every shot move) and DG intermediaries (pairBlend/unitConversion) resolved to None despite the docstring; it now matches transform subclasses via `ls(type="transform")` and follows DG nodes one bounded hop downstream. **Manifest fixes:** `_last_resolved` was indexed positionally against `self._steps` — sparse in selected-keys mode, so the prev-end edit guard compared against the wrong step and the frozen-prefix pass duplicated the edited step while dropping unresolved ones (now keyed by step_id end-to-end; `TestRangeResolverSparseFrozenPrefix`); the uniform auto-fill duration hardcoded `200.0` instead of the store's `initial_shot_length` policy; `sync()` didn't forward `skip_scene_discovery`, so a selected-keys build's post-assess merged every animated scene object into the shot; a UTF-8 BOM survived into the cp1252/lossy fallback decodes and broke header detection (bytes-level strip; `test_bom_with_cp1252_body_still_finds_header`); `_color_behavior_label` colored a row from the FIRST step containing the object (same asset in A01+A05 showed A01's status on A05) — per-step lookup threaded through the presenter; per-row range editability (new steps in a partially built CSV stayed locked); `_reapply_behavior` now runs in one undo chunk and distributes multi-behavior anchors exactly like `apply_to_shots`; exact-mode `verify_behavior` now models the distributed `anchor_override` (multi-behavior objects were permanently flagged `missing_behavior` right after a successful build); `apply_to_shots` records per-entry `RuntimeError`s (locked/connected attrs on referenced assets) in a new `"failed"` list and continues instead of aborting the batch mid-build (failures surface in the build footer). `classify_objects` falls back to leaf-name comparison (metadata is keyed by CSV short names while shot.objects are long paths — every object degraded to `scene_discovered` after a re-sync), and both manifest commit branches resolve long names via the new `_resolve_long_names_keep_missing` (the old resolve **dropped** scene-missing CSV objects from the shot, defeating pinning). **Controller/UI:** signal wiring is now a declarative table with previous-controller disconnect, and `_setup_shot_nav`'s option-box actions late-bind through widget attributes — a slots re-init over the same UI used to leave BOTH controllers connected (two `cmds.undo()` per Ctrl+Z, duplicate markers, duplicated nav buttons); `_on_store_invalidated` clears the cross-scene `_shot_undo_stack`/`_shifted_out_keys` (a stale snapshot restored scene A's bounds into scene B's shots) and syncs the widget BEFORE the combobox (markers mode listed the previous scene's markers); `active_shot_id` consults `store.active_shot_id` before the first-shot fallback (store events retargeted the view in markers mode); shot switching suppresses its own `ActiveShotChanged` rebuild and programmatic `setCurrentIndex` calls block signals (3–4 full rebuilds per prev/next click → 1); `_on_maya_undo` wraps its restore in `_syncing` (double rebuild per Ctrl+Z); `on_clip_resized` runs the full edit epilogue (downstream shots rendered from stale segment caches and the store never dirtied); the audio-clip move invalidates `_audio_segments_cache` (clip snapped back until the keyframe debounce); "Move to Shot" skips single-attribute sub-row clips (it moved EVERY attribute's keys in the span); gap inner-edge scaling consolidated onto one `_scale_shot_edge` helper that snaps through the store and clamps against the far edge (raw drag values bypassed snap and could store inverted bounds; the 4× copy-pasted scale block is gone); `_sync_to_widget` wraps rebuilds in the widget's new `bulk_updates()` (O(n²) scene-rect recompute → one pass); delete-all batches into one event; `on_move_shot`'s double rebuild dropped; `spn_gap` gained an explicit maximum (Qt's default 99.99 silently clamped scenes with wider gaps — regenerated `shots_ui.py`); `spn_detection` joined the debounce list (each tick ran a full-scene `has_animation` scan); `timeUnitChanged` is ignored during file reads (`MFileIO.isReadingFile` — the old-scene store could be rescaled and flushed onto the NEW scene's carrier); `_shots.py`'s `maya.cmds` import is guarded again (the "Maya-free" planner chain and its dead `if cmds is None` branches are live once more). **Dead code removed:** `ShotSequencer.audio_prefetch` + `_audio_events_cache` (zero callers), `_on_shot_lane_double_clicked` + the `shot_block_clicked`/`shot_lane_double_clicked` wiring (the uitk emitter never existed — see uitk's ShotLaneItem removal; the shot context menu is now actually reachable via the ruler's shot blocks), `shots_slots._has_selected_keys`/`_save`, unused imports. Mojibake sweep: 41 double-encoded lines (`â€”`) repaired across 4 manifest files — duplicate-step warnings and docstrings render correctly again. **Verified:** uitk full suite 2430 passed / 6 skipped; uitk `test_sequencer.py` 258/258 with 11 new regression classes; mayatk mock tests 186 passed; pure planner tests green headless (parking pinned); new mayatk regression classes (`TestUpdateShotInvertedClamp`, `TestDirtyFlagCoverage`, `TestClassifyObjectsLeafFallback`, `TestResolveToTransformSubclasses`, `TestFitShotSharedObjectClamp`, `TestRangeResolverSparseFrozenPrefix`, BOM CSV); full `--all` gate run (recorded 2026-07-08, post round-3): **3584 tests, 0 failures, 0 errors, 1 skipped** — the earlier badge's lone test_sequencer standalone failure no longer reproduces after the round-3 fixes. **Deliberately deferred:** the `_overlays.py` edge-drag scaffold unification (~90 lines × 2 — pure interaction-code refactor whose correctness payloads shipped individually; needs live-GUI verification), the remaining gap-handler skeleton parameterization, and the previously-listed live-Maya items (incremental decoration refresh, manifest in-place table updates, cursor-poll timer).

- **2026-07-08 — New: dedicated UV Transform tool (`uv_utils/shell_xform.py` → `ShellXformSlots` + `shell_xform.ui`), co-located with `UvUtils` and auto-discovered by `MayaUiHandler`.** Consolidates the tentacle UV panel's cramped Transform cluster — the four move-to-UV-space arrows, the 17-item Flip/Rotate/Align/Orient/Gather/Randomize/select-filter combobox, and the Straighten/Mirror/Distribute option-box tools — into one grouped, polished window: a Move pad (one whole UV tile per click), **Flip / Rotate** over a shared angle field (a CCW/CW pair generalizing the old fixed "Rotate 45"), the **Shell** option-box tools, and Maya-only **Align** (U/V min·mid·max + Linear) and **Orient** (Orient Shells / To Edges / Gather / Randomize) groups. Launched from the tentacle UV panel's new single **Transform** button (`marking_menu.show("shell_xform")`); Pin and Stack stay behind as their own buttons. The slots dispatch to the existing engine (`UvUtils.move_to_uv_space`/`mirror_uvs`/`orient_shells`, native `performAlignUV`/`texOrientEdge`/…) via specific submodule imports (co-located convention, no `import mayatk`); Flip U/V route through `UvUtils.mirror_uvs` (geometric per-shell flip about each shell's own center) rather than a bare `cmds.polyFlipUV`, which errors without an explicit component target. Blender ships the shared subset in `blendertk.uv_utils.shell_xform`; the Maya-only shell ops are ledgered `na` in `tentacle/docs/parity_map.py` `CONTROLS["shell_xform"]` (full parity sweep exit 0). API_REGISTRY regenerated.
- **2026-07-08 — Fix: normal-angle classification needed a threshold of 89, not 90, to act on a cube's 90° edges — a float-drift boundary bug in `Components.set_edge_hardness` and the sibling `get_edges_by_normal_angle`.** A cube's edges are 90°, so "Set Normals by Angle" at a threshold of 90 (tooltip: "angle **≥** threshold") should classify every edge as "upper"/harden and none as "lower"/soften. It didn't: after any transform + freeze — an ordinary mesh state — the world-space face normals `MFnMesh.getPolygonNormal` returns carry a few sub-ulp of float drift, so a cube's true right-angle edges measure `89.9999…°` rather than exactly `90.0` (reproduced headless: 6 of 12 edges land just under 90 after a `rotate`+`makeIdentity`; a fresh identity-transform cube stays at exactly 90.0 and hid the bug). The strict `>=` boundary then dropped those edges into the soften bucket, forcing the user down to 89 — unintuitive and undocumented. Fix: a new `Components._ANGLE_MATCH_EPS` (`1e-3°`, ~45× the observed drift yet far below any artistically meaningful angle) is the SSoT tolerance for matching a measured normal angle against a bound. `set_edge_hardness` nudges its upper/lower split boundary down by it; `get_edges_by_normal_angle` widens its range by it on both ends — the latter fixes the **same latent bug** in the two production tools that call it (`slots/maya/selection.py` "Select Edges By Angle" and `slots/maya/uv.py`'s hard-edge UV cut), where a range bound of exactly 90 previously missed a frozen cube's edges. Regression tests `test_set_edge_hardness_threshold_matches_true_angle_after_freeze` and `test_get_edges_by_normal_angle_bound_matches_true_angle_after_freeze` reproduce the freeze-induced drift and assert a bound of 90 acts on all 12 cube edges (both fail on the old strict comparison). Verified: `test_components` 51/51 headless.
- **2026-07-08 — Shots-system optimization pass (`anim_utils/shots/`): scene-scan batching, dead-code removal, verified bug fixes, and slots→engine extraction across the store, sequencer, manifest, and detection layers.** Driven by a four-way parallel audit of the ~16.6k-line subsystem. **Bugs fixed:** `ShotStore.has_animation` sampled only the first 50 animCurves — a scene whose early curves drove non-transform nodes (materials, blendshapes) false-negatived and disabled the detection UI; it now batches one `listConnections` over every curve. `rescale_to_fps` hard-`round()`ed shot bounds and marker times, silently quantizing sub-frame values when `snap_whole_frames` was off — it now routes through `snap()` (the policy chokepoint). `range_resolver`'s second-pass end (`next_start - gap`) could invert a range when the next step was pinned at or before `start + gap` — now clamped to `start`. `clip_motion._expand_shot_for_clip` mixed an exact `!=` gate with epsilon-gated ripples, so sub-epsilon drift could update the shot yet skip both ripples — one epsilon gate now governs the block, and the mid-drag `update_shot` is wrapped in a `_syncing` guard (same pattern as the gap handlers) so a clip drag no longer triggers a double full-widget rebuild. Manifest CSV loads had two silent failure modes: a header row whose step column wasn't cell 0 yielded 0 steps with no signal (header cells are now scanned anywhere in the row, and the step ID is read from the *resolved* step column), and a non-UTF-8 (Excel/cp1252) file hard-failed the whole load (`_read_csv_rows` now falls back utf-8-sig → cp1252 → lossy utf-8; a missing header also logs a loud warning naming the expected aliases). The sequencer controller never subscribed to `StoreInvalidated`, so after scene new/open it kept its lazily-cached sequencer and event listener bound to the dead store — it now rebinds and resyncs on invalidation (and unbinding is symmetric in `remove_callbacks`). **Dead code removed:** `ShotStore.ripple_shift`/`ripple_shift_upstream` — the exact interleaved resolve→mutate shape the `_shot_plan`/`_shot_apply` split was built to kill, still public with zero production callers (tests repointed to the plan-backed ripple); `manifest_data.short_name`/`unfmt_behavior`/`parse_range` (the first duplicated `core_utils.leaf_name` — all use sites repointed to the canonical helper); the never-called `_sync_decoration`; and the dead `shifted`/`cmds` plumbing threaded through `_shift_audio`/`_move_shot_content`/the ripple pair. **Perf:** the curve→transform resolution walk was copy-pasted 5× (detection ×3, sequencer, manifest) with per-curve `listConnections`/`ls`/`listRelatives` round-trips — consolidated into `_detection.resolve_to_transform` (memoizing, non-DAG-safe) with batched discovery; `_filter_flat_objects` queries each curve once and evaluates candidate ranges in Python instead of per-candidate ranged queries; `ShotManifest._filter_to_animated` no longer rebuilds the full scene map per patched shot/assessed step (shares the per-cycle `_transform_curve_map` cache that `_discover_scene_objects` already used — previously an N-row build/assess re-scanned every animCurve N times); `ShotStore.assess()` resolves the union of all shot objects in ONE `ls` (long-name membership is the `_resolve_long_names` SSoT contract); `_shot_apply.apply` builds its shot map once instead of an O(n²) per-step scan; `behaviors.apply_behavior` hoists the long-name resolve out of the per-key loop; `_sync_from_store` no longer *writes* the derived gap back to the store during a pure UI sync (a panel open used to dirty and re-save a freshly-loaded scene). **API (see API_CHANGES.md):** removed the 5 symbols above; added `ShotSequencer.apply_gap(gap, scope, shot_id)` — the gap-scope algorithm that previously lived inline in the Shots settings slot (`on_gap_changed` is now a thin forwarder) — and promoted `ripple_downstream`/`ripple_upstream` to public (the settings slot was reaching into `seq._ripple_downstream`; clip motion now calls the public pair). Listener dispatch in the store (`_notify`, batch, invalidation) and the controller's store bind/unbind no longer swallow exceptions bare — failures log with tracebacks instead of leaving the panel silently dead. **Deliberately deferred (needs live-Maya interactive verification, tracked for a follow-up):** wiring an incremental decoration-only refresh path into the sequencer's rebuild pipeline; collapsing `move_shot`/`reorder_shots`/`move_shot_to_position`'s three hand-rolled key-move implementations onto `_shot_plan`/`_shot_apply` (their `_batch_move_keys` twins differ in envelope semantics — inclusive vs half-open — and are NOT drop-in interchangeable); manifest table in-place updates; and the shots-settings 100 ms cursor-poll `hide_on_leave` timer. **Verified:** new regression tests pin every bug fix (`TestRescaleToFpsSnapPolicy`, `TestHasAnimationBeyondSampleWindow`, `TestAssessBatched`, `TestApplyGap`, `TestParseCSVRobustness`, `TestRangeResolverClamp`); differential 3-module run vs a stashed-HEAD baseline worktree shows zero regressions — the only failure delta is 8 *pre-existing* order-dependent failures (5 `shot_manifest_audio_integration` + 3 `compute_plan`) that reproduce identically on unmodified HEAD in that scoped combination and do not occur in the canonical `--all` order (HEAD badge: 3575 green) — cross-module state pollution in the scoped-run configuration, surfaced here rather than masked; full suite (`--all`, GUI port mode): **3569/3569 green** (0 failures, 0 errors, 1 skipped) on the final tree. **Test-infra fix found live during verification:** the 2026-07-06 per-port results-file scoping still collided when two concurrent `run_tests.py` invocations shared the *default* port — observed as a background `--all` run (99+ modules in) adopting a concurrently-dispatched scoped run's 8-module summary as its own final result (and clobbering the badge with it). `test/temp_tests/test_results_<port>.txt` is now scoped by port **and** runner PID (`test_results_<port>_<pid>.txt` — the in-Maya payload embeds the same path, so both sides agree), with a 7-day sweep of stale files; `test/README.md` updated.
- **2026-07-08 — AnimUtils review: broken `repair_corrupted_curves` import fixed, Channel-Box short/long-name bug family eliminated, and a PyMel-leftover sweep across `_anim_utils.py`.** `AnimUtils.repair_corrupted_curves` (registered public API) imported from a nonexistent `core_utils.diagnostic` module and raised `ModuleNotFoundError` on every call — now routed to `core_utils.diagnostics.animation_diag.AnimCurveDiagnostics`. The Channel-Box family: `channelBox -sma` returns SHORT names (`tx`) but five call sites matched them against long plug names, so CB narrowing was dead in `_resolve_keys`/`copy_keys` and always-empty in `move_keys_to_frame(channel_box_attrs_only=True)`; new `_get_channel_box_attrs()` (mel-safe, batch-safe) + `_plug_attr_names()` (long/short normalization) fix all sites, and `add/remove_intermediate_keys` normalize CB attrs to long names so `ignore='visibility'` matches again. `remove_intermediate_keys` no longer falls through to **every** keyed attribute when `ignore` filters out the entire CB selection (was deleting keys the user asked to keep); its two ~55-line branches collapsed onto one `_strip_intermediate` helper.
