- **2026-07-01 — `tie_keyframes`/`untie_keyframes` no longer guess: tie records exactly which bookend keys it inserted, and untie removes exactly those.** Untie previously flagged bookends by value equality alone (first/last key value == neighbor), which had four failure modes: it **deleted genuine animation keys** whose end value happened to duplicate the neighbor's but whose tangents were shaped (an authored overshoot returning to the same value); it **wiped a deliberate 2-key hold entirely** (both keys flagged → the whole curve deleted); a bookend that landed **inside** the keyed range (custom_range / negative padding) sits on the curve slope with a value *unequal* to its neighbors, so untie never removed it (tie→untie didn't round-trip); and **stacked ties** (two passes with different ranges) only got the outermost pair removed. `tie_keyframes` now writes a JSON list of the inserted times to a string attr on each anim curve (`TIED_KEYS_ATTR` = `mayatkTiedKeys`, merged across passes; silently skipped on referenced/locked curves), `untie_keyframes` removes exactly those keys and clears the record, and it **returns** the removed `{obj: {curve: [times]}}` dict. Curves without a record (tied before the metadata existed) fall back to the heuristic in `get_tied_keyframes`, now hardened: an end key is tied only if it duplicates its neighbor's value AND itself has flat/stepped tangents on both sides, on a curve with ≥3 keys — so shaped keys and 2-key holds survive. Also fixed a collision hazard the record surfaced: a bookend time coinciding with an **existing interior key** is now skipped outright (`MFnAnimCurve.find` guard) instead of `addKey`-ing over it — previously undefined, and with the record it would have marked the user's key for deletion. Object-list coercion in tie/untie/get_tied deduplicated into module-level `_resolve_keyed_objects`. Tests (each failed pre-fix): `test_untie_preserves_shaped_equal_value_end_keys`, `test_untie_does_not_wipe_two_key_flat_curve`, `test_tie_untie_round_trip_inside_range`, `test_tie_untie_stacked_bookends`, `test_tie_bookend_on_existing_interior_key_survives_untie`; plus `test_untie_heuristic_removes_legacy_bookends_without_metadata` locking the fallback contract for scenes tied before the record existed. Full `test_anim_utils.py` suite green under mayapy (incl. all existing tangent-preservation tie tests). Known pre-existing limitation (unchanged): tie's om2 `MFnAnimCurve.addKey` inserts bypass the undo stack (no `MAnimCurveChange`), so Ctrl+Z after tie has never removed the bookend keys; untie itself is fully undoable (all `cmds` calls).
- **2026-07-01 Fix — `optimize_keys` aborted mid-run (`RuntimeError: (kFailure): Unexpected Internal Failure` + `Connection not made … Destination is locked`) when a driven channel's *parent compound* was locked, leaving most flat keys unremoved.** Field report: 118 selected rig locators, error on `FWD_ARM_01_LOC.rotateZ`. The flat-key rebuild path already unlocked a locked destination plug, but rig locators commonly lock the whole `translate`/`rotate` compound rather than individual channels — and `MPlug.isLocked` *reads* inherited locks while *assigning* it only sets the plug's own flag, so the single-plug unlock read True, wrote a no-op, and `MDGModifier.doIt()` still failed on the disconnect (proven in a mayapy probe: child-locked OK, parent-locked reproduces the exact error, `lockNode` is harmless). Because `get_redundant_flat_keys` raised, every curve after the locked one *plus* the tangent-freeze and simplify phases went unprocessed — the "lots of flat keys left" symptom — and the partial edits weren't undoable (optimize runs with undo flushed off). Two-part fix in `get_redundant_flat_keys`: (1) unlock the destination's ancestor chain **top-down** (once ancestors are cleared, each plug's `isLocked` reading is its own flag, so exactly the genuinely-locked plugs are recorded and restored in `finally` — no spurious own-lock is ever added to a child); (2) a per-curve `except RuntimeError` that warns and skips the offending curve instead of aborting the whole batch, since remaining curves are independent. Also removed the dead `curve_ref = curve if as_strings else curve` conditional. Regression test `test_get_redundant_flat_keys_remove_parent_compound_locked` (failed pre-fix with the exact field error; asserts key removal, compound still locked, and no spurious child own-lock); full `test_anim_utils.py` suite 114 green under mayapy.
- **2026-07-01 Fix — `MatUpdater.update_materials`'s `dry_run` flag didn't prevent the move/copy step from writing real files.** Every other side-effecting stage (`disconnect_associated_attributes`, the network-connect step) already checks `config.get("dry_run")` and returns early with a `[Dry Run] Skipping ...` log, but the "Move files if requested" block — the one that calls `ptk.FileUtils.move_file(..., create_dir=True)` — had no such guard, so checking **Dry Run** in the Material Updater panel (`chk_dry_run`) still physically moved/copied real texture files on disk. Surfaced during a full mayatk test-suite audit: `test_env_info_raise_does_not_crash` (mocked `EnvUtils.get_env_info` to raise, `dry_run=True`, `move_to_folder="RelativeOnly"`) left a real `RelativeOnly/tex.png` in the repo root after a `run_tests.py --all` pass — the test only asserted the call didn't crash, so the stray write went unnoticed until the directory showed up in `git status`. Fixed by gating the whole move/copy block behind the same `config_obj.get("dry_run", False)` check used elsewhere in this file, with a matching `[Dry Run] Skipping move/copy to '{move_to_folder}'.` log. Verified: `test_material_updater_workflow.py` / `test_material_updater.py` / `test_material_updater_diagnostics.py` — 45/45 green under mayapy, and the stray directory no longer appears.
- **2026-07-01 Fix — `Attributes.temporarily_unlock(objects, attributes=...)` accepted an `attributes` scope but never used it, always unlocking the full standard TRS set instead.** The body called `get_lock_state(objects, unlock=True)` unconditionally regardless of what was passed in, so `temporarily_unlock(node, ["translateX"])` silently unlocked (and later re-locked) every standard transform channel on `node` — sibling attributes the caller never asked to touch. Fixed: when `attributes` is given, the context manager now resolves each requested `node.attr` plug (`cmds.attributeQuery(attr, node=node, exists=True)`), captures only those plugs' lock state via `cmds.getAttr(plug, lock=True)`, unlocks just that set, and restores exactly what it captured in `finally`; the `attributes=None` path is unchanged (still delegates to `get_lock_state`/`set_lock_state` for the full-TRS case). Regression test `test_temporarily_unlock_attributes_scoped` (failed pre-fix — asserted a locked sibling attribute stayed locked inside the `with` block) pins the contract; `test_core_utils.py` green under mayapy.
- **2026-07-01 Fix — `SelectionMacros`'s invert-selection helpers (`edit_utils/macros.py`) could crash with `TypeError: 'NoneType' object is not iterable` on the vertex/edge/face branches.** `cmds.filterExpand` returns `None`, not `[]`, when a query matches nothing (e.g. the object has no selected components of that type), and all three invert branches (`selectedVertices`/`allVertices`, `edges`/`allEdges`, `selectedFaces`/`allFaces`) fed the raw result straight into a set-difference comprehension without a guard. Defensive fix, same low-key pattern as the `dry_run` fix above: each of the six `filterExpand` calls now defaults to `or []`. No reproducing test found a live failure; applied because the None-return gotcha is the exact one `EditUtils.get_similar_mesh` was just fixed for elsewhere in this file's neighborhood (see `test_edit_utils.py`'s `test_get_similar_mesh_no_polygons_in_scene_no_crash`).
- **2026-07-01 Fix — `dynamic_pipe.ui`'s dialog title and main action button still read `Create Shader Network` / `CREATE STINGRAY SHADER`, a copy-paste leftover from an earlier Designer template that was never updated for Dynamic Pipe.** Cosmetic-only, no logic changes: `windowTitle` → `Dynamic Pipe`, button text → `DYNAMIC PIPE`. Caught during a docs/UI pass; `test_dynamic_pipe.py` (7 tests) doesn't assert label text, which is how the mismatch went unnoticed this long.
- **2026-07-01 Fix — `test_lightmap_baker.py`'s fake-UI stub (`_SlotUi`) never got the `cmb_scope` / `cmb_resolution` widgets added by the 2026-06-25 Resolution-dropdown/Scope change (below), so `TestLightmapBakerSlots` had been failing since that day.** `blendertk/test/test_blender_ui_handler.py` was updated for the new combos at the time, but this file's hand-rolled `_SlotUi`/`_ModeCombo`-style stub wasn't — `b000()` raised `AttributeError: '_SlotUi' object has no attribute 'cmb_scope'` (11 errors), and once that was added, `'_SlotUi' object has no attribute 'cmb_resolution'`, since `_resolution()`/`_set_resolution()` now read/write `cmb_resolution` and the stub still only carried the old `spn_resolution` spinbox (which production no longer reads at all). Found via a full `run_tests.py --all` pass — the only failing module out of 130. Added `_ScopeCombo` (defaults to Selected) and `_ResolutionCombo` (mirrors `cmb_resolution_init`'s item-data model — `currentData()` / `setCurrentIndex()`), wired both into `_SlotUi`, removed the now-dead `spn_resolution` stub, and fixed `test_cmb000_loads_preset_into_dials`'s assertion to check `cmb_resolution.currentData()` instead of the no-longer-written spinbox. 41/41 green under mayapy; full-suite re-run: 130/130 modules, 3482/3482 tests, 0 failures/errors.
- **2026-07-01 Fix — `connect_switch_to_constraint(anchor=…)` never worked and orphaned a locator.** The anchor was created and appended to the Python target *list* but never wired into the constraint as a real target, so the weight-alias count (unchanged) was always `< num_targets` (which counted the anchor) — the function hit the "weights don't match targets" guard, bailed, and left the anchor locator orphaned in the scene. Fixed by (1) reordering so the driven-node and duplicate-attribute checks run *before* any anchor is created (a pre-check failure can no longer orphan a helper), (2) wiring the anchor as a genuine additional target via `constraint_cmd(anchor_obj, driven_obj, maintainOffset=True)` — re-invoking the constraint command on an already-constrained object appends a new target+weight — so counts match and the anchor becomes a selectable space in the switch enum, and (3) validating the weight/target counts *before* the anchor helper is created (and deleting it if the constraint-add itself throws) so nothing is ever orphaned. Also hoisted the duplicated `constraint_type`/`constraint_cmd` lookup to one place. `maintainOffset=True` holds the object's current world pose when the anchor space is active — flag if snap-to-origin is wanted instead. Verified headless: a 2-target parentConstraint + anchor now yields 3 real targets/weights, a 3-entry enum (`A:B:world`), 3 condition nodes, and exactly one new locator. Contract locked in `test_connect_switch_to_constraint.py`.
- **2026-07-01 Fix — `create_locator_at_object` crashed with `Invalid path` on non-unique leaf names.** When the rigged object's leaf name was shared by another node in the scene, the final `cmds.parent(obj, loc)` returned a *partial* DAG path (`loc|leaf`) instead of a bare leaf. The rename pass then renamed the LOCATOR before the object, which changed that stored path's ancestor component out from under `obj` — `cmds.rename` raised `RuntimeError: Invalid path 'locator1|...'` (e.g. re-rigging an object already named `*_LOC`). Fixed by capturing each node's UUID *before* the rename pass and resolving it just-in-time via a `rename_by_uuid` helper, so ancestor renames can no longer invalidate a descendant's stored path — order- and collision-independent. Regression test `test_create_locator_naming.py` reproduces both the non-unique-leaf and re-rig-a-`*_LOC` cases (each errored pre-fix); the 26-test `test_freeze_restore_locator_rig.py` suite still passes (canonical `_GRP/_LOC/_GEO` layout unchanged).
- **Color Manager renamed to Color ID; palette swatches are now square (2026-06-30).** The `display_utils/color_manager.py` + `.ui` panel is renamed to `color_id` — engine class `ColorManager` → `ColorId`, `ColorManagerSlots` → `ColorIdSlots`, UI-handler key / marking-menu target `color_manager` → `color_id`, header text `COLOR MANAGER` → `COLOR ID`, help title → "Color ID". The `ColorUtils` helper keeps its name (imported by `hierarchy_manager` / `controls`); only its module path moved (`color_id`). The internal preset **storage** key `_PRESET_DIR = "mayatk/color_manager"` is deliberately retained (with a comment) so existing saved palettes and the `PresetManager` legacy-migration table survive the rename — it's never user-visible. The 12 palette swatches now use uitk `ColorSwatch.keep_square` so they render square and track the panel width (previously flat/narrow pills). The panel is also organized into three titled sections — **Palette** / **Channels** / **Actions** — for clarity (the swatch grid is wrapped in a titled `QGroupBox`; the channel-checkbox and action-button groups get titles). Verified end-to-end headless: the handler loads the panel as `color_id`, promotes 12 `ColorSwatch` widgets that render 50×50 square after show+resize, and the three group titles render. Tests: `test_display_extras.py` retargeted to `color_id` / `ColorId`, and the `DEFAULT_SWATCH_COLORS` palette tests (12 colors / all distinct / valid RGB tuples / desaturated) moved here from uitk's `test_color_swatch.py` — the palette is a mayatk symbol, so it's tested where it lives rather than reached downstream-out from uitk (which broke uitk CI on the rename). The desaturation check uses stdlib `colorsys` (Qt-free) at the same `<200/255` HSV-saturation threshold.
- **Hotkey collision checker — *this* was the real reason a uitk global command (Reopen Last UI / Repeat Last Command) was dead next session, after four prior rounds chased it in uitk (2026-06-30).** Field report + the decisive user clue: "next session, mapping the same key asks *again* to unbind from Maya." Proven empirically in fresh Maya (`MayaConnection`, `force_new_instance=True`, isolated `MAYA_APP_DIR` so the user's prefs were untouched): Maya assigns one key to **multiple** named commands across **hotkey contexts** — e.g. `n` → `artisanModifyValue` (global/viewport) **and** `TimeEditorToggleSoloSelectedTracks` (Time Editor context); `Ctrl+R` → `CreateReference` (global) **and** `ProfilerToolToggleRecording` (Profiler tool). `_find_bound_command` scanned the entire `assignCommand` registry, so it reported the **context** bindings too — but `_unbind_maya_hotkey`'s `cmds.hotkey(... name="")` can only clear the **global** one. So after the user accepted "free Maya binding," the editor's own collision query still found the un-clearable context binding: an *unresolvable* conflict that re-prompted every session, and (because Maya's native global binding had also not been durably freed) the key stayed eaten and the uitk command stayed dead. **Fix (matches the user's own diagnosis — "we shouldn't clear all of them, just the viewport context"):** `_find_bound_command` now queries **only** the global/viewport context via `cmds.hotkey(key, query=True, name=True, …)` (resolving the friendly runtime name via `assignCommand` only to *label* a confirmed conflict), so editor/tool-context bindings — which fire only while that editor is focused and never shadow an application Qt shortcut — are ignored. `_unbind_maya_hotkey` additionally calls `cmds.savePrefs(hotkeys=True)` so the freed key is durable immediately (a `cmds.hotkey` edit lives only in the in-memory set; Maya flushes hotkeys only on a *clean* exit, so a crash/hard-kill would otherwise restore Maya's binding next launch). **Verified end-to-end in real Maya across a hard-killed restart:** the checker reports the viewport binding only (`ModifyPaintValuePress` / `CreateReference`), the clear frees it, a re-check is empty *while `assignCommand` still lists the Time Editor / Profiler bindings*, and after a kill+relaunch the key is **still free — no re-prompt**. Tests: `test_hotkey_collisions.py::TestGlobalContextOnly` (5 new — global-context-only detection, context binding ignored when global is empty, runtime-name resolution, `NONE`/orphan fallback, modifier-kwarg mapping) + the 6 existing integration tests still green; **25 passed** in a launched Maya. New private helpers `_hotkey_mod_kwargs`, `_runtime_command_for`. No public API changed (`maya_collision_checker` signature/shape intact).
- **Shot Manifest — CSV mappings *and* behavior templates are now user-extensible, schema-validated, and self-documenting; built on the shared pythontk template system (2026-06-27).** The tool had two parallel, near-identical "declarative file" loaders — CSV `mapping/*.json` and `behaviors/*.yaml` — each globbed from *inside the installed package* (so a user's own file had to live in `site-packages` and died on upgrade), with no validation and a schema documented only in a module docstring. Both are unified onto the new `pythontk.TemplateSet` (storage) + `pythontk.SchemaSpec` (shape) primitives (see pythontk CHANGELOG 2026-06-27) instead of forking a third convention. **Schema as a dataclass:** `MappingSpec` (`mapping/_spec.py`; the existing `ColumnMap` graduated to a `SchemaSpec` and is now its nested `columns` block) and `BehaviorSpec` (`behaviors/_spec.py`) are the single source of truth for each file's shape — they validate a file, emit a copy-me skeleton, and generate the committed `MAPPING_FORMAT.md` / `BEHAVIOR_FORMAT.md` references (so docs can't drift). The polymorphic `audio_resolve` block became an OCP registry (`AUDIO_METHODS` descriptors drive validate/skeleton/docs; `_AUDIO_BUILDERS` the runtime resolvers, same key set — a test guards the two against drift), replacing the if/elif dispatch. **Extend with your own:** built-in files ship read-only; user files live under `user_config_root()/mayatk/shot_manifest_{mappings,behaviors}/` and a user file *shadows* a built-in of the same name ("duplicate to edit"), discovered automatically. `discover`/`load_mapping`/`resolve` and `load_behavior`/`list_behaviors` are preserved as façades over the new set (directory-override + `lru_cache` + `kind` filter intact); `load_mapping` now **validates** (warnings logged, hard errors raise `SchemaError` with a precise message instead of the old bare `KeyError`/silent-ignore — verified behavior-preserving: the shipped mappings resolve a CSV identically to the direct `ColumnMap` path). **UI:** the mapping selector sits under a titled **"CSV Mapping"** divider in the header menu and is built as a uitk **option-box template**: a `ComboBox` tagging each entry by source (built-in/user; real name in item data) and remembering the last-used selection, with just **two icon buttons** to its right — *Refresh* (rescan the folder) then *Open folder*. Mapping files are **managed externally, not edited in-place** (the earlier in-UI New/Edit/Duplicate/Delete/⋯-menu was dropped as the wrong model for template files — these aren't fields you tweak through a dialog): the user opens the folder, edits files, and refreshes. Opening an *empty* folder first **seeds** it with a documented `example.json` skeleton and the `MAPPING_FORMAT.md` reference (`_seed_mappings_folder`; a no-op once the folder holds anything, so it never clobbers user files). The combo and its square icon buttons are pinned to the header menu's row height (`fixed_item_height`, defaulting to the combo's hint) so the row lines up with the sibling buttons. The toolbar wiring is factored into `_wire_mapping_option_box` (unit-tested on a real `ComboBox` — toolbar shape *and* height match; a no-op under the mocked controller-test UI). The behavior *apply/verify* Maya code is untouched. `test/conftest.py` now sandboxes the ecosystem user-config root so no test reads/writes the real store (the persist-last-used path would otherwise touch it). Tests: `test_shot_manifest_mapping.py` (10, mayapy) + `test_shot_manifest_behaviors.py` (8) new; full shot_manifest suite 223 green under pytest (`_on_mapping_changed` kept back-compatible with a name-string arg).
- **Macro Manager — removed the `fit_to_content_on_show=False` window patch; size persistence is now handled generally by uitk (2026-06-27).** The panel needed a per-window opt-out so its hand-expanded table height survived across sessions — uitk re-fit the window to its content right after restoring the saved geometry, snapping the height back to the table's tiny content minimum ("resizes each show"). uitk's MainWindow now treats a restored geometry as authoritative and skips the on-show fit whenever a saved size was restored (uitk CHANGELOG 2026-06-27), so the opt-out is redundant: the panel keeps the default `fit_to_content_on_show=True` and persists its size via the general mechanism. Verified behavior-neutral — a first-ever show already collapsed via `restore_window_geometry`'s `adjustSize` fallback with or without the opt-out, so only the restore path ever mattered, and that's now handled for every window. `mock_tests/test_macro_manager_window.py` updated to assert the panel relies on the general behavior and carries no local patch (1 green).
- **HDR Manager — the incomplete-HDR warning now enumerates causes instead of asserting “online-only cloud file, make it available offline” (2026-06-27).** Same fix-the-anti-pattern pass as the Shot Manifest CSV diagnosis below. When `ImgUtils.validate_image_integrity` refuses a partially-present HDR (wiring it would crash Viewport 2.0's IBL path), `_validate_or_warn`'s footer/console/dialog text previously claimed the file was “almost always an online-only cloud file that hasn't finished syncing” and told the user to “Make available offline” — but a truncated file is just as often an interrupted download/export or a **disk that filled up mid-write**, where that advice is useless. The messages now state the checkable fact (“incomplete on disk”), enumerate the real causes (cloud file still syncing / interrupted transfer / full disk), and give the matching fixes (make sure the sync app is running and let it finish, or free up space and re-export/re-download) — the cloud “make available offline” hint is kept only as one option in the console detail, never asserted as the cause. Behavior unchanged (still refuses the image; no renderer side-effects). Test: `test_hdr_manager.py::TestValidateOrWarn::test_truncated_hdr_is_refused_with_dialog` updated to assert the enumerated guidance (cloud + sync + free-up-space) rather than the single “offline” phrase; class 3 green.
- **Shot Manifest — CSV path field is now editable (type/paste), and its mocked test suite was repaired (2026-06-27).** Follow-up to the CSV-load fix below. **(1)** The path line-edit shipped read-only (browse-only); it's now editable with uitk's built-in `"file"` path validator (live invalid-path action-color + tooltip via `set_validator`) and loads on commit (Enter / focus-out) when the path is non-empty and changed (`_setup_csv_path_editing` / `_on_csv_path_edited`), delegating validity to `_load_csv` (which strips the text, reports a missing file, and explains an unreadable file — full disk / stopped sync client). Picking a path from the recent-values list now loads it too (`RecentValuesOption.value_selected` -> `_on_csv_recent_selected`), at parity with Browse. **(2)** Repaired three pre-existing mocked-test bugs surfaced while verifying (test bugs, not product bugs): the shared `test/conftest.py` injected `sys.modules["maya.cmds"]` but never bound the `cmds` attribute on the mock `maya` module, so tests doing `import maya; maya.cmds` (`TestStoreAssess`, `TestAssessAudioStatus`) raised `AttributeError` — conftest now binds each injected submodule as a parent-module attribute (guarded with `hasattr` so a real mayapy `maya` is never clobbered); and `test_apply_audio_clip_warns_when_no_node_no_source` now patches `AudioUtils.has_track`→False (the shared mock's `objExists`/`attributeQuery` reported the track present, so the missing-track warning was skipped). The 6 `MayaTkTestCase` audio-integration tests that also appeared to fail under plain pytest are real-Maya tests (need real fps/scene) and pass under mayapy — verified headless (`currentUnit -> film`, 6/6 OK). Tests: `test_shot_manifest_csv_load.py` +4 (path-edit commit logic); the 4 previously-failing `test_shot_manifest.py` tests are green (full file: 229 pass under pytest).
- **Shot Manifest — a failed CSV load no longer disables the path field, and an unreadable cloud file gets an actionable message instead of a raw errno (2026-06-27).** Field symptom: loading a CSV logged `Failed to parse CSV: [Errno 22] Invalid argument` and the path line-edit stayed **disabled even after toggling the CSV checkbox**, so the user could neither inspect nor correct the path. Two distinct bugs. **(1) Stranded field:** `_load_csv` only enabled the CSV widgets (via `_load_data`) on *success* — every failure branch (missing file, parse error) returned early, so a remembered-but-bad path left the field disabled with no way to recover. It now calls `_sync_csv_widgets(True)` up front (attempting a load ⇒ CSV mode is active), so the field is always inspectable/browsable regardless of outcome. **(2) Cryptic error — corrected diagnosis:** the `[Errno 22]` surfaced when `open()` failed even though `isfile`/`os.stat` passed. The first attempt blamed a non-hydrated cloud placeholder and told the user to “make it available offline” — a **misdiagnosis**: cloud files (Dropbox/OneDrive/…) hydrate on demand fine, and a real read failure is far more often a stopped sync client or a **full volume** (in the field it was a full partition; the file's drive had only ~786 MB free). `_load_csv`'s `OSError` branch now delegates to `_describe_read_failure`, which **enumerates** the likely causes rather than asserting one — disk full, a stopped cloud sync app (only for cloud-managed files, via `is_cloud_placeholder`), a locked file, a disconnected drive — always includes the raw errno, and **appends the actual free space** when it's below `_LOW_DISK_BYTES` (1 GB) as a fact the user can act on (e.g. “O: has only 786 MB free”), never as the single asserted cause. `is_cloud_placeholder` is now used only to include/exclude the cloud clause — detection, not diagnosis (its pythontk docstring was corrected to say so). Tests: `test_shot_manifest_csv_load.py` — `LoadCsvFailureTest` (missing-file / OSError / malformed-CSV all enable the field) + `DescribeReadFailureTest` (+4: low disk surfaces the MB-free figure; cloud file lists the sync-client cause, not “offline”; local file omits the cloud cause; unknown free space still explains); full file 13 green; `test_file.py` +3 `free_space` cases.
- **Scene Exporter — log de-duplication: a completed task now reads as `[SUCCESS]`, and the redundant trailing per-check "Check passed" lines were dropped (2026-06-27).** Each task/check already logged `Completed {name} in {t}s`, and every passing check *also* logged a separate `[i/N] Check passed: {name}` at the end of the pipeline — two lines saying the same thing. The per-item completion line in `TaskFactory._manage_context` was promoted `INFO → SUCCESS` (so a finished task reads as a success), and `_process_check_results` no longer emits the now-redundant `Check passed` line (it only tracks failures); its unused `checks_only` arg and dead `progress`/`messages` locals were removed. Surfaced (and fixed) a latent pythontk logging bug in the process — see pythontk CHANGELOG 2026-06-27: promoting to `SUCCESS` exposed that the scene_exporter logger silently dropped `SUCCESS` records in-suite after a prior test raised its level. Test: `test_scene_exporter.py::TestSceneExporter::test_task_timing_logged` (docstring/level updated); full module suite 74 green.
- **`maya_collision_checker` can now free a conflicting Maya hotkey, on editable sets only (2026-06-25).** The Maya hotkey clash surfaced in the uitk Hotkey Editor was previously read-only ("Maya's bindings can't be auto-cleared from outside Maya's own editor"). The conflict now carries a `clear_action` that unbinds Maya's hotkey — `cmds.hotkey(keyShortcut=…, name="")` for press and release — so the editor can offer an **"Assign & free Maya binding"** option (uitk CHANGELOG 2026-06-25). Gated on the new `_is_hotkey_set_editable()`: Maya's factory `Maya_Default` set is read-only, so the action is provided only on a user set; on the locked set the conflict stays read-only and the description says to switch to a custom set. Verified in a fresh Maya 2025: on a custom hotkey set the checker reports the clash with a clear-action set (`has_clear_action`), running it removes the binding, and the re-check returns no conflict (`cleared_ok`).
- **Native Maya menus — a mapping that's stale for the running Maya version now fails fast and falls back to the hand-authored `<key>#submenu` overlay, instead of stalling for seconds and spamming errors (2026-06-25).** Field symptom: launching tentacle and touching certain marking-menu entries took "forever" and logged a wall of `setParent: Object 'MayaWindow|mainCacheMenu' not found`, `Wrong number of arguments on call to buildHelpMenu`, `Cannot find procedure "buildToonMenu"`, then `Failed to fully initialize menu '…' after 15 attempts`. Root cause (confirmed by auditing every `MENU_MAPPING` entry in a fresh Maya 2025): several mappings drifted — `buildToonMenu` was removed (Toon menu shell is now `mainCartoonMenu`), `buildHelpMenu` now takes **no** argument (shell is `MainHelpMenu`), and `mainCacheMenu` no longer exists (it's `mainPipelineCacheMenu`). For each, `MayaNativeMenus.get_menu` ran the stale init command, swallowed the raise as a *warning*, then built an empty placeholder and spun a 15-iteration `QApplication.processEvents()` populate loop on a menu that could never populate — and **that loop**, pumping Maya's deferred-UI backlog, was the multi-second cost (measured: toon ~10.3s, help ~5.5s; building all 39 menus = ~22s, vs the 7 genuinely-broken ones dominating). **Fix:** when the init command raises, `get_menu` now bails immediately — restores the menu mode and returns `None` (no placeholder, no doomed loop), logging at debug — and `MayaUiHandler._load_maya_ui` falls back to the registered `<key>#submenu` overlay (tentacle ships one per Maya menu; the same overlay `can_resolve` already documents as the fallback for an unresolvable native target), so the user gets a usable menu instead of a broken empty one. The genuinely-async backstop loop is also trimmed 15→6 (working menus stabilize in ~3). Working menus are untouched (verified: edit=31, mesh=38 actions, unchanged). End-to-end verified in a fresh Maya 2025: `get_menu(help/cache/toon)` → `None` in 0.001–0.024s (was 5–10s); `handler.get(help/cache/toon)` → `help#submenu`/`cache#submenu`/`toon#submenu`. Regression: `test_maya_menu_handler.py::TestNativeMenuFailFast` (mocks the init command to raise, asserts `get_menu` returns `None`, caches no placeholder, and never enters the populate loop). NOTE: the remaining stale `MENU_MAPPING` entries (constrain/create/deform/file/mash and the renamed shells) are now harmless — they fall back to overlays — but a full Maya-2025 re-map (shell-name-based lookup, dropping label ambiguity where Deform×3 / Constrain×2 collide) is a worthwhile follow-up.
- **Scene Exporter — the FBX-preset combo (`cmb000`) now persists its selection by preset *name*, so it stops resetting to "None" each session (2026-06-25).** Field symptom: the chosen FBX export preset never stuck — every session the combo showed "None" — while the panel's other combos (framerate, linear unit) restored fine. Root cause was upstream in uitk: combos persist by *index*, but `cmb000` is rebuilt from a `*.fbxexportpreset` directory scan on every show, so the saved index pointed at a different preset (or out of range → reset to item 0) the next session. Fixed by uitk's new opt-in `StateManager` `restore_by` mode (uitk CHANGELOG 2026-06-25); `cmb000_init` now sets `widget.restore_by = "text"` so the selection is keyed off the stable preset name (survives the directory reordering or the preset dir moving). One-line opt-in — the existing in-session restore-previous-selection logic (which handles `refresh_on_show` repopulates) is unchanged. Verified end-to-end by the uitk `test_state_manager.py` regression suite (reorder/shrink the item list → text mode survives, index mode resets to "None"); no Maya-side test change (the slot module hard-imports `maya.mel`, so it's AST-checked only).
- **Macro Manager — window no longer resizes to content on every show; the saved size now persists (2026-06-25).** Field symptom: dragging the panel taller to see more table rows didn't stick — each session's first show snapped the height back to a few rows. Cause: uitk's `MainWindow.showEvent` restores the saved geometry and then runs `fit_height_to_content` on first show (by design, to trim trailing dead space), but a `QTableWidget`'s `sizeHint` ignores row count, so the restored height was collapsed to the table's tiny content minimum. The panel is genuinely resizable, so its height *is* content, not dead space — `MacroManagerSlots.__init__` now opts out (`self.ui.fit_to_content_on_show = False`) so the restored geometry's height is authoritative; `restore_window_size` stays at its default `True`. Regression: `mock_tests/test_macro_manager_window.py` loads the panel through the real `MayaUiHandler` path (Qt offscreen, mocked `maya.cmds`, window never shown) and asserts the opt-out — 1 green; existing `test_macro_manager_slots.py` unaffected (4 green). Placed in `mock_tests/` (skips cleanly under the real-Maya runner) so it never constructs a `Switchboard` against the live registry there; under pytest the suite's `conftest` now sandboxes `QSettings`, since `SettingsManager.__init__` runs a legacy-registry *migration that writes to the real per-user store* on construction.
- **mock_tests — fix a pre-existing `sys.modules["mset"]` cross-suite isolation bug (2026-06-25).** Found while adding the above: in the full `mock_tests` pytest run, 9 `test_toolbag_helpers.py::TestRenderedTemplateExecutes` tests failed with `'NoneType' object has no attribute 'importModel'`. Cause: `test_marmoset_rpc.py`'s `TestAutostartHostGate.setUp` registers `addCleanup(lambda: sys.modules.pop("mset", None))` (it must simulate "not hosted by Toolbag"), which pops the shared `mset` module that `test_toolbag_helpers` installs **only at import time**; the rendered templates then `import mset` → fall back to `None`. The template-exec tests are the only ones that re-import `mset` (the rest reference the `_fake_mset` object directly), so only they broke, and only in full-suite order. Fix: `TestRenderedTemplateExecutes.setUp` now re-establishes `sys.modules["mset"] = _fake_mset`, making the class order-independent. Full suite: 171 passed (was 9 failed).
- **Macro Manager — two more live-testing fixes: `NONE` hotkeys (case) + category changes weren't saved (2026-06-25).** **(1)** The prior `keystring_to_token` guard only matched the exact string `"None"`, but Maya actually reports a keyless/orphan entry's key as `"NONE"` (all caps; case varies across versions) — so it still leaked through `live_hotkey_map`, rendered as the hotkey `NONE`, and two such entries falsely collided ("1 conflict(s)"). The guard is now case-insensitive (`str(ks[0]).strip().lower() == "none"`). `test_hotkey_collisions.py::TestKeystringToToken` covers `NONE` / `None` / `none`. **(2)** The Save-preset button dropped category changes on *unbound* macros (the common case): `_export_bindings` only captured macros with a hotkey, so a re-categorization of a keyless macro was never written. It now also captures any macro whose category differs from its mixin-derived default (`macro_category`), and the load path keeps categories intact — `_blank_bindings` seeds each macro's *default* category instead of blank, and `_import_bindings` falls back to the default when a preset entry carries only a key. Bound macros were unaffected (their category already persists to Maya's `runTimeCommand` via `set_macro`). New `test_macro_manager_slots.py` exercises the export/blank/import round-trip on a bare slots instance (`object.__new__` + stub controller) — 4 green under mayapy.
- **Macro Manager — three follow-up fixes from live testing (2026-06-25).** **(1) Only empty Category cells were editable.** The choice-capture delegate auto-opened the dropdown on the same double-click that created the editor, so the click's trailing mouse-release landed in the popup: on a populated cell it re-selected the already-current value (no-op → "not editable"), on an empty cell nothing was under the cursor so it opened fine. Fixed upstream by dropping the auto-`showPopup` (uitk CHANGELOG 2026-06-25) — the dropdown now opens on a deliberate click. **(2) `'None'` appeared in Hotkey cells.** Maya's `assignCommand … -keyString` reports a keyless/orphan entry's key as the literal string `"None"` (a runtime/name command whose hotkey was cleared); `keystring_to_token` only guarded falsy keys, so `"None"` leaked through `live_hotkey_map` and rendered as the hotkey text. It now treats `ks[0] == "None"` as no key. Regression: `test_hotkey_collisions.py::TestKeystringToToken` (None-sentinel → `""`, plus ctrl/shift/empty cases) — 5 green under mayapy. **(3) Tooltips echoed the columns.** `_macro_tooltip` tabulated `Macro` / `Category` / `Hotkey` rows (all already columns) plus a hotkey hint the Hotkey cell already shows. It now returns the macro's docstring *only when it carries detail beyond the first line* the Description column already shows — a single-line docstring *is* that column's content, so it gets no tooltip — and as plain text so Qt preserves the docstring's own line breaks (`fmt`'s HTML `body` collapses newlines into one run-on paragraph).
- **Macro Manager — Category column is now a double-click dropdown, not a persistent combo cell widget (2026-06-25).** Field symptom: the Category column embedded an editable `QComboBox` in every row (`setCellWidget`), so the column grabbed hover/select and read as a permanently-armed control — inconsistent with the Hotkey column's double-click-to-edit feel. The category cell is now a plain editable item; double-clicking it opens an in-cell dropdown via uitk's new `install_choice_capture` (the dropdown sibling of `install_hotkey_capture`; see uitk CHANGELOG 2026-06-25), seeded with `list_categories()` plus any custom category present in the bindings (kept in step with the filter combo via a shared `_all_categories()` helper). `_on_category_captured(row, col, value)` replaces `_on_category_changed(combo)`; because the delegate's emit is deferred a tick (editor already closed), it now safely rebuilds the filter combo + table so a newly-typed category appears everywhere and a category change re-filters the view. The shared dropdown primitive is unit-tested in uitk; the mayatk side needs live Maya to exercise end-to-end.
- **Preset selectors adopt the new uitk preset template (2026-06-25).** Preset selectors (curtain, macro_manager, and the `add_presets` header panels: scene_exporter / reference_manager / color_manager) now use the new uitk preset template — a `ComboBox` with an `option_box` toolbar (Refresh / Save / ⋯-menu) and inline naming. `curtain.ui` preset combo switched from `WidgetComboBox` to `ComboBox`.
- **Macro Manager — hotkey assignment is now captured in-cell, not via a modal dialog (2026-06-25).** Supersedes the "reusing uitk's `KeyCaptureDialog`" note in the original Macro Manager entry below: that dialog was removed (it also crashed under PySide6 — `QKeySequence(key | modifiers)` routed through a deprecated `QKeyCombination` overload). Double-clicking a Hotkey cell (or the context-menu **Assign**) now opens an in-cell key-capture editor via uitk's new `install_hotkey_capture` (see uitk CHANGELOG 2026-06-25) — press a chord and it commits immediately; Backspace clears, Esc cancels. The table is set to `NoEditTriggers` so a stray keystroke can't silently rebind. `_on_hotkey_captured(row, col, sequence)` converts the captured Qt sequence to Maya notation and persists via the unchanged `_set_binding_key`. The shared capture primitive is unit-tested in uitk; the mayatk side needs live Maya to exercise end-to-end.
- **Macro Manager Workspace Map panel — fix `'QTreeWidget' object has no attribute 'menu'` on load (2026-06-25).** `tree000` was declared as a plain `QTreeWidget` in `env_utils/workspace_map.ui`, but `tree000_init` calls `widget.menu.add(...)` (uitk `MenuMixin`, present only on uitk's `TreeWidget`). The `.ui` had drifted out of sync with its own compiled `workspace_map_ui.py`, which already created a `TreeWidget`. Promoted `tree000` to `TreeWidget` (+ `<customwidget>` block, header `uitk.widgets.treeWidget`) so the source `.ui` matches the compiled file and the tree's right-click menu builds.
- **Audio Clips option box — drop a bogus `setText` on a `QSpinBox` (2026-06-25).** `audio_clips_slots.py` passed `setText="Stagger"` to a `QSpinBox` via `menu.add` (no such method → `set_attributes` warning, set as a stray Python attr). Removed it; the same widget's `setPrefix="Stagger: "` already provides the label. An AST sweep of every `add("…SpinBox"/"…Slider", …)` call across mayatk/uitk confirms this was the only such mismatch.
- **Macro Manager — every macro now has a default category, and the redundant "Apply All" button is gone (2026-06-25).** Categories are now derived from the **defining `*Macros` mixin** (`DisplayMacros` → "Display", `SelectionMacros` → "Selection", …) — the single source of truth, since a macro's domain is already encoded by where it lives. New `MacroManager.macro_category(name)` (mixin-derived, acronym-aware so `UiMacros` → "UI") and `list_categories()` (the distinct set, sorted); `get_current_bindings` falls back to the derived category when a macro is unbound / has no live `runTimeCommand` category, so **no macro shows a blank category** anymore. The panel's category dropdown derives its options from `list_categories()` instead of a hardcoded list, so it can't drift from the macro code. The shipped `default` preset's selection macros (`m_object/vertex/edge/face_selection`, `m_invert_selection`, `m_toggle_UV_select_type`) were corrected from `"Edit"` to `"Selection"` to agree with their mixin (they live in `SelectionMacros`) — previously bound selection macros showed "Edit" while their unbound siblings derived "Selection". **Removed** the header **"Apply All"** button: per-row edits already register to Maya's hotkey system on edit-finished (`_set_binding_key` → `set_macro`), so re-applying the whole set was a near-redundant action that only invited "do I need to press this?" confusion; "Clear All" / "Reset to Default" remain. Tests: `test_macros.py` gains category coverage (every macro categorized, mixin mapping, default-preset agreement) and the previously environment-fragile `live_hotkey_map` headless test now mocks `assignCommand` so it's deterministic under a GUI-connected runner; 53/53 under mayapy.
- **Macro Manager — UI + headless management API for macro hotkeys (2026-06-25).** A new `edit_utils/macro_manager/` Switchboard tool (auto-discovered by `MayaUiHandler`) lets you assign a **hotkey** (live key-capture, reusing uitk's `KeyCaptureDialog`) and **category** to any discoverable `m_*` macro, filter the list by name/description/category, see duplicate-hotkey conflicts, and save/load named binding sets as presets. Each row shows a **humanized label** (`MacroManager.macro_label`, e.g. `m_toggle_UV_select_type` → "Toggle UV Select Type") with a **polished, color-coded tooltip** built from the macro's docstring (`macro_help`) via uitk's `fmt` — the docstring is the single source of truth, so improving it improves the tooltip. **Persistence is native:** assigning a hotkey registers it as a non-default `runTimeCommand` + `hotkey`, which Maya saves and restores on its own — no startup re-apply, so a set hotkey "just works" next session. On show, the panel **re-queries Maya's live hotkey registry** (`assignCommand`, via the new `ui_utils/hotkey_collisions.live_hotkey_map` / `keystring_to_token`) so it reflects bindings changed through Maya's own Hotkey Editor too. `MacroManager` (in `edit_utils/macros.py`) gained a UI-agnostic management API (single source of truth, usable without the panel): `list_available_macros`, `macro_label`/`macro_help`, `get_current_bindings` (live), `apply_bindings`, `clear_hotkey`/`unset_macro`, `find_conflicts`, `qt_sequence_to_maya_key`/`maya_key_to_qt_sequence`, `list_presets`/`load_preset`/`save_preset`/`delete_preset`/`get_active_preset`/`set_active_preset`, and `apply_saved_macros()` (apply a named template on demand). Presets are semantic (`{macro: {key, cat}}`) and stored via `pythontk.PresetStore` at the same location the panel's `uitk.PresetManager` uses, so headless and GUI share one set; the original 22 bindings ship as the built-in **`default` template** (loadable on demand — nothing is auto-applied, so out-of-box state has no keys). `userSetup.py`'s hardcoded `set_macros(...)` block is removed (Maya persistence replaces it); launch the panel via tentacle **Preferences → Macro Manager**. Headless tests cover discovery, key-format + keyString round-trips, conflict detection, apply/clear parity, and preset round-trips (`test/test_macros.py`).

- **Scene Exporter — output Format selector replaces the "Create GLB" task (2026-06-25).** The "Create GLB Alongside FBX" checkbox in the task list is gone; a fixed-width **Format** combo (`cmb004`) now sits in its own group to the right of the Presets group, with **FBX** (default), **GLB**, and **FBX + GLB**. The selection rides to `perform_export` as an `output_format` token (`b000` reads `cmb004.currentData()`); a legacy `create_glb=True` (older callers / saved templates) still maps to FBX + GLB. **GLB-only** writes the FBX to a temp dir, converts it, moves the `.glb` into the output dir, and deletes the temp FBX — so no transient `.fbx` ever lands in (or overwrites anything in) the output folder; a failed conversion fails the export rather than silently leaving nothing. `create_glb(fbx_path=None, announce=True)` gained params for that flow and now returns the `.glb` path. Layout lives in `scene_exporter.ui` (presets wrapped in `horizontalLayout_4`); the compiled `_ui.py` regenerates from it. Verified in a fresh GUI Maya: combo contents/width/position, and FBX→`.fbx` only / FBX+GLB→both / GLB→`.glb` only with the temp dir cleaned up.
- **Scene Exporter — header-menu "templates" now capture the full panel run config (2026-06-25).** Saving/switching a saved template did not update the **Tasks** (`cmb001`), **Checks** (`cmb002`), or **FBX preset** (`cmb000`) — only the menu's own Create-Log/Log-Level settings changed. Cause: `add_presets` builds a `PresetManager` parented to the header menu, which captured *only the menu's own items*; the task/check/preset widgets live on the panel (registered in `ui.widgets` via `register_children`, but out of the menu's scope). Fix: `header_init` now opts the template manager into the new uitk `scope="window"` and `exclude("txt000","txt001","txt003")` (output dir / filename / log output — machine-/scene-specific or transient; the preset combo is always excluded internally). A template now round-trips the full run config — 17 tasks, 15 checks, FBX preset, plus Create-Log/Log-Level/Timestamp/regex — with no chrome noise (window-scope captures only value-bearing widgets). Verified end-to-end in a fresh GUI Maya: save → mutate task/check/preset → reload restores them; excluded path/log fields stay put. Requires uitk's per-instance preset `scope` (see uitk CHANGELOG 2026-06-25).
- **Lightmap Baker — Resolution is now a fixed-size dropdown, plus a new Scope selector (2026-06-25).** Per request, the free `spn_resolution` spinbox (16–16384, any value) became a `cmb_resolution` combobox constrained to the standard power-of-two atlas sizes `256 / 512 / 1024 / 2048 / 4096` (default 1024; each size carried as item data, read back via `currentData()`), and a Quality preset now *snaps* the combo to the nearest listed size (`_set_resolution`), so preset and dropdown can't disagree on a non-listed value. New `cmb_scope` combobox (**Selected** / **Visible** / **Scene**, default Selected) gates which objects `b000` bakes: `_scope_objects()` resolves Selected to the raw selection (unchanged behavior), Visible to `DisplayUtils.get_visible_geometry`, and Scene to every mesh transform — so a whole-scene or visible-only bake no longer needs a manual select-all. Mirrored 1:1 in blendertk (`visible_get()` / `scene.objects` for the scope). Verified offscreen through the Switchboard: both `.ui` compile, the combos populate with the right defaults, and presets snap the resolution. Tests: `blendertk/test/test_blender_ui_handler.py` now loads `lightmap_baker` and asserts the two combos' contents/defaults + preset snapping (128/128).
- **`Components.set_edge_hardness` now aborts (instead of silently no-op'ing) when `unlock_normals=False` and the mesh still has locked normals (2026-06-25).** Locked vertex normals (FBX/Marmoset round-trips) silently block `polySoftEdge` from updating shading, so the prior default would flip the edge flag and appear to do nothing. The method now pre-flights the affected meshes: if any carry locked vertex normals and the caller opted out of unlocking, it emits a `cmds.warning` naming the offending objects and returns them so the caller can surface a user-facing message — no edits are made. Return type changed from `None` to `List[str]` (the blocked object paths; empty when the operation ran or had nothing to do). The `unlock_normals=True` path and the no-hardness early-exit are unchanged in behavior. Object DAG paths are now resolved up front and shared by the guard and the main loop, and the resolution was made collision-robust: a `map_components_to_objects` key is a *leaf name* (two `pCube1`s under different parents merge into one key), so the guard/unlock now resolve **every** object a key fronts rather than only the first edge's object — previously a locked collided sibling was silently skipped (the same silent no-op this feature exists to prevent). Tests: `test_components.py` — `test_set_edge_hardness_aborts_and_returns_locked_objects`, `…_detects_locked_across_name_collision`, `…_returns_empty_on_clean_mesh`, `…_unlock_requested_returns_empty`, `…_no_values_returns_empty`; 49/49 under mayapy.
- **`AnimUtils.scene_has_animation()` — canonical "does anything move over time?" check (2026-06-24).** New lightweight static helper backing the playblast/preview early-exit: it tests for time-input animation curves (`animCurveTL/TA/TU/TT`), so it covers *all* keyed content (transforms, cameras, blendshape morphs, visibility, lights, materials, …), not just transforms, and excludes driver-based set-driven keys (`animCurveU*`). Distinct from the transform-scoped `ShotStore.has_animation` (which exists for shot detection); this is the SSoT for the general question. Consumed by tentacle's Maya Export Playblast to bail on a static scene. Tests: `test_anim_utils.py::TestSceneHasAnimation` (empty→False, keyed transform→True, keyed camera focalLength→True, driven-key-only→False); 4/4 under mayapy.
- **HDR Manager — the rotation slider gains an option box (exact angle + viewport/render visibility toggles); Visible checkbox, tilt, and the preview-scale slider removed (2026-06-24).** Reworked the view controls into one compact control: `slider000` (Y rotation) became a uitk `Slider` whose option box holds, left-to-right, a reusable `ValueOption` (exact angle, typeable + live) then **two** non-persistent (`settings_key=False`) `ToggleOption` icon buttons — **viewport visibility** (`screen` icon → new `HdrManager.preview`, sizing the dome's `skyRadius` to `PREVIEW_SKY_RADIUS`=1000, Arnold's own default, on; 0 off) and **render visibility** (`camera` icon → the `camera` flag, the relocated old `chk000`). The dome shows as a **Viewport 2.0 backdrop without rendering** (lighting unaffected — the dome is at infinity); its exact size is fine-tuned manually in the viewport. Wired in `_setup_rotation_slider` (deferred, like `add_reset_buttons`, since wrapping reparents the widget; the two same-type toggles are built directly so both option instances are captured — `find_option` returns only the first). `_on_viewport_visible`/`_on_render_visible` apply synchronously (a deferred toggle starves a live IPR, see 2026-06-21); `_sync_ui_to_scene` pushes live state via `set_on(emit=False)` + `ValueOption.refresh()`; `hdr_map_visibility`/`hdr_map_preview` read the toggles; `create_network` gained `preview=`. **Removed** (handled manually in-viewport per request): the **tilt** control (`HdrManager.tilt`, `spn_tilt`) and the continuous **preview-scale** slider (`sld_preview`) — the prior same-day `tilt`/`sld_preview` work was folded into this. Tests: engine `test_preview_toggles_sky_radius`/`test_preview_true_with_manual_radius` + `create_network` forwarding; slot `TestViewToggleSlots` + repointed `TestVisibilityToggleSynchronous`; `.ui` guard `TestRotationSliderUi` (`slider000` is a `Slider`; `chk000`/`spn_tilt`/`sld_preview` gone); 68 green under mayapy **with Arnold loaded**, and the panel loads + value-leftmost + viewport-before-render toggle order verified offscreen through the Switchboard. **VP2.0 visual confirm pending** — whether `skyRadius>0` textures the HDR into the viewport is GUI-only; the attribute wiring is correct regardless. Built on the uitk `Slider`/`ValueOption` (see uitk CHANGELOG).

- **Arnold preview bridge — packed masks now supersede their redundant fallbacks (2026-06-22).** When a material carried both a packed mask (ORM/MRAO/MSAO/Metallic_Smoothness) and a separate Specular or Ambient_Occlusion map, both drove the same Arnold slot (`metalness`, or the baseColor-multiply `input2`); with `force=True` connections the surviving driver was order-dependent. `_iter_base_textures` now drops the Specular metalness proxy whenever any metalness-driving map is present, and drops a standalone Ambient_Occlusion map whenever a packed AO-carrying mask (ORM/MRAO/MSAO) is present — so each property has exactly one deterministic driver. Test: `test_arnold_bridge.py::test_packed_mask_supersedes_specular_and_standalone_ao`.
- **Arnold preview shader — MSAO mask no longer renders every object green (2026-06-21).** Field report: a Stingray material carrying a Unity HDRP **MSAO** mask rendered green under the Arnold preview bridge. Cause: `ArnoldBridge._PACKED_LAYOUTS["MSAO"]` routed AO as `("", "whole")`, so `_connect_packed` wired the **entire** packed `outColor` (R=Metallic, G=AO, B=Detail) into the baseColor `aiMultiply` — for the common non-metal case R≈0 zeroed red and the AO green channel dominated, tinting every surface green. ORM/MRAO already broadcast a single AO channel; only MSAO used the buggy "whole" mode (introduced with the 2026-06-21 `_PACKED_LAYOUTS` refactor below). Verified empirically with a headless `kick` render: the old whole-`outColor` wiring renders a mid-gray base at BGR ≈ `[0.006, 0.32, 0.006]` (G/R ≈ 53, green); broadcasting the **green** channel renders neutral `[1.0, 1.0, 1.0]`. Fix: MSAO AO is now the single G channel broadcast to `input2R/G/B` like ORM (R) / MRAO (B); the dead "whole" branch and the `(channel, mode)` ao-tuple were dropped (ao is now a channel string). Tests: `test_arnold_bridge.py::test_msao_channel_routing` strengthened to assert the multiply is fed by `.outColorG` (not the whole `outColor`) — the old assertion passed *even with the bug* because the compound connection still showed under `input2`; 27/27 under mayapy with Arnold.
- **HDR Manager — Visible toggle applies synchronously so a live Arnold IPR updates (2026-06-21).** Field report: with the Arnold RenderView/IPR open, enabling **Visible** did not show the HDR backdrop. Confirmed the network is sound — a `kick` render of the camera-visible skydome the manager builds is correct (HDR backdrop) at `skyRadius` 0 *and* large, so it's not a setup/`skyRadius` issue. Root cause was a regression from an earlier same-day attempt that deferred `chk000` through `_defer_to_idle` (`cmds.evalDeferred(lowestPriority=True)`) by analogy to the map-swap: but a checkbox toggle has **no combo-popup-teardown re-entrancy** to escape (the only thing `cmb000`'s deferral guards against), and during an *active* IPR — which keeps the idle queue busy — a `lowestPriority` callback gets **starved**, so the `camera`-flag `setAttr` was delayed/never ran and toggling Visible appeared to do nothing. Fix: `chk000` sets the flag **synchronously** again, exactly like the other live controls (`spn_intensity` / `spn_exposure` / `slider000`, which all set their attrs in-band and update the IPR fine through Arnold's normal attribute-edit callback). Tests: `test_hdr_manager.py::TestVisibilityToggleSynchronous` (chk000 applies the checkbox state in-band, schedules nothing on the idle queue). **Visual confirm in a live IPR pending** (IPR can't run headless); it now mirrors the Intensity control's update path.
- **HDR Manager — selecting a map in the dropdown now actually applies it: the deferred apply was starved by `evalDeferred(lowestPriority=True)` (2026-06-21).** Field report: "changing the HDR in the combobox doesn't seem to set the HDR" — the field shows the new map but the scene (and the popup's accent-strip marker) stay on the old one. **Verified live in a fresh GUI Maya** (driven over the command port): the manager's in-place swap is correct (`mgr.hdr_env = B` → the single skydome/file node repoints immediately), so the failure is the *deferral*. `cmb000` schedules `_apply_selection` via `_defer_to_idle` → `cmds.evalDeferred(..., lowestPriority=True)`, and a `lowestPriority` callback **does not run while Maya sits idle** — measured: a normal-priority `evalDeferred` fires at the next idle (~0.6 s, and reliably once an Arnold IPR or any UI activity keeps the loop alive) but the `lowestPriority` one had **not run after 7 s** of a quiet session. So after the user picked a map and just looked at the result, the apply never executed → "the dropdown doesn't change the HDR" (the prior `lowestPriority` was an unverified "let Arnold's IPR settle first" precaution that traded the black-render for a dead apply). Fix: `_defer_to_idle` drops `lowestPriority` — plain `cmds.evalDeferred(callback)` still escapes the combo-popup-teardown re-entrancy (same Maya idle queue the nested Qt loop never services) but runs promptly. Also hardened: every *bail* in `_apply_selection` (`not path` / integrity reject / Arnold-unavailable) now calls `_select_active_in_combo()` so a genuinely-refused pick (e.g. an un-synced cloud HDR) re-mirrors the dropdown to the live scene instead of leaving the field/marker disagreeing. Tests: `test_hdr_manager.py::TestDeferToIdle` now pins **normal** priority (was lowestPriority); `TestFailedApplyResyncsCombo` covers the re-mirror; 59 green offscreen. **Confirm in the live panel** (the command-port "idle" is deader than a real user's session, which exaggerates the delay).
- **Arnold preview shader / Game Shader — packed-map FBX round-trip confirmed (inline smoothness→roughness flip + original texture embedded) (2026-06-21).** Verified (no code change) that the packed-map handling already does exactly what's wanted: the texture factory inserts a `reverse` node to flip Unity **smoothness → roughness** for correct Maya rendering, while the *original* packed file stays referenced so FBX export embeds it intact (alpha = smoothness) for Unity. Headless `FBXExport` (ASCII + embedded media) of both a **Stingray** and a **Standard Surface** material with an MSAO mask shows the `reverse` flip node present *and* the source `MaskMap` texture in the exported FBX — Stingray via the direct `outColor → TEX_metallic_map` slot, Standard Surface via the `_ensure_fbx_safe_connection` custom-attr guard.
- **HDR Manager — applying an HDR now makes Arnold the active renderer (fixes "render is black even with Visible on") (2026-06-21).** Field follow-up: the render stayed black after selecting an HDR, even with Visible=True. Re-verified empirically (fresh Maya + Arnold, no live session): a `kick` render of the exact `aiSkyDomeLight` + `file` network the manager builds is **non-black** (mean 0.65, full-frame HDR backdrop), identical across `skyRadius` 0 / 100 / large — so the network setup is sound (the `skyRadius=0` suspicion was ruled out). The real cause is the **active renderer**: a fresh scene defaults to `mayaSoftware` (loading mtoa does **not** change it — confirmed in mayapy), and `aiSkyDomeLight` renders in *no* renderer but Arnold, so a Maya Software/Hardware render is black regardless of the Visible flag. Fix: the `hdr_env` setter — the single choke point for every apply path (`create_network` / UI swap / add flow) — now calls new `HdrManager._ensure_arnold_renderer()` (delegates to `RenderUtils.set_renderer`, the renderer-selection SSoT; no-op if already Arnold) once a valid texture is committed (a refused *incomplete* image returns first, leaving the renderer untouched). Help text notes the switch. Tests: `test_hdr_manager.py` +`test_apply_sets_arnold_renderer` / `test_refused_image_leaves_renderer_unchanged`; 55 green under mayapy. **Note:** if the renderer was *already* Arnold and the render is still black, the remaining cause is live IPR not re-translating — that needs an interactive repro (IPR can't run headless).
- **HDR Manager — the dropdown/refresh now reflects a skydome made *outside* the manager (no more false "None") (2026-06-21).** Field report: "refreshing resets to None even when an HDR is set; it should show the current HDR — None only when there truly is none." The `hdr_env` getter matched **only** the canonically-named `aiSkyDomeLight_`, so a dome created via Arnold's own *Lights* menu, an import/reference, or a rename resolved to `None` — the panel showed "None" despite a live HDR, then built a *second* dome on the next pick. Reproduced in mayapy (`hdr_env` → `None` for a `myCustomDome` skydome). Fix: `hdr_env` now **adopts any** `aiSkyDomeLight` — preferring the canonical name, then a dome already wired to a `file` texture, else first-by-name — so `_select_active_in_combo` highlights the real active map and "None" shows only when there's genuinely no dome (or the explicit None entry is picked). Because the getter adopts the existing dome, the setter reuses it (`self.hdr_env or _create_skydome()`) instead of duplicating. Tests: +`test_hdr_env_adopts_foreign_skydome` / `test_hdr_env_prefers_canonical_over_foreign` / `test_setter_reuses_foreign_skydome_no_duplicate`.
- **HDR Manager — rotate-HDR was a silent no-op; now actually rotates the dome (2026-06-21).** Field report: "the rotate HDR option isn't working." The `rotation` setter called `cmds.rotate(transform, degrees, rotateY=True, …)` — **object first** — but `cmds.rotate`'s positionals are `(angleX, angleY, angleZ, *objects)`, so Maya parsed the *angle* as an object (`Object N is invalid`) and raised on **every** set (a valid transform too — the 2026-06-16 note wrongly blamed only stale transforms). The try/except then swallowed it, so the dome never moved. Verified in mayapy: the old call raised "Object 50.0 is invalid"; `setAttr rotateY` sets it cleanly. Fixed by setting `rotateY` directly (unambiguous; the dome only ever spins around Y from a default pivot). Tests: `test_hdr_manager.py::TestRotationSetterRobust` gained `test_rotation_applies_to_transform` (asserts `rotateY` actually changes — the coverage gap that let this ship), and the swallow test now patches `setAttr` (the call the setter makes).
- **HDR Manager — Intensity / Exposure / Resolution (+ advanced Samples / Diffuse / Specular) are now uitk spin boxes with per-field reset buttons (2026-06-21).** Per request, the six `HDR Levels` / `Advanced` spin boxes were promoted from plain `QDoubleSpinBox` / `QSpinBox` to uitk `DoubleSpinBox` / `SpinBox` (matching the rest of the uitk-based panel: wheel-step modifiers, HUD feedback, themed text colour), and `_initialize_ui` now calls `self.sb.add_reset_buttons(self.ui)` so each carries the option-box **reset-to-default** icon (click → registry default; Alt/Ctrl+click → bypass to default, greyed/restorable). Done in `_initialize_ui` rather than `__init__` because the spin boxes aren't wired onto `self.ui` until `register_children` runs. Verified offscreen (PySide6): the panel loads, `spn_intensity`→`DoubleSpinBox`, `spn_resolution`→`SpinBox`, and `add_reset_buttons` wires all 6. Tests: `TestHdrSpinBoxesAreUitk` static-guards the `.ui` (all six promoted, customwidgets declared).
- **HDR Manager — dropdown no longer rebuilds its model on every open (occasional first-click-dropped fix + efficiency) (2026-06-21).** `_refresh_combo` (run from `before_popup_shown` on every dropdown-open) did a recursive `sourceimages` disk walk + full clear/repopulate each time; churning the item model right as the popup shows can desync the popup view so the first click is dropped ("I have to reopen it to select"). It now still scans (the scan is the change-detector that catches a file dropped into `sourceimages` while the panel is open) but **skips the destructive clear/repopulate of the item model when the available HDRs are unchanged** (new `_listed_paths_match` — normalized, order-independent membership compare), only rebuilding when a file was actually added/removed — so the model isn't churned out from under the opening popup. Tests: `TestRefreshComboRecursive` gained `test_skips_rebuild_when_listing_unchanged` / `test_rebuilds_when_listing_changed`. (Audited scriptJob usage while here — all ecosystem scriptJobs go through `ScriptJobManager`; none fire during combo browsing, so this churn, not a scriptJob, was the HDR combo's flakiness.)
- **HDR Manager — black-render-on-HDR-switch: deferral moved from a Qt tick to Maya idle (the prior fix was insufficient) (2026-06-21).** Field follow-up: switching/selecting an HDR under live Arnold IPR *still* rendered black. Re-verified empirically in a fresh Maya + Arnold (no live session) that the skydome network is sound — a `kick` render of the exact `aiSkyDomeLight` + `file` network `HdrManager` builds lights the scene (non-black; the exported `.ass` carries the `skydome_light` + image node + texture) — so the breakage is purely the IPR re-entrancy, not the setup. The earlier fix deferred `cmb000`'s scene mutation via `QtCore.QTimer.singleShot(0)`, but `singleShot(0)` is serviced by *whatever Qt event loop is currently spinning* — including the combo popup's **nested teardown loop** — so the mutation still ran inside the re-entrancy a live IPR re-translates from. New `HdrManagerSlots._defer_to_idle` routes the deferral through `cmds.evalDeferred(lowestPriority=True)` (Maya's idle queue, which the nested Qt loop never services; lowest-priority so Arnold's own deferred IPR updates settle first); `cmb000` calls it for every apply. Tests: `test_hdr_manager.py::TestDeferToIdle` pins the `evalDeferred(lowestPriority)` contract; the deferral tests now record via the stubbed `_defer_to_idle`; 14 slot tests green under standalone. **Interactive-only to fully confirm** (IPR can't run headless), but the offline render rules out any setup regression.
- **RenderUtils — `supports_ipr()`; the render button disables the IPR option instead of failing after the click (2026-06-21).** New `RenderUtils.supports_ipr(renderer)` reports whether a renderer can start IPR — it queries the renderer's registered `startIprRenderProcedure`, and treats the known optional renderers (Arnold / V-Ray / Redshift) as IPR-capable without forcing a costly plugin load just to gate a checkbox (`start_ipr` stays the backstop, so a renderer that ultimately exposes no procedure still fails safe). The tentacle render-button option box now gates its **IPR** checkbox on this (disabled + unchecked for renderers without IPR, e.g. Viewport 2.0) rather than letting the user tick it and showing an after-the-fact "IPR isn't available" message box. Tests: `test_render_utils.py` (+4: unknown → False, Viewport 2.0 → False, loaded Arnold → True, installed-but-unloaded optional → True).
- **Arnold preview shader — dropped the redundant Rebuild button, renamed Add/Remove to "Add/Remove Network", and extended map coverage; `_connect_texture` refactored DRY (2026-06-21).** The panel's **Rebuild Bridge** button was removed (it duplicated the **Force** checkbox, which already rebuilds an existing network via `add(force=True)`); the library `ArnoldBridge.rebuild()` primitive is kept. The two action buttons are renamed **Add Network** / **Remove Network** (the network is an in-Maya `aiSurfaceShader` preview, not an external-app bridge) — labels, tooltips, help text, and footer messages updated. The `_connect_texture` if/elif wall was refactored into shared helpers (`_make_file`, `_chan_plug`, `_wire_roughness`, `_wire_bump`, `_connect_packed` + a `_PACKED_LAYOUTS` channel table), and map coverage now spans **all** registry-resolved types the pipeline can emit — added **MRAO** (packed, mirrors ORM/MSAO; `_iter_base_textures` precedence extended so two packs never double-wire), **Bump/Height** (object-space bump), and the **Specular/Glossiness/Smoothness** spec-gloss set (gloss/smoothness invert to roughness; specular → metalness proxy). Behavior for the previously-handled maps is preserved. Tests: `test_arnold_bridge.py` +6 wiring tests (MRAO/Specular/Glossiness/Smoothness/Bump+Height/Normal-tangent); `test_rebuild_button` removed (button gone), `test_rebuild` kept; 25/25 under mayapy with Arnold.
- **New `RenderUtils` (`render_utils._render_utils`) — renderer enumeration/selection + Render-View control (2026-06-21).** A thin, reusable home for render plumbing so UI panels stay declarative (SSoT): `get_available_renderers()` (built-ins + registered renderers + installed-but-unloaded plugins detected via `MAYA_PLUG_IN_PATH` — Arnold/V-Ray/Redshift), `set_renderer()` (loads the plugin on demand, sets `defaultRenderGlobals.currentRenderer`), `render_camera()` / `redo_previous_render()` (Render-View procs), and `start_ipr()` (delegates to the renderer's **registered** `startIprRenderProcedure` — Arnold/V-Ray/Redshift/Maya Software each register one — so the full, correct, re-entrancy-safe IPR-start runs: MtoA's `arnoldIprStart` creates the options node, closes the legacy Render View, enables progressive refinement, and `evalDeferred`s the whole thing so IPR translation never fires inside the calling UI event. Reimplementing it by hand (`arnoldRenderView mode=open` + `Run IPR`) left the RenderView blank/black — opening the window synchronously from the button click hit exactly that re-entrancy. Returns False when no IPR procedure is registered). Registered in `DEFAULT_INCLUDE`. Tests: `test_render_utils.py` (11, mayapy; interactive/IPR calls patched). `check_cmds_syntax.py` gained `renderWindowRenderCamera` (interactive Render-View proc).
- **HDR Manager — switching HDRs no longer blacks out the render; every apply deferred off the combo signal (2026-06-21).** Follow-up to the headerless fix below: once selecting actually applied, *switching* HDRs turned the Arnold RenderView black and it stayed black even after reopening it. Verified the cause empirically in a fresh Maya + Arnold (no live session): the network stays structurally intact across an in-place `fileTextureName` swap, and a **batch RTT render of the swapped texture is correct** (brightness ratio exactly matched the new HDR, identical to a full rebuild) — so the data/translation is fine. The breakage is interactive-only: `cmb000` mutated the scene *synchronously inside the combobox `currentIndexChanged` callback* (the in-place swap, and the None-clear's `cmds.delete`), and a live Arnold IPR re-translating the skydome from inside that mid-popup-teardown re-entrancy corrupts the render — the same hazard the cold-start path already deferred against. Fix: `cmb000` now only schedules `_apply_selection` (`QTimer.singleShot`) for **every** action; the single deferred apply does clear / in-place-swap / build after the popup tears down (swap preserves the live light settings; build still applies the UI knobs; casual applies surface a bad file in the footer/console without a modal). Tests: the live-swap, None-clear, and incomplete-image cases now assert `cmb000` *defers* (no synchronous mutation) and that running the deferred apply performs the swap/clear; 35 slot/Qt tests green offscreen. **Live RenderView behavior still warrants a confirming look** (IPR can't run headless).
- **HDR Manager — dropdown now displays the active map; selecting a map actually applies it (2026-06-21).** Field report: "the combobox header (`HDR Map:`) means I can't tell which HDR is active, and selecting one does nothing." Root cause was the header itself: with a header, `ComboBox.check_index` snaps `currentIndex` back to `-1` after every pick, so (a) the combo always painted the fixed `HDR Map:` label instead of the chosen map, and (b) the slot's `widget.currentData()` read `None` → `cmb000`/`_apply_selection` silently no-opped. The header had only been added (2026-06-19) as a side-channel to keep `restore_state = not has_header` False; that goal is now met directly — `_refresh_combo` populates **headerless** and re-asserts `restore_state = False` after each `add()`. The combo is now a true live mirror: new `_select_active_in_combo` points it at the scene's wired HDR — selecting a **listed** file's row, surfacing an **unlisted** active file (e.g. a Link-mode HDR outside `sourceimages`) as a transient row so it's still shown rather than reported as a false *None*, or selecting the explicit **None** entry only when nothing is wired (so "no HDR" reads as *None* not a blank box). Called from `_sync_ui_to_scene`, on dropdown-open/header-refresh (`_refresh_and_sync_combo`), and on scene events. ScriptJob monitoring broadened beyond `SceneOpened`/`NewSceneOpened` to also cover `SceneImported` (re-scan + sync) and `Undo`/`Redo` (sync only — the file list on disk is unchanged, so no recursive `sourceimages` walk runs per undo); each subscribe is guarded so an unavailable event can't block panel open. Tests: `test_hdr_manager.py` — `TestComboHeaderBreaksSelection` reproduces the header→`currentData()==None` bug **against the real uitk ComboBox** (the stub-widget tests couldn't catch it) and pins the headerless fix; `TestSelectActiveInCombo` (active-path / None-fallback / unlisted-fallback); the two `_refresh_combo` tests now assert *no header* + `restore_state` False. 35 slot/Qt tests green offscreen; module imports clean under mayapy. **Live behavior (interactive Maya panel) still warrants a confirming look** — the marking-menu/panel path can't run headless.
- **Unity bridge — Unity Project field gets option-box buttons; project actions moved off the header (2026-06-19).** Following uitk's `BridgeSlotsBase` switch to option-box buttons on the Output Dir row, the **Unity Project** field overrides `_configure_output_dir_options` to show a **recent-projects** history button + an **option menu** (▾) carrying the project actions — **Set Project…** (browse, shared `_pick_output_dir`), **Open Unity Project**, **New Unity Project…** — co-located with the field they act on. Those three left the header menu (`HEADER_MENU_ITEMS` is now just Clear Log). The old `...` browse button is gone. blendertk mirrors this (`compare_panel_surface.py --panel unity_bridge` clean). Validated via an offscreen option-box harness + the extapps panel suite (400 passed) for the shared base.
- **Unity bridge — "Launch Editor" toggle replaced by a "Launch Unity" mode (Don't launch / Open Editor / Headless) (2026-06-19).** Field report: "Send to Unity" copied the FBX but Unity never opened — the launch was a default-off checkbox the user never ticked. The `LAUNCH_EDITOR` bool is now a `LAUNCH_MODE` choice param: *Don't launch* (default — Unity imports on focus), *Open Editor* (windowed instance), *Headless (batch)* (`-batchmode -quit` import-and-exit; tooltip flags it needs a batch-capable license). Delivery logic lives in `unitytk.CopyToAssetsDeliverer` (`LAUNCH_MODE` + back-compat for the old bool); blendertk mirrors this. Tests: `test_unity_bridge.py` `params_defaults` asserts `LAUNCH_MODE` default.
- **Unity bridge — removed the "Unity Studio" delivery mode; collapsed to a single Send-to-Unity flow (2026-06-19).** *Unity Studio* turned out to be a separate **paid, browser-based** product (created in Unity Cloud's Asset Manager), not this desktop FBX hand-off — the same-day "two delivery modes" addition had reused the name for a version-aware *desktop* launch, colliding with the real product and confusing users ("it opens Unity with the assets, but this was supposed to send to Unity Studio"). Per user direction (Studio isn't free → drop it) the `existing`/`studio` split is gone: `cmb000` collapses back to the single `copy_to_assets` delivery target (label "Copy to Project"), every param is always visible (removed `MODE_PARAM_KEYS`/`_SHARED_KEYS`/`_relevant_param_keys`), and the Send button is always "Send to Unity" (removed `_sync_send_button`/`_current_mode`/the `b000` studio branch). **Launch Editor** (opt-in), the **Unity Version** picker, and **New Unity Project…** survive as plain-Unity features (version-aware launch / create). The shared `unitytk.CopyToAssetsDeliverer` is untouched (still honors the `UNITY_VERSION` launch param). blendertk mirrors this (`compare_panel_surface.py --panel unity_bridge` clean). Tests: `test_unity_bridge.py` — `test_slot_single_delivery_mode_no_studio` replaces the two-mode/visibility test.
- **Unity bridge — two delivery modes: Existing Project + Unity Studio (2026-06-19).** The `cmb000` dropdown now selects the delivery *target* (friendly labels over internal `existing`/`studio` stems via `_format_combo_label`). *Existing Project* = the prior copy-into-a-project-you-point-at flow (keeps the opt-in **Launch Editor** toggle). *Unity Studio* adds a dynamic **Unity Version** dropdown (populated at panel build from `unitytk.UnityFinder.find_editors()`), a **New Unity Project…** header action (`unitytk.UnityLauncher.create_project`), and always launches the chosen Editor (version-aware) after copying. Parameter visibility follows the mode (`_relevant_param_keys` → `MODE_PARAM_KEYS`); the Send button label tracks it ("Send to Unity" / "Send to Unity Studio"). Delivery stays DRY on the single shared `unitytk.CopyToAssetsDeliverer` (now honoring a `UNITY_VERSION` launch param); the blendertk slot mirrors this (`compare_panel_surface.py --panel unity_bridge` clean). Tests: `test_unity_bridge.py` (+1 mode/visibility → 12 under mayapy); live-verified in a fresh Maya — both modes render, the version dropdown populated from the installed editors, end-to-end Existing-mode send copies the FBX into `Assets/`.
