- **Unity bridge — exposed an export Scope option (2026-06-19).** New `SCOPE` choice param (Selected / Entire Scene / Visible Only, default Selected) leading the panel's **Export** section; `UnityBridgeSlots.b000` now resolves the export set from it via `_resolve_scope_objects` instead of always using the live selection — *Entire Scene* gathers every non-intermediate mesh, *Visible Only* uses `DisplayUtils.get_visible_geometry(shapes=True, inherit_parent_visibility=True)` (that flag is what actually walks the transform chain and drops hidden geometry; without it the helper returns every renderable shape). Tests: `test_unity_bridge.py` (+4 — SCOPE default + all/selected/visible resolution); 11/11 under mayapy. **Env note:** the panel rendered blank in live Maya until `unitytk` was editable-installed into Maya's Python — it's mayatk's first `unitytk` consumer, and the modern `pip install -e` meta-path finder loses to the monorepo-root namespace shadow (`import unitytk` → bare `…/_scripts/unitytk` namespace pkg, no `CopyToAssetsDeliverer`, slot import fails → no slot attaches). Fix: reinstall `mayapy -m pip install -e unitytk --config-settings editable_mode=compat` (plain-path `.pth`, matching the siblings).
- **Shader Templates — stopped baking machine-specific texture paths into shipped templates (2026-06-19).** Field report: restoring a template warned `Texture file …/Aircraft_Alphas/../../../../../../../../../Cloud/Code/_scripts/mayatk/test/temp_textures/Test_Base_Color.png doesn't exist`. The `../…` chain is Maya's project-relative path storage — the six templates had been **saved from a test run** with an unrelated project open, so `getAttr fileTextureName` returned the active-project dir spliced (via a climb to the drive root) to the repo's test-fixture dir, and `GraphSaver` wrote that mashup straight into the YAML. The stale path only surfaced when restoring **without** first loading textures (empty `available_map_types` → the baked path applied verbatim). Root-cause fix in `GraphCollector._create_node_entry`: a `file` node whose texture resolves to a known `map_type` is a user-supplied slot (re-resolved at restore from `texture_paths`), so its path is **no longer persisted** — only `map_type: null` nodes (the fixed Maya-install StingrayPBS cube/IBL maps) keep their path. Restore hardening in `GraphRestorer._restore_node`: a map_type slot with no resolved texture now **drops** any path the template still carries instead of applying it (defends against legacy/hand-edited templates the data scrub can't reach — Maya shows its missing-texture placeholder, no spurious warning). Data fix: scrubbed 21 stale `fileTextureName` entries across the six shipped templates (load→scrub→dump round-trips byte-identical to the saver's own format, CRLF preserved). Tests: `test_shader_templates.py` (+3: `test_save_omits_map_type_texture_paths` pins the saver; `test_restore_drops_stale_map_path_when_no_texture` pins the restore guard; `test_shipped_templates_have_no_baked_map_paths` static-guards the committed YAML against any map_type'd path or `..` mashup); 12/12 under mayapy.
- **HDR Manager — the dropdown now mirrors the live scene HDR, not a persisted UI value (2026-06-19).** Field report: on a fresh scene the combo restored its last-session pick and (signals unblocked) fired `cmb000`, auto-loading a stale HDR instead of showing the true state. Root cause: the combo had no header, so uitk's `restore_state = not has_header` left it `True` → the switchboard saved/restored its index across sessions (Blender's combo already avoids this via a header). Fix: `cmb000_init` sets `restore_state = False`, and `_refresh_combo` populates with `header="HDR Map:"` (the no-selection state at index -1) so the combo stays non-persistent across every repopulate; `_sync_ui_to_scene` now also selects the row matching the live skydome's wired file (or index -1 / "HDR Map:" when no skydome), so a new scene reads as None. Tests: `test_hdr_manager.py` asserts the header is passed on both `_refresh_combo` paths; the real-ComboBox `add(header=…)` → `restore_state=False` mechanism verified offscreen. **Needs live confirmation in interactive Maya** (QSettings persistence is session-scoped).
- **HDR Manager — removed the separate "Set HDR" button; selecting a map now applies it (2026-06-19).** The dropdown is the panel's sole apply action. With a live skydome a pick still swaps the file texture in place (unchanged cheap `setAttr`); with no network yet the build is **deferred to the next event-loop tick** (`QTimer.singleShot` → renamed `_apply_selection`, formerly `b000`) so the mtoa load + render-node creation never run synchronously inside the combobox `currentIndexChanged` callback (the documented mid-popup-teardown crash). `b000` and the "Set HDR" `QPushButton` are gone from the slot and the `.ui`; help text + docstrings updated. Tests: `test_hdr_manager.py` — cold-start selection now asserts a single deferred `_apply_selection` was scheduled and the scene wasn't synchronously mutated; None-token apply tests retargeted to `_apply_selection`; slot suite green under the mocked-cmds harness (the 6 real-Maya manager tests remain mayapy/Arnold-gated).
- **Bridge slots' header menus declared as data on the shared base (2026-06-19).** Following uitk's new default `BridgeSlotsBase.header_init`, the five Maya bridge slots (marmoset / substance / blender / unity / rizom) dropped their near-identical hand-rolled `header_init` for a `HELP_SPEC` dict — plus a `HEADER_MENU_ITEMS` tuple where the menu differs from the Open / Refresh / Clear-Log default (unity → Open Unity Project; rizom → Open UV Editor / Open Scripts Folder / Refresh Scripts). Behavior, objectNames, and help content preserved; verified each slot's menu resolves to existing handlers under mayapy.
- **Migrated onto pythontk's new `geo_utils` namespace (clean break, 2026-06-19).** Followed pythontk's relocation of the polyline/point-cloud geometry cluster out of `MathUtils` into `Polyline` / `PointCloud`: `Rail` now subclasses `ptk.Polyline` (was `ptk.CurtainRail`); `tube_rig` uses `ptk.Polyline.order_points` / `.smooth` / `.resample` (were `arrange_points_as_path` / `smooth_points` / `dist_points_along_centerline`); `curve_to_tube` uses `ptk.Polyline.simplify` (was `simplify_rdp`); `geometry_matcher` uses `ptk.PointCloud.pca_transform` (was `get_pca_transform`); `mat_utils._cluster_objects_by_distance` uses `ptk.PointCloud.cluster_by_distance`; `xform_utils` uses `ptk.PointCloud.hash_points`. Behavior unchanged — namespace-only.
- **Unity bridge + every Maya hand-off bridge migrated onto the general `pythontk.core_utils.app_handoff` base (2026-06-19).** New `env_utils/unity_bridge` panel: export the Maya selection and copy the FBX into a Unity project's `Assets/` (via the shared `unitytk.CopyToAssetsDeliverer` Strategy — Unity imports on focus, no fresh-instance launch, never disturbs a running editor). Discovered by `MayaUiHandler` (`marking_menu.show("unity_bridge")`); exposed by a **Unity Bridge** button in the tentacle Maya Scene menu. As part of the same effort, **all** Maya hand-off bridges now express themselves through the one general base (Template-Method skeleton + pluggable `Deliverer` Strategy, per-app discovery as an `AppSpec` dataclass): `BlenderBridge` is a thin `ScriptLaunchBridge` (config = a `ScriptLaunchSpec` dataclass), with the Maya selection + FBX export (incl. the strip-materials path) extracted to the shared `env_utils/handoff_export.MayaExportMixin` (its `_produce` hook, reused by the Unity bridge); `MarmosetBridge` is a `HandoffBridge` whose `MarmosetEngine` became a `pythontk.Deliverer` (launch / round-trip RPC) and whose `_produce` builds the FBX + material + bake-pairs sidecars; `SubstanceBridge` is a `HandoffBridge` that self-delivers (its stateful Painter launch/attach + JSON-RPC + managed-instance registry stays on the class, split into the `_preflight`/`_produce`/`_deliver` hooks); `RizomUVBridge` / `MarmosetEngine` exe-discovery consolidated onto `AppLauncher.resolve_app_path` (dropping their duplicated `_scan_install_dirs`). Public `send()` signatures and all internal helpers preserved. New dependency: `unitytk`. Tests: `test_unity_bridge.py` (8, mayapy — real export → `Assets/`); `test_blender_bridge.py` (12), `test_mat_marmoset_bridge.py` (render+export), `mock_tests/test_marmoset_bridge.py` (37+8), `test_substance_bridge.py` (71), `test_uv_rizom_bridge.py` green after the refactor.
- **Scene Exporter — new "Check Max Texture Size" validation check (2026-06-19).** Added `check_texture_file_size` to the export validation pipeline: it fails the export when any texture feeding the export materials exceeds a configurable on-disk size, flagging un-downsized authoring maps (e.g. an 8K master) that would bloat the shipped asset. Surfaced as a ComboBox in the **Materials** check section (`_texture_size_options`: OFF / 4 / 8 / 16 / 32 / 64 / 128 MB) **defaulting to 16 MB**; OFF disables it. Scoped via the new shared `_TaskDataMixin._get_export_file_nodes()` helper (walks export-material shading history → `file` nodes), which `resolve_invalid_texture_paths` was also refactored onto (DRY). Each texture path is resolved through `MatUtils.resolve_path` so project-relative paths still measure the real file — the default-on `convert_to_relative_paths` task runs **before** checks and rewrites paths to workspace-relative form, which a bare `os.path.isfile` would skip (silently passing every texture). Missing files are left to `check_valid_paths`; UDIM sets probe the first tile (consistent with the path tasks). Tests: `test_scene_exporter.py` (+6: in-definitions/16 MB default, OFF passes, oversized fails, under-limit passes, missing-file ignored, relative-path resolved).
- **Scene Exporter — `check_objects_below_floor` now honors its documented 0.5 default tolerance from the UI (2026-06-19).** The check is registered as a QCheckBox, so enabling it passed `True` into `tolerance`, which `max(0.0, float(True))` coerced to **1.0** — silently doubling the advertised "default 0.5 unit" tolerance from the tooltip. It now treats the boolean `True` as "use the default" (new `_DEFAULT_FLOOR_TOLERANCE = 0.5` class constant); an explicit `None` still means a strict `0.0`, and real numeric tolerances are unchanged. Tests: `test_scene_exporter.py` (+3: checkbox-True→0.5, None→strict 0.0, numeric respected). Full module 74/74 green under mayapy.
- **Material Updater — "Discover Maps in sourceimages" gap-fills materials from unconnected on-disk siblings (2026-06-18).** New opt-in header checkbox (`chk_discover_sourceimages`, default off). When enabled, `MatUpdater.update_materials` resolves the project `sourceimages` folder and forwards it as the generic `discover_dir` to `MapFactory.prepare_maps` (the mayatk-level flag `discover_sourceimages` is converted to the factory's DCC-agnostic param, then dropped — it never leaks through as a factory kwarg). Each material's texture set is then gap-filled with same-base-name textures sitting in `sourceimages` that share a recognized map type but were never wired in (e.g. a Normal on disk that nobody connected). Pure additive: only **missing** map types are added; a connected slot is never replaced (per user direction — an overlapping file is generally the same file anyway). The discovery itself lives upstream in `pythontk` so every `prepare_maps` consumer (map_converter, game_shader, blendertk, …) gets it for free. Tests: `test_material_updater.py` (+2: flag→`discover_dir` forwarded + not-leaked; disabled→no `discover_dir`); 17/17 + workflow 22/22 green under mayapy.
- **Scene Exporter — confirmed no subfolder duplicate-copy bug; added a guard test (2026-06-16).** Verified the partner concern to the HDR Manager fix: `convert_to_relative_paths` → `MatUtils.copy_textures_to_sourceimages` already skips any texture already under sourceimages **at any depth** (`lower.startswith(si_abs + "/")`), so a texture in e.g. `sourceimages/textures/wood.png` is left in place, not duplicated into the root. Locked in by `test_scene_exporter.py::test_copy_textures_skips_file_already_in_sourceimages_subfolder` (subfolder texture: nothing copied, root stays clean, original untouched). No production change needed — the existing guard was already correct.
- **HDR Manager — files already in a sourceimages *subfolder* are used in place, not duplicated into the root; dropdown now scans recursively (2026-06-16).** Regression from the add-flow rewrite: `_add_hdrs` only treated a file as "already there" when it sat at exactly `sourceimages/<name>`, so Copy/Move on a file in e.g. `sourceimages/hdr/` duplicated it into the root. Restored the broader "already under sourceimages (any depth)" check (`_is_under_dir`) — such a file is wired in place with no I/O for every mode. To keep that coherent (an in-place file must still appear selected, not re-trigger the earlier "nothing in the combobox" symptom), `_refresh_combo` now scans sourceimages **recursively** (`get_dir_contents(recursive=True)`), so subfolder HDRs list automatically — and the add flow leaves them where they live. Copy/Move still duplicate genuinely *external* files into the root as before. Tests: `test_copy_mode_file_in_sourceimages_subfolder_not_duplicated` (subfolder file wired in place, root stays clean) + `TestRefreshComboRecursive` (recursive flag passed; subfolder HDR lands in the combo); 35/35 under mayapy.
- **Arnold Bridge — `add` no longer crashes (and aborts) when a bridge helper is in scope (2026-06-16).** Field report "the ai bridge isn't working": `add` raised `ValueError: No object matches name: aiMultiply1` from `cmds.listConnections` (in `_get_shading_engine` ← `get_bridge`), which propagated all the way up through `_run`→`b000` and aborted the whole operation — so *nothing* got bridged. Cause: a bridge helper node (e.g. `aiMultiply1`) was in the resolved scope and got deleted mid-loop by an earlier target's force-rebuild (`remove` → `cmds.delete`); the later iteration then called `listConnections` on the vanished name. Fix: `_get_shading_engine` now returns `None` for a non-existent node, so `get_bridge`/`has_bridge`/`add` skip it cleanly ("no shading engine — skipped") and carry on bridging the real materials. Tests: `test_arnold_bridge.py` — `test_get_shading_engine_nonexistent_returns_none` (direct) + `test_add_force_with_helper_in_scope_skips_not_crashes` (end-to-end: helper deleted mid-loop is skipped, the material's bridge still rebuilds); 20/20 under mayapy with Arnold.
- **HDR Manager — added map now shows selected in the dropdown; rotation slider can't crash on a stale network (2026-06-16).** Two fixes from a field report ("map shows green as set but nothing in the combobox; setting a value throws instead of a clean early return"). (1) After a Copy/Move add the map was wired but the dropdown stayed blank: the combo stores raw `get_dir_contents` filepaths, which mix slash styles (`os.path.join` on Maya's forward-slash workspace path → `C:/proj/sourceimages\x.hdr`), while `_add_hdrs` passed an `os.path.normpath` result (all backslashes), so the exact `findData` missed. New `_select_combo_path` matches path-normalized + case-folded and selects the row (Link-mode files outside sourceimages legitimately return no match). (2) The `rotation` setter now guards `cmds.objExists(transform)` and wraps `cmds.rotate` in try/except — a stale/partly-deleted transform made `cmds.rotate` treat the angle as the object (`TypeError: Object 140.0 is invalid`); it now no-ops cleanly (debug-logged) so a slider drag never errors up into the UI. (Note: the reported traceback's line numbers proved a *stale loaded module* — a full Maya restart is needed to pick up these source edits.) Tests: `TestSelectComboPath` (mixed-slash match / no-match) + `TestRotationSetterRobust` (stale transform + rotate-failure swallowed, cmds-only, no Arnold); 33/33 under mayapy.
- **HDR Manager — one "Add HDR(s)…" dialog for loose files *and/or* a whole folder; folder-icon button removed (2026-06-16).** Per request, the separate add affordances are merged: the option-box folder-icon `browse` button is gone, and a single **Add HDR(s)…** button now sits in the option menu (▸) **above** the Copy/Move/Link mode combo. It opens one dialog (`_pick_hdr_paths` — a non-native `QFileDialog` in `ExistingFiles` mode with the internal views switched to extended selection, so `selectedFiles()` returns files, a folder, or a mix) where the user picks loose files and/or a folder; selected folders are expanded to their `.hdr`/`.exr` contents. The single-file and folder-batch import paths are unified into one `_add_hdrs(paths, *, where, careful)` core (removed `_add_hdr`, `_import_into_sourceimages`; `_add_hdrs_from_folder` is now a thin wrapper): a lone explicit file gets the **careful** UX (modal on a bad file, overwrite prompt), while a folder/multiple is a **bulk** add (skip+count, reuse existing, no per-file dialog). Note this also fixes the case where HDRs live in a `sourceimages/hdr/` **subfolder** (this user's layout): the dropdown scans the sourceimages root only (`get_dir_contents` is non-recursive), so Copy/Move now bring subfolder files up to the root where the dropdown looks, instead of the old wire-in-place that left them invisible. Tests: `test_hdr_manager.py::TestAddHdr` (cancel noop / single-file careful / single-truncated → modal / folder + multi-file bulk, no per-file modal) + existing `TestAddHdrsFromFolder` retargeted to the wrapper; 29/29 under mayapy. Non-native dialog construction verified offscreen (views switch to extended-select; a directory surfaces via `selectedFiles()`).
- **HDR Manager — popups stay digestible: filename + fix, never the raw path; batch never spams a dialog (2026-06-16).** Follow-up to the error-feedback overhaul. `_notify` gained a `dialog_text` arg: the modal now shows the short, digestible body (filename + actionable fix) while the **full raw path stays in the Script Editor** (the console `detail`), instead of dumping the whole path/paragraph into the popup. `_validate_or_warn`'s refusal popup now reads "*X isn't fully downloaded (…) — Make available offline, then retry. (Full path in the Script Editor.)*" with no raw path. The folder-batch path (`_add_hdrs_from_folder`) was already dialog-free per file — incomplete/corrupt files are skipped into a single count, never a per-file popup or per-file path enumeration. Tests updated (`TestNotify`: dialog shows the message not the raw detail; `dialog_text` overrides; `TestValidateOrWarn`: popup omits the full path); 24/24 under mayapy.
- **HDR Manager — "Add All HDRs From Folder…" batch import (2026-06-16).** A new option-box-menu action (▸, beside the Copy/Move/Link mode) opens a directory picker and imports **every** `.hdr`/`.exr` in the chosen folder at once, using the current mode: Copy/Move bring each into the `sourceimages` root so they list in the dropdown; Link wires them in place. Incomplete/corrupt files (e.g. un-synced cloud HDRs) are **skipped and counted** — no per-file dialog — already-present names are reused rather than re-copied/clobbered, and the last good one is set active (`add_hdr_folder` slot → `_add_hdrs_from_folder`). Per the request, an empty folder pick **falls back to the workspace `sourceimages` folder**. Reuses existing primitives (`ptk.get_dir_contents`, `ptk.ImgUtils.validate_image_integrity`, `_refresh_combo`, `_notify`); `src` is fetched lazily (Link skips it). Tests: `test_hdr_manager.py::TestAddHdrsFromFolder` (link-mode adds complete / skips truncated / empty-folder + all-truncated warnings); 21 pass / 9 Arnold-gated skip + cmds-name check green.
- **HDR Manager — incomplete-HDR message now names the real cause (online-only cloud file) + actionable fix (2026-06-16).** Diagnosis from the field: a user's whole HDR folder "failed as corrupt" — all were ~1.3 MB stubs of 8K Dropbox files (validator confirmed correct: 4 known-good local HDRs pass, the 3 Dropbox files truncate at ~82/4096 scanlines). They're **online-only cloud files that haven't finished syncing**, which Python can't detect (`os.stat`/`os.lstat.st_reparse_tag`/`GetFileAttributesW` all follow the cloud reparse point → report a plain local file) and can't hydrate by reading. So the fix is messaging, not detection: the refusal now reads "*X isn't fully downloaded (truncated: N/M scanlines)*" and the dialog explains it's almost always an un-synced Dropbox/OneDrive file and to right-click → **Make available offline** / **Always keep on this device**, with the truncated/corrupt re-export case as the fallback. The colour palette is also unified — feedback now uses uitk's `LOG_COLORS`-sourced footer/dialog colours (one SSoT in `pythontk.LoggingMixin.LOG_COLORS`). Tests updated (`truncated` in footer, `Make available offline` in dialog).
- **HDR Manager — error feedback overhaul: full console detail + colour-coded footer + dialogs (2026-06-16).** All panel feedback now routes through a single `HdrManagerSlots._notify(message, *, level, detail, dialog)` helper that surfaces a failure in three places at once: a **colour-coded footer** (uitk's new `Footer.setText(..., level=...)` — green success / amber warning / red error), the **Maya Script Editor** (full *detail* — path + reason — logged at the matching level via the `LoggingMixin` stream handler, not just the elided one-liner), and a modal **MessageBox** (`self.sb.message_box`, auto-coloured `Error:`/`Warning:` prefix) for failures that need the user to act before retrying. Action-required failures raise the dialog (incomplete/corrupt HDR on explicit **Set HDR**/**Add HDR**, missing sourceimages, copy/move I/O error); casual combobox selection of a bad file stays quiet (footer + console only, `dialog=False`); high-frequency status (the dropdown's "no sourceimages" / file count) is colour-only to avoid log spam. Success/selection/context-menu messages are levelled too (success/info/warning). Tests: `test_hdr_manager.py::TestNotify` (footer level set / dialog uses level prefix + carries full detail) and `TestValidateOrWarn` (truncated HDR → error footer + modal; suppressed dialog path); 18 slot/validator pass + 9 Arnold-gated skip under mayapy.
- **HDR Manager — loading an incomplete/truncated HDR no longer crashes Maya (2026-06-16).** Root cause (from two `MayaCrashLog` dumps): an `ACCESS_VIOLATION` in `OGSATILIntegration.dll!AtilImageHandler::GetIBLIntensity` ← `LoadTexture` ← `AcquireTextureFromFileNode` ← `OGSApplicationBridge::finishLightsTranslation`, on the next viewport idle refresh. Viewport 2.0 loads the skydome's file texture to compute the light's IBL intensity; when the image is **incomplete** the ATIL load returns null and the un-null-checked `AtilImage::Width` dereferences it → hard crash. The offending file was a **partially-synced Dropbox 8K `.hdr`**: its header declared 8192×4096 but only **82 of 4096 scanlines** were actually on disk (1.3 MB). This fired regardless of how the HDR was applied (it's an async viewport refresh), which is why the earlier `cmb000` timing change didn't help. Fix: `HdrManager.hdr_env` setter now **refuses** an existing-but-incomplete image (logs a clear warning, builds nothing) via the new `ptk.ImgUtils.validate_image_integrity` — a hard guard protecting every caller (slots, `create_network`, external code); a merely *missing* file is still allowed (Maya shows a checker, never reaching the IBL path). Slots add UX: `_validate_or_warn` gates the **Set HDR** button, **Add HDR** browse (incl. the `_import_into_sourceimages` "already inside sourceimages — even a subfolder" no-copy path that wired the original file in place), and `cmb000` live-swap, footering "looks incomplete or corrupt (…); re-download or re-export it" instead of crashing. The `cmb000` cold-start change (only live-swap an existing network; defer creation to **Set HDR**) is kept as hardening/UX. Tests: `test_hdr_manager.py` — `TestValidateOrWarn` (missing allowed / truncated-HDR refused) + `TestHdrSelectionDoesNotEagerLoad::test_selection_of_incomplete_image_is_skipped` + Arnold-gated `test_setter_refuses_incomplete_image`; 6 slot/validator pass + Arnold-gated skips under mayapy. **Needs live verification in the user's Arnold Maya** (the VP2.0/OGS crash path can't run headless).
- **Scene Exporter — fixed broken/overlapping panel layout under Blender's `windows11` style (2026-06-16).** `scene_exporter.ui`'s `central_widget` carried a stale explicit `minimumSize.height=240` that was *smaller* than its real content minimum (~268). Qt's `qSmartMinSize` replaces a widget's computed layout minimum with such an explicit value even when it under-reports, which dragged the window's `minimumSizeHint` below content; uitk's first-show auto-fit then snapped the window to 240 and the output group + footer overlapped the File box (the expanded output group squeezed out of view, so it read as "always collapsing"). Zeroed the bogus min height (kept the min width 600). The durable fix lives upstream in `uitk` (`MainWindow._content_min_height` floors auto-fit on the central widget's true layout minimum); this `.ui` edit just removes the bad data. No code/test change here — verified via the uitk regression `TestFitRespectsTrueContentMin` and a live load of this panel (window fits content, no overlap, collapse state persists).
- **Reference Manager — metadata column renamed "Notes" and hidden by default, with a header toggle (2026-06-16).** The per-file metadata column (index 4) is now labeled **NOTES** instead of COMMENTS (the code already called it "Notes" everywhere internally; the stored sidecar key stays `Comments` for back-compat), and the filter-target combo's matching option is now **Filter: Notes**. The column is **hidden by default** — a new **Show Notes Column** checkbox in the header menu (`chk_show_notes_column`, default unchecked) toggles it. Visibility is view-only (`setColumnHidden`): notes are still fetched and remain filterable even while the column is hidden, and the toggle persists via QSettings like the other header options; the saved state is reapplied on panel load (deferred post-init). Tests: `test_reference_manager.py::TestNotesColumnVisibility` (hidden when unchecked / shown when checked / safe-hidden when checkbox absent); 35/35 green under the mocked-Qt suite.

- **Scene Exporter — relative-path conversion no longer breaks external texture links; default output dir falls back to the scene folder (2026-06-16).** Two fixes to `env_utils/scene_exporter`: (1) `SceneExporter.perform_export` now defaults the export directory to the **open scene's folder** when none is given (exports the FBX alongside the `.ma`/`.mb`), instead of erroring; an unsaved scene with no dir still aborts with a clear message. (2) The `convert_to_relative_paths` task was silently breaking links — it rewrote an absolute external texture path to a project-relative one without ensuring the file actually lived under `sourceimages`, so the relative path pointed at a missing file. It now **copies external textures into `sourceimages` first** (only those not already there; same-name/different-size collisions are skipped with a warning rather than clobbering) via the new `MatUtils.copy_textures_to_sourceimages` (orchestrates the existing `_resolve_texture_targets` / `_paths_from_file_nodes` / `move_texture_files` primitives), then remaps. The copy is a persistent asset consolidation (intentionally survives the post-export scene restore). Tooltip + docstrings document the behavior. Tests: `test_scene_exporter.py` (scene-dir fallback ×2, external-copy-resolves, name-collision-not-clobbered, within-batch-basename-collision); scene_exporter 59/59 + mat_utils 35/35 green under mayapy.

- **Blender bridge — template-driven object send (2026-06-15).** New `env_utils/blender_bridge/` co-located subpackage modeled on the marmoset/substance bridges: a `BlenderBridge` engine (`mtk.BlenderBridge`, named after its target like `RizomUVBridge`) + a `templates/` dir of executable Blender import recipes (`import` / `import_and_frame` / `replace_scene`, each declaring `BRIDGE_MODES` + `__KEY__` placeholders) + a `parameters.py` `AttributeSpec` registry (Include Materials / Embed Textures / Apply Unit Scale / Include Animation / Triangulate / Frame in View). `send(objects, template, mode, params)` derives the FBX export options from the params, exports to a temp FBX (shader-less duplicates → `initialShadingGroup` when `INCLUDE_MATERIALS` is off, originals untouched), `render_template` substitutes the FBX path + param values, and a **fresh** Blender launches with the rendered script (`--python`, detached — never attaches to a running Blender). `BlenderBridgeSlots` (subclass of `MayaBridgeSlotsBase`) gives the dynamic template combo + per-template parameter widgets + presets; discovered by `MayaUiHandler` (`show("blender_bridge")`), exposed via the Scene **header** menu's **Blender Bridge** button. Counterpart of blendertk's new `MayaBridge`. Tests: `test_blender_bridge.py` (discovery / template render / launch-args / real strip-materials path; 9/9 under mayapy).

- **`test_group_combine` runs headless (2026-06-14).** Switched the material-assignment setup from the GUI-only `cmds.hyperShade(assign=)` (which silently no-ops under `mayapy` standalone, so the grouping tests saw one material and failed `1 != 2`) to the project's own `MatUtils.assign_mat` (shading-group `cmds.sets`/`connectAttr` — works headless and in GUI). Also cleared pre-existing lint in the file (unused `mtk`/`sys` imports, a dead `shapes` local) and the source module (E402 import-order, an F541 f-string). No production behavior change.

- **`MatUtils._cluster_objects_by_distance` delegates to pythontk (2026-06-14).** The proximity flood-fill (spatial-hash grid) that subdivides material groups in `combine_objects(cluster_by_distance=True)` was extracted to the DCC-agnostic `ptk.MathUtils.cluster_points_by_distance`; this method now only gathers each object's world bbox centre and maps the returned index-clusters back to objects. Behavior-preserving (same single-link result) — the algorithm is now shared with the Blender `btk.combine_objects` port instead of duplicated.

- **`mayapy-package-manager.bat` is now a thin wrapper (2026-06-14).** The ~280-line interactive
  package-manager UI moved to the shared, interpreter-agnostic `m3trik/package-manager.bat`; this file
  now only detects Maya, resolves `mayapy.exe`, and hands off (`call`) to it — so blendertk's new
  `blenderpy-package-manager.bat` reuses the identical menu (one SSoT, two DCC wrappers). UX is
  preserved; decorations are now ASCII (dropped `CHCP 65001`) to avoid the cmd UTF-8 box-drawing parse
  bug. `test_mayapy_package_manager.py` retargeted (menu structure → generic, wrapper → thin + handoff).

- **`MatUtils` report formatters moved upstream to `ptk.MatReport` (2026-06-13).** `format_mat_info_html`/`_text` and `format_texture_info_html`/`_text` (plus the private `_fmt_size_auto`/`_path_as_link`) now delegate to `pythontk.MatReport` — the ~150 lines of pure record→HTML/text formatting were DCC-agnostic and are now a shared SSoT (so blendertk's new material-info report reuses the identical output). Behavior-preserving (verbatim port; the record schema is unchanged); the now-unused `html`/`urllib.parse` imports were dropped. Motivation: the Blender materials slot mirrors this Maya workflow and feeds the same formatter.

- **`ui_utils.calculator` math engine moved upstream to `ptk.MathUtils` (2026-06-13).** `CalculatorController.calculate` / `convert_unit` now delegate to `ptk.MathUtils.eval_expression` / `convert_length_unit` (the safe-eval + length-conversion logic was DCC-agnostic). Behavior-preserving — `test_calculator.py` (9 tests) passes unchanged; the Maya time helpers (`get_fps_value` / `get_current_time` / frames↔sec) stay here. Motivation: blendertk's new co-located calculator panel reuses the same engine (one SSoT, two DCC panels).

- **Curtain drape engine moved upstream to `ptk.math_utils.drape` (2026-06-12).** `edit_utils.curtain` keeps only the Maya halves: `Rail(ptk.CurtainRail)` adds the selection/NURBS-curve resolvers, `CurtainMesh(ptk.CurtainDrape)` adds the `polyPlane`/`MFnMesh` build + post-ops (`build()` now calls the engine's `prepare()`); all drape math, params, and seeded features are inherited unchanged. Behavior-preserving — the full `test_curtain.py` suite (61 tests, incl. golden drape-shape checks) passes unchanged in fresh standalone Maya. Motivation: blendertk now builds curtains from the same engine (one SSoT, two DCC builds). Public imports (`CurtainMesh`, `Rail`, `catenary_shape`, `sag_profile`) unchanged.

- **`MatUtils.convert_bump_to_normal` rewritten — it never converted anything (2026-06-11)** (`_audit/texture_pipeline_audit_2026-06-11.md`): the old bump2d network was broken four ways (the "directx" branch's `reverse` node inverted ALL THREE channels; `wrap_mode` was validated then ignored; `bumpInterp=1` told bump2d the height input was already tangent-space normals; the returned file node pointed at a never-written file with a "bake manually" print). Now a thin wrapper over `ptk.MapFactory.convert_bump_to_normal` (whose orientation was fixed upstream the same day): converts the file node's texture on disk and optionally creates a wired `file`/`place2dTexture` pair with colorSpace `Raw`. Signature drops the meaningless `filter_type`/`wrap_mode`; `create_file_node=False` now returns the written image path. Test asserts the real file, neutral-normal pixels, and Raw tagging.
- **Second-pass audit fixes (2026-06-11)** (`_audit/second_pass_audit_2026-06-11.md`): `DisplayUtils.get_visible_geometry(shapes=True)` always returned `[]` — doubly broken: `cmds.ls(type="geometry")` is not a queryable type (silently `[]`) and the per-node check compared concrete `nodeType` to the abstract `"geometry"`; it now lists the concrete renderable types (`mesh`/`nurbsSurface`/`subdiv`, `noIntermediate`) directly. `EditUtils.merge_vertices(selected_only=True)` ran the selection-based merge once per shape in the objects loop — hoisted to run once (and the non-mesh skip message routes through `cmds.warning`). Dead legacy `core_utils/preview_old.py` deleted (referenced only in a docstring). The 9 bare `except:` clauses in `uv_diag`/`maya_connection`/`namespace_sandbox` are now `except Exception:` (no longer swallow KeyboardInterrupt/SystemExit). Regression tests in `test_display_utils`/`test_edit_utils`; display(15) + edit(37) green in fresh standalone Maya; cmds gate PASS.
- **Audit fix pass (2026-06-11)** (`_audit/mayatk_audit_2026-06-11.md`): removed a leftover debug `print` in `NodeUtils.get_type` that spammed the Script Editor on every call; **renamed misspelled public API** `Components.get_contigious_edges`/`get_contigious_islands` → `get_contiguous_edges`/`get_contiguous_islands` (call sites in edit_utils.bridge, xform_utils, tests, and tentacle updated); the cmds validation gate is green again (`FBXExportSplitAnimationIntoTakes`, `arnoldRenderToTexture` added to the plugin suppression list — both are real fbxmaya/mtoa commands the standalone checker can't resolve); deprecated `CoreUtils.temporarily_unlock_attributes` shim deleted (use `Attributes.temporarily_unlock`); missing `@staticmethod` added to the `selected`/`undoable`/`reparent` decorators. core_utils/components/node_utils suites green (104 tests) in fresh standalone Maya.

- **2026-06-10 `EnvUtils.export_scene_as_fbx` — fixed a 90-minute FBX-export hang on dense/faceted meshes; `FBXExportHardEdges` now defaults OFF.** Reported as "can't export `welding_prod:Mesh` (~14.9M-tri photogrammetry scan)" — it never finished. Bisected each FBX option in isolated timed `mayapy` subprocesses on a faceted (all-hard-edge) dense mesh: **`FBXExportHardEdges` ("Split per-vertex Normals") is the sole culprit** — a 2M-tri faceted mesh exceeds **200s** with it on, **~3s** with it off, regardless of `FBXExportSmoothingGroups`/`FBXExportInputConnections` (both measured free). On an all-hard mesh it splits nearly every shared vertex and the SDK's pass is pathologically super-linear (the earlier "it's free" reading was on an all-*soft* sphere with nothing to split). It's **not** the Python layer — the same wrapper exports in 3s with the option off. Hard-edge shading is already carried by the exported normals (and Autodesk flags this option "not recommended"), so the default in `export_scene_as_fbx` flips `True → False`; pass `FBXExportHardEdges=True` to force split-normal output. `export_scene_as_fbx`'s only caller is tentacle `scene.py tb003`; both DCC bridges use the separate `FbxUtils.export` path (substance already set it `False`), so no other caller changes. **Also fixed (surfaced by the new test):** the MEL `FBXExport` call fed `file_path` verbatim, so a Windows path with backslashes (e.g. from `os.path.join`) errored as MEL escape sequences — now normalized to forward slashes at the MEL boundary. Regression coverage: `test/test_env_utils.py::TestExportSceneAsFbxDefaults` (default applies HardEdges off + file written; explicit override still honored), 14/14 env_utils green under `maya.standalone`; faceted 2M-tri mesh verified exporting via defaults in 6.8s (was a hang).
- **2026-06-09 Lightmap Baker — Atlas-by-Material packing (opt-in): one shared EXR + per-object `scaleOffset` per material group.** Answers the per-object map explosion / cross-bake naming collisions / wasted texels of the lighting-only path, without regressing it: **Per-Object stays the default** (one full-resolution map each), and a new **Packing** combobox (`cmb002`) adds **Atlas by Material**. Atlas is built **bake-full-then-pack** — it reuses the per-object bake byte-for-byte, then consolidates: `LightmapBaker.pack_atlas(mapping)` groups objects by primary (dominant-face) material, area-weights each by world surface area (`polyEvaluate worldArea`), lays them out with the new `pythontk.ImgUtils.compute_atlas_layout` (shelf packer → exact-tiling `scaleOffset` rects), composites the per-object EXRs into one atlas with `ImgUtils.assemble_atlas` (correct UV→row vertical flip), and names it after the group's texture set (`<base>_Lightmap.exr`, deterministic per material → re-runs overwrite the same atlas). Every object keeps its 0-1 lightmap UVs; `commit_lightmap` gained `scale_offsets=` and stamps the rect into the per-shape `lightmapInfo` marker, so `_publish_lightmap_metadata` rides the real rect on `data_export` (the C# `LightmapRecord.scaleOffset` already binds it as the renderer's `lightmapScaleOffset`). Atlas is **Lighting-Only only** (the fused/unlit stock shader has no scaleOffset to bind — Fused ignores Packing); a single-object group is left as its own identity-rect map (no re-encode); the slot degrades to a plain per-object commit if packing can't run (e.g. no cv2), so a bake is never lost. Backward-compatible: absent `scaleOffset` (old markers, non-atlas commits) → identity `[1,1,0,0]`. Tests: `test_lightmap_baker.py::TestPackAtlas` (real-geometry grouping, area-weighted rects, single-object rename, consolidation removes per-object maps, scaleOffset rides the manifest) + slot routing (`TestLightmapBakerSlots`: atlas consolidates+commits with rects, per-object skips packing, fused ignores packing) — 37/37 green under `maya.standalone` (incl. the Arnold end-to-end). Pure primitives covered upstream in `pythontk` (`test_img.py`, +15).
- **2026-06-09 `GameShader` — output format can follow the selected workflow profile (per-map container + bit depth).** The Output combobox (`cmb003`) gained a **"Profile default"** option (appended last to preserve existing saved format indices — combobox state persists by index): when selected, `create_network` passes `output_profile` (the `cmb002` workflow key) through to `MapFactory.prepare_maps`, so each map is written in its profile's exact format from the upstream output-template engine (e.g. UE → TGA, height → 16-bit PNG) instead of one global extension. Picking a concrete format forces that container for all maps (unchanged default). No behavior change for existing programmatic `create_network` callers (the profile is only applied when set). Verified headless: new `test_output_profile_drives_per_map_format` (UE → TGA) + the full `test_game_shader.py` suite green.
- **2026-06-09 Arnold Bridge — Switchboard panel (`arnold_bridge.ui` + `ArnoldBridgeSlots`); ops are now selection-neutral.** Added a co-located UI panel for the bridge (mirrors `game_shader`/`lightmap_baker`: ships its own `.ui` + `<Tool>Slots`, auto-discovered by `MayaUiHandler`, no `DEFAULT_INCLUDE`/compiled `_ui.py`; `fmt`/uitk import guarded so the headless `ArnoldBridge` import stays clean). The panel is a thin driver over `ArnoldBridge`: a **Scope** combo (Selected Objects / All Scene Materials), a **Force** checkbox (rebuild-if-exists), **Add / Remove / Rebuild** buttons, and a header **Select Bridged Materials** action; feedback via the footer. tentacle exposes it from the materials **Tools ▸ Conversion** list (`MaterialsSlots.b026` → `marking_menu.show("arnold_bridge")`), next to Game Shader. **Bug fix found wiring the panel:** `add`/`remove` left newly-created/deleted shading nodes selected, so a follow-up action (Remove after Add) read the wrong viewport selection and silently no-op'd ("No materials in scope"). A `_selection_neutral` decorator now restores the caller's selection after each op (filtering deleted nodes), making the ops selection-neutral — the correct contract for a material tool. Covered by `test_arnold_bridge.py::ArnoldBridgeSlotsTest` (6: stubbed-sb/ui slot driver over real geometry — add/remove selected scope, empty-selection warning, all-scene scope, force-rebuild + rebuild-button by UUID, select-bridged), all green under real Arnold in `maya.standalone`; the panel itself isn't loaded headlessly (offscreen QPA can't), the slot logic is verified through stubs.
- **2026-06-09 Arnold render bridge split into its own lifecycle-managed module — `mayatk.ArnoldBridge` (add/remove/rebuild after creation, any scope).** The `aiStandardSurface` bridge logic was entangled in `GameShader`'s creation factory (`setup_arnold_nodes` + `connect_arnold_nodes`, ~230 lines), so it was **creation-only**, **texture-list-driven**, and had no remove path. Extracted to [`mat_utils/arnold_bridge.py`](mayatk/mayatk/mat_utils/arnold_bridge.py) `ArnoldBridge` with `add` / `remove` / `rebuild` / `has_bridge` / `get_bridge`, operating on given materials, given objects, the selection, or the whole scene (via `MatUtils.get_mats`). `add` is **introspection-based** — it walks each base material's connected `file` nodes (`listHistory` → `cmds.ls(type="file")`) and resolves map type from the file name via the same `ptk.MapFactory.resolve_map_type` SSoT `GameShader` uses, so a bridge can be added long after the material was built with no texture list; packed-map precedence (ORM > MSAO > Metallic_Smoothness) mirrors creation. The bridge owns **dedicated** `file` nodes (never shares the base material's) — Stingray and Arnold require conflicting `colorSpace`/`alphaIsLuminance` on the same map and read different output plugs, so a shared node can't satisfy both; dedicated nodes also make `remove` trivially correct (delete the `aiSurfaceShader` history island, with the base material's own history subtracted as a guard). `remove` is backward-compatible with bridges made by the old `create_arnold=True` path. `GameShader.create_network(create_arnold=True)` now **delegates** — it builds the base shader fully, then calls `ArnoldBridge().add(materials=shader)`; the two inline Arnold methods (and now-dead `Attributes`/`Tuple` imports) are deleted from `game_shader.py`. Whole-scene fallback (bare `add()`) skips textureless materials so it can't bridge `lambert1`/`initialShadingGroup`; explicit targeting bridges unconditionally (so a solid-color material still gets a bridge). Behavior of the wiring itself is preserved verbatim (incl. the existing `Raw` colorspace — see follow-up note below). New `test/test_arnold_bridge.py` (12: add/idempotency/force-rebuild by UUID, remove-restores-base, scope by material/object/scene, scene-fallback-skips-textureless, MSAO channel routing) — `skipUnless` mtoa, all green under real Arnold in `maya.standalone`; the 3 `test_game_shader.py` tests that called the removed methods migrated to the bridge suite, and the end-to-end `create_network(create_arnold=True)` delegation verified (base material exported via `surfaceShader`, bridge drives `aiSurfaceShader`, round-trip remove leaves the base + its file nodes intact). The Arnold network remains scene-only (no FBX representation) — only the Stingray/Standard Surface material exports.
- **2026-06-09 `GameShader` / `MaterialUpdater` — texture writes + convert-format menus honor the new `ImgUtils` capability table.** GameShader's PBR roughness/metallic saves now route through `ImgUtils.save_image` (backend-correct for EXR/HDR/DDS via the upstream capability table) instead of raw `PIL.Image.save`, so `.exr`/`.hdr` outputs — previously offered in the menus but unsupported by the raw PIL writer (it raised on save) — now actually write (via OpenCV), and `.dds` is newly available. Both GameShader's output-extension menu (`cmb003`) and MaterialUpdater's Convert-Format menu now source `ImgUtils.writable` (currently identical to the recognized set; future-proofs the menus to auto-exclude any format that is ever marked read-only). No Maya-side behavior change. GameShader save routing verified by the full `test_game_shader.py` suite (75 tests) under a headless standalone Maya.
- **2026-06-09 Lightmap Baker — name the lightmap after the material's texture set, not the (long) node name; footer progress reaches 100%.** A Bistro object named `Bistro_…_Flower_Pot_01A_2442` was producing `…_2442_Lightmap.exr`. `TextureBaker.bake` gained a `stem` resolver (`callable(long_name) -> str` or `{long_name: stem}` dict; default = leaf, unchanged for generic callers); `LightmapBaker._texture_set_stem` resolves each object's texture-set base via `MatUtils.get_texture_paths` + `ptk.MapFactory.get_base_texture_name` (the `game_shader` helper) → output follows the texture-set convention (`Plants_Metal_Base_01_Lightmap.exr`). `bake_separated` snapshots the stems **before** the white card replaces the real materials (otherwise it'd name files after the blank card). Collisions (objects sharing a material / duplicate leaf names) are disambiguated with a numeric suffix instead of silently overwriting. Progress: the footer bar now reaches 100% — `bake` emits a final `(total, total)` completion tick (the per-object ticks report 0..N-1 as each *starts*); the slot formats that tick as "Baked N objects." instead of "(N+1/N)"; swallowed `on_progress` exceptions are now logged at debug. (The separate render window during an Arnold bake is mtoa's own RTT view, not ours.) Tests: `TestBakeNaming` + `TestBakeStemEndToEnd` (texture_baker), `TestTextureSetStem` (lightmap); 11/11 + 33/33 green.
- **2026-06-09 Lightmap Baker — lighting-only is now the default (keeps the PBR maps); Unity bridge via `data_export`.** The fused/unlit path threw away albedo/normal/specular (it bakes everything into one map and assigns an unlit shader) — wrong for a general lightmap baker. The panel now exposes a **Mode** combobox (`cmb001`): **Lighting Only (keep maps)** (default) vs **Fused Unlit (single map)** (the old lowest-end case). Lighting-only runs `bake_separated` (white-card irradiance) → new `LightmapBaker.commit_lightmap`, which **changes nothing** about the object's material or UV order — the full PBR material and texture UV0 are kept, the lightmap stays a separate HDR on UV index 1 (already where `create_lightmap_uvs` puts it), to be composited `albedo × lightmap` by the engine. The wiring rides to Unity on the shared `data_export` carrier (`DataNodes.set_export_string("lightmap_metadata", …)` — a JSON manifest `{version, objects:[{name, map, uvIndex, intensity, scaleOffset}]}` regenerated from per-shape `lightmapInfo` markers, so it's additive across bakes and subtractive on revert; rides the FBX as a user property, no per-object sidecar file). New `revert_lightmap` + unified `revert` (handles fused commit and/or lighting-only marker) used by **Revert to Source** and the pre-bake clear. `_stamp_commit` refactored onto a shared `_set_string_attr`. unitytk consumes the manifest (`LightmapMetadataController.cs`). Tests: `TestCommitLightmap` (material/UVs untouched + manifest content, additive publish, unified revert), Mode-aware slot tests (default lighting-only + explicit fused). 30/30 + 7/7 green.
- **2026-06-09 Lightmap Baker UI panel (`lightmap_baker.ui` + `LightmapBakerSlots`) — single-button, non-destructive.** Added a Switchboard panel for the lightmap workflow, co-located with its logic in `light_utils/` (mirrors `hdr_manager`): [`light_utils/lightmap_baker.ui`](mayatk/mayatk/light_utils/lightmap_baker.ui) + a `LightmapBakerSlots` class in [`light_utils/lightmap_baker.py`](mayatk/mayatk/light_utils/lightmap_baker.py). The panel is deliberately one action — **Bake Lightmaps** (`b000`) does the whole no-sidecar pipeline: `revert_unlit` (so the bake samples the real material) → `bake_fused` (pack lightmap UV2, Arnold-bake, dilate) → `commit_unlit`. Quality combobox (from the shared `preset_store()`) fills the Resolution/Samples dials (source of truth at bake time); header menu has **Revert to Source** + **Open Sourceimages Folder**. The bake writes into the project's `sourceimages` (the conventional, portable home for a material-referenced texture — the fused EXR is wired into a Maya file node; falls back to `bake_fused`'s `<scene>/baked_lighting` default when there's no project). No Mode/Create-UVs/Dilate toggles, no Prepare/Restore buttons. Progress is reported through the **footer's built-in progress bar** (Esc-cancellable, `processEvents` per object) rather than a popup: `TextureBaker.bake` / `bake_fused` gained an `on_progress(done, total, name) -> bool` callback (return `False` to cancel; a UI drives a progress bar without the primitive knowing Qt), and the slot wraps the bake in `footer.progress(...)`.
  - **Naming:** `TextureBaker.bake` / `bake_fused` gained a `suffix=` arg (file = `apply_affix(leaf, prefix, suffix)`), and `commit_unlit` now names the unlit shader after the baked file (dropping the verbose `lm_unlit_`), so the shader / file-node / texture-set names line up. The panel exposes a name-affix `LineEdit` (`txt000`, default `_Lightmap`) with the shared `_affix_mode.add_affix_mode_menu` Prefix/Suffix/Auto option-box picker (same control as `game_shader` / `image_to_plane`); the slot resolves it via `resolve_affix` → so output follows the `<object>_Lightmap` texture-set convention instead of `lightmap_<verbose-object-name>`. Discovered by `MayaUiHandler` (no `DEFAULT_INCLUDE` entry, like `hdr_manager`); `fmt`/uitk import guarded so the headless `mayatk.LightmapBaker` import stays uitk-free.
  - **Approach B (lightmap is the primary UV):** the in-memory `prepare_unlit_export`/`restore_unlit_export` were replaced by scene-persistent `commit_unlit`/`revert_unlit`. `commit_unlit` makes the lightmap UV0 (texture UVs slide to UV1) + assigns an unlit `surfaceShader` driven by the fused EXR, so the object shows the baked result *and* exports correctly to Unity (stock `Unlit/Texture`, no sidecar, no export-time fix-up). It's **non-destructive**: nothing is deleted (source material/UVs preserved), and a JSON restore record (UV order, shading snapshot, created nodes) is stamped on the shape via `COMMIT_ATTR` — so `revert_unlit` works from a fresh baker / after save+reload, and re-baking auto-reverts first. `bake_separated` stays on the API as the advanced (sidecar) path but is dropped from the UI per the no-sidecar requirement. Tests: `TestUnlitExport` → `TestCommitUnlit` (persistent revert from a fresh instance, idempotent commit, revert-all, multi-material per-face) + `TestLightmapBakerSlots` (revert→bake→commit order, no-output skips commit, revert-to-source routing); full suite 23/23 green under mayapy.
- **2026-06-09 Bake reorg + rename: `BakeLighting` → `TextureBaker` (moved to mat_utils); `LightmapBaker` stays in light_utils.** The two bake classes were easy to confuse, and the primitive proved to be a *generic material/texture op* — it imports only `MatUtils`, renders an object's shaded surface to a texture and assigns it, and has zero `light_utils` coupling — so it moved to [`mat_utils/texture_baker.py`](mayatk/mayatk/mat_utils/texture_baker.py) as `TextureBaker` (its content being lighting is just what the render captures; it bakes no AOVs). The lighting *workflow* that composes it — [`light_utils/lightmap_baker.py`](mayatk/mayatk/light_utils/lightmap_baker.py) `LightmapBaker` (+ `light_utils/lightmap_presets/`) — stays in light_utils. Public surface: `mayatk.TextureBaker` (mat_utils) / `mayatk.LightmapBaker` (light_utils); old `mayatk.BakeLighting` removed (no external callers, so no deprecation alias). `DEFAULT_INCLUDE`, all imports, and tests updated (`test_bake_lighting.py` → `test_texture_baker.py`); 5/5 + 15/15 green. (`API_REGISTRY` refreshes via the bi-weekly routine.)
- **2026-06-09 NodeUtils.get_shape / get_shapes — input-flexible (transform | shape | component).** They previously accepted only a *transform*: `cmds.listRelatives(node, shapes=True)` returns nothing for a shape, so `get_shape(<shape>)` silently returned `None` — a footgun for any helper that resolves a shape from caller-supplied input (surfaced while wiring `LightmapBaker`, whose `MatUtils` shading snapshot calls `get_shape` internally and no-oped on a shape arg). Now `get_shapes` strips a component/attribute suffix (`pCube1.f[0]` → the owning node) and, when `listRelatives` finds nothing, falls back to returning the node itself if it is already a shape (still honoring `no_intermediate`). Made a `classmethod` to reuse `is_intermediate`; transform behavior is unchanged. Tests `test_node_utils.py::test_get_shape_input_flexibility`. (Also a light_utils docstring pass clarifying the deliberate layering: `BakeLighting` = reusable bake *primitive*, `LightmapBaker` = the lightmap *workflow* that composes it — not redundant.)

- **2026-06-09 LightmapBaker — separated (white-card) irradiance path (Phase 4, completes guideline #1: fused + separated).** `bake_separated(objects, …)` bakes a **lighting-only** lightmap (albedo on UV1, lighting on UV2, combined `albedo×lightmap` by Unity's lightmap system or a custom shader — the opt-in alternative to the no-sidecar fused path). Implemented as a thin **white-card wrapper around `bake_fused`**: `_apply_white_card` temporarily shades every object with one shared white Lambert (so the bake captures diffuse irradiance normalized to white albedo — Phase 0b measured this beats divide-by-albedo, which is catastrophic on dark albedo; the white card over-bounces indirect ~13%, accepted), runs the identical UV2/targeting/bake/dilation pipeline, then `_restore_white_card` always restores the original shading and deletes the temp nodes. `prefix` defaults to `"lightmap_irr_"` so it never clobbers fused output. Tests `test_lightmap_baker.py::TestSeparated` (white-card apply-to-all→restore mechanics; Arnold end-to-end produces a 3-ch irradiance EXR and restores the original material). 14/14 green.
- **2026-06-09 LightmapBaker — quality-tier presets via `pythontk.PresetStore` (Phase 4, guideline #10).** `LightmapBaker.preset_store()` exposes a shared store (built-in + writable user tiers, the same config root uitk's `PresetManager` uses) and `LightmapBaker.from_preset(name, **overrides)` constructs a baker from it. Built-ins ship as JSON in [`light_utils/lightmap_presets/`](mayatk/mayatk/light_utils/lightmap_presets/): `preview` (256/2), `quest` (1024/4), `desktop` (2048/8). A preset only stores the quality dials (`resolution`, `samples`) — everything else in `bake_fused` already derives from resolution (gutter padding, dilation width) or has a sound default, so presets stay minimal and add no `bake_fused` coupling; `overrides` win, extra keys (`description`) are ignored. The presets dir is a package (docstring-only `__init__.py`) so `packages.find` discovers it and the JSON ships in the wheel (a bare data dir would be source-only — `from_preset` would fail for pip installs). Tests `test_lightmap_baker.py::TestLightmapPresets` (tiers listed; resolution/samples applied; overrides win; unknown raises). 12/12 green.
- **2026-06-09 LightmapBaker — fused-unlit Unity export prep (Phase 3, consumption Option A: no sidecar).** `prepare_unlit_export(mapping)` / `restore_unlit_export()` set the scene up for the "no sidecar" Unity path decided after weighing the four consumption models (a custom UV2 shader or Unity's lightmap-atlas import would each be a maintained sidecar / re-implement Bakery; fused→unlit is the only truly zero-sidecar route and matches the fused MVP). Per object, reversibly: (1) promote the object's lightmap UV set to **channel 0** via `UvUtils.reorder_uv_sets` — a stock Unity *Unlit/Texture* shader samples UV0, so the FBX's UV0 must be the lightmap unwrap (the only shader-agnostic way to bind it); (2) assign an unlit `surfaceShader` driven by the fused EXR read as **raw linear HDR** (`MatUtils.create_file_node(color_space="Raw")`) so it previews flat and the FBX carries the texture reference. FBX export itself rides the existing `SceneExporter`/`FbxUtils` (not rebuilt). Restore puts UV order + shading back and deletes the created nodes. Shader/SG creation, assignment, and capture/restore go through the canonical `MatUtils` helpers (`create_mat`/`assign_mat`/`get_shading_assignments`/`apply_shading_assignments`) rather than hand-rolled `cmds.sets` — SSoT, and the data-snapshot restore rebuilds **per-face multi-material** assignments exactly (not just the primary SG). The same MatUtils path backs `bake_separated`'s white-card override. Tests `test_lightmap_baker.py::TestUnlitExport` (promote→wire→restore round-trip; multi-material per-face restore; no-lightmap mesh keeps UV order but still wires). 15/15 green.
- **2026-06-09 LightmapBaker — per-object lightmap-set targeting (Phase 2 fix, surfaced by C5M validation).** Validating `bake_fused` on the C5M production scene (176 meshes) exposed a real-content bug: a scene's lightmap set is **not named uniformly** — `create_lightmap_uvs` reuses pre-existing sets under their own names (measured on C5M: `UVChannel_2`×37, `Lightmap`×15, `lightmap`×12, `LightMap`×8), but `bake_fused` passed the single hardcoded canonical name `"lightmap"` to the baker, so every reused, differently-named set was missed and the bake silently landed on the *current* (texture) UV channel instead. Fix: `BakeLighting.bake(uv_set=)` now also accepts a `{long_object_name: set_name}` **dict** for per-object targeting, and `bake_fused` builds it by resolving each object's actual set via `UvDiagnostics.find_lightmap_uv_set` (the name it detects is fed straight back, so it is always a real set on the shape). Confirmed end-to-end: the prior "UV set 'lightmap' not found" warning is gone and reused-name meshes bake into their real set (6/6 clean 3-ch EXR, 0 NaN/Inf). C5M also confirmed all 176 sets — created and reused — already land at channel **index 1** (no reorder needed on the reuse path). Regression test `test_lightmap_baker.py::TestLightmapBakerComposition.test_targets_reused_noncanonical_set_name` (a valid untagged `UV2` is targeted by name, not `"lightmap"`); existing composition test updated for the dict signature. 6/6 + 5/5 green.
- **2026-06-09 LightmapBaker — fused-lightmap orchestrator + UV-set-targeted baking (Phase 1b-ii of the Arnold lightmap baker).** New [`light_utils/lightmap_baker.py`](mayatk/mayatk/light_utils/lightmap_baker.py) `LightmapBaker.bake_fused(objects, …)` is a thin composition (owns no bake/UV logic): ensures a packed UV2 via `UvUtils.create_lightmap_uvs`, bakes the fused beauty pass **into that set** with Arnold RTT (HDR EXR), then edge-pads island gutters and writes opaque RGB. Two supporting changes: (1) `BakeLighting.bake` gained a `uv_set=` parameter — both backends sample the *current* UV set, so it is made current per object for the bake and **restored afterward** (`_set_current_uv_set`/`_restore_uv_sets`, guaranteed via `finally`); a shape lacking the set is baked on its current set with a warning. (2) Seam dilation reuses the new upstream `pythontk.ImgUtils.dilate_image`; the coverage mask is derived from **Arnold's RTT alpha channel**, never luminance — measured on Cornell, a luminance/divide mask corrupts dark-but-valid texels (shadow contact, near-black albedo), whereas the alpha mask gave **0.00e+00 covered-region luminance drift** while filling packed-island gutters from ~54%→100% coverage (0 NaN/Inf). Alpha is dropped on write (a fused lightmap renders as opaque RGB on a stock unlit shader — no sidecar). Registered as `mayatk.LightmapBaker`. Covered by `test/test_bake_lighting.py::TestBakeUvSetTargeting` (set/restore mechanics + Arnold restore-after-bake) and `test/test_lightmap_baker.py` (dilation drops alpha / fills gutter / no-alpha no-op; composition wiring with a fake baker; Arnold end-to-end fused RGB at UV2 index 1). 10/10 green across both files under `maya.standalone`.
- **2026-06-09 UvUtils.create_lightmap_uvs — native lightmap UV2 generation (Phase 1b of the Arnold lightmap baker).** New `UvUtils.create_lightmap_uvs(objects, uv_set=, map_size=, planes=, force=, freeze_history=)` generates a packed, non-overlapping lightmap UV set **natively via `polyAutoProjection`** (no RizomUV dependency). Per mesh: an existing *valid* lightmap is reused (Phase 0a's `find_lightmap_uv_set` + new `UvDiagnostics.is_bakeable_lightmap`, which checks non-overlapping + within-0-1 via `_analyze_uv_set`) unless `force`; otherwise a set is created, packed into the unit square with gutter padding (reusing `calculate_uv_padding`), placed with the texture set at channel index 0 / lightmap at index 1 (the channel engines bind), and stamped with the `lightmapUVSet` tag so `cleanup_uv_sets` protects it and downstream tools detect it unambiguously. `freeze_history` (default **False**, preserves modeling history) optionally bakes the projection and deletes construction history for export-bound meshes. Composes existing UV helpers — no duplicate unwrap logic added. Covered by `test/test_uv_utils.py::TestLightmapUvs` (valid+tagged+index-1 set; reuse-when-valid; a generated-lightmap-survives-`cleanup_uv_sets` integration; `is_bakeable_lightmap` rejects out-of-bounds). 70/70 across uv_utils + uv_diagnostics + uv_cleanup_actions green under `maya.standalone`. (The failing TDD iteration was a *test* bug — it asserted current-set restoration after calling `is_bakeable_lightmap`, which sets the current set as a documented side effect — not a method bug; the method correctly restores the current set.)
- **2026-06-09 BakeLighting — fixed the Arnold backend, which never actually worked (two latent bugs found by the lightmap-baker Phase 0b spike).** [`bake_lighting.py`](mayatk/mayatk/light_utils/bake_lighting.py): (1) `arnold_available()` ended with `"arnoldRenderToTexture" in (cmds.listCommands() or [])`, but **`cmds.listCommands()` does not exist in Maya 2025** and that call sat *outside* the method's `try`, so it raised `AttributeError` whenever `mtoa` was loaded — i.e. every `backend="auto"`/`"arnold"` bake threw before doing anything. Now probes the command directly: `hasattr(cmds, "arnoldRenderToTexture")` (mtoa registers it on load). (2) `arnoldRenderToTexture` names its output after the mesh **shape** (`pCubeShape.exr`), but `bake()` looked for the **transform** name (`pCube.exr`), so the file was always "missing", logged a warning, and the object was dropped from the result dict. `_bake_with_arnold` now **diffs the output dir** before/after the bake to find the actually-written file (shape-name-agnostic, also robust to any RTT renaming) and returns it; `bake()` maps that to the prefixed convention. New `test/test_bake_lighting.py` (skips unless mtoa loads): `arnold_available()` returns a bool without raising, and a real cube bake returns an existing `bake_bakeCube.exr`. 2/2 green under `maya.standalone` (real Arnold render). The `convertSolidTx` fallback path is unchanged.
- **2026-06-09 UvDiagnostics — `cleanup_uv_sets` can no longer delete or demote a lightmap UV set; added lightmap detection (Phase 0a of the Arnold lightmap baker).** A lightmap pipeline needs the mesh to carry *two* UV sets — the texture `map1` (index 0) and a non-overlapping lightmap channel (index 1, the set engines bind as `uv2`) — but `cleanup_uv_sets(keep_only_primary=True)` deleted every non-primary set, so it would silently nuke the lightmap. Worse, a **latent bug** made it actively hostile: `_find_primary_uv_set` scores candidates by *fewer overlaps* and *less area outside 0–1* — which is exactly a lightmap's profile — so on a mesh that already had a clean lightmap it would pick the **lightmap as "primary"** and delete the real (tiling) texture UVs. Fixes in [`uv_diag.py`](mayatk/mayatk/core_utils/diagnostics/uv_diag.py): (1) new `UvDiagnostics.find_lightmap_uv_set(shape)` — layered detection, **tag first** (`lightmapUVSet` string attr the bake pipeline stamps) then **name** (`lightmap`/`lightmapUV`/`UV2`/`UVChannel_2`, case-insensitive); index/geometry are deliberately *not* used for auto-detect (a clean texture UV is indistinguishable from a lightmap by geometry, and treating any 2nd set as protected would defeat cleanup). (2) `cleanup_uv_sets` gains `protect=()` (explicit never-touch names) and `protect_lightmaps=True` (auto-protect a detected lightmap) — protected sets are excluded from the delete pass, the duplicate pre-pass, and from `_find_primary_uv_set` candidacy (new `exclude=` arg), so the texture set stays primary. (3) `UvSetCleanupResult` gains a `protected` field for transparency (also reported in `dry_run`). The sole production caller (the tentacle UV-cleanup button) now preserves lightmaps by default — intended. Covered by `test/test_uv_diagnostics.py::TestLightmapProtection` (7: detect-by-name, detect-by-tag, none-when-absent, cleanup-preserves-lightmap, primary-never-picks-lightmap, explicit-`protect=`, and an unprotected-junk-still-removed regression). 37/37 across `test_uv_diagnostics` + `test_uv_cleanup_actions` green under `maya.standalone`.
- **2026-06-08 Mirror — the `—` (negative axis) toggle now actually does something, and is disabled where it can't.** `EditUtils.mirror` always passes `polyMirrorFace` an explicit `pivot`, which makes its `axisDirection` flag (the only thing the axis sign fed) a **no-op** — so checking `—` changed nothing for *any* pivot (proven in mayapy: `x` and `-x` produced identical geometry across object/world/center/border). Two fixes in [`mirror.py`](mayatk/mayatk/edit_utils/mirror.py): (1) **Border pivot honors the sign** — `_resolve_pivot` mapped both `x` and `-x` to `xmax`; it now maps `+axis → max face` / `-axis → min face` (`-x → xmin`, etc.), so the sign flips which bounding-box face the mirror reflects across (geometry doubles toward `+X` vs `-X`). (2) **Center pivot symmetrizes** — reflecting the whole object across its own center just overlaps it, so the *Bounding Box (center)* pivot now routes through `EditUtils.cut_along_axis(delete=True, mirror=True)` (cut at center, keep one half, mirror across the cut plane); the sign picks which half survives. `cut_along_axis`'s sign convention is inverted vs. the panel (`x` there *deletes* the `+X` half), so the slot flips it so the UI's `+X` keeps the `+X` half. (3) **Disabled when irrelevant** — for Manip/Object/World the mirror reflects across a fixed plane (sign genuinely can't matter), so `_sync_axis_sign_enabled` (wired to the pivot combo + run at init) disables **and unchecks** `chk001` there, enabling it only for the two bounding-box pivots (`_axis_sign_relevant`). Header help updated. Preview rollback is already covered: `MirrorSlots.MUTATES_SELECTION = True` triggers the contract's preserve, whose rollback handles both the deleted-original (polySeparate) and survived-but-baked-in-place (polyCut on a historyless mesh) cases. Covered in `test/test_edit_tools_geometry.py`: `_resolve_pivot(4,'-x')=='xmin'` (was `'xmax'`), `_axis_sign_relevant` (false for 0/1/2, true for 3/4), a `test_border_pivot_sign_flips_side` geometry regression (distinct footprints for `+x`/`-x`), and `test_center_symmetrize_sign_convention` pinning the `cut_along_axis` half-selection the inversion depends on. 12/12 mirror tests green under `maya.standalone`.
- **2026-06-08 marmoset_rpc — stopped the Toolbag plugin's HTTP server from auto-starting inside Maya (spurious `[marmoset_rpc] listening on http://127.0.0.1:8765` at every launch).** The in-Toolbag plugin at [`marmoset_bridge/marmoset_rpc/plugin_src/marmoset_rpc/__init__.py`](mayatk/mayatk/mat_utils/marmoset_bridge/marmoset_rpc/plugin_src/marmoset_rpc/__init__.py) auto-started its RPC server **on import**, and tentacle's `MayaUiHandler` slot discovery (`discover_slots=True, recursive=True` over the whole `mayatk` root) imports *every* `*.py` under the package to introspect its classes — via `pythontk.FileUtils.get_classes_from_path`, which executes each module to read real class objects. That walk executed the plugin's load-time autostart, binding port 8765 with a stray daemon thread inside Maya even though nobody touched the bridge (the temp loader name is scrubbed from `sys.modules`, so the bound socket was the only trace). Reproduced exactly by replaying the recursive scan: the trigger file is the plugin's `__init__.py`. Fix encapsulates the start *decision* in `server.autostart()`, gated on `_hosted_by_toolbag()` (the `mset` module only exists inside Toolbag's interpreter) **and** the existing `MARMOSET_RPC_AUTOSTART` opt-out; `__init__.py` is now a thin `autostart()` call, so importing the file is side-effect-free everywhere except its actual host — restoring the repo's "no side effects on import" standard for this entry point. Toolbag still auto-starts the server on plugin load exactly as before. Covered by `test/mock_tests/test_marmoset_rpc.py::TestAutostartHostGate` (no-op without `mset`; binds when a fake `mset` is present; `MARMOSET_RPC_AUTOSTART=0` forces off even inside Toolbag) — 46/46 marmoset_rpc mock suite green. (A currently-running Maya launched before the fix keeps its stray 8765 server until restarted; new launches are clean.)
- **2026-06-08 Scene Exporter — fixed the "Export Scene Data Node" carrier silently not shipping in *Selected* export mode (the very mode it was added for).** The `export_data_node` task appends the hidden `data_export` carrier to `self.objects`, but `perform_export` only re-selects from `self.objects` when `export_visible` is true (the `visible`/`all` modes) — in `selected` mode it exports the *live* selection and never re-selects, so the appended carrier never reached the FBX. Proven in mayapy by a real selected-mode export + re-import: `carrier in self.objects: True` but `data_export PRESENT in exported FBX: False`. Fix in [`_scene_exporter.py`](mayatk/mayatk/env_utils/scene_exporter/_scene_exporter.py): the `selected` branch now folds any task-pipeline-added nodes not already selected (e.g. the carrier) into the export selection via `cmds.select(extras, add=True)` — keeping the user's selection authoritative while honoring task additions, and a no-op in `visible`/`all` (which replace the selection from `self.objects`, already containing the carrier). This makes the option's "ships regardless of export mode" guarantee actually hold. Regression `test_scene_exporter.py::TestExportDataNodeOption::test_carrier_ships_in_selected_mode_real_export` does a real selected-mode export and re-imports to assert the carrier traveled (fails on the old build). 54/54 scene-exporter + 104/104 shots/audio/data-node/preparers green.
- **2026-06-08 Shots/Audio → FBX robustness pass: locked the Unity clip-name join contract, added post-export visibility, DRY'd the shots preparer.** Three phased follow-ups to a whole-system critique. (1) **Name-join contract locked.** The scheme hinges on take name == imported `AnimationClip.name` == `shot_metadata` `clip` key; verified `StrUtils.sanitize` reduces names to strictly `[A-Za-z0-9_]` and that Unity preserves those *verbatim* on FBX import — leading digit (`01_Start`), embedded punctuation (`Fade_In`), space→`_` (`Wipe_Out`), and collision suffix (`Wipe_Out_1`) — via new Maya-level guards in `test_shot_export_view.py` and a real-Unity round-trip `unitytk/test/test_clip_name_contract_integration.py`. Two findings the probe surfaced: every shots FBX also carries a default `Take 001` clip (benign — no record joins it), and Unity silently drops a take whose frame range contains no animation (mitigated by the default `smart_bake`; only bites if baking is disabled *and* a shot spans a dead range). (2) **Post-export visibility.** The Scene Exporter's `export_data_node` now logs what actually shipped (`Embedded on data_export: 2 shot(s), 4 audio event(s).`) so a silently-empty metadata export is distinguishable from a populated one — `test_scene_exporter.py` covers the shot/audio counts. (3) **DRY.** The shots pre-export refresh is now one canonical `ShotStore.refresh_export_view()` (no-arg, no-op without shots — leaves no empty carrier), shared by `enable_auto_export` and the Scene Exporter instead of two inline copies. 80/80 shots + preparers + exporter green. (Deliberately *not* done: a single global producer registry to fully merge the session-hook and Scene-Exporter refresh paths — the registration-timing cost outweighs the gain, and the Scene Exporter is the correct composition root for wiring concrete producers; render-opacity's `prepare_for_export` is left unwired — it's a per-object safety net for hand-authored opacity already covered by `key_fade`/behaviors, and a whole-scene visibility scan on every export isn't warranted.)
- **2026-06-08 Scene Exporter — "Export Scene Data Node" global option (default ON) so embedded metadata ships regardless of export mode.** The shared `data_export` carrier is a *hidden* node, so the exporter's `Visible`/`Selected` modes silently omitted it — shot/audio metadata wouldn't reach the FBX unless the shots-specific "Export Shots as Animation Takes" task was enabled, and that task early-returns when no shots are defined, so **audio-only scenes never carried their manifest**. New generic, default-on `export_data_node` task (General section) refreshes the carrier from every live producer — Shots `publish_export_view` + Audio `prepare_for_export`, each guarded and a no-op when there's nothing to write (a metadata-free scene leaves no node behind) — then appends `data_export` to the export set. Subsystem-agnostic, so an audio-only scene now ships its `audio_manifest`; runs right before `apply_declared_takes` (which stays the opt-in animation-takes feature). Covered by `test/test_scene_exporter.py::TestExportDataNodeOption` — default-on, runs-before-takes, includes+publishes with shots, includes+bakes with **audio and no shots**, and a clean no-op without metadata; 67/67 scene-exporter + shots green.
- **2026-06-07 Audio + Shots unified on one composable before-export hook — any-export, side-by-side verified in real Unity.** Both subsystems already shared the `data_export` carrier (Audio via *proxy* attrs mirrored from `data_internal` — `audio_manifest` + keyed `audio_clip_*`; Shots via *plain* `fbx_takes`/`shot_metadata` strings), but only Shots had an auto-export hook — `AudioClips.prepare_for_export` (and `RenderOpacity.prepare_for_export`) had **no production caller**, so the audio manifest only rode out if something explicitly baked it. Generalized the single-`refresh` shots hook into a shared **export-preparer registry** on `FbxUtils` so any number of subsystems compose through one `kBeforeExport`/`kAfterExport` pair: `FbxUtils.register_export_preparer(name, fn)` / `unregister_export_preparer(name)` (each preparer stamps its data onto `data_export`, then declared takes are realized; preparers run in registration order, each fault-isolated so one failure can't abort the export). Hook lifecycle is **reference-counted** off the registry — installed on the first preparer (or explicit `enable_auto_takes()`), torn down when the last is gone. `enable_auto_takes` lost its `refresh=` param (superseded by the registry; no external caller used it). New opt-in conveniences: `ShotStore.enable_auto_export()` / `disable_auto_export()` (refactored onto the registry) and `AudioClips.enable_auto_export()` / `disable_auto_export()` — one call each makes the manifest / shot view ride into **any** FBX export (File ▸ Export, Game Exporter, scripts), republished fresh with no staleness window, composing cleanly (distinct attrs on the one carrier). Also pruned the dead `behaviors` field from `shot_metadata` (never populated — behaviors are object-level — and never read). Covered by `test/test_fbx_export_preparers.py` (7: registry compose/order, ref-counted teardown, fault isolation, explicit-enable independence, and a real **Audio + Shots** composition exporting one ASCII FBX carrying `audio_manifest` + `shot_metadata` + `fbx_takes`) — 100/100 across shots/audio/data-nodes green. End-to-end in **real licensed Unity** (unitytk): `test_shot_metadata_integration`, `test_audio_events_integration`, and the new `test_shots_audio_sidebyside_integration` (one FBX → **both** `ShotMetadataController` and `AudioEventController` auto-attach to the prefab root from the single `data_export` GameObject; clips join by name) all pass — confirming the C# compiles and the two systems coexist through one import.
- **2026-06-07 Shots → FBX: animation takes + embedded metadata for Unity (export-agnostic, no new modules).** Each shot in the Shots system can now export as its own named Unity `AnimationClip` via FBX takes, with the rest of the shot data (description, objects, section) embedded *inside* the FBX as a JSON blob on the shared `data_export` node — readable engine-side, no sidecar files. Centralized on the existing `DataNodes` system; everything is additive (new methods/attrs, both off by default — nothing regresses when unused). New surface: `ShotStore.to_export_view()` does a **single** clip-name resolution feeding both the `fbx_takes` and `shot_metadata` channels, so the take name and the metadata `clip` join-key can't drift (default name-only naming; `"sequence"` NN_ strategy available via `clip_name_strategy`); `ShotStore.publish_export_view()` projects both channels onto `data_export` as plain string attrs (confirmed to ride into the FBX as user properties); `DataNodes.set_/get_export_string` + `FBX_TAKES`/`SHOT_METADATA` channel constants; `FbxUtils.apply_takes` / `apply_takes_from_node` / `reset_takes` (generic — enables bake-complex, sets the **union** bake range, clears prior takes, defines one AnimStack per take; shot-agnostic, realizes whatever takes the scene declares).
