Scene Exporter gains the opt-in task **"Export Shots as Animation Takes"** (publishes-then-applies and includes `data_export`; default off). `ShotStore.auto_publish_export` (opt-in) republishes the channels on every save so metadata rides along with any FBX export. **Universal takes (opt-in):** `FbxUtils.enable_auto_takes()` registers a `kBeforeExport`/`kAfterExport` pair (via `ScriptJobManager`, same path as the existing `kBeforeSave` hook) that realizes the declared takes on *any* FBX export — File ▸ Export, Game Exporter, scripts — then clears state so nothing leaks; `disable_auto_takes()` / `is_auto_takes_enabled()` round it out. `enable_auto_takes(refresh=...)` takes an optional pre-apply callback (DI) so a producer can regenerate the channels from its live state before each export; `ShotStore.enable_auto_export()` / `disable_auto_export()` wire that to `publish_export_view` — one call makes **every** FBX export carry the active store's current shots with **no staleness window** (republished fresh at export time), staying opt-in so unrelated sub-asset exports aren't forced to take it. Contract + Unity `AssetPostprocessor` sketch in [`docs/shot_export_unity.md`](docs/shot_export_unity.md). Covered by `test/test_shot_export_view.py` — 16/16 green (clip naming / sequence / deterministic de-dupe / minimal-overlap / leaf-object logic, a fresh-republish test that mutates the store *after* enabling auto-export and asserts the late shot still reaches the FBX (the staleness fix), a Maya ASCII-FBX round-trip asserting one AnimStack per shot and that the metadata attr name + value survive export, the exporter task wiring publish→include-node→apply, the auto-takes hook applying on a plain `cmds.file` export then clearing, and a full **shot-manifest-CSV → ShotStore → exporter task → ASCII-FBX** pipeline test confirming shot names become AnimStacks and the CSV-sourced description/section land in the embedded `shot_metadata`). Both linchpins — the `FBXExportSplitAnimationIntoTakes` MEL API + string-attr export round-trip, and that `kBeforeExport` fires for FBX and the plugin honors callback-set takes — were verified in mayapy *before* coding.
- **2026-06-07 Cylinder UV unwrap — sews pre-existing UV cuts shut before seaming (default).** `cut_cylinder_seams` / `unwrap_cylinder` cut the auto-seam straight onto whatever UVs the mesh already had, so stray borders from an earlier projection / manual edit survived into the result (e.g. a mesh carrying a 6-shell `polyAutoProjection` came out with 6+ shells instead of the intended 3). They now `polyMapSew` all edges first, so the result's shells come **only** from this operation's seams. New `sew=True` parameter on `_seam_cut_one` / `cut_cylinder_seams` / `unwrap_cylinder` (pass `sew=False` to cut onto the existing UVs). Covered by `test/test_uv_utils.py::TestUvCylinderUnwrap.test_sew_clears_preexisting_uv_cuts` (a cylinder pre-seeded with a messy auto-projection unwraps to 3 clean shells with the default, but keeps the stray shells with `sew=False`). 28/28 uv_utils green.
- **2026-06-07 Cylinder UV unwrap — one bad mesh no longer aborts the whole selection.** `unwrap_cylinder(unfold=True)` unfolded **all** meshes in a single batched `u3dUnfold` call inside one try/except, so a mesh u3dUnfold rejects (`// Error: Mesh has non-manifold UVs. Clean up the mesh before using unfold.`) skipped the unfold for *every* selected mesh. Now each mesh is seeded → unfolded → packed → unflipped **independently**, wrapped per-mesh: a rejected mesh logs `unwrap_cylinder: unfold skipped for <mesh> (<reason>)` and is left seam-cut with its seed projection, while the rest unfold normally. (Per-mesh u3dUnfold is the same result as the batch — shells are independent — and the layout was already per-mesh.) Covered by `test/test_uv_utils.py::TestUvCylinderUnwrap.test_multi_mesh_skips_non_manifold_keeps_good` (two good cylinders flanking a welded-cube non-manifold mesh: no exception, both cylinders unfold to clean shells). 27/27 uv_utils green.
- **2026-06-07 Cylinder UV unwrap — ~6-11x faster pack.** `unwrap_cylinder(unfold=True)` spent ~90% of its time in `u3dLayout`, whose cost is roughly quadratic in its `resolution` (~1.2 s at 4096, ~4.7 s at 8192) — and it was being passed the full `map_size`. But the pack is **pixel-identical from ~256 up** (verified: same shells / 0 degenerate / 0 flipped / same fill on the reference scene) because `shellSpacing` is already normalized, so `resolution` only sets pack precision, not the gap. Capped it at `min(map_size, 1024)`: a turned column now unwraps in ~0.4 s instead of ~2.5 s (map 4096) / ~5 s (map 8192). `u3dUnfold`'s `mapsize` has no such cost and is left at `map_size`. No behavior change (26/26 uv_utils green).
- **2026-06-07 Cylinder UV unwrap — made `unwrap_cylinder(unfold=True)` clean on low-poly tubes and stopped the layout collapsing / mirroring shells.** Testing the unfold path against a second reference mesh (an 8-sided turned vase, single-row bands) surfaced four real defects the earlier 12-sided validation hid; all fixed and the result now matches the hand-authored target exactly (9 shells, 0 degenerate, 0 mirrored, packed in 0-1). (1) **Faceting read as creases** — on a low-poly tube the polygon facet dihedral can meet the crease threshold (an 8-sided tube facets at exactly 45°), so `get_auto_seam_edges` cut every vertical facet edge and shattered the tube. It now classifies **axial-ness first**: an edge running lengthwise (parallel to the revolution axis) is faceting and feeds the single lengthwise column; only a **circumferential (ring) edge** with a sharp dihedral is a genuine step / cap crease. (2) **Band collapse** — the planar `_seed_shell_uvs` folded a tubular band's front and back onto each other (a single-row ring degenerated entirely), which `u3dUnfold` then collapsed. The seed is now **axis-aware**: a band that wraps the axis (radial normals) gets a **cylindrical** projection about the axis (an unrolled, non-folded strip); a cap / flat step keeps the thinnest-axis **planar** projection. (3) **Packer collapse** — `polyLayoutUV` collapses these cylindrically-seeded shells to points, so packing switched to **`u3dLayout`** (the proven `tb000` packer) followed by `polyNormalizeUV(normalizeType=1, preserveAspectRatio=True)` to fit the area-scaled pack back into 0-1. (4) **Packer mirroring** — `u3dLayout` mirrors some shells to pack tighter, leaving the texture mirrored; new `UvUtils._unflip_reversed_shells` flips any reversed (negative-winding) shell back about its own UV center, in place (packing and 0-1 fit preserved). Diagnosed by isolating each pipeline stage (faceting → shells; seed → fold; layout → collapse; pack → mirror) on the reference scene. `cut_cylinder_seams` (seam-only) and the `get_cylinder_seam_edges` loop seamer are unchanged. Covered by two new `test/test_uv_utils.py::TestUvCylinderUnwrap` regressions on an 8-sided cylinder — `test_low_poly_cylinder_seam_ignores_faceting` (faceting not cut → body + 2 caps, not shattered) and `test_low_poly_unfold_clean_shells` (non-degenerate, non-mirrored, in 0-1) — plus a robust `pluginInfo`-based Unfold3D skip guard (the old `if not loadPlugin(...)` skipped whenever the plugin was already loaded). 26/26 uv_utils green in Maya 2025.
- **Cylinder UV unwrap — fixed `unwrap_cylinder(unfold=True)` collapsing every shell to a point, and packed the result.** The seam cutting was correct (right shells), but `u3dUnfold` **collapsed each shell to a zero-area point** whenever the source mesh carried a degenerate, axis-aligned planar UV projection (the common case — e.g. a tube planar-projected along its axis, where each lengthwise band projects to a *line*). `u3dUnfold` can't recover from a zero-area seed. Fix: a new `UvUtils._seed_shell_uvs` planar-projects **each UV shell from the world axis of its thinnest 3D extent** (discarding the flat dimension → guaranteed non-zero-area seed) right before the unfold, so every section flattens instead of collapsing. After unfolding, the shells are **packed into the 0-1 square** (`polyLayoutUV layout=2`, `rotateForBestFit` aligning to U/V when `orient`), so the tool now emits a clean, non-overlapping, in-bounds layout (verified against a real reference scene: 9 shells, 0 degenerate, laid out at least as cleanly as the hand-authored target). The UV pipeline stays as construction history (a consistent chain — `u3dUnfold` itself emits a `polyTweakUV` node — that re-evaluates correctly), so the caller's modeling / deformer history is left intact rather than baked away. Diagnosed by isolating the pipeline step-by-step (seam cut → shells correct but bands already zero-height from the projection → `u3dUnfold` → all 9 collapsed). `cut_cylinder_seams` (seam-only, no unfold) is unchanged. Covered by `test/test_uv_utils.py::TestUvCylinderUnwrap.test_unwrap_unfold_does_not_collapse` (deliberately seeds a degenerate `md="y"` projection, then asserts every shell has non-zero UV area and is packed into 0-1). 24/24 uv_utils + 24/24 curve_to_tube green in Maya 2025.
- **Cylinder UV unwrap — rewritten as a single hard-crease + axial-column auto-cut (`get_auto_seam_edges`).** Replaces the earlier `segment_angle` experiment (removed): one clean algorithm now peels a turned / stepped cylinder into per-section UV shells, validated against a real reference scene (a 12-sided turned baluster: produces the same 9 shells / 102 seam edges as the hand-authored target). New `UvUtils.get_auto_seam_edges(mesh, angle=45, invert_seam=False)` returns two complementary cuts: (1) **hard creases** — every edge whose two faces meet at ≥ `angle`° (cap rims + ~90° step rings), so each smooth section / flat step / cap separates while shallow chamfers stay merged with their neighbour; (2) **one lengthwise column** — a single column of axial edges at one angular position about the tube axis, which opens every tubular section into a flat strip (flat steps & caps are already planar, so they keep their shape and need no opening cut). The tube axis is the odd-one-out eigenvector of the vertex covariance (`_revolution_axis`, robust whether taller-than-wide or wider-than-tall); 3D boundary edges (an open tube's rims) are already UV borders and left uncut; the column is chosen by `_pick_axial_column` (adaptive angular window from the inter-column gap, `invert_seam` lands it on the far side). `cut_cylinder_seams` / `unwrap_cylinder` now take `angle` (was `segment_angle`) and route through it; `unwrap_cylinder` is the single "auto unwrap cylinder" entry point. `get_cylinder_seam_edges` (lengthwise-loop + caps) is **reverted to its prior form and retained as the lower-level seamer for Curve to Tube's smooth swept tubes** (curved tubes break the straight-axis assumption, so they keep the loop seamer — its 24 tests stay green). Covered by `test/test_uv_utils.py::TestUvCylinderUnwrap` — `test_auto_seam_smooth_cylinder_three_shells`, `test_auto_seam_stepped_cylinder_five_shells` (2 caps + 2 bands + flat step annulus, Euler stays 2), `test_angle_threshold_controls_creases` (a 120° threshold treats the 90° steps as soft), `test_auto_seam_invert_opposite_column`. 23/23 uv_utils + 24/24 curve_to_tube green in Maya 2025.
- **Bevel — fixed the preview/undo system (lost material, re-beveled different edges, stacked on every value change) and the launch crash.** Two issues, both only visible once the panel actually opened: (1) **Launch crash** — `BevelSlots.__init__` registered the Width/Segments spinboxes as deferred widgets (`connect_multi`) *before* wrapping them in option-box reset buttons (`add_reset_buttons`); the wrap reparents each spinbox and invalidates its Python wrapper, so `_process_deferred_widgets` crashed with `Internal C++ object (QSpinBox) already deleted` and the marking-menu panel never opened. `add_reset_buttons` now runs first (and the same reorder was rolled out to every sibling preview tool — Mirror, Cut On Axis, Bridge, Duplicate Linear/Radial, Curtain, Curve to Tube — that had the latent ordering; the underlying switchboard was *also* hardened to self-heal a reparented deferred widget — see uitk CHANGELOG). (2) **Broken rollback** — `BevelSlots` didn't declare `PRESERVE_GEOMETRY`, so `polyBevel3` (in-place, with construction history) on a **historyless** mesh (frozen/imported/combined) had its auto-created orig-shape deleted by the preview's node-diff rollback, **baking the bevel in**: each value change stacked another bevel, the baked topology renumbered the edges so the captured `e[0]` then pointed at a *different* physical edge, and the shape swap dropped the material. `BevelSlots.PRESERVE_GEOMETRY = True` (mirrors Bridge / Cut On Axis) makes Preview snapshot the owning mesh and restore it (topology + edge numbering + material) on rollback, so every refresh re-bevels the same captured edges from a pristine mesh. Covered by `test/test_edit_tools_geometry.py::TestBevelPreviewRollback` (declares the opt-in; refresh on a historyless cube matches a clean single bevel and disable restores counts, `e[0]` position, and material — the position check catches the renumbered-edge symptom that symmetric face counts miss).
- **Preview — fixed multi-material meshes losing their shading through the hermetic preview (the whole object rendered bright green).** Single-material (object-level) shading survived the geometry snapshot/restore, but **per-face / multi-material** assignments did not: the rollback's node-diff deletion of the op's `groupId` nodes (and the `delete(ch=True)` in the in-place geometry restore) strip the component shading. **Investigated whether this could be fixed "at the source" by having the rollback's in-place geometry restore also copy the per-face shading from the duplicate `PRESERVE_GEOMETRY` already holds — it cannot.** Empirically (multi-cycle real-Preview repros under `maya.standalone`): reasserting per-face shading onto a *bare in-place-rebuilt* mesh (right after `outMesh→inMesh` + `delete(ch=True)`) leaves the shape in BOTH a whole-object set and component sets at once, an overlap Maya silently tolerates until the **next poly op collapses it to the whole-object material and drops every other one**. Per-face shading only sticks when reasserted on a **freshly-built** mesh. So `Preview` snapshots each operated mesh's shading **as data, once, at enable** (`MatUtils.get_shading_assignments` → `{shading_group: None | [face_indices]}`, keyed by transform UUID) and reasserts it (`apply_shading_assignments`) **after each clean op** — after the forward preview build, after the **commit** replay (inside the undo chunk), and after the **disable** rollback. `apply_shading_assignments` was hardened to drive multi-material assignments **entirely through face components** (a clean-slate `initialShadingGroup` pass, then each material as components, then the dominant material base-coats any faces the snapshot doesn't cover — e.g. a bevel's new chamfer faces): a whole-object base coat is exactly what created the whole+component overlap above, so it's avoided. Single-material snapshots are still applied as one clean whole-object assignment. This lives in the shared `core_utils/preview.py`, so it fixes Bevel, Bridge, and Cut On Axis alike. New `MatUtils.get_shading_assignments` / `apply_shading_assignments` primitives in `mat_utils`. The snapshot is only taken for meshes that actually carry per-face material (object-level shading survives the preview natively), so single-material tools pay nothing. Covered by `TestBevelPreviewRollback::test_preview_preserves_multi_material_through_commit` (matA whole-object + matB on one face survive enable→refresh→commit with zero green faces, asserted live after enable and after the value-change refresh too) and `TestMatUtils` round-trip / base-coat cases; geometry 38/38, mat_utils 35/35, preview 28/28, bridge 9/9, curtain 61/61, curve_to_tube 24/24, duplicate 45/45 green under `maya.standalone`.
- **Duplicate Grid — fixed instance mode mutating the source's shape (switching Instance↔Unique in preview deleted the object, leaving a husk of empty transforms).** Instance mode built the prototype by instancing the *source itself* (`cmds.duplicate(obj, instanceLeaf=True)`), so the copies shared the source's own shape; reparenting those instances during replication then made Maya **renumber that shared shape** (`pCubeShape` → `pCubeShape4`). That both mutated the user's node (violating `duplicate_grid`'s documented no-mutation contract) and corrupted the hermetic preview's path-based rollback: the renamed source-shape path landed in the contract's `created` set, defeating the `_shares_surviving_instance` guard, so a mid-preview refresh (e.g. the combobox switching modes) could delete the shared shape — wiping the source and leaving the grid as empty transforms. Fix: the prototype is now **always a real copy** (never `instanceLeaf`); instance mode shares one **fresh** shape across the *copies* via the instanced replication, leaving the source pristine (`mode="instance"` copies still share a single shape — just not the source's). Regression test `test_instance_mode_leaves_source_shape_pristine` (source shape same node/name, copies share a shape that is not the source's) fails on the old build and passes now; full grid suite 25/25 green under `maya.standalone`. (`DuplicateGridSlots.MUTATES_SELECTION` stays as a cheap defensive backstop — the source is no longer touched, so rollback's node-diff already restores cleanly, but it guards the headlessly-untestable interactive path.)
- **HDR Manager — exposed more skydome knobs: Resolution in the main panel, plus a collapsible *Advanced Options* group.** `Resolution` (importance-sampling res — the biggest lever for clean light/shadows from a sun baked into the HDR) joins Intensity/Exposure in the always-visible *HDR Levels* section. A new `CollapsableGroup` *Advanced Options* (collapsed by default, state persisted by name) holds `Samples` (`aiSamples`, IBL noise) and `Diffuse`/`Specular` (`aiDiffuse`/`aiSpecular` contribution scales — e.g. kill blown-out HDR reflections while keeping diffuse fill). `HdrManager` gained `resolution`/`samples`/`diffuse`/`specular` properties and `create_network` forwards them; the skydome's scalar knobs (`intensity`/`exposure`/`resolution`/`samples`/`diffuse`/`specular`) now all route through shared `_get_light_attr`/`_set_light_attr` helpers (which `intensity`/`exposure` were refactored onto) that fall back to the Arnold default + no-op the write when an attribute is absent, keeping the manager robust across mtoa versions. Verified with 3 new regression tests (round-trip to the real attrs, defaults without a skydome, `create_network` forwarding) — full suite 8/8 with Arnold loaded. Also fixed a load-order crash the bigger `.ui` exposed: `HdrManagerSlots.__init__` populated the combobox / synced widgets inline, but the switchboard constructs the slots instance *mid-load* (before `register_children` wires child widgets onto `self.ui`), so `self.ui.footer` was `None` → `AttributeError`. The initial `_refresh_combo`/`_sync_ui_to_scene`/plugin-gate now run from a deferred `_initialize_ui` via `QTimer.singleShot(0)`, after the UI is fully registered.
- **Combine — optimized for large selections (the tentacle Polygons *Combine* button hung/ran forever on big counts).** Three layers scaled badly with the selection size, each fixed without changing results:
  - **No viewport suspension.** `EditUtils.combine_objects` now wraps its heavy work in a new `CoreUtils.suspended_refresh()` context manager (`cmds.refresh(suspend=True/False)`, no-op in batch). In interactive Maya the per-command idle redraws were the dominant cost and the reason it appeared to hang only live — the highest-leverage fix.
  - **O(N) command bursts in the reparent guard.** The `@CoreUtils.reparent` decorator's `_prepare_reparent` issued one `cmds.listRelatives(parent)` per object; it now does a single batched parent query and **fast-returns when every node is world-rooted** (the common case → no group can be orphaned, so no temp-null is needed). The mixed/parented path is unchanged, so the orphan-group handling (`test_combine_preserves_parent_group`) still holds.
  - **~5 cmds calls per object for material grouping + an O(N²) clustering scan.** `group_objects_by_material` previously called `get_mats([obj])` per object; it now resolves the whole scene's shading-engine membership once via a new `MatUtils._materials_by_object` (one pass, a handful of calls). `_cluster_objects_by_distance` was a naive O(N²) pairwise flood-fill; it now buckets objects into a spatial hash grid sized to the threshold and only compares the 27-cell neighbourhood — **provably identical** clusters (cell == threshold guarantees any in-range pair shares or neighbours a cell), but ~O(N) when the selection actually spreads out.
  - Covered by `test/test_group_combine.py::test_materials_by_object_matches_get_mats` (batched map == per-object `get_mats`, grouping still splits by material) plus the existing combine/cluster/reparent cases; `test_group_combine` (7) and `test_mat_utils` (31) green under Maya 2025.
- **Duplicate Grid — collapsed the Instance + Group checkboxes into a single *Output* mode combobox, and named results after the source.** `duplicate_grid`'s `instance`/`group` booleans are replaced by one `mode` arg (`DuplicateGrid.MODES = ("combine", "instance", "copy")`): **Combine** `polyUnite`s every copy into one mesh, **Instance** makes instanced copies (shared shape), **Copy** makes independent copies. Instance/Copy are *always* grouped (the old loose-copies `group=False` path is gone) and every mode names its output after the source object (`objects[0]`) via `Naming.generate_unique_name` — combined mesh as `{src}_grid`, the group as `{src}_grid_grp` — rather than the fixed `grid_duplicated_group`. The panel's `chk001`/`chk002` checkboxes became a uitk `ComboBox` (`cmb000`, populated in `DuplicateGridSlots.__init__`, default Instance); `perform_operation` reads `cmb000.currentData()`. `.ui` updated and `_ui.py` regenerated with `pyside6-uic` to match the sibling panels; the preview/rollback safety is unchanged (Preview tracks new nodes by scene-diff, not the return shape). **Launch-crash fix:** `add_reset_buttons` now runs *before* `connect_multi` in `__init__` — wrapping a spinbox in its option box reparents it and invalidates the widget's Python wrapper, so registering the spinboxes as deferred widgets first (via `connect_multi`) left the slot pipeline holding dead wrappers and `_process_deferred_widgets` crashed with `Internal C++ object (QSpinBox) already deleted` (the panel wouldn't open). Wrapping first means the deferred pass captures the fresh, post-wrap wrappers. Combine also handles a lone copy (e.g. a 1×1×1 grid) by skipping `polyUnite` — which rejects a single input — and just renaming it. Covered by `test/test_edit_tools_duplicate.py` — `TestDuplicateGrid` rewritten to the `mode` API plus new `test_combine_returns_single_named_mesh` / `test_combine_single_object_does_not_crash` / `test_result_named_after_source` / `test_instance_mode_shares_one_shape` / `test_copy_mode_makes_unique_geometry` / `test_invalid_mode_raises`, and `TestDuplicateGridPreviewRollback` now exercises all three modes (source + shape survive enable→disable→refresh, scene left clean). 22 grid tests green under `maya.standalone`.
- **HDR Manager — fixed slow panel open and folded the Add-HDR controls onto the dropdown's option box.** Opening the panel no longer force-loads Arnold: `HdrManagerSlots.__init__` used to call `arnold_available()`, which runs `loadPlugin("mtoa")` and boots the whole renderer (seconds) just to set a footer string. Added `HdrManager.arnold_loaded()` — a cheap, side-effect-free `pluginInfo(..., loaded=True)` query used for UI gating; the real `loadPlugin` now happens lazily on the first mutating action (Set HDR / Add HDR / selection change). The `hdr_env` property also short-circuits to `None` when `mtoa` isn't loaded, so UI syncs skip the `ls` (and its "Unknown object type" warning). UI cleanup: the standalone `b001` "…" button was removed and its two affordances moved onto `cmb000` (already a uitk `ComboBox`) as option-box icon-button plugins — a `browse` button (folder icon, the Add-HDR dir-dialog launcher, wired to the new `_add_hdr` callback) and the `option_box` menu button (the Copy/Move/Link import-mode picker). `_add_mode` now reads `cmb000.option_box.menu.cmb_add_mode`; the dead `_browse_for_hdr` helper was dropped. Verified headlessly under `maya.standalone`: `arnold_loaded()` returns a bool without changing the plugin load state, and the module imports + empty-scene reads are clean (no warnings).
- **Per-field *reset-to-default* buttons rolled out across the Tier-1 preview tools; Curtain refactored onto the shared uitk helper.** Each of **Bevel, Duplicate Linear, Duplicate Radial, Duplicate Grid, Bridge, Cut On Axis, and Curve to Tube** now gives every parameter spinbox a small option-box reset button (plain click → field's default; **Alt/Ctrl+click** → bypass to default, greyed, restorable on the next click — non-persistent per session), and **Mirror** (which has no numeric params) gets the button on its **Pivot / Merge Mode** combos (the Axis checkboxes are a mutually-exclusive group, so they're intentionally left alone). The default is resolved from the panel's `StateManager` at click time, so it needs no per-field wiring — one `self.sb.add_reset_buttons(self.ui)` (or `(self.ui, "cmb000-1")` for Mirror) in each slot `__init__`, right after the existing `connect_multi`/preview wiring. **Curtain** dropped its private `_add_param_reset_buttons` loop and now calls the same `sb.add_reset_buttons(self.ui, skip=("s025","s026","s027"))` (the X/Y/Z Position triplet still skipped — it shares a tight row with **Get**); the reusable batch helper now lives in uitk (`Switchboard.add_reset_buttons`, see its CHANGELOG). Verified headlessly by loading each real `.ui` through a uitk `Switchboard` and asserting every parameter widget received a `ResetOption` (Bevel 2, Duplicate Linear 12, Radial 17, Grid 4, Bridge 5, Cut On Axis 2, Curve to Tube 4, Mirror 2 combos); the helper itself is unit-tested in uitk.
- **Duplicate Grid — fixed the preview deleting the user's source object, rewrote the build for speed, and gated bulk grids behind a confirmation.** Three issues:
  - **Source destroyed on preview rollback (both instance and non-instance modes).** `duplicate_grid` grouped the originals into a temp node and flattened the copies *through world*, which vacated the originals' short names and let a copy collide with them ("More than one object matches name"). Preview's path-based diff then couldn't tell them apart and deleted the source — its UUID was gone after toggling Preview off (verified in Maya). The rewrite **never reparents or renames the originals** — it reads their bounding box in place and only duplicates them — so there's no name collision and the source is untouched (matching how `DuplicateLinear`/`DuplicateRadial` already work). `DuplicateGridSlots` also now declares `MUTATES_SELECTION = True` for the same instance-shared-shape UUID-restore backstop as linear/radial.
  - **Slow builds.** The old path built a deep nested hierarchy and then **flattened it one cell at a time** (an `O(X·Y·Z)` recursion of per-cell `cmds.parent` + `cmds.ls`). The rewrite replicates *flat* along each axis via a new `DuplicateGrid._replicate_axis` (per axis: duplicate the running group once per step, offset, batch-reparent its children up) for `O(X+Y+Z)` command calls and no post-build flatten, and batches the isolation-set add into one call. Typical grids are now sub-second (10×10×10 ≈ 1s; 10×10×5 ≈ 0.3s). Profiling confirmed this is the best available approach — a per-cell `cmds.instance` loop is ~40× slower (parenting into a growing group is quadratic), and nesting-then-single-flatten is worse still (deep instance hierarchies are slow to duplicate/delete); the residual superlinear cost at very large N is intrinsic to Maya's instance duplication.
  - **Accidental huge grids.** `DuplicateGridSlots._confirm_bulk` prompts (`sb.message_box` Yes/No) before building when the total copy count exceeds `BULK_THRESHOLD` (1000). The approved magnitude is cached so a confirmed build doesn't re-prompt on every preview refresh; declining during a live preview switches the preview off (deferred, since `disable()` no-ops mid-refresh) so dragging the count up doesn't re-prompt each tick.
  - Covered by `test/test_edit_tools_duplicate.py`: `TestDuplicateGridPreviewRollback` (source + its shape survive enable→disable→refresh in both modes; scene left clean) and new `TestDuplicateGrid` cases (no reparent/rename of the source, grid positions, negative-direction layout, `MUTATES_SELECTION` declared).
- **Duplicate Radial — fixed the first/last copy overlapping on a full revolution.** A 360° sweep (`end_angle - start_angle` a multiple of 360) made the end angle coincide with the start, so the inclusive-endpoint spacing (`i / (num_copies - 1)`) placed the last copy exactly on top of the first — `num_copies=6` over 0–360 looked like 5, and the two stacked copies at the start angle drifted apart when one was moved ("starting object duplicated twice in the same position"). `DuplicateRadial._create_and_transform_instances` now detects a whole-revolution sweep and drops the shared endpoint (divides over `num_copies` instead of `num_copies - 1`), so a full circle yields `num_copies` evenly-spaced, non-overlapping copies; **partial arcs are unchanged** — they still keep both endpoints (3 copies over 0–180 stay at 0/90/180). Covered by `test/test_edit_tools_duplicate.py::TestDuplicateRadial` (`test_full_circle_does_not_overlap_first_and_last`, `test_partial_arc_keeps_both_endpoints`).
- **Curtain — rail Position fields drop the X/Y/Z prefixes for red/green/blue value coding.** The `s025`/`s026`/`s027` Position spinboxes lost their `"X "`/`"Y "`/`"Z "` prefixes (`curtain.ui`); `CurtainSlots._color_code_position_fields` now tints their value text via the uitk `set_text_color` helper using the shared `pythontk.Palette.axes()` (Maya/3D RGB convention: x=`#E06666`, y=`#6AA84F`, z=`#6FA8DC`). The row stays compact and reads per-axis at a glance, and the existing tooltips still name the axis as a textual fallback. `_align_spinbox_prefixes` naturally skips the now-prefixless fields, so the per-section value alignment elsewhere is unaffected. Verified end-to-end by loading `curtain.ui` through a uitk `Switchboard` with the theme's `QAbstractSpinBox`/`QLineEdit` color rules applied and pixmap-checking the rendered text: the fields promote to `DoubleSpinBox`, carry empty prefixes, and render red/green/blue. (Required a uitk fix so `set_text_color` tints at the spin-box level — a color set on the embedded line edit is overridden by the theme's `QAbstractSpinBox` color; see uitk CHANGELOG.)
- **Curtain — *Hanging Points* now maps ~1:1 to visible folds; the rail header reads as one clean pleat per point (was two cusps per fold).** The body fold density and the top gather/pinch frequency were both tied 1:1 to the spans, but a fold reads as an *out*-ridge (~2 spans), so the rail showed ~2 pinches per fold — busy and unnatural (e.g. 12 points → 6 folds but 12 cusps). A new `CurtainMesh._BELLY_HUMPS_PER_SPAN = 2` constant **decouples** the two: the belly now runs that many half-sine humps per pleat-span (2 = one out-bulge + one in-recess = **one full fold**; `_drape` belly → `sin(π · _BELLY_HUMPS_PER_SPAN · phase)`; sway envelope tracks the same phase), while the catenary **sag + push-pull gather still fire once per hang point** — one clean pleat at the rail. So the dialed hang-point count now reads ~1:1 as folds (you set roughly the fold count you want, at **half** the old value) with **half the top cusps**. To keep the depth from ballooning when each span doubles in width, the sag width is **normalized by `_BELLY_HUMPS_PER_SPAN`** (`sag_width = span_world / _BELLY_HUMPS_PER_SPAN`, the per-hump width), so halving the dial reproduces the previous sag/gather depth rather than deepening it — the "smoother transition at the top" with the rest of the look unchanged. `_resolve_resolution` adds `× _BELLY_HUMPS_PER_SPAN` columns (≥8 per hump) so the doubled belly stays resolved. **Migration:** the shipped presets and the `.ui` default were re-pointed to preserve their prior fold count (Shower Curtain 12 → 7; Stage Swag 4 → 3, floored at 3 — and its gravity bumped 0.7 → 0.93 so the now-wider 2-span swag keeps its old depth; `.ui` default 8 → 5), so presets/Reset-to-Defaults look the same; an existing **saved scene value will now read as ~that many folds** (re-dial to taste — e.g. a saved 12 → set 6). Covered by `test/test_curtain.py::TestFoldsPerPleat` (pinches == hanging points, out-ridges == spans i.e. doubled vs the old half-hump-per-span, sag depth normalized to the per-hump width).
- **Curtain — every parameter field gained a *reset-to-default* button (uitk `ResetOption`).** Each spinbox now carries a small option-box icon button: a plain click resets the field to its default, and **Alt/Ctrl+click** bypasses it to default (greyed out) and restores your value on the next click — a one-click way to reset or temporarily neutralise a single parameter. Bypass is non-persistent (each session starts with all parameters active). Wired via `CurtainSlots._add_param_reset_buttons` looping `sb.option_box.set_reset()` over the Hanging/Drape/Folds/etc. spinboxes (the X/Y/Z **Position** triplet is skipped — it shares a tight row with **Get**); the default is resolved from the panel's `StateManager` at click time, so no per-field wiring. The reusable plugin lives in uitk (see its CHANGELOG). Because the option-box wrapping reparents each spinbox into its own container, **`_align_spinbox_prefixes` now buckets by the nearest `CollapsableGroup` ancestor** (not the immediate parent) so the per-section value-column alignment survives the wrapping.
- **Curtain — random hanging-point *spacing* (`hang_jitter` + `hang_seed`, Hanging group `s023`/`s024`).** The hanging points were always evenly spaced; `hang_jitter` (0–1) now randomizes the *interior* spacing (the outer ends — and a closed rail's seam — stay pinned so the cloth still spans the full rail), with `hang_seed` picking the pattern. Spans become uneven, and a wider gap bellies and **sags further** (the catenary now scales by the span's own width, not `total/spans`). The drape's span math was refactored behind `CurtainMesh._make_hang_points` (the u-positions, computed in `__init__` so the offset helpers always have them) + `_span_at(u)` (maps a rail position to its `(span, local t)` via `bisect`); `_drape`/`_sway_offset` use it and mid-fold forks anchor on each hang point's *actual* (jittered) position. Jitter caps each interior shift at 0.4 of a span, so points stay strictly ordered (no zero-width spans). **Identical to before at `hang_jitter=0`** (`_span_at` reproduces the old `u*spans` phase — covered by a test). New `test_curtain.py` cases: off-is-uniform, interior-perturbed/ends-pinned/strictly-increasing, seed-changes-pattern, drape-shifts, and phase-parity.
- **Curtain — Set Position for the generated rail + a *Get* button that grabs the selection's bbox center (Rail group, `s025`/`s026`/`s027` + `b002`).** The generated rail was always centered at the origin; a Position (X/Y/Z) now sets where it's centered, and a small **Get** button to the right of the fields fills it from the **bounding-box center of the selected object(s)** (combined world bbox via the `BoundingBox` helper; ignores the panel's own auto-rail; sets all three fields with signals blocked then fires one re-drape). `Rail.make`'s `y` param was generalized to `center=(x, y, z)` (no external callers used `y`); `_field_rail` passes the Position through and `_sync_driver`'s rebuild signature now includes it so moving the rail re-syncs the driver curve. Covered by `MakeRailTest` (center offsets the straight rail's midpoint / the closed ring's center, keeps it level at center-y).
- **Select Result is now a first-class Preview feature (like the Preview/Create buttons), not per-panel boilerplate.** `core_utils/preview.Preview` gained two optional constructor args — `select_result_checkbox` and `result_provider` (a callable returning the operation's current result node name(s)). When both are passed, Preview owns the whole feature itself: it (de)selects the result after **every** preview build and on **commit** (deferred), per the toggle, and wires the checkbox live — so any panel on the Preview system gets Select Result by passing two kwargs instead of hand-wiring it. On commit the apply runs **after** `finalize_func` (so cleanup that changes the selection — e.g. Curtain discarding its auto-rail — can't clobber the result); during preview it runs right after the operation, outside the contract. Default off → fully backward-compatible. **Curve to Tube and Curtain migrated** to it: each now passes `select_result_checkbox` + `result_provider=lambda: self.last_*` and **drops** its `_apply_result_selection` method, the `perform_operation`/`_finalize` apply-calls, and the `chk00N.toggled` wiring (Curtain's `_finalize` is now just the auto-rail discard). The selection mechanics still live in the shared `apply_result_selection` primitive (`object_mode=True` always, since the result is selected as an object and any leftover component selection is collapsed first). Coverage moved to the right layer: new `test/test_preview.py::TestPreviewSelectResult` (on/off, live toggle, component-selection clear, unconfigured no-op, provider-error safety, full enable→commit lifecycle, plus primitive missing-widget fallback + deferred-reread-settles); the panels' duplicated selection tests were removed (preview 28 / curtain 51 / curve_to_tube 24, all green).
- **Select Result — fixed a preview started with the toggle *off* still showing the result selected (shared `apply_result_selection`).** Same class of bug as Curve to Tube: an op can leave its result in a **component** selection (the Curtain build ends with `polySoftEdge`, which leaves the mesh's edges selected; Curve to Tube's seam cut leaves map components) — and a transform-only `select(deselect=True)` can't clear that, nor does `selectMode(object=True)` collapse an existing component selection (verified in mayapy). The shared `core_utils/preview.apply_result_selection` now, under `object_mode`, **collapses any leftover component selection onto the result objects (`select(replace=True)`) before deselecting**, so "off" reliably clears it. `CurtainSlots._apply_result_selection` now passes `object_mode=True` (it previously omitted it — that's why the curtain preview-initial-off still selected). Covered by `test/test_curtain.py::SelectResultTest::test_off_clears_leftover_component_selection` (seed a `.e[0:5]` selection, toggle off → nothing selected); Curve to Tube's 28 selection tests stay green (the collapse is a no-op there since its `create` already pre-collapses to an object selection).
- **Curtain — mid-folds run nearer the top + wider/varied widths; Sway cap raised 3 → 6.** The mid-fold forks previously faded in over a wide top fade (`_MIDFOLD_FADE` 0.12), leaving the top ~12 % of the drop fold-free; the top and tip fades are now **decoupled** — a small `_MIDFOLD_TOP_FADE` (0.04) ramps the fork in just under the rail so it reads nearly to the hooks, while the wider tip fade still peters out the lower end smoothly. The **rail row itself (v = 1, the hook line) stays pinned** (the small fade is still zero exactly at the rail). Fork **widths widened and vary more** (`width_frac` ranges 0.18–0.42 / 0.45–0.85, was 0.18–0.30 / 0.35–0.55) so forks affect a bit more area with more variety; selection/anchoring/randomization unchanged. The **Sway** slider max is raised from 3 to 6 (`s020`; `CurtainMesh.sway` was already unclamped above). Covered by `test_midfolds_run_to_near_the_top` (longest fork is near-full strength just below the rail) alongside the still-green `test_midfolds_pin_top_edge` (rail row untouched) and the relief/anchor/push-both-ways tests.
- **Curtain — added a push-pull *Gather* control at the hanging points (`round_gather`, Drape group `s022`, beside Round).** `round_points` only ever *eases* the cusp into a smooth dome — it reduces sag near each pin but can't rise above the rail or dip back, so it pushes without pulling. Gather adds the missing pull: the fabric puckers **up** above the rail right at each hanging point (the *push*) and **dips** lower just inside it as the slack falls off (the *pull*), easing back out by mid-span so the center sag is untouched — the characteristic ripple of a gathered/pleated header. It's a separate dial (default off, independent of Round), so existing presets/curtains are unchanged. The shape lives in the **pythontk primitive** (`MathUtils.catenary_sag` gained a `gather` arg — the `ring²·(2.4 − 3.4·ring)` lobe, `ring = cos²(πs)`: −1 at the support, +0.18 dip just inside, 0 at center); `CurtainMesh` gained the `round_gather` param and `_drape` threads it through `sag_profile` (calibrated ×0.5 so a full slider lifts the pin ~half a sag-unit, matching the other shaping dials). Covered by `test/test_curtain.py` (`test_gather_puckers_up_at_pins_and_dips_inside` — vs a no-gather build, lifts up at the pins, dips inside, leaves in/out x·z untouched) and pythontk `test_math.py` (push-above-baseline / pull-inside / center-untouched / off-matches-catenary).
- **Curtain — added a lateral *Sway* fold control (`sway` + `sway_seed`, Folds group `s020`/`s021`).** The existing folds only push the cloth in/out (along the in-plane normal); Sway additionally leans a **random subset** of the folds **left or right along the rail** (the in-plane *tangent*), with each fold's direction and amount random per seed — so pushed-in and -out areas drift sideways, not just in/out. The lean rides the belly envelope (`|sin(π·phase)|`, zero at the pinned hang points so adjacent oppositely-leaning spans can't tear, peak mid-span) and grows toward the free hem. `CurtainMesh` gained `sway`/`sway_seed` params, `_make_sway` (per-span signed leans, ~half non-zero) and `_sway_offset`; `_drape` now threads the rail tangent and adds `tan * lateral`. Old presets are unaffected (Sway defaults off). Covered by `test/test_curtain.py` (off→empty, random-subset structure, seed-changes-pattern, pinned-at-hang-points, and a straight-rail build that shifts vertices along X but not in/out Z).
- **Curtain panel — added a Select Result toggle (`chk005`, under the preset combo).** Mirrors Curve to Tube: with it on the curtain is selected on commit (and live during preview) so you can see the result; with it off it's explicitly **deselected**. `_finalize` no longer unconditionally `cmds.select`s the curtain — selection is re-asserted (after the auto-rail discard) per the toggle. The select/deselect logic was promoted to a **shared `core_utils/preview.apply_result_selection(widget, results, *, object_mode, defer)` primitive** (re-reads the checkbox each pass so a settled toggle wins; `object_mode` for ops that leave component selection; immediate + optional idle re-assert); both `CurtainSlots` and `CurveToTubeSlots._apply_result_selection` now delegate to it (DRY — was duplicated). The panel's previously-unused **footer now reports the result's triangle count** (`_update_footer`, updated each build; default hint until then), like Curve to Tube. Covered by `SelectResultTest` + `FooterTest` in `test/test_curtain.py` (on selects / off deselects / missing-widget fallback / live toggle / tri-count readout).
- **Curve to Tube — fixed live-polygon normals flipping when the control curve is edited (curveWarp deformer drive).** Root cause (localized via `test/temp_tests` repros): `cmds.extrude`'s swept-surface normal orientation is **not invariant under path edits** — a large enough control-CV move globally flips the NURBS surface normal (confirmed: surface outward-fraction 1.0 → 0.0), which `nurbsToPoly` and the build-time `_conform_poly_outward` (`polyNormal` reverse) faithfully propagate, inverting the live mesh. No `cmds.extrude` option fixes it (swept all 16 combos of `useProfileNormal`/`fixedPath`/`reverseSurfaceIfPathReversed`/`useComponentPivot` — every one flips) and no baked `polyNormal` mode fixes it (any baked reverse-or-not is frozen at build time while the input orientation isn't). **Fix:** open polygon tubes now build a **straight, already-conformed base mesh** (same `extrude → nurbsToPoly` topology — exact sides, RDP rings, caps, UV seams — laid out along +X with rings at the RDP points' cumulative-chord positions scaled to the driver's arc length) and drive it with a **`curveWarp` deformer** keyed to the (resampled) source curve. A deformer only *repositions* vertices, so the face winding — and the normals — can never flip on a live edit; the warped cross-section is also rounder than the old extrude (constant radius vs the extrude's pinch/bulge on bends). Editing the curve re-warps; baked builds collapse the deformer into the mesh. Live and baked still share topology, and a deformer leaves UVs untouched, so the seams/shells are unaffected. Closed (torus) paths and the no-plugin fallback keep the curved `extrude → nurbsToPoly` build; finishing steps are shared via the new `_finish_poly_tube`. Adds a `curveWarp` plugin dependency (ships with Maya; loaded on demand by `_load_curvewarp`, with graceful fallback). New `_polygon_tube_warped`; `test_live_polygon_normals_stable_on_curve_edit` drives four aggressive edits (incl. the `cv[0]` +5y that used to invert to ~2.5% outward) and asserts the capped tube's signed volume stays positive (outward); `test_live_polygon_source_curve_resampled_and_drives` now asserts a `curveWarp` node in the live graph.
- **Cylinder/tube UV unwrap spun off into reusable `UvUtils` helpers (`uv_utils/_uv_utils.py`).** The seam logic that was private to Curve to Tube now lives on `UvUtils` as a shared, testable surface: `get_cylinder_seam_edges(mesh, sections=None, invert_seam=False, cap_faces=None)` returns `(length_loop, cap_rings)` — one lengthwise edge loop plus the ring(s) where each end cap meets the body — handling **open tube** (boundary → rim-vertex seed), **capped cylinder** (caps as explicit indices *or* auto-detected n-gons, so it's exact for any section count when the caller supplies them), and **closed torus** (the loop whose edge count differs from `sections`). `invert_seam` seeds the loop from the diametrically-opposite start-ring vertex so the seam lands on the back side. `cut_cylinder_seams(objects, invert_seam, history)` cuts those seams (`polyMapCut`); `unwrap_cylinder(objects, invert_seam, unfold, orient, map_size)` cuts then unfolds (Unfold3D) + orients to lay the body out as a clean strip and each cap as its own shell. Covered by `test/test_uv_utils.py` (`TestUvCylinderUnwrap` — capped → 3 shells/topology-preserving, open → 1 strip, invert → opposite-side disjoint loop, seam-edge counts).
- **Curve to Tube polish (`nurbs_utils/curve_to_tube.py`).** UV seaming now **delegates to `UvUtils.get_cylinder_seam_edges`** (caps are closed first, capturing the new cap faces by index so seams are exact for any section count — no more capturing rims before `polyCloseBorder`). Smoothing is now **by angle**: the body is softened (`polySoftEdge 180`) then the **cap rings are re-hardened** (`polySoftEdge 0`) so the tube shades smooth while the cap edges read crisp. The **open ends are now pinned across Path Res**: RDP keeps a closer first-interior point at finer tolerances, swinging the endpoint tangent and visibly rotating/tilting the end rings as Path Res changed; a fixed end-tangent anchor (`_END_ANCHOR_FRAC`, one sample ~3 % in from each open end) holds it, dropping end-ring drift from ~11 %-of-radius to ~0 (only the interior re-tessellates now). The `CurveToTubeSlots` panel gained a **Select Result** toggle (`chk004`, beside Keep History): on it selects the finished tube(s) so you can see the tessellation, **off explicitly deselects** them (the build can leave the new mesh selected, so "off" actively clears it). The (de)select is applied as the final action of **every build — preview AND commit** (`perform_operation`, deferring via `evalDeferred` only when `contract is None`, the Preview's sanctioned commit hook), so the toggle governs the live preview selection too, not only the committed result; on commit it is also the operation's final action (not a separate `finalize_func`), immune to the operation's own leftover selection. **The preview previously ignored the toggle entirely** — `create` always selects its result (to clear the seam components) and the (de)select was only applied on the commit replay, so previewing with Select Result off still selected; applying on every build fixes it (Curve to Tube installs no selection scriptJob, so a preview-time (de)select can't trigger a rebuild). The `chk004` read is defensive (falls back to selecting if the widget is absent) and is **re-read inside the apply on every invocation** (not snapshotted once): on the commit path the `lowestPriority` deferred re-assert then runs *after* any deferred state restore settles and re-reads the correct value, so the settled choice wins. Covered by `test_selection_respects_toggle_preview_and_commit` (real Preview, both phases, both outputs) and `test_commit_reread_settles_after_restore` (deferred re-read). **Root cause of the toggle "having no effect":** the polygon build's `polyMapCut`/`polySoftEdge` on the seam edges left those **edges selected in component mode**, so `select(transform, deselect=True)` couldn't clear it (polygon "stayed selected") and a later NURBS object-selection didn't show (NURBS "never selected"). `CurveToTube.create` now ends with `_reset_object_selection(results)` — force object mode + select the result transform(s) — so every caller (script or UI) gets a clean object selection, and the commit (de)select also forces object mode defensively. `chk004` is also wired **live** (`chk004.toggled → _apply_result_selection`) so toggling the option immediately (de)selects the current result instead of only being consulted at the next commit (`_apply_result_selection` takes `*args` to absorb the signal's bool). Covered by `test_create_leaves_clean_object_selection` (only the result transforms selected, no lingering components), `test_chk004_toggle_applies_immediately`, and the commit-toggle test driving the real `create`. The panel's **footer now doubles as a live stats readout** (`_update_footer`, called after each build): polygon → `tris · faces · verts`, NURBS → span counts, multi-curve prefixed with the tube count; it updates as the preview re-sweeps and shows a hint when idle. Covered by `test_update_footer_reports_stats`. **Caps now defaults OFF**. Covered by new `test_polygon_caps_have_hard_edges` (cap-ring edges hard, body soft) and `test_polygon_ends_fixed_across_path_res` (end-ring vertices invariant across Path Res), plus the existing UV-seam/topology tests (straight-minimal counts bumped +2 for the end anchors).
- **New tool — Curve to Tube (`nurbs_utils/curve_to_tube.py`).** Sweeps a circular profile along selected NURBS curve(s) into a tube, output as a **NURBS surface** or a **polygon mesh** (the `CurveToTubeSlots` panel's `cmb000` picks the type and greys out the options that don't apply). `CurveToTube.create(curves, output_type, radius, sections, path_divisions, degree, caps, quads, live, cleanup)` is pure geometry (classmethods returning node names), callable straight from a script. **NURBS** path extrudes a `circle` along the curve (`extrudeType=2`, component-pivot + fixed-path + profile-normal); it **self-conforms outward** by reversing the U direction when a mid-path ring's normal points inward (the extrude could emit either orientation depending on how the curve was drawn — it was coming out inverted) and sets a fine **display smoothness** (the viewport `3` preset) so the surface renders smooth, not faceted. **Polygon** path places rings by **RDP curvature simplification**: the curve is densely sampled and run through `pythontk.MathUtils.simplify_rdp` (Ramer-Douglas-Peucker), which keeps points where the path bends and drops near-collinear runs. A working curve is built **through** those kept points (`cmds.curve`, periodic-closed for loops), the profile circle is extruded along it, and `nurbsToPoly(uType=2, uNumber=sections, vType=1)` converts the surface to *exactly* `sections` sides around with one ring per span — so rings land **only** at the curvature-selected points, dense on tight bends, sparse on straight runs, and **decoupled from the source curve's CV spacing** (`path_divisions` / "Path Res" scales the RDP tolerance as a fraction of arc length). Building *through* the points (rather than `insertKnotCurve`, which kept the curve's uniform CV-span baseline and over-tessellated the gentle bends) is the key — on a tight-arc-vs-gentle-arc test it concentrates ~4.5× more rings on the tight bend at a far lower total count. The mesh gets `polySoftEdge(180)` for smooth shading and a normal-conform (`polyNormal`) so it faces outward regardless of curve direction, and is **UV-seamed** for a clean unwrap — one `polyMapCut` along the length plus each cap's rim, so a capped tube is 3 UV shells (body strip + 2 caps; the rims are captured on the open tube before `polyCloseBorder` makes them internal). A **Keep History** toggle keeps the construction chain (collapsed to a baked mesh otherwise). For a live polygon the source curve **is resampled in place** to the RDP points (`cmds.curve -replace`, identity/UUID preserved) so it stays the editable live driver; the hermetic `Preview` restores it on rollback (`CurveToTubeSlots.PRESERVE_GEOMETRY = True`, see the Preview generalization below). The hidden inputs (profile circle, NURBS surface) are parented under the tube. A baked polygon builds a throwaway path through the points and leaves the source curve untouched. Live and baked share the same topology. All paths are `cmds`-based, so a committed tube is a single clean Ctrl+Z; a closed/periodic curve makes a watertight torus. (Earlier iterations tried procedural reshaping, Sweep Mesh, `insertKnotCurve`, and `rebuildCurve rebuildType=4` — none combined exact sides, a clean tube, *and* strong bend concentration the way RDP-through-points does.) Driven by the hermetic `Preview`, validated to require a NURBS curve. Registered `nurbs_utils.curve_to_tube: CurveToTube`; launched from tentacle's NURBS panel (**Curve to Tube** button, `b058`). Covered by `test/test_curve_to_tube.py` (19 tests — exact sides around, RDP bend-focus + **known tight/gentle-arc concentration ratio**, path_divisions monotonicity, identical live/baked topology, outward normals, watertight caps/torus, **UV-seam shells**, source-curve resampled-and-drives, clean single-undo, validation).
- **Preview `PRESERVE_GEOMETRY` generalized from meshes to meshes + NURBS curves (`core_utils/preview.py`).** The hermetic Preview's in-place geometry restore (`CleanupContract`) — which snapshots preserved originals and, on rollback, rebuilds any that survived but were mutated in place — was mesh-only (`_mesh_signature`/`_restore_mesh_in_place` returned/handled nothing for non-mesh shapes). Renamed to `_geo_signature` / `_geo_diverged` / `_restore_geo_in_place` and taught them NURBS curves (signature = spans/degree/form + world bbox; restore = feed the duplicate's `local` into the original's `create`, then `delete -ch`). This is what lets Curve to Tube resample its source curve in place under a live Preview and have it restored cleanly on toggle-off / refresh. Mesh behavior is byte-for-byte unchanged (the mesh branch is the original logic); `test_edit_tools_geometry.py` (CutOnAxis preserve-rollback), `test_preview.py`, and the Mirror/Duplicate preserve paths stay green.
- **`EditUtils.dissolve_coplanar()` — planar decimation (limited dissolve).** Angle-driven, feature-preserving complement to `decimate`: walks each mesh's interior edges (OpenMaya) and `polyDelEdge(cleanVertices=True)`-merges those whose two faces are within `angle_tolerance` degrees of coplanar, leaving creases/corners/silhouette/borders untouched and keeping clean n-gons (no triangulation). At a tiny tolerance it's **lossless** on hard-surface meshes (a 5×5×5 subdivided cube → 6 faces, bounding box unchanged); curved surfaces are left alone (use `decimate`/QEM there). Tentacle's Subdivision **Decimate** button (now `tb000`) exposes both algorithms behind an option-box combobox — Reduce (QEM %) vs Planar (Coplanar) — with the irrelevant options greyed out per selection. Tests: `test_edit_utils.py` (cube→6 lossless, sphere untouched).
- **`EditUtils.decimate()` — reusable polyReduce helper; Curtain math promoted to pythontk.** Extracted the scattered `polyReduce` logic (curtain's mesh reduce + the Subdivision panel's Reduce button) into one boundary-preserving **`EditUtils.decimate(objects, percentage, preserve_borders, preserve_hard_edges, preserve_uv_borders, preserve_quads, symmetry, …)`** (undoable, deletes history by default; early-returns at `percentage <= 0`); the Curtain generator's `reduce` now routes through it, and tentacle's new Subdivision **Decimate** button calls it. Separately, the Curtain module's general math/signal primitives were promoted to `pythontk.MathUtils` (the SSoT): `smoothstep`, `ricker`, `catenary`/`catenary_sag`, `safe_normalize`, vector `lerp`, and the coherent grain generator (now `pythontk.BandLimitedNoise`). `curtain.py` consumes those via thin aliases (`catenary_shape`/`sag_profile` remain importable for back-compat) and keeps only the curtain-specific `_v_arms` fold composition local. No behavior change — the noise is bit-identical (same seed/octave draw order); `test_curtain.py` (43) + `test_edit_utils.py` (+2 decimate tests) stay green.
- **Curtain — real "Mid Folds" V-forks, renamed creases, dropped dead seed/presets.** The old **Mid Folds** slider only added V-shaped creases — it never *folded* anything — so it's renamed **Creases** (`creases` + `crease_seed`, was `mid_folds`/`fold_seed`; behavior unchanged). **Mid Folds** is now a genuine adjustment matching how a heavy gathered drape actually forks (`mid_folds` intensity + `mid_fold_seed`): on a seeded random ~1/4–1/2 of the *interior* hang points, a downward **V** apexes *on* the hang point and fans its two arms out and down into the neighbouring spans, interrupting their plain in/out belly. Lengths vary (some stop high, some run nearly to the hem) as do width/depth (narrow-deep vs wide-shallow); the seed does most of the look's work (`_make_midfolds`/`_midfold_offset`). Modelled in global `u` (continuous across hang points by construction — no per-span cracks) on a shared `_v_arms()` primitive that also backs `_crease_offset`. Each fold arm is a **Ricker/Mexican-hat wavelet** (`_ricker()`, the zero-integral negated 2nd-derivative-of-gaussian used for wrinkle synthesis), so a crease pushes the cloth *out* at its line and *in* to either side — material-conserving the way fabric buckles, rather than ballooning one way (fixes folds reading only as outward bulges); arm spread + fold width are span-relative so the fork shape is invariant to hang-point count, while depth stays absolute. The dead **Seed** field (`seed`) is gone — it was never wired into the engine and drove only the always-subtle grain/jitter, which now uses a fixed internal seed (`_BASE_SEED`). **`irregularity`** is now **coherent, band-limited** surface grain — a few smooth zero-mean wave octaves (`_make_billow`/`_billow_offset`, 1/f amplitude roll-off, horizontal frequency leading vertical so it reads as soft vertical-ish wrinkles) — replacing the old per-vertex white noise that read as static; the per-vertex RNG is dropped from `_drape`. UI: `grp_folds` reordered to Mid Folds (`s019`, new) / Mid Fold Seed (`s010`, repurposed from the dead Seed) / Creases (`s014`) / Crease Seed (`s015`) / Irregularity (`s008`). Dropped the **Flat Backdrop** and **Booth Ring** built-in presets (Stage Swag + Shower Curtain remain, with their creases preserved). Tests renamed (`test_creases_*`) and extended (`test_midfolds_*`, incl. direct checks that forks anchor on a hang-point subset and leave the top edge pinned).
- **New tool — Curtain (`edit_utils/curtain.py`).** Procedural draped-cloth generator, split by responsibility (SRP): **`Rail`** (rail geometry — generate / resolve-from-selection / sample / measure / resample), **`CurtainMesh`** (the *deformation* — drape a grid into pleated, gravity-sagged cloth), and **`CurtainRig`** (the *rig* — `_add_wire` deformer + `_add_clusters` controls, orchestrated by `attach`). The cloth hangs from a *rail* — a selected NURBS curve, polygon edge path, locator chain, or a generated **straight** rail (bow it with `curvature`; the default is flat) — and is pinned at N evenly-spaced **hanging points**. Each hanging point is a pleat (the fabric gathers there); the spans between them belly into folds and sag down a true **catenary** (`y = a·cosh(x/a)`, `catenary_shape()`) — wider gaps fall further, scaled by `gravity`. Controls: `height`, `hanging_points`, `gravity`, `tension` (catenary shape; peak stays at 1 so `gravity` owns the depth), **`round_points`** (round the sharp cusp at each pin into a smooth dome — `sag_profile()` blends the catenary toward `sin²`), `fullness`, `taper` (top-gather ↔ hem-flare), **`mid_folds`** + **`fold_seed`** (seeded **V-shaped creases** that radiate down from random points near the top and run various lengths — the diagonal break-lines of gathered fabric), **`end_bend_left`/`end_bend_right`/`end_bend_falloff`** (signed per-end sideways bend with an edge ramp — e.g. panels curling toward the camera), `irregularity` (now dialled much subtler — the deliberate relief comes from fullness/mid_folds), `seed`, `density` (segments/unit), `reduce` (polyReduce decimation %), `thickness`, **`invert`** (reverse normals), `closed`. Mesh built once via `polyPlane(ch=False)` + `MFnMesh.setPoints`; vector math delegates to `ptk.MathUtils`. `CurtainSlots` drives it through the hermetic `Preview` (live re-drape via `connect_multi`); a rail is **auto-created on preview** when none is selected (so you never have to select an unrelated object) and dropped on commit. In generated mode the drape reads **Width/Curvature live each refresh** (previously the captured auto-rail froze them, so they appeared to do nothing); the owned driver curve resyncs only when a rail-shaping field actually changes. The **preset combo lives in the panel** (`cmb000`); ships built-in presets (Flat Backdrop, Stage Swag, Shower Curtain, and a closed-ring Booth Ring) through uitk's `PresetManager` (built-in tier from `edit_utils/presets/curtain/`). The panel groups its fields into collapsible `uitk.CollapsableGroup` sections (Rail, Hanging, Drape, Folds/Noise, Ends, Mesh — the niche Folds/Noise and Ends start collapsed, each section's open/closed state persisted across sessions) with spinbox value columns aligned per group via font metrics (`_align_spinbox_prefixes`). Mesh-softening is always on (no toggle). `CurtainRig` (wire deformer + per-CV cluster controls) remains a tested library primitive for programmatic rigging but is no longer wired into the panel. Registered `edit_utils.curtain: (CurtainMesh, Rail, CurtainRig)`; launched from tentacle's Deformation panel. Covered by `test/test_curtain.py` (35 tests).
- **Cut On Axis preview — fixed cuts stacking on historyless meshes.** Changing a value (Cuts/Offset/Axis/Pivot) while Preview was on added a *new* cut without rolling back the previous one — on frozen/imported meshes only. Root cause: `polyCut(ch=True)` on a mesh with no upstream history spawns an intermediate orig-shape that holds the only pristine copy; the hermetic preview's node-diff rollback deleted that orig-shape (counted as a "created" node), which *baked* the cut into the visible mesh instead of reverting it. Fix is in the shared preview contract: a new `PRESERVE_GEOMETRY = True` opt-in (set on `CutOnAxisSlots`) makes `CleanupContract` snapshot the operated meshes and, on rollback, restore in place any that survived but diverged from the snapshot — preserving the original shape's identity/UUID and skipping the restore when node-diff already reverted (so meshes *with* legitimate history keep it). Regression coverage in `TestCutOnAxisPreviewRollback` (`test_edit_tools_geometry.py`) drives the real `Preview` through enable→refresh→disable on a historyless cube. Mirror / duplicate (`MUTATES_SELECTION`) paths unchanged.
- **Marmoset bridge — Toolbag engine bundled back into `marmoset_bridge` (reverses the `pythontk` carve-out below).** The DCC-agnostic engine had been moved up to `pythontk.marmoset_utils`, but a Toolbag-SDK engine isn't a generic pythontk utility, so it now lives with its consumer (mirroring `substance_bridge`). `mayatk.mat_utils.marmoset_bridge` is again self-contained: `_marmoset_engine` (`MarmosetEngine`), `templates/`, `_toolbag_helpers`, `toolbag_log`, `template_params`, and `marmoset_rpc` (+ in-Toolbag `plugin_src`) all live here; `MarmosetBridge` subclasses the local engine. Import the live-session RPC client from `mayatk.mat_utils.marmoset_bridge.marmoset_rpc` (was `pythontk.marmoset_utils.marmoset_rpc`). Public `MarmosetBridge` API unchanged. The `test_toolbag_helpers` / `test_marmoset_rpc` suites moved into `test/mock_tests/`; mock + live bridge tests updated.
- **Marmoset bridge split — engine moved to `pythontk.marmoset_utils`** (breaking, no shim). `MarmosetBridge` is now a thin `pythontk.marmoset_utils.MarmosetEngine` subclass that keeps only the Maya glue — FBX export of the selection, the `MatManifest` material sidecar, and DAG-based high/low bake-pairs classification — and overrides `send(objects=...)` to delegate to the engine with the produced file paths. The Toolbag-side helpers, `templates/`, `marmoset_rpc`, and the log/launch/render logic all moved upstream. Import the live-session RPC client from `pythontk.marmoset_utils.marmoset_rpc` (was `mayatk.mat_utils.marmoset_bridge.marmoset_rpc`). `parameters.py` (AttributeSpec widget specs) + the Switchboard slots/`.ui` stay here; the slots' log-helper imports now come from `pythontk.marmoset_utils.toolbag_log`. Mock + live bridge tests updated (37 mock tests pass).
- **Texture Path Editor — symmetric relocate modes + Reload Scene Textures** — follow-up to the consolidation. Three changes to round out the path-management workflow:
  - **Set Directory** (`tb_set_texture_directory`) gains an option-box combobox: *Leave textures in place (path only)* (default) / *Copy textures to new directory* / *Move textures to new directory*. Same same-name+same-size collision protection as Normalize Paths — different-size collisions skip-and-warn rather than silently rebinding to a wrong file.
  - **Find & Copy Textures** (`tb_find_and_copy_textures`) gains a Copy/Move combobox; the button text auto-syncs to match the active mode (*Find & Copy Textures…* ↔ *Find & Move Textures…*) so the active verb is visible on the menu item itself. Move uses `MatUtils.move_texture_files(delete_old=True)`.
  - **Reload Scene Textures** — new plain QPushButton under General that calls `MatUtils.reload_textures(refresh_viewport=True)` so Maya re-reads every file/aiImage/pxrTexture/imagePlane node from disk. Useful after Find & Copy / Normalize Paths relocate files.
  - Tooltip cleanup: removed "(▸)" option-box hints (it's a known UI element); Resolve Missing tooltip now names the cascade order explicitly (`Stem → Texture → Fuzzy`); Normalize combobox labels say "external textures" instead of bare "externals".
- **Tool help text migrated from header-menu "Instructions" buttons to the new `Header.set_help_text(...)` API** — every Switchboard tool used to bury its instruction tooltip behind an `Instructions` `QPushButton` in `widget.menu` (under a `Separator → "About"` divider). The new uitk `Header` exposes the help text via a leftmost `?` button in the header bar; hovering shows the tooltip and clicking pops it open. 38 mayatk slot classes migrated (audio_clips, blendshape_animator, shots, shot_manifest, shot_sequencer, color_manager, exploded_view, bevel, bridge, cut_on_axis, duplicate_{grid,linear,radial}, dynamic_pipe, mirror, naming, snap, hierarchy_manager, reference_manager, scene_exporter, workspace_map, hdr_manager, game_shader, image_to_plane, marmoset_bridge, mat_updater, render_opacity, shader_templates, substance_bridge, texture_path_editor, channels, image_tracer, shadow_rig, telescope_rig, tube_rig, wheel_rig, calculator, rizom_bridge). See [`m3trik/docs/HEADER_HELP_INVENTORY.md`](../m3trik/docs/HEADER_HELP_INVENTORY.md) for the full migration list and the uitk-side implementation notes.

- **Texture Path Editor consolidation** — collapsed the path-management menu surface from 13 entries to 5 (4 header + 1 context). Mirrored global/row commands replaced with smart scope (selection if any, else all). The six "Resolve Missing by …" variants fold into one **Resolve Missing Textures** action button with strategy checkboxes (Stem / Texture / Fuzzy; cascade in safest-first order, stop at first hit) in its option_box flyout. **Set Directory** / **Find & Copy Textures** / **Normalize Paths** replace `lbl010` / `lbl_find_copy` / `lbl013` plus their per-row twins. **Normalize Paths** is new behavior: rewrite paths under sourceimages to relative without moving files, UDIM-safe, with an option_box combobox controlling externally-located valid textures — *Leave untouched* (default), *Copy to sourceimages*, or *Move to sourceimages*. Collision policy: same-basename + same-size in sourceimages = safe rebind without overwriting; different size = skip + warn (never silently rebind to a wrong texture, never destroy the external). The two action items that actually carry options — **Normalize Paths** (`tb_normalize_paths`) and **Resolve Missing Textures** (`tb_resolve_missing_textures`) — are uitk `PushButton`s (`tb_*` per convention) auto-wired by name, with per-button option_box flyouts populated by matching `_init` methods (standard pattern, cf. `naming_slots.tb003_init`, `audio_clips_slots.tb001_init`). Plain action items stay as `QPushButton` wired via `clicked.connect`. No inline menu state widgets, no modal dialogs. Added **Select Absolute Paths** as a counterpart to Select Broken Paths for previewing what Normalize would touch. Removed slots: `lbl010`, `lbl_find_copy`, `lbl013`, `lbl014`, `lbl015`, `resolve_missing_by_{stem,fuzzy,texture}`, `row_set_texture_directory`, `row_find_and_copy_texture`, `remap_to_relative`, `row_resolve_by_{stem,fuzzy,texture}` — replaced by `set_texture_directory`, `find_and_copy_textures`, `tb_normalize_paths`, `tb_resolve_missing_textures`, `select_textures_for_objects`, `select_broken_paths`, `select_absolute_paths`, `open_source_images`. Any custom hotkeys or scripts referencing the old slot names will need rebinding.
- **Channels wheel-scroll — symmetric ladder + axis-swap fix** — wheel-scroll on a channel-box value cell now mirrors uitk `SpinBox`'s symmetric ladder. The two user-visible bugs both root in the table layer:
  - **Alt-held wheels did nothing**: `TableWidget._emit_wheel_scrub` read only `event.angleDelta().y()`, which is 0 on some Qt builds when Alt is held (Qt transposes the delta onto `.x()`). The widget now reads `delta.y() or delta.x()`, so Alt and Ctrl+Alt both fire reliably. (Fix in uitk; the channels module is just the consumer.)
  - **Ctrl+Shift fell to the default 0.1 step**: the old `_wheel_step` matched modifiers with `(mods & MASK) == MASK`, which can return False under PySide6 when the live `KeyboardModifiers` flag set is compared to a `KeyboardModifier` flag value. Detection now uses `bool(mods & SPECIFIC)` per modifier, making the comparison robust to PySide5/6 type quirks.
  - **Modifiers source**: `_on_wheel_scrolled` now takes the modifiers from the `cellWheelScrolled` signal payload (added in uitk) rather than polling `QApplication.keyboardModifiers()`. Removes a race against the wheel event.
  - **Ladder** (matches uitk `WheelStepMixin`):

        plain         ×1       (default)
        Ctrl          ×10      (coarse)
        Ctrl+Shift    ×100     (very coarse)
        Alt           ÷10      (fine)
        Ctrl+Alt      smallest representable step (10⁻ᵈᵉᶜⁱᵐᵃˡˢ; for ints = 1)

    For int attrs `Alt` collapses to 0 (no sub-1 step exists) -- the wheel notch is honestly a no-op there. The earlier *Ctrl+Alt = snap-to-integer* iteration was reverted: it visually matched `Ctrl` for any integer-aligned starting value, so it looked broken even when working correctly. The smallest-step gesture is now distinct from `Ctrl` regardless of value alignment.
  - **Breaking**: `WHEEL_MOD_FINE` / `WHEEL_MOD_SMALLEST` class attributes (overrideable in subclasses) are gone; per-app hotkey customization will need a different mechanism if/when it's needed.

- **RizomUV Bridge unwrap fix + hard/organic split** — replaced the broken `unwrap.lua` (passed `Auto={HardEdge=true, ..., Skeleton={}}`, which RizomUV silently ignores → zero seams selected → mesh re-imported as one welded shell, i.e. "all edges sewn"). Two new presets ship: `unwrap_hard.lua` (driven by `Auto={SharpEdges={AngleMin=…}, QuadLoopCutter=true, ...}`, canonical Titus/3ds-max signature) and `unwrap_organic.lua` (QuadLoopCutter off, higher default angle, leans on HandleCutter / PipesCutter / StretchLimiter). New `SHARP_ANGLE` parameter exposes the dihedral threshold in the panel.
- **RizomUV Bridge pack fix** — wrapper `ZomLoad` was `XYZ=true` (positions only), so incoming UVs were dropped on load. Pack/optimize then ran on zero islands and the file round-tripped with degenerate UVs ("UVs gone"). Wrapper now loads `XYZUVW=true, UVWProps=true` to bring mesh + existing UVs through together. Matches the C4D bridge and Titus batch-load signatures.
- **RizomUV Bridge pack attributes exposed** — added five user-tunable `ZomPack` knobs: `PACK_RESOLUTION` (texel-density baseline), `PACK_MAX_MUTATIONS` (solver iterations), `PACK_ROTATE_ENABLE`, `PACK_TRANSLATE`, `SCALING_MIX`. Hardcoded `Translate=true` and the absent `Rotate.Enable`/`MaxMutations`/`Resolution` are now driven from the panel; preset Lua across `pack`/`optimize`/`unwrap_hard`/`unwrap_organic` updated in lockstep. `Margin` and `Quality` remain absent (still crash 2020.1, comment in `parameters.py`).
- **RizomUV Bridge FBX UV-set + normalize fix** — wrapper was passing `FBX=true` (not a real Rizom flag — silently dropped, UV-set name not preserved) and omitting `NormalizeUVW`, which defaults to `true` and rescales the incoming layout to 0-1 (corrupts pack/optimize input). Wrapper now emits the canonical `FBX={UseUVSetNames=true}` nested table (matching the adevra Maya-bridge and Titus commented reference) and explicit `NormalizeUVW=false` on load. Fixes "UVs come back gone" on optimize/pack round-trips where the round-trip writes UVs into a different UV-set name than the bridge then reads back.
- **RizomUV Bridge optimize-preset rewrite + Resolution combobox** — `optimize.lua` now uses the Titus optimize signature: `ZomIslandGroups({Mode="DistributeTilesContent", FreezeIslands=true, UseTileLocks=true, UseIslandLocks=true, GroupPaths={"RootGroup"}})` followed by a `Scaling={Mode=0, Mix=true}` + `LayoutScalingMode=0` `ZomPack`. Layout-preserve invariants are hardcoded so the pre-scale / layout-scale / mix-scale widgets auto-hide for this preset — relax/rotate/recursion/resolution/mutations stay user-tunable. `PACK_RESOLUTION` is now a `choice` combobox (256/512/1024/2048/4096/8192) instead of a free-form int spinbox.
- **RizomUV Bridge version-gated params** — `MaxMutations` / `Resolution` / `Rotate.Enable` and the FBX-load `FBX={UseUVSetNames=true}` table all crash RizomUV 2020.1 with an access violation (same failure mode as the already-skipped `Margin` / `Quality`). Bridge now parses the version out of the install path (`RizomUV 2020.1` → `(2020, 1)`) and uses `MIN_VERSIONS` in `parameters.py` to gate them: the panel hides the affected widgets and `_construct_full_script` strips the corresponding lines before send. Gate threshold is `(2022, 0)` — adjust downward if an earlier release is confirmed to support these. Slot wires the strip in via a `_VersionedParamsProxy` so `referenced_keys` is version-aware everywhere it's called.
- **RizomUV Bridge optimize-preset second pass + better errors** — even with version-gated params, `optimize.lua` was still crashing 2020.1; the remaining culprits were the Titus-style `ZomIslandGroups({Mode="DistributeTilesContent", FreezeIslands=true, UseTileLocks=true, UseIslandLocks=true, GroupPaths={...}})` block plus the `AuxGroup` / `WorkingSet` keys on `ZomPack`. Preset now mirrors `pack.lua`'s `DistributeInTilesEvenly` / `MergingPolicy=8322` / `GroupPath` (singular) pattern but keeps the layout-preserve intent via scalar-only `Scaling={Mode=0, Mix=true}` + `LayoutScalingMode=0`. Tile-assignment preservation is lost (was Titus-only) but the relax+repack workflow works on 2020.1. Wrapper also drops the speculative `NormalizeUVW=false` (Titus Batch reference omits it with XYZUVW). Crash error message now includes the tail of Rizom's stdout/stderr and the detected version — the bare `exit code N` was useless for debugging.
- **ReferenceManager filter toggle migration** — `txt001_init` now uses `uitk.ToggleOption` (via `option_box.set_toggle`) instead of a hand-rolled two-state `ActionOption`. Eliminates the local enable/disable cycle wiring; the toggle owns its visuals (red when disabled, per `pythontk.Palette.status()["error"]`) and emits a single `toggled(bool)` signal connected to `_toggle_filter`.
  - **Persistence fix**: filter state now survives across Maya sessions correctly. The previous `set_action(states=…)` form auto-persisted `current_state` keyed off the widget's `objectName`, but `self.controller._filter_enabled` reset to `True` on every session start — producing a visual/flag desync where the icon could read "disabled" while the controller had reverted to enabled, requiring two clicks to actually disable. The new wiring gives the toggle a dedicated persistence key (`"reference_manager_filter"`), then syncs `self.controller._filter_enabled` from the toggle's restored state at `txt001_init` so the icon and predicate flag always agree.
- **GameShader Affix Hardening** — `_create_single_network` now applies the user prefix/suffix via the new `pythontk.StrUtils.apply_affix` so `Mat_brick_Albedo.png` no longer yields `Mat_Mat_brick`, and trailing/leading underscores are collapsed. `create_network` also routes `prefix`/`suffix` into `MapFactory.prepare_maps` so batch-mode set keys group consistently regardless of whether source filenames already carry the prefix.
- **PyMEL Removal Complete** — production code, type hints, docstrings, narrative comments, docs, and packaging metadata fully scrubbed of `pymel`/`PyMEL`/`PyNode` references. `mayatk` runs on `maya.cmds` + `maya.api.OpenMaya` exclusively. Migration tracker (`PYMEL_MIGRATION.md`) retired.
- **Render Opacity Pre-Export Safety Net** — added `RenderOpacity.prepare_for_export()` (per-object or scene-wide) that mirrors animated `opacity` onto `visibility` for every object missing the dual-key. Closes the silent-failure path where hand-authored opacity animation produced an empty Unity controller. Idempotent; preserves manually-keyed visibility (skips when vis_keys ≥ opa_keys). Updated stale `test_no_opacity_attr_falls_back_to_visibility_only` to reflect the production auto-promote-to-opacity design.
